Veyra City · Realism upgrade report

From prototype look to GTA V days and Cyberpunk nights

A whole-game audit of what makes Veyra City look like a toy today, why it happens in the code, and the verified fixes, order of work and acceptance tests that take it to a believable open world on an RTX 4070 at 1440p.

1. Executive summary

Verdict

Veyra City scores about 2.5 out of 10 against GTA V by day and Cyberpunk 2077 by night. The foundations are good: the city is laid out from real data, roads and bridges drive cleanly, cells stream, and there is an automated test and capture harness. What you see on screen, though, is first-pass placeholder content. 92 % of the buildings are flat boxes with painted windows. Trees are under 500 triangles, the grass is identical tufts, and the water is an opaque plane that reflects only the sky. Rain is unlit lines, the people are built from boxes, and the project contains no recorded sound at all.

On top of that, a handful of settings bugs make it look worse than it is:

  • Gameplay runs with no temporal anti-aliasing, because GameFlow forces MSAA at start-up, and that switches STP/TAA off.
  • An uncommitted LOD bias of 0.4 shows low-detail models five times closer than intended.
  • Road repairs render as hard black polygons.
  • A fake diagonal light grid is painted on the night sea.
  • Neon signs use a 5×7 debug font.

The honest message: tuning will not take this to GTA V. The last 22 world iterations show it: weeks of adjusting procedural pines and tufts moved the score very little. The content itself has to be replaced with properly modelled, textured and lit assets, and the renderer needs global illumination, exposure, height fog, reflections and volumetrics. That is a multi-month production plan (chapters 16-18). About twenty fixes of under a day each will still visibly improve the game this week.

Scores by area

AreaScorevs 10 = GTA V / CyberpunkFindingsCriticalHighWhy
Buildings, facades and skyline2.5161992 % of buildings are flat, low-poly shells with shader-painted windows, one-template suburbs, box landmarks and a dark night skyline, so only the procedural window and interior system and a few kits lift it above a greybox.
Trees, bushes and forests2.51416Trees under 500 triangles that are static, unlit from behind, repeated clones and alias at the edges read as a mobile or kids' game, nowhere near GTA V forests or Cyberpunk street greenery.
Grass, ground cover and terrain2.51216Sparse identical card clumps on noise-generated procedural soil, flat basemap hills, and no clutter, wind, wetness or biome identity put the ground roughly at an early-2000s or mobile level, far from GTA V or Cyberpunk.
Water: sea, lake and shore21224By day the water is a flat, sky-only, opaque tinted plane that never shows the skyline, hills or bed; at night it is near-black with a fake diagonal light grid, far from GTA V harbour water or Cyberpunk's wet-neon reflections.
Rain, smoke and other effects2.51415Rain, wet roads, spray, tyre smoke, steam, lens drops and nitro all exist, but each is an unlit blob or line generated in code, with no lighting, no contact with the world and no flipbook detail. Wet nights look like rain lines pasted over a mostly dry scene.
Lighting, sky, fog and post-processing2.51226By day the image is clean but flat and CG, with no GI, no sky occlusion, no distant shadows and no real sky. Nights are crushed to black with clipped emissives and no wet reflections. That is far from GTA V by day or Cyberpunk at night.
Image quality: the pixelated look3.51215The pipeline settings are close to AAA, but gameplay runs with no temporal AA, a 0.4 LOD bias, aliased procedural facades, a debug pixel font on signs and low-resolution terrain, so in motion the image reads as shimmering, blocky and soft, far below GTA V or Cyberpunk 2077.
Roads, street detail and signage31315The road geometry, markings and kerbs are correct, but hard-edged procedural patches, uniform asphalt, no decals, sparse primitive furniture, no road signage and pixel-font neon put streets far below GTA V by day and Cyberpunk by night.
People, traffic and cars2.51125The only convincing vehicle is a real-brand-derived download, while the people are primitive mannequins and traffic is low-poly with frozen wheels, no drivers and sprite lights, far below GTA V/Cyberpunk 2077 crowds and traffic.
Performance, pipeline and development process3.51416Good foundations (perf harness with stats.json and verify, time-sliced cell streaming, HLOD v1, PSO warm-up), but four things leave almost no budget for AAA content: draws are submitted from a CPU-bound main thread with no GPU Resident Drawer or occlusion, street content is generated at runtime, texture streaming is off, and the LOD bias drift plus uncontrolled perf runs make the measurements unreliable.
Beyond graphics: audio, HUD and world life31424The driving camera and HUD layout are competent. But audio is a placeholder synth in a silent world with no music, and traffic, pedestrians and activities lack the reactivity and density that make GTA V and Cyberpunk feel alive.

Why it looks like a kids' game: your list, answered

What you sawWhat is actually wrong (root cause in the code)What fixes it
Buildings2,494 of 2,708 buildings (92 %) are C#-extruded shells with one quad per wall. Windows, frames and sills are painted on by the facade shader, so the sun lights every wall evenly and there is no self-shadowing. All 1,923 suburban houses (71 % of the city) use one gable-box template. A 3 m procedural texture repeats, with no per-building colour, weathering or decals. All 11 landmarks except the lighthouse are generic boxes, and the 214 kit models are stretched up to 53 %. (BLD-01..08)A modular facade generator with real depth (15-30 cm reveals, sills, cornices, balconies) on shared trim sheets, fitted to the data footprints. A 16-archetype house kit, a storefront set, 10 hand-made landmarks, decals and vertex wear, LODs and impostors.
Trees5 procedural meshes under 500 triangles each serve 67,499 trees. Foliage uses stock URP Lit: no translucency, no leaf normals, no wind, and a hard alpha test that speckles. Far forests become uniform stipple, and LODs switch per cell with a visible pop. (VEG-01..08)Real trees (SpeedTree, or licensed scanned trees, 8-25k triangles at LOD0 plus impostors). A Veyra foliage shader with translucency, wind and dithered alpha. Species palettes per district, undergrowth, and GPU-driven instancing.
GrassUnity terrain-detail tufts scattered evenly at one density, bright lime green on bare brown soil, with no wind and no ground tint. Land cover comes from Perlin noise, not the map data. Terrain textures are procedural noise at 3.9 m per splat texel, and distant hills are a flat olive basemap. (TER-01..06)A GPU ground-cover renderer whose density comes from a land-cover map baked from Veyra data, with a grass shader that has a root tint, AO and wind. Scanned CC0 terrain sets, a height-blend terrain shader with macro colour and normal maps, and ground clutter.
WaterThe water is opaque and Lambert-lit, and reflects only the sky. The sea is a flat 24 km plane with two sine waves faked in the normals. A 512² ripple map repeats every 9 m, and there is no depth colour, refraction or shore foam. At night a world-fixed diagonal light grid is painted on. (WAT-01..06)Transparent water with depth absorption and refraction from the camera textures, a low-resolution planar reflection plus probes, and wave geometry (FFT/Gerstner clipmap, or Crest 5). Intersection and shore foam, rain rings, and real night reflections.
SmokeAll effects are Shuriken particle systems built in C# with textures drawn by SetPixel at runtime. Tyre smoke is a faint, flat, unlit blur that glows white at night. There is no VFX Graph, no flipbooks and no lighting. (VFX-03, VFX-05)VFX Graph with six-way lit smoke from baked flipbooks (8×8, 2048²), soft particles, and correct night response.
RainA 6,000-particle box around the camera falls at 24 m/s (real rain falls at about 9 m/s), unlit and one colour. It has no splashes and no occlusion under bridges. Wetness exists only in the road shader, so facades, cars and trees stay dry. The puddle ripples form a regular grid. (VFX-01, -02, -04, -07)40-60k GPU streaks in VFX Graph with depth-collision splashes and a baked rain-occlusion map, one shared wet layer in every shader, a precomputed ripple texture, and rain mist plus volumetric haze.
"Pixelated"Several things combine:
  • no temporal AA in gameplay (IQ-01)
  • an LOD bias of 0.4 (IQ-02)
  • hard step() edges on the painted windows, which form moiré (IQ-03)
  • hard alpha test on tiny leaves (VEG-03)
  • a 5×7 bitmap font on every neon sign (RD-04)
  • 3.9 m terrain texels and a 2 m basemap from 450 m (IQ-04)
  • bilinear filtering and no mip streaming (IQ-07)
STP with MSAA off at render scale 0.87, LOD bias 2, analytic anti-aliasing in the facade shader, dithered foliage alpha with coverage-preserving mips, SDF text for signs, and higher terrain resolution with 16x aniso and mip streaming.
PeoplePedestrians are built at runtime from box, sphere and limb meshes, swing their limbs like sticks, and are not skinned. (LIFE-01)Rigged, textured humans (for example the MIT-licensed Rocketbox library, or Mixamo-retargeted characters) with a tiered crowd: skinned near, baked animation in the middle distance, impostors far away.
Lighting (the hidden cause)There is no global illumination (a flat ambient set by script), exposure is fixed with an ACES toe that crushes nights, fog is one global term that turns aerial views white, and there is no real sky. Wet streets have no reflections, and the night has only 48 weak unshadowed lights. (LGT-01..07)Adaptive Probe Volumes with day/night scenarios, custom auto-exposure, analytic height fog with aerial perspective, a physically based sky, 300-350 m shadows, a larger and smarter night light budget, and volumetric fog.

Fix this week: the quick wins (each under a day)

FindingChangeVisible result
IQ-01GameFlow.cs:1063-1071: stop forcing MSAA 2x/4x into the URP asset. With STP on, write MSAA 1 and set only the render scale per tier (0.67 / 0.77 / 0.87 / 1.0), and move the tier values into one versioned asset.Temporal AA comes back: far less shimmer on leaves, windows and thin lines.
IQ-02PC_High lodBias 0.4 → 2 (ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:203, uncommitted). Make the WorldBuildBatch camshot restore the value in a finally block.Full-detail trees and buildings come back at normal distances.
RD-01RoadWear.cs:84: _PatchAmount → 0 until a feathered patch texture exists.The black polygons disappear from every road.
WAT-02Delete the night "cityGlow" streak block (VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:89-95) and sample the realtime sky cubemap directly.No more diagonal orange grid on the night sea.
BLD-09RooftopDetails: skip the parapet ring and roof slab on stepped and hangar records (BUG-123).The floating steel frame over the plaza tower goes away.
VFXWorldEnvironment.cs:584: rain falls at 10 m/s instead of 24, drifts with the wind global, and its rate scales with intensity. VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:87: ripples about 9× smaller, with only ~30 % of cells firing.Rain reads as falling drops, and the ripple grid disappears.
LGT-02 / -08Night exposure about +1.5 EV with emission reduced by the same factor. Fog, haze and street lights follow the rendering camera (WorldEnvironment.ViewCamera). Capture times become 07:30, 12:00, 18:45, 19:45, 22:00 and 22:00 wet.Nights become readable instead of black, the aerial white-out goes away, and golden hour is finally reviewed.
IQ-03Facade shader: an fwidth-based anti-aliased step instead of step() for windows, mullions and frames.No more saw-tooth and triangle patterns on towers.
RD-04Neon stopgap: draw the sign atlas from an OFL TrueType font at 64-96 px cap height instead of PixelFont.Signs stop looking 8-bit.
IQ-04 / IQ-07Terrain: alphamap 512, basemap 1024 shown up to 1,000 m, pixel error 2. Texture importer: 16x aniso on ground and facades, and coverage-preserving mips on foliage and grass.Sharper ground and no balding grass or leaves at distance.
VEG-09 / TERPlaza bushes use the tree foliage material. Grass blades get a darker base, random start rows, and coverage-preserving import.The giant glossy paper leaves go, and tufts sit in the ground.
LIFE-02Switch the default and review car from the badge-stripped real production car (GameFlow.cs:277) to an original one. Delete the centred tail-light spot (CarLampFX.cs:70-84).Removes a legal risk from every capture and the floating red disc at night.
VFX-10Lens drops and speed streaks off by default in the chase camera. Dim tyre smoke at night (VehicleFX.cs:236, 409).Less arcade overlay, and no white-glowing smoke at night.

Each change is described in full in its finding card. Re-capture all 18 cameras afterwards (at the new times) and compare with the gallery in chapter 3.

The big moves, in order of look gained per week of work

  1. Phase 0, settings and measurement (1-2 weeks). Quick wins, one owner for quality settings, release-build measurement, and review captures at golden hour, dusk and night.
  2. Phase 1, shader parity and GPU-driven rendering (3-5 weeks). The custom Veyra shaders cannot receive APV, decals, cookies, light layers or the GPU Resident Drawer today. Fix that first, then enable the GPU Resident Drawer, GPU occlusion and texture streaming to pay for everything else.
  3. Phase 2, light the world (4-6 weeks). APV global illumination with day/night scenarios, auto exposure, height fog, a real sky, longer shadows, decals, a wet layer on every surface, reflections, and volumetric haze.
  4. Phase 3, nature and water (6-10 weeks). New trees and foliage shader, GPU grass and ground cover from data, the terrain shader, and the water rebuild.
  5. Phase 4, the city (10-16 weeks). Modular buildings, the house kit, storefronts, landmarks, street furniture, signage, HLOD and impostors, and the D7 race venues.
  6. Phase 5, life, effects and sound (8-12 weeks). VFX Graph rain and smoke, real people and traffic cars, and recorded audio with a mixer and ambience.

Decisions only you can make

  • Real-brand cars (BUG-066, LIFE-02, LIFE-10). The player car in every capture is a badge-stripped real sports car. CC-BY covers the modeller's mesh, not the manufacturer's design or trade dress. CLAUDE.md says real-brand cars are never used. Recommendation: drop the five rebadged cars, and keep the 17 "homage" models out of hero use until each is reviewed.
  • Kit buildings from real-city packs. Several CC-BY building packs replicate New York, San Francisco and Chicago and may carry real landmarks or shop names. CC-BY also requires in-game credits, which exist only for cars today. Recommendation: replace them with the original modular kit (Phase 4) rather than invest in fitting them.
  • Buy or build. Water (Crest 5 versus a custom shader), trees (SpeedTree versus scanned trees from Fab or Megascans), volumetric fog (an MIT package versus commercial). Chapter 17 lists the options with their licences.
  • Unity 6.7 LTS. Supported SSR, GTAO and real-time Surface Cache GI arrive in 6.7. Per CLAUDE.md, upgrade only at a milestone boundary, on a branch.

2. How this review was done

Evidence base

  • Captures: the latest review captures from the Dev player at 2560×1440, PC_High tier: Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21 (18 cameras at 12:00 and at 22:00 wet) and Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22 (4 cameras after the last pine change).
  • Code and assets: the code, shaders, Blender tools, URP and quality settings, packages, logs and stats.json files of the current working tree (HEAD 68da8b6 plus ~40 uncommitted files from another session).
  • Project docs: PROJECT_STATUS, BUG_TRACKER, WORLD_ITERATION_LOG, and the two earlier audits (AUDIT_AAA_COMPARISON, VISUAL_BENCHMARK_REPORT). Their claims were re-checked against today's code, not copied.

Process

  1. 11 area audits, each reading the relevant code and screenshots. Every finding needs a file and line, or a screenshot location.
  2. Independent verification of all 76 critical and high findings by a second, sceptical reviewer: 48 confirmed, 28 corrected (the corrected text is what is printed), 0 refuted, and 14 further findings added. Medium and low findings are marked self-checked.
  3. Four web-research tracks (174 sources):
    • how GTA V builds its daytime look
    • how Cyberpunk 2077 builds its wet neon night
    • which features Unity 6.6 URP really has
    • production methods, budgets and asset licences
  4. A completeness critic that looked for gaps, contradictions between areas, and weak findings. Its output is folded into the plan (chapter 16) and Appendix A.
  5. My own spot checks of the claims that drive the plan:
    • the runtime MSAA override
    • the LOD bias
    • the road patch strength
    • the night-water streak code
    • the rain speed
    • the pixel-font signs
    • the absence of audio files and of VFX Graph
    • the missing shader keywords
    • the measured frame budget

How to read a finding card

Each card gives what the player sees, the root cause in the code (with file and line), the fix, its cost, the evidence, and whether it is already tracked in BUG_TRACKER.

  • Severity: critical means it alone breaks the AAA look. High means clearly visible in normal play.
  • Effort: S is under a day, M is 1-3 days, L is 1-2 weeks, XL is more than 2 weeks. All are estimates for one experienced developer.
  • Impact: rated 1-5 by how much the look improves.
  • Editor notes: marked in yellow. They correct cross-area details, such as Unity 6.6 feature availability or duplicates.

Limits

  • Stills only. Shimmer, LOD pops and motion are judged from the code, not from video.
  • Noon only by day. All daytime captures are at noon, so golden hour and dusk could not be judged. That is itself a finding (LGT-08).
  • Laptop measurements. Frame timings come from a laptop RTX 4070 in Dev builds. Some runs used the Silent power plan (BUG-120), which distorts CPU numbers, not GPU numbers.
  • Estimated costs. Every millisecond and week figure is an estimate until measured.
  • Partial web access. Some research pages could not be fetched; those claims are marked uncertain in the caveats.
  • Licence terms change. Check each one on the day you download an asset.

3. The game today, in pictures

All images below are unedited frames from the Dev player's own review capture (2560×1440, PC_High tier), taken today at 12:00 and at 22:00 in rain. They are the same 18 review cameras the project uses for every card, so every claim in this report can be re-checked with one command (VeyraCity.exe -reviewCapture).

What the eye picks up first (in the order a player notices it)

  1. Wrong scale and repetition: leaves as big as hands, identical trees, identical tufts, identical window grids. Real places never repeat this regularly.
  2. Missing micro-detail and clutter: no litter, signs, poles, cables, gutters, cracks, puddle edges, wildflowers or parked cars between the hero objects.
  3. Lighting that does not tie objects together: no bounce light, little ambient occlusion, no haze gradient by day, and black voids at night.
  4. Primitive figures and simple effects: box people, line rain, tiled ripples and flat water read as placeholders.
  5. Aliasing: alpha-tested foliage, fine window grids and an upscaled pixel font crawl and shimmer, which players describe as "pixelated".

4. Buildings, facades and skyline

Score today: 2.5 / 10   92 % of buildings are flat, low-poly shells with shader-painted windows, one-template suburbs, box landmarks and a dark night skyline, so only the procedural window and interior system and a few kits lift it above a greybox.

State (2,708 data records, Logs/C/assembler.txt 2026-11-11 11:57). 2,494 buildings (92 %) are BuildingGenerator shells and 214 (8 %) are kit models: 15 distinct of 78 usable CC-BY Sketchfab pieces, placed in D1 (134), D2 (65) and D6 (15). The shells average about 150 tris each (371,760 tris / 2,494, estimate). Each one is a single quad per wall with a 0.3 m cornice and plinth band. Houses are gable boxes with a porch, the 26 stepped towers all use the same 3-tier template, and towers get a steel crown box. The 1,923 suburban houses (71 %) are one template. Kits average about 6k LOD0 tris, and 208 of them are stretched up to 53 % in height.

Facade depth comes only from the shader. VC_Facade draws windows, frames, sills, mullions, slab lines and shopfronts as step() masks on flat walls. A single-box interior mapping fakes the room depth. There are no real recesses, sills, mullions or balconies.

Textures are procedural (PaletteRecipes): 2048² maps covering 3 m (about 680 px/m), repeated 4 × 4.7 times per 12 × 14 m UV tile. There are 12 facade materials with one window rule each (3 m bay, 3.5 m floor), no per-building tint, and grime only as a street-level band plus streaks under sills. There are no decals (BUG-118). Kit textures are 512-4096 px JPG/PNG, capped at 2048 for a whole building.

Ground floors are a 4.5 m storefront band wrapped round all four sides, plus shops painted by the shader. Roofs get runtime flat-colour boxes in the middle third of the roof and a parapet round the full data box; that parapet causes the BUG-123 floating frame. Massing is 594 boxes, the 26 identical stepped towers and 11 landmarks kept as plain shells (Veyra Spire is a 52 × 52 × 342 m box).

At night the shells show seeded windows, but 0 of 134 kit materials emit and towers have no crown lighting. Shells have a single LOD, and HLOD merges those same boxes.

Why it looks fake:

  • Silhouette: extruded rectangles.
  • No self-shadowing: without reveals, cornices or balconies the sun lights each wall evenly.
  • Weak scale cues: uniform 3.5 m floors, windows on gable ends, stretched kits.
  • Repetition: 3 m texture period and identical window grids.
  • Clean, plastic materials: no tonal drift, leaks or decals.
  • Poor lighting integration: coarse 128 px probes, and interior-mapping triangles where reflections should be.
  • Aliasing: hard step() masks and a 7 px bitmap sign font give the 'pixelated' look.

GTA V instead uses hand-built, trimmed and decal-weathered buildings with LOD/SLOD chains. Cyberpunk layers balconies, signage and lit architecture.

Production plan (durations are estimates, built with idempotent Editor tools, Veyra data as the envelope):

  • P0, 1 week: the quick wins, including fixing BUG-123 and kits at night.
  • P1, 5-6 weeks: a modular facade and massing system (FacadeAssembler) on 2-3 shared 4096² trim sheets plus 2048² tileables (256-512 px/m at street level). It covers 6 style families × 20-30 modules, a storefront set and a crown library. LOD0 budgets: midrise 10-30k tris, tower 30-80k. LOD1 is 40 % and LOD2 10 % with baked 512² facade pages.
  • P2, 3-4 weeks: a 16-archetype house kit for the 1,923 houses, 4-10k tris each, with yard dressing.
  • P3, 5-6 weeks: 10 hand-made hero landmarks, 20-120k tris each (Spire ≤ 250k, split into pieces ≤ 250 m).
  • P4, 2-3 weeks: HLOD atlases, octahedral impostors and architectural night lighting, then retire or calibrate the kits.

Frame budget: triangles p95 ≤ 8 M, SetPass p95 ≤ 450, p99 ≤ 16.6 ms. Building textures should stay at or below about 1 GB BC7 (estimate). Rollout order: D1 and D6 (hero cameras), then D5, D2, D3/D7/D8.

Findings and fixes (16)

BLD-0192 % of buildings are flat shells (one quad per wall) with painted-on windows critical effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Towers and blocks look like boxes wrapped in window wallpaper. Walls are perfectly flat, windows sit flush with the surface, corners are razor-sharp, and there are no sills, ledges or balconies (CAM_06 left tower, CAM_05 centre stepped tower).
Root cause

BuildingGenerator emits each wall as one quad and lets VC_Facade paint windows, frames and sills as masks. Real facades owe their realism to self-shadowing: 15-40 cm window reveals, sills, piers and cornices cast sun shadows, collect AO and catch specular edges. Without them sunlight lights the whole wall evenly and the silhouette stays a box. GTA V models every street-facing building with real recesses and trims; Cyberpunk adds layered balconies, AC units and signage geometry.

Fix

Add an idempotent Editor tool, Assets/_Veyra/Editor/FacadeAssembler.cs, that replaces Walls()/Box() with modular facades inside the data footprint and height. Per wall: snap bays to the wall length (window modules 2.7-4.2 m; corner and pier modules absorb the remainder). Floor heights per typology: ground 4.5-5.5 m, office 3.8-4.2 m, residential 3.0-3.2 m. Model the modules in Blender (Tools/Blender/vc_tools.py, VC_Unity_Prop): window reveal 0.15-0.3 m, sill and lintel, mullions 0.05-0.1 m, spandrel, balcony, cornice, plinth. Each module is 300-1,500 tris, UV'd onto 2-3 shared 4096² trim sheets. Merge per building and per material; far LODs merge per 250 m chunk. LOD0 budgets: midrise 10-30k tris, tower 30-80k; LOD1 40 %, LOD2 10 %. Keep the existing UBX data colliders. Pilot on about 20 D1 records around CAM_05/06, verify with the review captures and stats.json, then roll out district by district.

Cost
Estimate for a downtown view: about 40 buildings at LOD0, 120 at LOD1 and 300 at LOD2, roughly 3-4 M tris, inside the 8 M p95 budget (BUG-117). Merging per building keeps SetPass flat; recess geometry adds shadow-caster tris, so drop casting on LOD2.
Evidence
Logs/C/assembler.txt: records 2708; kit models placed 214; shells kept 2494; shells = 371,760 tris in 1,687 meshes (about 150 tris per shell, estimate)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BuildingGenerator.cs:96-112 Walls() emits one quad per wall side; :373-392 box/tower = Box() plus a 0.3 m cornice band and a 0.12 m plinth only (:384-390)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:127-137 punched windows are a step() mask on the flat wall; the only depth is the interior mapping (:31-57, :195-212)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png left half (2560x1440 px: x 0-1125): 30+ storey limestone shell, identical flush windows on one plane, the corner is a single hard edge
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png centre: the stepped tower is three extruded boxes with a uniform window grid
Tracked as
BUG-004
BLD-021,923 suburban houses (71 % of the city) are one gable-box template high effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Westhaven streets are rows of the same two-storey box with a flat-coloured roof plane and identical windows on every side (gable ends too). Eaves are paper-thin, and there are no gutters, garages, fences or yard clutter (CAM_16).
Root cause

One parametric shape with one proportion rule. Windows come from the same 3 m x 3.5 m shader grid as offices, so they ignore rooms, storeys and gables. Roofs are single planes with no thickness, fascia, gutters or tile normal, so they read as cardboard. GTA V suburbs (Mirror Park, Vinewood) use dozens of hand-built houses with garages, porches, fences, bins and parked cars: the variety of silhouettes, not of textures, is what sells them.

Fix

Build an original house kit in Blender. 16 archetypes (ranch, split-level, two-storey colonial, bungalow, duplex, townhouse row) × roof forms (gable, hip, cross-gable, dormers), all from shared parts: wall panels with real 0.1 m window frames and trims, 0.2 m thick roofs with fascia, gutters and downpipes, porches, garages, chimneys. Yard dressing from seeds: fence or hedge, bins, mailbox, AC unit, garage door, a parked-car slot. Budgets: LOD0 4-10k tris, LOD1 40 %, LOD2 10 %, HLOD box beyond 400 m; one 4096² house trim sheet plus 2048² tileables (siding, shingle, brick).

1,923 instances of about 16 meshes. Draw them instanced through the GPU Resident Drawer once the VC shaders support DOTS instancing (BUG-115), or merge per chunk at LOD1+. Estimate ≤ 1.5 M tris for a suburban street view.

BLD-03Ground floors are painted bands: no recessed shopfronts, awnings or doors; at night the shop glow blows out into one white strip high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
At street level shops are coloured stripes printed on a flat wall (CAM_06 left base) and a fence of flat panels on Westhaven apartments (CAM_16 left). At night Neon Mile has one continuous over-bright white band (CAM_13).
Root cause

Storefronts are the most-seen 5 m of a driving game, yet they come from the same flat UV mask as the upper floors, on all four sides. Without a 0.3-0.6 m recess, frames, awnings, door depth and readable signs there are no shadow lines and no parallax. At night every shop uses the same high emission and lit share, so the band merges into one clipped strip. GTA V and Cyberpunk build each shopfront as geometry with interior cards and individual lighting.

Fix

Add an original storefront module set (10-12 modules: café with awning, glass shop, shuttered unit, bank lobby, bar with neon frame, workshop, vacant unit with posters). Each module has a 0.3-0.6 m recess, 0.08 m frames, stall risers, door leaves, 0.6-1.2 m awnings and sign boxes. Place them only on walls facing a road (nearest segment from road_network_geometry.json, as NeonSignBuilder already does), one shop per 6-12 m; other sides get service doors, louvres and a plain plinth; no shops on D5 apartments unless their kind is retail. Interiors: a 2-layer parallax card per shop from a 4096² shop-interior atlas. Night: 2700-5000 K per shop, emission ≤ +3 EV over the lit wall, 15-25 % of shops dark; remove the ×1.35 boost (:242) and lower street litFrac 0.86 to 0.6 (:223). Budget: 1.5-4k tris per module on the shared trim sheets.

Cost
Estimate: 1,500-3,000 shop modules within 150 m downtown, instanced or merged per building. A parallax interior card is 1-2 texture samples, cheaper than today's room ray-cast. No realtime lights added.

BLD-04Procedural 3 m wall tiles, no per-building colour and almost no weathering high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Every building of a style has the same colour and pattern. Limestone blocks and brick courses repeat in a perfect grid, and walls are spotless except a dark band at the bottom, so buildings look new and plastic (CAM_06 left).
Root cause

Materials are math-generated (hash + fbm). The albedo is calibrated, but they lack the large-scale irregularity of real walls: sun bleaching, water runs from sills and cornices, soot, repairs, paint patches. 2048 px over 3 m spends resolution on micro grain while the 3 m period becomes the dominant visible pattern. GTA V hides tiling with vertex-colour blends, dirt decals and per-building trims; Cyberpunk layers materials with world-space grime and dense decals.

Fix
  1. Per-building tint: add a colour list to BuildingGenerator.MB, write a seeded tint (value ±12 %, hue ±4°, saturation ±10 %, from 6-8 swatches per style) into vertex colour and multiply it in VC_FacadeSurface. 2) Macro variation: two world-space noise octaves (20 m and 60 m) at ±8 % albedo and ±0.1 smoothness, as VC_Road's _MacroVariation already does. 3) Replace the 3 m recipes with 4-8 m tileables at 2048² (256-512 px/m), either CC0 scans with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads or Blender bakes, and use hex/stochastic tiling to hide the period. 4) Weathering: edge/AO masks baked into the trim sheets; streak masks under sills, cornices and roof edges; soot near the ground and vents. Add 30-50 original decals (leaks, patches, posters, tag-free graffiti) via the URP Decal feature (BUG-118), seeded per building within 120 m.
Cost
Vertex colour adds 4 bytes per vertex; macro noise is 2 cheap samples. Cap decals at 2-4 per building within 120 m (estimate < 0.5 ms GPU at 1440p with DBuffer).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaletteRecipes.cs:150 'Wall textures cover ~3 m x 3 m'; Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraPalette.cs:22 TexSize 2048 (about 680 px/m) and :115-116 Wall3m/WallBrick = 4 × 4.67 repeats per 12 × 14 m UV tile
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraPalette.cs:146-181: 12 Veyra/Facade materials, one fixed window rule each, shared by 2,494 shells
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:62 albedo = texture × per-material _BaseColor; Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BuildingGenerator.cs:36-39 meshes carry no vertex colour, so there is no per-building tint
VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:153-165 grime = a street-level band plus streaks under sills only; BUG-118: no URP Decal renderer feature

BLD-05Massing is extruded rectangles: the skyline reads as boxes with no podiums, setbacks or sculpted crowns high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
From the sea the skyline is a cluster of grey rectangular blocks of similar tone with flat tops. The tallest tower is a plain box with a thin mast, and large featureless cubes line the waterfront (CAM_03).
Root cause

Skylines are read by silhouette: podium/tower split, tapering setbacks, chamfered or notched corners, sculpted crowns, mechanical screens, and materials that catch light differently. Here each footprint is extruded straight to the data height, so every silhouette is a rectangle and towers differ only in height. Uniform grey values flatten depth. In GTA V and Night City every tall tower has a recognisable crown and shaft.

Fix

Add a massing grammar to the modular generator. The data footprint stays the outer envelope and the data height the top. Rules: a podium (1-4 floors, full footprint); a shaft inset 2-6 m on the street sides; 0-3 setbacks at 55-90 % height by typology; corner treatments (2-6 m chamfer, notch, rounded corner). For all 72 records over 100 m, take crowns from a 12-type original library (lantern, spire reaching the data height, mechanical screen, sloped glass, lit band, open frame, helipad), never the same crown within 500 m. Use 3-5 material families per district with value contrast (light stone, dark glass, bronze). Validate with a silhouette-variety report (unique outline hashes per chunk) and CAM_03/CAM_04 recaptures. Colliders stay the data UBX boxes.

Cost
Setbacks add only about 200-2,000 tris per tower. Crowns are 2-10k tris at LOD0 (LOD2 at 10 %) and need no extra materials when they use the facade trim sheets.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Data/world_building_placements.csv: shapes house 1,986, box 594, cylinder 92, stepped 26, hangar 6, tower 2, mast 2; 153 records over 60 m, 72 over 100 m
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BuildingGenerator.cs:361-371 every stepped tower uses the same 3 tiers (scale 1/0.78/0.56 at 55/82 % height); :391 and :443-452 Crown() = steel box + mast + red cube

BUG-004
BLD-06Curtain-wall glass shows a repeating diagonal two-tone pattern instead of reflections high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Glass towers look like a tiled grid of grey and green triangles (CAM_05 right-hand tower). Every pane shows the same flat ceiling/side-wall split; the glass shows fake rooms instead of reflecting the sky and neighbouring towers.
Root cause

From a low camera the view ray changes slowly across a facade, so every pane hits the same ceiling/side-wall edge of an identical 3 × 3.5 × 3.6 m box. With untextured flat faces that becomes a hard diagonal split repeated hundreds of times. Real curtain walls at street angles are dominated by reflections distorted per pane (pillowing), and 128 px probes on a 400 m grid cannot reflect neighbouring towers. Cyberpunk/Spider-Man-style interiors use textured, varied five-face rooms.

Fix
  1. Interior mapping v2 in VC_FacadeSurface: a five-face room atlas (back, sides, floor, ceiling per room type; 32 original rooms, 4096²) sampled with proper UVs; per-window random depth of 0.6-1.4 bays, width mirroring and a mid-depth furniture/curtain card. On curtain walls use 50-70 % blind or film cover by day. 2) Reflection first by day: F0 0.04 with Fresnel, a per-pane normal tilt of ±0.3-0.6° and a slight bow (replacing the ±0.01 at :256), glass tint per building. 3) Box-projected probes per downtown block (about 150 m spacing, 256 px, time-sliced by ReflectionProbeDirector), or a baked skyline cubemap per district at 60 m height. 4) Mullion caps as real geometry on LOD0 (BLD-01). Verify with CAM_05/07 recaptures.
Cost
Room sampling stays 2-4 texture reads within 240 m, as today. A 256 px probe is 4× the memory of a 128 px one (about 1.5 MB each, estimate); time slicing keeps refresh at ≤ 1 face per frame.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:51-55 room side walls, floor and ceiling are flat constants (wallTone ×0.85, floorTone ×0.8, a pale ceiling); only the back wall samples the atlas (:47)
VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:202-211 rooms fade only with distance (40-240 m) and Fresnel^4; :256 per-pane normal tilt only ±0.01
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraPalette.cs:173-178 curtain walls use roomDepth 1.2 bays (3.6 m) for every pane
Assets/_Veyra/World/ReflectionProbeDirector.cs:10-12 and Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraReflectionProbes.cs:13-15: 128 px realtime probes on a 400 m grid at terrain + 14 m
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png right third (2560x1440 px: x 1700-2560): the diagonal triangle pattern covers the whole facade

BLD-07At night the 214 kit buildings and every tower crown stay black; the skyline is a dark silhouette with uniform speckles high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
The night skyline is mostly black blocks. Lit windows are identical tiny warm dots, with no lit crowns, floodlit stone, LED edges or visible beacons; the tallest tower's top is dark (CAM_04 22h).
Root cause

Night appeal in GTA V and Cyberpunk comes from architectural lighting as much as from windows: floodlit crowns, lit lobbies, LED edge strips, rooftop signs, readable beacons, mixed colour temperatures and varied window brightness. Here only the shells get procedural windows. The kits use URP Lit without emission. Verify with CAM_04/07/13 22h captures: ≥ 90 % of buildings ≥ 12 m show lit pixels.
Cost

BLD-M1Kit buildings are stretched along their up axis by up to 53 % to reach the data height, distorting storeys and windows high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 added by verifier
You see
Kit towers have warped, overly tall storeys and elongated windows next to correctly scaled shells. Some look smeared or bent (CAM_03 kit at x≈1295-1360), so the city's scale cues contradict each other.
Root cause

Kits are chosen by style and footprint, and the height gap is then closed by non-uniform scaling. Floor height is the strongest scale cue on a facade: a 3.5 m storey stretched to 5.4 m makes windows, doors and trims read as giant or toy-like, and the texture texels stretch vertically too. GTA V and Cyberpunk never scale buildings non-uniformly. They pick or assemble a building whose floor count matches.

Fix

In BuildingAssembler, reject candidates whose required up-scale exceeds ±8 % (keep the shell, or try the next kit). Where the data height exceeds the kit, insert whole repeated floor slices (cut the kit at a mid-storey in Blender with vc_tools.py into base/mid/top LOD sets) or raise a podium or crown to absorb the remainder, keeping the data height as the top. Log the stretch distribution per run in assembler.txt (target: max ≤ 8 %). Recapture CAM_03/05/06.

Cost
No runtime cost. Floor-slice stacking adds about 1-3k tris per repeated floor (estimate), within the midrise/tower budgets.
Editor note
Same issue as BLD-12; counted once.

BLD-13No LOD chain or impostors for the shells; the HLOD reuses the same boxes high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 verified, corrected
You see
Distant blocks read as blank grey cubes with no structure (CAM_03 waterfront), and the far skyline is made of the same boxes as up close.
Root cause

Without baked far representations, detail at distance depends on procedural masks that fade out, and nothing else carries the far read (roof silhouettes, lit crowns, baked AO, colour variation). GTA V uses per-building LODs plus SLOD hierarchies with baked textures down to whole-block meshes, so far blocks keep their shape and colour.

Fix

Once modular facades exist:

  • Per building: LOD0 = modules; LOD1 ≈ 40 % (reveals collapsed into normal-mapped planes, trims kept); LOD2 ≈ 10 % (the envelope plus a baked 256-512² facade page with albedo, normal and window-emission mask, packed into per-cell 4096² atlases by an idempotent Editor baker). Screen-size transitions are about 0.25 / 0.08 / 0.015. Use dithered cross-fade only after adding the LOD_FADE_CROSSFADE multi_compile to VC_Facade and covering it in VC_Variants.shadervariants; otherwise use plain switches.
  • HlodBuilder: merge LOD2 per 1 km cell (≤ 30k tris) and keep night emission from the baked mask.
  • Towers over 100 m and landmarks: a custom octahedral impostor baker (12×12 frames, 2048²) beyond 2 km.

Validate: LODGroup.lodCount ≥ 3 on 100 % of building LODGroups; recapture CAM_01/03/04; stats.json triangles p95 ≤ 8 M. Texture memory: unique 512² pages for every building are about 2.8 GB BC7 for three maps (estimate), so share pages by archetype or use 256² pages for buildings under 30 m.

Cost
Unique 512² BC7 pages for all 2,708 buildings are about 0.7 GB (estimate); pack them per cell into 4096² atlases and stream with the cells. LOD2 and impostors cut far tris and shadow casters.

BUG-038
BLD-09Floating rectangular frame over the stepped tower (BUG-123): the RooftopDetails parapet is built on the full data box medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
A thin rectangular frame hangs in the sky beside the top of the stepped stone tower in the Crown Plaza view (CAM_05, top centre).
Root cause

RooftopDetails assumes every UBX box has a flat roof filling its footprint. On stepped towers the top tier is 56 % of the footprint, so the parapet ring floats up to 26 m outside the tower at roof height. It is spawned at runtime and has no collider, which is why the edit-mode render and the editor ray probes never found it.

Fix

Give RooftopDetails the real roof outline. BuildingGenerator writes a RoofOutline list (building id, top-tier rect, roof type flat/pitched/vault, height) to a ScriptableObject in Assets/_Veyra/Data (editor-generated, idempotent), and RooftopDetails reads it instead of the colliders. Skip hangars, houses and kits without a flat roof; for kits use the top face of the renderer bounds after FitHeight. Interim fix (under an hour): skip the parapet and finish slab when the collider id maps to a stepped or hangar record. Add a Dev-player validator that raycasts down from each rooftop prop corner and fails when no roof mesh lies within 0.15 m. Recapture CAM_05 at 12h and 22h and close BUG-123.

Cost
None; it removes props.

BUG-123
BLD-10Hard-edged procedural windows and a 7 px bitmap sign font read as 'pixelated' medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Window edges stair-step and crawl on mid-distance facades while driving, and neon signs use chunky blocky letters (CAM_13 'DUMPLINGS').
Root cause

Binary step() masks have infinitely sharp edges. When a window is 4-30 px wide they alias every frame, and STP smears them into crawling stairs. Real neon is smooth bent tube; a 5×7 bitmap font scaled up 3× is literally pixel art.

Fix

Replace step(a, x) with an analytic AA step, saturate((x - a) / max(fwidth(x), 1e-4) + 0.5), for every window, frame, sill, mullion, fascia and shutter mask in VC_FacadeSurface (same CBUFFER, no new keywords). In the long term real geometry (BLD-01) replaces most masks. Signs: generate an MSDF/SDF atlas (4096 × 2048, 64-96 px glyphs) from an SIL OFL font (licence file in ArtSource/Downloads), or model tube letters as spline meshes for hero signs, and render a round tube profile plus halo in Veyra/Neon. Recapture CAM_06/07/13 and compare edge crawl in a 5 s Recorder clip.

Cost
fwidth per mask costs a few ALU ops. MSDF costs 1-3 samples per sign pixel, which is negligible.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:97-135 window, frame, sill, mullion and shutter edges use step(); the anti-alias fade starts only when a window cell is below about 1.5 px (:140-151)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/NeonSignBuilder.cs:20 and :36 (48 rows of 21 px, 7 px glyphs × 3), :374-379 PixelFont.Draw into a 1024 px wide atlas
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png (2560x1440 px): blocky pixel letters on the DUMPLINGS sign at x 695-1035, y 385-440
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md BUG-074: render scale 0.9 + STP on PC High

BLD-11Rooftops: untextured flat-colour boxes in the middle third of the roof; house roofs are flat colour planes medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Seen from hills and bridges, roofs are flat slabs with a few grey boxes: no ducts, pipes, ladders, skylights, cooling towers or billboards. House roofs are smooth coloured planes without tiles, fascia or gutters (CAM_03 tops, CAM_16).
Root cause

Roofs are seen constantly from bridges, hills and the aerial cameras. Untextured flat-colour primitives without AO read as placeholders, and the middle-third rule leaves roof edges empty. GTA V roofs are dense with modelled plant, ductwork, pipes, railings, skylights and signs, and tiled roofs have real relief.

Fix

Build an original rooftop prop kit in Blender: 20-25 props (HVAC units in 3 sizes, ductwork runs, exhaust stacks, a cooling tower, lift overrun, skylights, railings, ladders, dishes, solar arrays, billboard frames, helipad markings, water tanks). Each is 200-3k tris at LOD0, textured on a shared 2048² trim + mask, and uses serialized library materials (VeyraMaterialLibrary) instead of runtime Material copies. Place them along the real roof outline (BLD-09): perimeter ducts and railings, plant in the middle, parapets with coping, density by typology. Pitched roofs: 0.2 m thick roof mesh with fascia, gutters and ridge caps, plus a tile/shingle material with normal or parallax (2048², 2-4 m tile). Draw the props instanced (InstancedPropRenderer or BRG).

Instanced props are about 1-3k tris per roof within 600 m; cull them beyond 600 m and let the HLOD carry the baked roof colour.

BLD-12Kit library: 214 kits from 15 models, stretched up to 53 %, clashing with the shells in style and exposure medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Downtown mixes near-black, low-poly kit blocks with pale procedural towers. Some kits look vertically stretched with overly tall windows, and the same few models repeat (CAM_05 left, CAM_06 right).
Root cause

The third-party packs differ in texel density (estimate 20-60 px/m for one 2048 map over a 30-60 m facade), albedo range, modelling detail and style. Non-uniform stretching breaks the floor-height cues the eye uses to judge scale. Their albedo is not calibrated to the shells' 0.15-0.55 range, so they read too dark (likely cause; verify by measuring the mean albedo).

Treat kits as interim:

  1. Forbid non-uniform stretch over 10 % (BuildingAssembler.cs:147). If the data height does not fit, keep the modular shell, or add or remove modular floors.
  2. Calibrate kit albedo: an Editor pass measures each _BaseMap's mean linear albedo and sets _BaseColor so walls land in 0.15-0.55 and glass in 0.03-0.2, as VeyraPalette's MinAlbedo/MaxAlbedo do.
  3. Keep only kits with real facade depth and ≥ 128 px/m at street level.
  4. Add CC-BY attribution to the credits and re-check real-landmark likeness.
  5. Replace kits progressively with the in-house modular facades (BLD-01).

Measure with the kitbash contact sheet and Logs/C/assembler.txt (max stretch ≤ 10 %).

BLD-M2Facade 'building seed' is a 24 m world-grid hash, so shops, lit windows and patterns change mid-wall medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
A single building switches abruptly from shopfronts to plain windows partway along its ground floor (CAM_06 left tower, shops start at x≈350). Neighbouring buildings share identical window states, which breaks each building's identity.
Root cause

BuildingGenerator meshes carry no per-building ID (MB has only V/N/UV/T, BuildingGenerator.cs:36-39), so the shader derives its seed from world position on a 24 m grid. Grid cells cut through buildings: a long facade gets several 'buildings' worth of shop and occupancy decisions with hard seams, while small neighbours share one. Real buildings are coherent units, and GTA V/Cyberpunk author per-building variation.

Write a per-building seed (and the BLD-04 tint) into vertex colour or UV2 in BuildingGenerator.MB (seeded from the record's 'seed' column) and read bseed from it in VC_FacadeSurface instead of :73. Drive occupancy (:222) from the same seed plus floor index rather than a 60 m world grid. Same channel in HlodBuilder merges. Verify with CAM_06/07 12h and 22h recaptures: there must be no shop or lit-state change at an arbitrary point along a wall.

Cost
4-8 bytes per vertex. It also removes a hash. No runtime cost change.

BLD-M3Runtime rooftop parapet ring hangs as a floating frame above the stepped tower in the hero plaza medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
A large thin rectangular frame floats in the sky above the stepped stone tower, wider than its top tier, in the main downtown plaza camera (CAM_05, x≈1010-1325, y≈50-500).
Root cause

RooftopDetails derives each roof from the building's UBX collision box, which spans the full data footprint, and since iteration 21 it adds a parapet ring along that box's edges. On stepped records the actual top tier is inset (scale 0.56), so the ring floats around empty air. The tracker notes the frame predates iteration 21, so an earlier runtime element may also contribute (plausible, not proven). A floating object in a hero view instantly breaks realism.

Fix

In RooftopDetails, look up each roof's record shape (id → world_building_placements.csv) and, for stepped and hangar records, build the parapet and roof slab at the top tier's extents (HalfW/HalfL × 0.56) or skip them. Better, emit parapets in BuildingGenerator per tier, where the geometry is known. Add a Dev-player validator that every roof prop's base is within 0.15 m of a roof surface (raycast down). Recapture CAM_05 12h/22h and close BUG-123 only if the frame is gone.

BUG-123

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • BUG-123 interim fix: in RooftopDetails, skip the parapet ring and roof-finish slab for stepped and hangar records (id → shape lookup from world_building_placements.csv), then recapture CAM_05 12h/22h.
  • Analytic anti-aliasing: replace step() with fwidth-based AA steps for the window, frame, sill, mullion, fascia and shutter masks in VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:97-135 (no new keywords).
  • Interior mapping: sample the side walls, ceiling and floor from the interior atlas and randomise room depth (0.6-1.4 bays) per window in VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:31-57. This removes the diagonal triangle pattern in CAM_05.
  • Storefront band only on the road-facing wall(s), not on all four sides, and not on D5 apartments (BuildingGenerator.cs:375-377).
  • Per-building tint: add vertex colour to BuildingGenerator.MB (seeded ±12 % value, ±4° hue, from palette swatches per style) and multiply it in VC_FacadeSurface.
  • Night balance: remove the ×1.35 street boost (VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:242), lower street litFrac from 0.86 to 0.6 and raise the block occupancy floor from 0.15 to 0.35 (:223). This removes the white strip in CAM_13 and the dead blocks in CAM_04.
  • Kits at night: in KitbashMaterials, map kit glass/window material slots to the M_Glass_Curtain_* Veyra/Facade materials so the 214 kits get night windows.
  • Crown lights: add an emissive crown band and a blinking red beacon to Crown() for towers over 60 m, registered as night-only emissives in WorldEnvironment.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Geometric depth: 100 % of buildings within 300 m of a drivable road have window reveals ≥ 0.12 m and LOD0 tris ≥ 4k (houses), ≥ 10k (midrise), ≥ 30k (towers over 60 m). Measure with an Editor stats pass (Veyra/Buildings/Facade Stats → Logs/BLD/facade_stats.json) over all building LOD0 meshes.
  • LOD coverage: 100 % of building LODGroups have ≥ 3 levels with LOD1 at 35-45 % and LOD2 at 8-12 % of LOD0 tris, plus an HLOD or impostor. Check with LODGroup.lodCount and the tri ratios in the same stats pass (closes BUG-038).
  • Variety: in every 250 m chunk no two adjacent buildings share the same archetype, material and tint, kit/module reuse ≤ 3 times per street block, and no wall shows a texture period under 6 m without macro variation. Measure with a validator report plus CAM_03/05/06/16 recaptures reviewed against the previous captures.
  • Texel density: street-level facades (below 20 m height, within 30 m of a road) at 256-1024 px/m, everything else ≥ 128 px/m. Measure with a texel-density script over LOD0 UVs and the material texture sizes.
  • Rooftops: 0 floating roof props (Dev-player validator: every prop base within 0.15 m of a roof surface), and every flat roof ≥ 12 m tall has ≥ 3 distinct prop types. Confirm with the validator log and a CAM_05 recapture (BUG-123 closed).
  • Night: at 22:00 ≥ 90 % of buildings ≥ 12 m tall show lit windows, every tower over 60 m has ≥ 2 lit crown elements and a beacon, and shop bands never clip (no pixel at 1.0 in all channels across more than 5 % of a band). Measure with luminance analysis of the CAM_04/07/13 22h_wet captures.
  • Performance: Dev player R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT and R_BRIDGES routes at 1440p on the RTX 4070 hold p99 ≤ 16.6 ms, no hitch > 50 ms, triangles p95 ≤ 8 M, SetPass p95 ≤ 450. Read from stats.json via Tools/World/card_checks.sh.
  • Landmarks: all 11 landmark records render a bespoke asset (no 'A4 hero pending' line in Logs/C/assembler.txt), and each is recognisable in CAM_01/03/04 silhouettes. Confirm with the assembler log and the review contact sheet.

Assets to make or source

  • 2-3 original facade trim sheets at 4096² (masonry/brick/stone; concrete/stucco/render; metal/glass/curtain wall) with albedo, normal and mask (metal R, AO G, smoothness A), authored in Blender. Any CC0 scan sources (e.g. ambientCG, Poly Haven) get a licence file in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • 8-12 tileable wall materials at 2048² covering 4-8 m real size (brick variants, limestone, render, painted concrete, metal cladding, siding), with height maps for hex tiling.
  • A modular facade module library: 6 style families × 20-30 modules (window bays 2.7-4.2 m in 3 floor heights, corners, piers, spandrels, balconies, cornices, plinths, entrances), 300-1,500 tris each with LOD1/LOD2, exported via VC_Unity_Prop.
  • A storefront set: 10-12 original modules (recess, frames, awnings, doors, sign boxes) and a 4096² shop-interior parallax atlas (32 interiors), with no real brands.
  • Interior mapping atlas v2: 32 rooms × 5 faces (back, left, right, floor, ceiling) at 4096², plus furniture/curtain cards, all original.
  • A suburban house kit: 16 archetypes with shared parts (wall panels, 0.2 m roofs with fascia and gutters, porches, garages, dormers, chimneys), a 4096² house trim sheet, and 20 yard props (fences, hedges, bins, mailboxes, AC units).
  • A rooftop prop kit: 20-25 props (HVAC, ducts, stacks, cooling tower, lift overrun, skylights, railings, ladders, dishes, solar arrays, billboard frames, water tanks) on a 2048² trim.
  • A crown library of 12 original tower crowns with emissive lighting elements, plus architectural lighting cards (floodlight wash, LED strip, beacon).
  • 10 original hero landmarks (Veyra Spire, Exchange Tower, Grand Lumen Hotel, Starlight Theater, Vantage car park, Crown Motor Gallery, Falcon Control Tower, Millbrook Clock Tower, Blackridge summit masts) with unique 4096² sets, LOD chains and impostors; design review against real-landmark likeness.

  • An SIL OFL font for signage (licence file in ArtSource/Downloads), converted to an MSDF atlas, and an attribution list for all CC-BY-4.0 kit packs (KB-01 to KB-09) in the credits.

5. Trees, bushes and forests

Score today: 2.5 / 10   Trees under 500 triangles that are static, unlit from behind, repeated clones and alias at the edges read as a mobile or kids' game, nowhere near GTA V forests or Cyberpunk street greenery.

Vegetation is the weakest-looking part of the world, and the reasons are structural. All 67,499 trees come from five procedural meshes built by Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py. Their LOD0 counts are 86 to 471 triangles (Logs/B1/trees.txt), against roughly 10-25k for an AAA tree. They are shaded with stock URP Lit: no translucency, no normal map, no vertex AO and no wind. The wind globals are set every frame, but only the sky and water shaders read them. The 2048x1024 atlas is shared by five species and alpha-tested at 0.45 with MSAA off and STP at 0.9 render scale, which gives the speckled, 'pixelated' crown edges in CAM_16. Rendering picks LOD per 250 m cell and switches LOD0 straight to LOD2 at 90 m with no crossfade; LOD1 is never used and there are no impostors. Far broadleaf trees lose their crowns and show as bare sticks (CAM_18). Forests are uniform cones at about 38 trees per hectare on bare brown soil, so CAM_11 reads as green noise. There is no undergrowth and no per-instance colour, and a single pine covers both mountain districts. The plaza bushes pick up the wrong material (tiling M_Leaves), which makes their leaves about 40 cm long and glossy (CAM_05). The fix path is: source or author AAA trees (SpeedTree, or licensed Fab/Asset Store packs with licence files) with vertex wind/AO data; write a VC_Foliage shader with two-sided translucent lighting, 3-level wind, wetness, per-instance hue and dithered alpha/LOD; move to GPU-driven per-instance LOD with octahedral impostors; add a canopy tint and undergrowth derived from the data; and give each district its own species palette.

Findings and fixes (14)

VEG-01Trees are sub-500-triangle procedural card models with toy silhouettes critical effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Pines look like stacks of faceted green paper plates with spiky tops. Broadleaf crowns are flat blobs on a pole. Every tree of a species has the same outline, like a model-railway set.
Root cause

A numpy/bmesh generator builds every tree from fewer than 500 triangles: straight tubes, randomly oriented 2.5 m cards, and an opaque stepped cone as the pine core. People recognise real trees by silhouette detail (gaps, drooping tips, sub-branches) and self-similar branching. With neither present, the brain classifies them as toys. GTA V near trees are modelled limbs with leaf-cluster cards (estimated 10-20k tris). Cyberpunk uses SpeedTree-class assets with 3+ LODs and impostors.

Fix

Stop iterating vc_trees.py for final trees. Source or author AAA trees: SpeedTree Modeler (Indie/Pro subscription, output owned) or licensed Fab/Asset Store packs (CC0 where available), with a licence file per pack in ArtSource/Downloads. Specs: broadleaf LOD0 12-25k tris, conifer 8-15k, palm 4-8k, shrub 1.5-4k; LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %, plus an octahedral impostor. Model the trunk with 2 branch levels and use alpha cards only for leaf/needle clusters at branch tips; 3 variants per species. Export through Tools/Blender/vc_tools.py VC_Unity_Prop (base-centre pivot, <name>_LOD0-2) and bake vertex colours (R trunk weight, G branch phase, B leaf flutter, A AO). Keep TreeSwap.cs as the idempotent hook, extended to species-to-variant lists; normalise heights to the data's 10/12/14 m x CSV scale. Keep vc_trees.py only for fixtures.

Cost
Only affordable together with per-instance LOD + impostors (VEG-05/VEG-08). Estimate: about 2-3 M vegetation tris in a CAM_10 view, within BUG-117's 8 M p95 budget.
Evidence
Logs/B1/trees.txt:1-5 - LOD0 tris: Broadleaf 448, Broadleaf2 448, Pine 471, Palm 270, Bush 86
Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py:263-300 - broadleaf = 8-sided straight trunk tube + 6 branch tubes + 140 randomly oriented 2.5 m cards clamped to an ellipsoid
Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py:302-350 - pine = opaque stepped cone_mass (5 tiers) + 12 whorls x 8 needle cards
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_10_BR_Halcyon_Drive_12h.png - left pines (x~290-690 of 2560) and right pines (x~2000-2400) show stepped faceted tiers, identical ragged tops and identical silhouettes

VEG-02Foliage uses stock URP Lit: no translucency, normal map, AO or backface handling high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Canopies are dark, almost black-green masses with no glow where the sun passes through the leaves. Pines are darker than the grass, and leaves look like matte plastic cut-outs.
Root cause

URP Lit has no transmission, so a backlit leaf gets only ambient light and canopies go black against a bright sky. With Cull Off and no normal flip, back faces are lit with the front-face normal. There is no normal or thickness map and no baked AO gradient (inner crown darker, tips brighter), and albedo is flat. GTA V, RDR2 and Cyberpunk foliage shaders use a sun-facing translucency term, crown-spherical normals, vertex AO and leaf specular.

Fix

Write Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Foliage.shader (HLSL reusing VC_Common/VC_ForwardPass like VC_Facade; SRP Batcher compatible; ForwardLit, ShadowCaster, DepthOnly, DepthNormals, MotionVectors passes). Features: (1) two-sided lighting: flip N with SV_IsFrontFace; (2) translucency: thicknessMap x lightColor x pow(saturate(dot(V,-L)),6) x shadow, strength 0.6-1.0, also for Forward+ additional lights; (3) asset crown-sphere normals blended 50 % with a tangent normal map; (4) vertex-colour AO on diffuse and specular; (5) leaf smoothness 0.25-0.35 (stored in metallic alpha per CLAUDE.md), F0 0.04; (6) per-instance hue/value (VEG-07). Create the materials in Editor code (Shader.Find only there), register them in VeyraMaterialLibrary and add the keywords to VC_Variants.shadervariants. Calibrate leaf albedo with VeyraPalette Min/MaxAlbedo checks.

Cost
About 20-30 extra ALU per foliage fragment; alpha-tested overdraw dominates the cost, so pair with depth priming/prepass (VEG-08).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TreeSwap.cs:144-166 - M_Tree_Foliage created from 'Universal Render Pipeline/Lit', only _BaseMap set, _Smoothness 0.12, cutoff 0.45, _Cull 0
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Materials/M_Tree_Foliage.mat:24 (URP Lit guid 933532a4...), :46 _BumpMap empty, :108-109 _Cull 0/_Cutoff 0.45, :125 _Smoothness 0.12
Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py:213-233 - one-sided cards with bent normals; no vertex colour/AO written
Docs/WORLD_ITERATION_LOG.md:34 - iteration 22: pines 'still darker than the grass'
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_10_BR_Halcyon_Drive_12h.png - near pines left (x~130-900 of 2560) and right (x~1990-2400): crowns near-black green, much darker than the sunlit grass beside them
Tracked as
new
VEG-03Hard alpha-test on tiny leaves without MSAA gives speckled, 'pixelated' crowns high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Tree crowns have crunchy, broken pixel edges where single leaves turn into 1-2 px dots that flicker in motion. Bushes have hard paper-cut edges. This is the 'pixelated' look.
Root cause

A binary alpha test (cutoff 0.45) on leaves only a few texels wide gives all-or-nothing sub-pixel coverage. At 0.9 render scale and without MSAA there is no partial coverage, and temporal upscaling cannot rebuild thin, high-contrast leaf-against-sky edges, so they speckle and shimmer. MSAA-era games used alpha-to-coverage. TAA-era games (Cyberpunk, RDR2) author larger soft-edged leaf clusters and use dithered coverage that TAA/DLSS resolves.

Fix

In VC_Foliage: (1) derivative-sharpened alpha, a' = (a - cutoff)/max(fwidth(a),1e-4) + 0.5, then clip(a' - IGN noise rotated per frame) so STP resolves soft coverage; (2) foliage mip bias -0.5; (3) keep coverage-preserving mips. Re-author atlases with a 2-4 px soft alpha edge and leaves of at least 24 px at LOD0 (>=512 px/m): one 2048x2048 atlas per species, BC7, aniso 8. Render-scale A/B for foliage: 0.9 vs 1.0 on PC_High. On PC_Ultra, A/B MSAA 4x + AlphaToMask with STP off (SMAA) against STP and keep the lower measured flicker. Fix ReviewCameraRig.WarmUp to render at least 8-16 frames with the camera static (STP history) before Render, so stills match gameplay.

Cost
Dithered clip is free. MSAA 4x at 1440p is estimated at +1.5-2.5 ms on a 4070, so only on Ultra.
Editor note
At the default Quality 2 gameplay actually runs MSAA 4x (GameFlow.cs:1063-1071, IQ-01), but no Veyra or URP Lit foliage material uses alpha-to-coverage, so the speckle remains. The plan picks STP with MSAA off (IQ-01), which this fix assumes.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png - street trees at right (x~2060-2380, y~550-690): ragged, dotted crown edges against sky and hill
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:28-30 - m_MSAA 1, m_RenderScale 0.9, m_UpscalingFilter 4 (STP); ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:7 m_CurrentQuality 3
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Textures/Vegetation/T_Foliage_Atlas.png.meta:14-15 coverage-preserving mips at 0.45, :38 aniso 1
Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py:9,41,284-285 - one 2048x1024 atlas for 5 species; 512 px tile on a 2.5 m card (~205 px/m, computed)
Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/ReviewCameraRig.cs:119 (AA None, relies on STP) and :130-142 (WarmUp renders only one frame before capture)
Tracked as
new
VEG-04Distant forests read as uniform green stipple on brown soil high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
From the air or a mountain viewpoint, the forest is an even sprinkle of identical dark-green cones on brown ground, like a model railway or a noise filter instead of a continuous canopy.
Root cause

At 38 stems per hectare with crowns of about 3 m radius, the canopy covers an estimated 10-15 % of the ground, so brown terrain dominates. All cones share one tint and nothing casts shadows beyond 160 m, so the only signal left is a uniform dot pattern, which the eye reads as high-frequency noise. Real conifer stands show over 70 % canopy closure, clumps and clearings, and sunlit tops over dark gaps. GTA V (Chiliad) and RDR2 tint the terrain under forests, use dense far LODs or impostors, and vary hue per tree.

Fix

(1) Idempotent Editor step in WorldBuildBatch: bake a 1 m/px canopy-density mask from the placement CSVs (Gaussian splat per crown) into the terrain splat set as a dark needle-litter/canopy-shadow layer (albedo about 0.04-0.06). It is derived from the data and adds no new positions. (2) Octahedral impostors beyond about 150 m (VEG-05), lit with baked normals, per-instance hue ±6 % and value ±12 %. (3) Match far-LOD/impostor crown width to LOD0 and check crown widths against reference photos. (4) Impostor shadow casting in the last cascade to about 400 m, or bake canopy shadow into the mask. (5) For denser stands, ask the data owner for forest polygons or density in the design repo; never invent tree positions.

Cost
2-triangle impostors for about 45k far pines are about 0.1 M tris (estimate) versus about 1.9 M if every 42-tri cone is drawn.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_11_BR_Switchbacks_Aerial_12h.png - lower two thirds: evenly spaced identical cones on brown-olive soil, no cast shadows, hard forest edge at right (x~1920-2240, y~510-720)
WorldSource/placement/vegetation_D4_blackridge.csv - 45,685 pine_tree_14m + 672 deciduous rows; mean ~38 per occupied hectare (auditor's computation)
Tools/Blender/vc_trees.py:302-323,330 + Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TreeSwap.cs:62 - far mesh is LOD2 = opaque cone, 3 tiers x 6 sides
Assets/_Veyra/Scenes/VeyraCity.unity:933188-933190 - pine LodDistance 90, DrawDistance 1400, ShadowDistance 160; VC_URP_PC_High.asset:57 shadow distance 160
Assets/_Veyra/World/InstancedPropRenderer.cs:72 - yaw + uniform scale only
Tracked as
new
VEG-05Per-cell binary LOD pops, LOD1 unused, far broadleaf crowns vanish high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Groups of trees change shape and brightness at once as you drive past 90 m. Street trees vanish at 260 m. Far broadleaf trees show as bare sticks without crowns.
Root cause

LOD is chosen per 250 m cell (up to 1000 instances, CityBuilder.cs:31,973-982), so hundreds of trees swap at once, LOD0 straight to LOD2, an 85-90 % shape jump with no dither. LOD1 was removed deliberately because the cell-wide far band made it too expensive (TreeSwap.cs:59-60: 6.4 M tris p95). The far pine is an opaque cone that shades brighter. Far broadleaf crowns probably vanish because LOD2 has only 22 alpha cards and no opaque core (hypothesis; confirm in the Frame Debugger). StreetTrees cuts off hard at 260 m.

Fix

Choose LOD per instance, not per cell, with a Burst culling job per camera: LOD0 under 40 m, LOD1 40-120 m, LOD2 120-200 m, impostor beyond 200 m (x DistanceScale). Only per-instance banding plus impostors keeps LOD1 within budget; check against BUG-117 (<=8 M tris p95). Crossfade across a 10 % overlap band with complementary dither, using a per-instance fade property read by VC_Foliage. Fade out dithered over the last 15 % of DrawDistance. Bake impostors from LOD0 with an Editor ImpostorBaker (8x8 hemi-octahedral, 2048x2048, albedo/alpha + normal/depth); luminance difference at the switch under 5 %. Route StreetTrees through the same path, camera-centred and per frame. Interim fix: add an opaque crown core to broadleaf LOD2 in vc_trees.py.

Cost
Per-instance culling/LOD for 67k instances: estimated 0.2-0.4 ms on Burst workers or about 0.05 ms as a compute pass.

VEG-06No wind: every tree and bush is frozen, even in a storm high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Nothing in the vegetation ever moves. Leaves, pines and palms stay perfectly still in full rain, so the forest feels like a static diorama.
Root cause

The wind globals exist, but no foliage shader reads them. Trees render with URP Lit, which has no vertex deformation, and the generated meshes carry no wind weights (vc_trees.py writes no vertex colours). Motion is the strongest sign of life in peripheral vision. GTA V sways all foliage with weather-driven wind and palms bend in storms; SpeedTree/Cyberpunk use 3-level wind (trunk bend, branch sway, leaf flutter).

Fix

VC_Foliage vertex stage: (1) trunk bend: offset = windDir x strength x (h/H)^2 x amp x (0.6 + 0.4 sin(t x 0.7 + phase)), keeping height roughly constant; (2) branch sway: vertex colour R weight, G phase, amplitude 0.05-0.3 m; (3) leaf flutter: B weight, 3-8 Hz noise, 1-3 cm. Per-instance phase = hash(world position). Apply the same deformation in ShadowCaster, DepthOnly and MotionVectors passes, and set RenderParams.motionVectorMode = Object so STP does not ghost. Map weather in WorldEnvironment: 0.15 clear, 0.5 rain, 0.9 storm, with gust noise. Bake weights in Blender for new assets; for the current cards, derive them from height and distance to the trunk axis. Drop flutter beyond 150 m; impostors get trunk sway only.

Cost
About 20 vertex-shader ALU, negligible on a 4070. The motion-vector pass adds a draw per near prototype, so limit it to LOD0/LOD1.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:52-54 - tooltip says 'vegetation and rain lean with it', WindStrength 0.3
Assets/_Veyra/World/VeyraGlobals.cs:16-17,51-53 - _VC_WindStrength/_VC_WindDirection are published every frame
Only consumers: Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_SkyDome.shader:120,131 and VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:39; M_Tree_Foliage.mat:24 is URP Lit (no vertex animation)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/review.txt:7 - CAM_10 22h_wet runs at rain 1.00, wetness 1.00 (a still cannot show motion; the code shows there is none)
Tracked as
new
VEG-07Five meshes for 67,499 trees: no species mix, district identity or per-instance variation high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
The same tree repeats everywhere: one pine across Blackridge and Redwood Valley, one broadleaf along every avenue. Neighbouring trees are identical, and Neon Mile and Ironport have no greenery at all.
Root cause

Repetition breaks realism fastest: the eye spots an identical silhouette within 2-3 instances. One mesh per species, yaw-only rotation and one material give no species mix, age spread, lean or colour noise, and no district character. GTA V gives each biome its own species set (Chiliad conifers, Vinewood palms, scrub oaks), each with variants. Cyberpunk mixes palms, planters and dead street trees per district.

Fix

Give each district a species palette. Assign species/variant by a deterministic position hash, generalising TreeSwap.Variant (TreeSwap.cs:77-126) to species-to-variant lists that do not duplicate on re-run. Palettes: D4 3 conifers x 3 variants + snags; D8 conifers + alder/birch-like near water, and ask the data owner for a tall-conifer type (heights come from the data); D5 4 garden broadleaf + hedges; D1 2 street species in grates; D2 2 palm species + coastal shrubs; D3/D6 planter and street-tree rows requested via BUG-008. Per instance: hue ±6 %, value ±12 %, tilt ±3°, XZ scale ±8 %. StreetTrees picks the district street species per placement. Validator: no two trees within 15 m share variant and yaw bucket.

Cost
Each variant adds one instanced draw per visible cell per LOD; about 30 prototypes is roughly +60-120 draws (estimate), trivial on the GPU-driven path (VEG-08).
Evidence

VEG-08CPU per-cell instancing and alpha overdraw leave no budget for AAA trees medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Indirect: tree quality was capped at fewer than 500 triangles and LOD1 was dropped because the frame budget is already near its limit.
Root cause

Coarse per-cell culling and LOD spend triangles on far trees and shading on overdrawn alpha cards. Without per-instance culling, impostors and a depth prepass, there is no headroom for 10-25k-triangle trees or a foliage shader. AAA open worlds cull vegetation per instance and switch to impostors early.

Fix

Make InstancedPropRenderer GPU-driven. Per prototype, keep a StructuredBuffer of instances (position, yaw, scale, hue seed, variant), streamed per 1 km cell. A compute pass per camera does frustum and optional Hi-Z occlusion culling (previous-frame depth), picks LOD or impostor and the crossfade value, and writes compacted visible lists plus IndirectDrawIndexedArgs for Graphics.RenderMeshIndirect. Shadow casters are culled against the light cascades. Enable depth priming (Auto) or a foliage DepthOnly prepass with an Equal depth test so cut-out pixels shade once. Measure tris p95, SetPass and GPU ms in Logs/<card>/stats.json for R_SMOKE and CAM_10/11/16.

Cost
Target vegetation at 3 ms GPU or less and 0.3 ms CPU or less at 1440p on the 4070 (budget proposal, to be measured).
Evidence
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:81 - BUG-117: triangles p95 up to 7.8 M (budget 8 M), SetPass p95 414-462; proposes 'impostors for far trees'
Assets/_Veyra/World/InstancedPropRenderer.cs:84-114 - every camera loops over all cells on the CPU and calls RenderMeshInstanced per cell x submesh
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TreeSwap.cs:59-61 - LOD1 removed after 'B1 run 1 ... 6.4 M triangles p95, GPU p95 9.7 ms'
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_High.asset:51 - m_DepthPrimingMode 0: alpha-tested foliage is shaded per overdraw layer

VEG-09Plaza bushes use the tiling M_Leaves material: giant glossy paper leaves medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see

Root cause

The plaza bush prefab gets the library's tiling M_Leaves through the importer name map, not the atlas material that TreeSwap gives the instanced bush. Its atlas UVs therefore sample a 1 m tiling leaf pattern: leaves are 3-4 times real size, glossy, and cut by unrelated alpha. At close range leaf size is a primary scale cue, so the plaza reads as miniature.

Proper: an urban shrub set (boxwood-like ball, laurel-like shrub, ornamental grass, flowering shrub, clipped hedge module), 1.5-4k tris, with a branch skeleton visible inside and 4-8 cm leaves on an atlas of at least 512 px/m. 3 variants each, yaw random, scale 0.85-1.15, hue ±5 %. Draw them through InstancedPropRenderer instead of GameObjects.

Cost
No added cost; instancing the plaza bushes removes per-object renderers.

VEG-10No undergrowth or forest floor; trunks plug straight into bare soil medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Under the pines there is only flat brown dirt with scattered grass tufts. Trunks go straight into the ground, and there are no ferns, saplings, logs, rocks, needle litter or lakeside reeds.
Root cause

A forest reads as real through layered vertical structure: canopy, understory (1-4 m), ground layer (0-0.5 m) and litter, plus contact detail where trunks meet the soil. With only a canopy layer over flat terrain albedo, trees look placed on a tabletop. GTA V's Paleto/Chiliad forests and RDR2 use dense fern, shrub, sapling and deadfall scatter, rocks and ground decals that blend the trunk bases.

Fix

Add an idempotent 'UndergrowthScatter' step to WorldBuildBatch: deterministic Poisson scatter seeded per 250 m chunk, with density from the data-derived canopy mask (VEG-04), slope and splat. Keep it off roads by reusing PropRoadClear's carriageway test. Types: fern 0.5-1 m, sapling 1-3 m, shrub, fallen log, stump, rock cluster, each 0.5-3k tris with LODs, drawn through InstancedPropRenderer with dithered fade at 120 m or less; colliders only on logs. Add a needle-litter terrain layer (CC0 photoscan, 2048x2048 albedo/normal/ORM) under the mask, plus a root-flare/trunk-base decal. Confirm with the user that decorative scatter derived from the data trees is acceptable under the 'never invent coordinates' rule.

Cost
Budget estimate: at most 0.6 ms GPU at 1440p for about 3-6k undergrowth instances within 120 m.

VEG-11Bark and trunks: 256x512 noise texture, no normal map, straight 8-sided tubes medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see

Root cause

A 256x512 bark texture without a normal map shows no grazing-light relief under noon sun, so trunks read as painted plastic. Straight 8-sided tubes with no flare, knots or bend give CG-perfect silhouettes. AAA bark uses photoscanned 2k tiling sets with normal/AO, root-flare geometry, and a moss/dirt blend by height and upward-facing normal.

Fix

Source 4 CC0 photoscan bark sets (Poly Haven/ambientCG, licence files in ArtSource/Downloads): 2048x2048 albedo, normal (marked as Normal Map) and ORM (smoothness in metallic alpha), at least 512 px/m. On new trunks, use 12-16 sides at LOD0 with flared bases, knots, branch collars and a slight bend, plus UV2 for a base/moss mask. The bark variant of VC_Foliage adds a world-up moss/lichen blend and wetness darkening. Check albedo against the existing Bark spec (VeyraPalette.cs:222-223, MinAlbedo 0.05 / MaxAlbedo 0.20).

Cost
One 2048x2048 BC7 texture is about 5.3 MB with mips; 4 sets x 3 maps is about 64 MB of VRAM, acceptable on 12 GB.

VEG-12Foliage ignores night wetness and lights: black cut-outs in rain, no neon greenery medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
At 22:00 in rain every tree is a flat black cut-out, with no wet glints and no response to headlights or street lights. Neon Mile has no trees or planters to catch neon.
Root cause

The foliage material ignores the global wetness (no smoothness rise, no darkening). Without translucency or local-light response, trees at night are silhouettes only, so they add nothing to the wet-neon look. Cyberpunk's nights depend on specular glints and neon spill on every surface, foliage included; GTA V's rain makes leaves and bark glossy.

Fix

In VC_Foliage, read _VC_Wetness: leaf smoothness lerp(0.3, 0.6), bark lerp(0.15, 0.5), albedo x lerp(1, 0.8). Apply the translucency term to Forward+ additional lights (GetAdditionalLight with shadows), so headlights and street lamps light the leaves from behind. Add the keywords to VC_Variants.shadervariants. Ask the data owner for D6/D3 planter and street-tree rows (BUG-008) and use glossy-leaf street species there.

Cost
Additional-light transmission in the Forward+ loop: estimated +0.1-0.3 ms in dense neon scenes.
Evidence
