VEG-M1Foliage ignores rain wetness: trees stay matte and dry in wet neon nights medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 added by verifier
You see
In rain, roads glisten but trees, bushes and palms keep the same dry, matte look. Leaves never darken, bead or catch neon highlights, so greenery looks pasted into the wet scene.
Root cause

Foliage uses stock URP Lit with constant smoothness and does not read the global wetness. Wet leaves get darker albedo (about 20-30 %) and much glossier specular. Without that they do not respond to rain, and at night they reflect no neon, so they break material consistency with the wet roads. Cyberpunk and GTA V drive foliage wetness from the weather system.

Fix

In VC_Foliage (see VEG-02), read the existing wetness global from VC_Common.hlsl: albedo *= lerp(1, 0.75, wet), smoothness = lerp(0.3, 0.7, wet x upFacing), plus a small view-dependent sheen. Keep it SRP Batcher compatible and use no new keyword (a uniform lerp). Check with CAM_10/13 22h_wet stills against 12h stills.

Cost
A few ALU per fragment; negligible.
Editor note
Same issue as VEG-12; counted once.
Evidence
Wetness/rain globals are read only by Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Road.shader, VC_RoadSurface.hlsl, VC_SkyDome.shader (declared in VC_Common.hlsl/VC_Input.hlsl); no foliage shader exists
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TreeSwap.cs:157 - foliage _Smoothness fixed at 0.12 on URP Lit; M_Tree_Foliage.mat:125

VEG-M2Review stills capture STP after one warm-up frame, so foliage aliasing evidence is skewed low effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 added by verifier
You see
Review screenshots may show more speckled, crunchy tree edges than gameplay, because temporal AA has not converged when the still is taken.
Root cause

STP accumulates jittered samples over many frames. With one warm-up frame, the capture is close to a raw 0.9-scale, alpha-tested image, so the review evidence for the 'pixelated foliage' complaint mixes real aliasing with a capture artefact. Without a converged baseline the VEG-03 fixes cannot be measured fairly.

Fix

In ReviewCameraRig.WarmUp, render N frames (default 16, configurable) with the camera static and Time advancing before Render(). Record N in review.txt. Re-capture CAM_16/10 and compare flicker against an in-game Recorder clip.

Cost
Capture-time only; no runtime cost.

The capture code renders two warm-up frames plus the final one (VeyraReviewCapture.cs:170-171); same issue as IQ-08.

new

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • Plaza bushes: in PlazaBuilder.cs (after line 137), assign the TreeSwap materials M_Tree_Foliage/M_Tree_Bark to the instantiated VC_Tree_Bush renderers. This removes the giant glossy 40 cm leaves in CAM_05.
  • Per-instance tint variety now: extend the TreeSwap.Variant hash split (TreeSwap.cs:77-126) to 3 tinted material copies per species (_BaseColor value ±10 %, hue ±5 %). This breaks the uniform stipple in CAM_11 without a new shader.
  • Broadleaf far LOD: add an opaque ellipsoid crown core to broadleaf LOD2 in vc_trees.py, like the pine cone_mass, and re-export. Far trees stop showing as leafless sticks (CAM_18).
  • StreetTrees: pick broadleaf A or B per placement by position hash instead of always the first prototype (StreetTrees.cs:47-53), and centre on the camera rather than the player.
  • Use the existing LOD1: add a mid band (MidMesh, 60-200 m) to InstancedPropRenderer and select it per instance for cells within 300 m. This halves the LOD0-to-LOD2 shape jump at 90 m.
  • Review captures: make sure the review camera waits at least 8 frames before capture so the STP history is converged and foliage stills match gameplay AA (ReviewCameraRig.cs:119 relies on STP).

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Tree assets: LOD0 broadleaf 12-25k tris, conifer 8-15k, palm 4-8k, shrub 1.5-4k; LOD1 35-45 %, LOD2 8-12 %, impostor beyond 150-200 m; at least 3 variants per species. Measure with the LOD triangle log (Logs/B1/trees.txt equivalent written by the import/TreeSwap step) and the Frame Debugger.
  • Variety: at least 4 tree species per forested district and at least 2 per urban district; no two trees within 15 m share variant and yaw bucket; per-instance hue ±6 % and value ±12 %. Measure with a validator report over the placement cells and a per-1 km-cell variant histogram.
  • Motion: wind active on 100 % of foliage materials, amplitude scaling with _VC_WindStrength (0.15 clear, 0.5 rain, 0.9 storm). Measure with a 10 s Recorder clip at CAM_10: mean optical-flow or frame-difference of foliage pixels above 0 at WindStrength 0.3 and at least 3x larger at 0.9.
  • No LOD pops: no single-frame luminance jump above 5 % in foliage regions during a 60 s R_SMOKE drive. Measure by frame-difference spike detection on a Recorder capture.
  • Edge quality: foliage temporal flicker (mean absolute luminance difference of foliage-mask pixels between consecutive frames, static camera at CAM_16, STP on) under 2 %. Leaf texel density at least 512 px/m at LOD0, checked with a texel-density debug view.
  • Canopy read: in CAM_11, at least 70 % of the pixels inside forest areas read as canopy (green-value classifier on the screenshot) instead of terrain; LOD0-to-impostor luminance difference at the switch under 5 %.
  • Lighting: backlit canopy (sun behind trees, CAM_09/10) has mean luminance at least 1.5x that of the same view with translucency off, and pines are no darker than the adjacent grass by more than 20 % in CAM_10_12h. Measure by screenshot region statistics.
  • Performance at 1440p on an RTX 4070, PC_High: vegetation GPU time at most 3.0 ms and CPU at most 0.3 ms; total triangles p95 at most 8 M and SetPass p95 at most 450 (BUG-117 budgets). Measure with Logs/<card>/stats.json from the Dev player on R_SMOKE and CAM_10/11/16.

Assets to make or source

  • Conifers: 3 species x 3 variants (spruce-, fir- and pine-like), matching pine_tree_14m height. LOD0 8-15k tris, LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %, plus impostor, with vertex wind/AO colours. From SpeedTree Modeler (subscription; output owned) or licensed Fab/Asset Store packs, with the licence file in ArtSource/Downloads/<pack>/.
  • Urban/garden broadleaf: 4 species x 3 variants (plane-, linden-, oak- and maple-like), matching deciduous_tree_10m. LOD0 12-25k tris, same LOD/wind specs.
  • Palms: 2 species x 3 variants (12 m), LOD0 4-8k tris, frond atlas with a translucency map.
  • Shrubs and hedges: boxwood-like ball, laurel-like shrub, flowering shrub, ornamental grass, coastal shrub, 2 m clipped hedge module. 1.5-4k tris each, LOD0 + LOD1.
  • Forest undergrowth: 2 ferns, 2 saplings, 2 fallen logs, 2 stumps, snag/deadwood, rock clusters (share with the terrain domain). 0.5-3k tris each.
  • Leaf/needle atlases per species: 2048x2048 (4096 for hero broadleaf) with albedo+alpha (soft 2-4 px edge), normal, and thickness/translucency + roughness + AO packed. Photoscan CC0 or from the licensed tree pack; nothing ripped from games.
  • Bark: 4 CC0 photoscan sets (Poly Haven/ambientCG), 2048x2048 albedo/normal/ORM, licence files stored; plus a forest-floor needle-litter terrain layer, 2048x2048 CC0.
  • Octahedral impostor atlases for every tree variant (2048x2048, 8x8 hemi-octahedral frames, albedo/alpha + normal/depth), baked by a new Editor ImpostorBaker tool. Amplify Impostors (Asset Store EULA) is the paid alternative.
  • New original shader Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Foliage.shader (+ VC_FoliageSurface.hlsl): two-sided translucency, 3-level wind, dithered alpha/LOD fade, wetness, per-instance hue; variants added to VC_Variants.shadervariants.

6. Grass, ground cover and terrain

Score today: 2.5 / 10   Sparse identical card clumps on noise-generated procedural soil, flat basemap hills, and no clutter, wind, wetness or biome identity put the ground roughly at an early-2000s or mobile level, far from GTA V or Cyberpunk.

The ground is the weakest layer in the day captures. Grass is Unity's built-in detail layer: about 9.5 M clumps of 3 crossed upright cards with up-facing normals. They sit on 3.9 m cells over procedural FBM ground whose cover is synthesised from altitude, slope and Perlin noise. The result is evenly spaced lime stickers on brown soil, with no wind, no terrain tint, hard bases and a 120 m cut-off. Past 450 m the terrain falls back to a 512 px-per-km basemap, so the hills turn flat olive. Stock URP Terrain Lit with 8 layers has no height blending, no triplanar, no macro variation and no wetness response. The same grass detail system also causes the 50-165 ms DetailMesh spikes in BUG-120. Getting to AAA needs four things: a data-driven land-cover bake, a GPU-driven ground-cover and clutter renderer, a custom VC_Terrain shader with macro maps, and scanned or authored CC0 material sets.

Findings and fixes (12)

TER-01Grass is sparse, evenly scattered identical clumps: 'stickers' on bare soil critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Grassland is brown soil dotted with evenly spaced, identical lime-green clumps (0.6–1.1 m wide, ~2–4 m apart). It looks like stickers. There are no dense swards or natural patches, and the pattern is the same from 1 m to 120 m (CAM_18, CAM_09, CAM_10).
Root cause

Ground cover is Unity's built-in detail layer at 3.9 m cells with at most 9 instances per cell, a single 3-card prototype, and density = splat weight x 9 with no clustering noise. At ~0.2 clumps/m2 of ~0.5 m2 each, most of the soil stays bare, and per-cell jitter gives an even polka-dot. Real grass is a continuous sward with patchiness at 1-10 m. GTA V/RDR2-style systems scatter dense GPU-instanced blades by clump masks and colour the soil under them like grass.

Fix

Replace the detail layer with a GPU-driven ground-cover renderer. Editor/GroundCoverBaker.cs (idempotent) bakes per-250 m chunk density maps (R8, 0.5 m/texel = 512², one channel per species) from the land-cover bake (TER-03) plus the GrassMasks rules into Art/Terrain/GroundCover. World/GroundCoverRenderer.cs and Art/Shaders/VC_GroundCoverCull.compute then do the per-frame work:

  • jittered candidates on a 0.2 m grid within 20 m, thinned with distance out to 150 m
  • sample density, TerrainData.heightmapTexture and the normal
  • frustum + Hi-Z culling
  • append into 3 LOD buffers, drawn with Graphics.RenderMeshIndirect (extend the InstancedPropRenderer.cs pattern)

Modulate density with 2-3 octaves of clump/blue noise to get swards, thin patches and bare spots. Targets: 150-400 blades/m² near the camera, ≥90 % coverage within 30 m. Take the material from VeyraMaterialLibrary and give the compute shader a serialized field (no Shader.Find).

Cost
Estimate: 250-600k visible instances, 1.0-1.5 ms GPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070 with compute culling. CPU cost is near zero and the DetailMesh jobs (BUG-120) go away. Measure with the Profiler and stats.json.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:15,35-36: built-in detail layer, InstanceCountMode, DetailRes 256 per 1 km tile = 3.9 m cells

TER-02Grass blades shade flat: up normals, hard base, no wind, no ground tint, aliasing high effort M (1-3 days) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Each clump is a flat, bright, saturated green wall with a ruler-straight bottom edge. It never moves, ignores the ground colour, looks blurry and stair-stepped up close, and thins out with distance (CAM_14 foreground, CAM_10 right slope).
Root cause

Stock URP/Lit cards with all normals up get ground-plane lighting: flat, maximum brightness at noon, no root AO, translucency or specular. All blades start at texture row 0, which gives a straight base, and alignToGround is unset, so cards stand vertical on slopes. Wind globals exist, but no grass shader reads them. A 256 px alpha-tested texture is magnified near the camera, rendered at 0.9 scale and upscaled by STP, with no coverage-preserving mips, so blades look soft near the camera and thin out at distance.

Fix

Write Art/Shaders/VC_Grass.shader (HLSL, SRP Batcher, Forward+, instanced from TER-01):

  • root colour from the terrain macro colour map at the instance position, lerped to a tip colour
  • root AO from 0.35 to 1.0 along height
  • normal 50/50 between the blade and terrain normals
  • translucency ~0.3, smoothness 0.25-0.35
  • wind from the existing _VC_WindStrength/_VC_WindDirection plus a 256² gust noise, bend ∝ h², per-instance phase
  • roots aligned to the terrain normal, sunk 0-5 cm

LOD0 (≤20 m): real blade strips with no alpha. LOD1/2: atlas cards with mipMapsPreserveCoverage and a dither fade. Interim: alignToGround 0.6 on the DetailPrototype, preserve-coverage mips with alphaTestReferenceValue 0.4, aniso 4. Author 3-4 species per biome in Blender on a 2048² atlas. Cover the keywords in VC_Variants.shadervariants.

Cost
Blade LOD0 at 60-150 tris per clump only within 20 m. Estimate: wind vertex maths adds <0.2 ms. Using no alpha at LOD0 removes overdraw and alpha-test cost.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:162: every card vertex normal = Vector3.up
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:105-121: all 70 blades start at texture row 0 at alpha 1 (straight base), only darkened to 0.6 at the root; colours lerp (0.22,0.36,0.12)-(0.42,0.5,0.2)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:101,136-145: 256x256 texture on stock URP/Lit, alpha clip 0.4, cull off; Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Grass/M_Grass.mat:11,88,96
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Grass/GrassBlades.png.meta:14-15,38: mipMapsPreserveCoverage 0, alphaTestReferenceValue 0.5 (cutoff is 0.4), aniso 1
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:26-34: DetailPrototype without alignToGround (cards stay vertical on slopes up to the 32° limit)

TER-03Land cover is Perlin noise, not data: no biomes or per-district ground high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Coast, mountains, lake shore and suburbs share the same grass/brown-soil mix in soft blobs that have nothing to do with forest, roads or slopes. Forest ends in smooth curves on flat green pasture, and the lake shore is dark brown (CAM_11, CAM_18).
Root cause

Because the source splats are empty, CityBuilder builds the cover from altitude, slope and noise only. It ignores districts, tree cover, water, roads, drainage and land use. Real landscapes read as real because the ground follows causes: needles under trees, mud in hollows, wear along paths, mown grass near houses. GTA V's countryside and Cyberpunk's Badlands paint cover from masks like these per biome. Here every district gets one rule set and one grass species, so the world reads as generic.

Fix

Add Editor/LandCoverBaker.cs, idempotent, writing to Data/ and Art/Terrain. It derives a 1 m land-cover ID map (8192x6144) only from Veyra data: district polygons, road corridors, building footprints, sea/lake polygons, tree placement density, and slope/curvature/flow accumulation computed from the data heightmap (heights unchanged). Rules live in Data/land_cover_rules.csv:

  • D2: dune grass, sand, salt scrub
  • D3: gravel, oily concrete, weeds in cracks
  • D4: rock, scree, needle litter, ferns
  • D5: mown lawn, flowerbeds, driveways
  • D7: short mown grass
  • D8: meadow, wildflowers, farmland rows
  • D1/D6: paving, tree pits

Write the splats at alphamapResolution 512 (1.95 m) and feed the same map to the ground-cover species channels (TER-01) and the macro colour map (TER-05).

Cost
Bake time only. Estimate: alphamaps at 512 are ~2 MB per tile, ~96 MB for all 48 tiles; consider BC7 control textures in the custom shader (TER-04). Keep 256 on the Android tiers.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:533-548: comment 'source splat maps are almost empty'; weights come from height, slope and two Perlin octaves; grass base weight is 0.8+0.6n everywhere (:543); forest floor by altitude 55-140 m (:542)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:488,496: alphamapResolution 256 per 1 km tile = 3.9 m per splat texel
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:26-34: one grass species for all 8 districts
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_11_BR_Switchbacks_Aerial_12h.png: green and brown blobs unrelated to tree cover; a smooth treeline ends on flat pasture at x~1790-2300, y~770-1215
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_18_RV_MirrorLake_Morning_12h.png: the lower half is dark brown soil (forest-floor weight at 143 m altitude)
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:43 (BUG-058: one grass splat everywhere)

BUG-058
TER-04Stock URP Terrain Lit: no height blend, stretched cliffs, tiling, never wet high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Layer transitions are soft, smeary cross-fades. The CAM_14 cliff face is vertically streaked green-grey rock. Terrain has no rain response at night (code-level finding; the 22:00 wet captures were not opened).
Root cause

URP Terrain Lit blends linearly by splat weight. Height blend is off and is unsupported with 8 layers. Its top-down planar UVs stretch texels on 60-70° faces (about 2-3x, estimate) and turn the strata sine into contour bands on slopes. It has no anti-tiling, macro variation or triplanar, takes two passes for 8 layers, and never reads _VC_Wetness. AAA terrain uses height-based blends, triplanar cliffs, macro variation and puddle wetness: the cues the eye uses to tell soil from rock.

Fix

Write Art/Shaders/VC_Terrain.shader (HLSL on URP terrain includes, SRP Batcher, holes, instanced per-pixel normal):

  • Texture2DArrays for albedo+height, normal and mask
  • both control maps in one pass, through per-tile materials made by the editor
  • top-4 weights per pixel with height blending (transition 0.05-0.2)
  • distance tiling: UV and UV x 0.25 blended from 20 to 80 m, plus stochastic tiling on soil
  • triplanar only where slope > 40°
  • macro tint and macro normal (TER-05)
  • wetness from _VC_Wetness: albedo x 0.65, smoothness up to 0.55, puddles in low mask-height areas

Remove the strata sine from the rock albedo once the rock is triplanar. Materials are editor-authored assets referenced through VeyraMaterialLibrary; add the keywords to VC_Variants.

Cost
Estimate: one pass with top-4 sampling costs about the same as or less than today's two passes; triplanar only on steep pixels. Target ≤1.5 ms terrain at 1440p; measure with the GPU profiler.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:486: Terrain.mat created from 'Universal Render Pipeline/Terrain/Lit' (Editor only)
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Materials/Terrain.mat:90,92: _EnableHeightBlend 0, _HeightTransition 0
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:577-584: 8 layers = two 4-layer passes; URP height blend only works with ≤4 layers
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:540,543: rock weight ≤3 from 26-40° slopes, grass base 0.8-1.4 everywhere, so cliffs keep ~25 % grass tint (estimate)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Common.hlsl:9: _VC_Wetness is read only by VC_Road/VC_RoadSurface/VC_Water/VC_Input; no terrain shader in Art/Shaders
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TerrainLayerMaps.cs:63-64: rock strata = sin(v), projected top-down
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_14_AC_Lighthouse_Golden_12h.png: cliff band x~0-1660, y~640-870 shows vertical smeared streaks and green tint on rock
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_11_BR_Switchbacks_Aerial_12h.png: summit x~380-1150, y~100-260 shows horizontal banding (planar-projected strata)

TER-05Distant hills render as a flat olive basemap high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Beyond ~450 m the hills are one flat khaki-olive colour with no mottling, rocks, paths or vegetation tone. The far hill in CAM_18 looks like felt next to the treed hill beside it.
Root cause

Past 450 m, URP draws the baked basemap: 512² per km of averaged layer colour. The cover is a noise mix of grass, forest floor and dirt, so the average is a single olive with no normal detail. There is no macro colour map and no far grass or scrub tone, and grass ends at 120 m. The far land loses the variation that cues scale. GTA V keeps far terrain lively with baked colour and vegetation maps plus tree impostors.

Fix

Bake a world macro colour map (4096x3072 BC7, ~2 m/px) from the land-cover map (TER-03) plus near grass and canopy tones. Bake a macro normal map (4096x3072 BC5) from a high-pass of the data heightmap (ridges and gullies; no height change). In VC_Terrain (TER-04), blend detail layers into the macro map between 150 and 600 m, add 2 noise octaves (60 m and 300 m) for ±12 % brightness and saturation, and apply the macro normal everywhere. Interim on PC tiers: basemapDistance 1000-1500 m and baseMapResolution 1024 (CityBuilder.cs:497,560), or set terrainQualityOverrides (QualitySettings.asset:219) per tier. Far tree impostors belong to the vegetation track.

Cost
Estimate: macro maps ~16 MB BC7 + ~12 MB BC5 with mips, one extra sample each. A 1000 m basemap distance adds full layer shading to more pixels; measure the GPU cost.

TER-06Terrain layer textures are procedural noise with fake relief high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Near ground looks like smeared noise or a camouflage print. There are no real pebbles, clods, needles or cracks, and lighting barely changes across it, so it reads as a mobile-game texture (CAM_14 foreground gaps, CAM_10 verges).
Root cause

Luminance-derived normals put bumps where the colour is dark, not where geometry is, so lighting contradicts the albedo and the surface reads as printed. FBM noise lacks shapes the eye recognises: stones with contact shadows, clods, litter. A single scale of 73-171 px/m blurs up close and repeats further out. AAA terrain uses scanned or sculpted sets with true height and AO, plus detail and macro layers.

Fix

Replace the 8 procedural sets with 10-12 scanned or authored sets: meadow soil, mown lawn, needle floor, leaf litter, cliff rock, scree, beach sand, wet sand/mud, gravel shoulder, dry dirt, farmland, urban dirt. Source CC0 sets (ambientCG or Poly Haven) with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads, or author them in Blender. Size: 2048² (4096² for rock and forest floor). Packing: albedo+height in alpha, normal (Normal Map, BC5), mask R metallic 0 / G AO / B height / A smoothness = 1-roughness. Calibrate albedo to Appendix B. Use 3-4 m near tiles plus TER-04 distance tiling (≥400 px/m within 10 m) and a shared 512² micro-normal within 15 m. Keep TerrainLayerMaps.cs only as a fallback.

Cost
Estimate: 12 layers x 3 maps at 2048² BC7/BC5 ≈ 150-200 MB with mips. Use Texture2DArray streaming and 1024² on the Android tiers.
Evidence

Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TerrainLayerMaps.cs:149-168: height = blurred albedo luminance, normal = Sobel of that, AO from blur difference

TER-07Grass detail patches cause 50-165 ms CPU spikes high effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Stutters while driving past grassland. The worst profiled frames take 137-221 ms and are dominated by terrain grass mesh generation.
Root cause

Unity's detail system builds per-patch instance data on worker jobs as 125 m patches come into range. Each patch holds up to 32x32x9 = 9,216 instances (calculated). In the contaminated smoke profile these jobs coincide with every 130-220 ms frame. The main-thread wait is not proven, and frames still exceed 50 ms in today's clean runs, so grass is the prime suspect but not yet confirmed. Either way, these hitches break the CLAUDE.md rule of no hitch over 50 ms.

Fix

First, re-profile R_BRIDGES and R_SMOKE (Dev, performance power plan, no stray VeyraCity.exe) with Profiler deep markers on the worst frames, and save the results to Logs/PROF. If DetailMesh is still the cause, apply the interim fix within a day:

  • SetDetailResolution(256, 16) (GrassBuilder.cs:36), giving 62.5 m patches
  • detailObjectDistance 120 → 90 m
  • re-run 'regrass' (WorldBuildBatch.cs:37-42)

The permanent fix is TER-01's GPU renderer. Accept when no frame exceeds 50 ms and DetailMesh is under 2 ms per frame.

Cost
This is a perf finding. The interim changes trade a little more draw overhead for smaller jobs (estimate); verify with the Profiler.
Evidence

Logs/PROF/smoke.txt:34-39: frames 136.7-220.6 ms list DetailMesh.MeshGenerationJob at 51-164 ms but WaitForJobGroupID only 2-3 ms (markers likely summed across threads)

TER-08Objects pasted onto the ground: bare verges, no contact bands or shore transition medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 self-checked
You see
House walls rise straight out of flat green lawn with no path, plinth or grime. Road verges are bare flat green strips. Grass stops at the lake in a straight line, and pine trunks pierce bare soil (CAM_16, CAM_18, CAM_10).

Add URP Decal Projectors (DBuffer, 2048² atlas) for foundation grime and junction tyre-dirt, with a 60 m draw distance. Blend boulder and rock shaders to the macro colour within 0.3 m of the ground. Related: BUG-070 (corridor cut edges).

Cost

TER-09No ground clutter: no stones, twigs, needles, leaves, litter or rubble medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
The ground between grass clumps is empty everywhere: no pebbles, fallen needles, cones, branches, flowers, weeds in cracks or urban litter. Forest floors and verges look like clean CG planes (CAM_10 under the pines, CAM_09 foreground).
Root cause

Micro-clutter carries scale and history. Objects of 5-30 cm tell the eye how far away the ground is, needles explain the trees, and litter explains the city. Without it the ground reads as an empty plane. GTA V scatters weeds, stones and litter by material and land-use masks, and Cyberpunk dresses urban ground densely with debris.

  • pebbles
  • twigs and pine cones
  • needle and leaf cards
  • flowers and ferns per biome
  • urban weeds in cracks, paper and cans (original, unbranded), rubble on D3 and building sites

Add an original rock and outcrop kit on slopes > 35°, placed deterministically on the data heightmap (heights sampled, seeded, idempotent). LODs: LOD0+LOD1 for props < 2 m, LOD0-2 for outcrops. Shadows only within 30 m. Keep clutter out of carriageways using the existing masks.

TER-11Grass at constant density to 120 m, then nothing medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Clumps keep the same density to the ~120 m limit and then stop. The same grassland beyond is bare olive terrain, so the near field reads as a sticker carpet ending at an invisible line (CAM_09 crest against the mid hill).
Root cause

There is no distance thinning or fade, and the terrain under the grass is brown-mixed. Where the grass ends, the colour steps from lime clumps to brown and olive soil. GTA V and Cyberpunk thin grass gradually with distance, enlarging the survivors, and colour the terrain underneath with the grass tone. The eye then never sees the boundary.

Fix

In the GPU ground-cover renderer (TER-01), thin the kept fraction from 1.0 to 0.15 between 30 and 150 m and scale the survivors by 1.0-1.8x to conserve coverage. Add a dither fade over the last 20 m. In LandCoverBaker (TER-03) and the macro map (TER-05), give grass-covered terrain the grass tone so no colour step appears past the draw distance. Draw distance: 150 m on PC_High/Ultra, 80 m on the Android tiers, via terrainQualityOverrides or the renderer's own tier settings.

Cost
Thinning lowers the far instance count. Estimate: a net saving compared with uniform density to 150 m.

TER-M1Inland lake shore has no shore material: soil and grass run straight into the water medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
At Mirror Lake, dark soil and grass clumps meet the water in a hard line, with no wet band, mud, pebbles, sand or reeds. The lake looks pasted onto a field (CAM_18).
Root cause

Shore cover is keyed to absolute sea level, not to distance from any water body, so inland water gets the generic upland mix. Real shores show a 1-5 m band of wet, darker soil, mud, gravel and sedge, which tells the eye where water meets land. GTA V and Cyberpunk paint shorelines with distance-to-water masks plus wet-darkening and reed or rock clutter.

Fix

In LandCoverBaker (TER-03), compute a signed distance field to all water polygons (sea, plus the lake polygon used by MirrorLake.LoadOrNull) from Veyra data, without changing heights. Bands:

  • 0-1.5 m: wet mud/pebble layer
  • 1.5-4 m: gravel/sand
  • 2-8 m: sedge/reed ground-cover species

Bake the distance into a macro channel. VC_Terrain applies albedo x0.7 and smoothness 0.45 within 1 m of water, independent of rain. Add the reed species to the TER-01 atlas.

Cost
Bake time only; one extra macro-map channel sample in the terrain shader (negligible, estimate).
Evidence

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • Under-grass ground colour: where the grass detail density is > 0, push the splat toward L_Grass/L_Lawn and cut forest floor and dirt (CityBuilder.cs:542-548, or a post-pass in GrassMasks.cs). The gaps then read as short grass instead of brown soil. This is the largest single cut to the 'sticker' look.
  • Blade texture: start each blade at a random row between 0 and 40 px and darken the bottom 15 % to ~0.35 (GrassBuilder.cs:105-121). Shift the blade colours ~15 % less saturated, toward the L_Grass base (0.27, 0.33, 0.16). This breaks the hard base and the lime cast.
  • GrassBlades import: mipMapsPreserveCoverage = true, alphaTestReferenceValue 0.4, anisoLevel 4 (GrassBuilder.cs:127-131; currently GrassBlades.png.meta:14 = 0). Stops thinning and sparkle at distance.
  • Detail patch size 32 → 16 cells (GrassBuilder.cs:36), then rerun regrass (WorldBuildBatch.cs:37-41) and re-profile BUG-120 on the performance power plan.
  • Basemap: baseMapResolution 512 → 1024 and basemapDistance 450 → 1000 m on the PC tiers (CityBuilder.cs:497,560). Hills keep layer detail further out; measure the GPU cost.
  • Road grass margin HalfWidth + 2.5 m → + 0.5 m for rural classes (GrassBuilder.cs:70), so verges stop being bare flat strips. Verify no grass on the DriveSurface ribbons.
  • heightmapPixelError 4 → 2 on the PC tiers (CityBuilder.cs:559), then check the CAM_11 and CAM_18 silhouettes and GPU time.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Grass coverage: ≥90 % of grass/lawn-classified terrain pixels covered within 30 m of the camera and ≥60 % at 30-100 m. Measure with a grass-only mask render from CAM_09/10/14/18 and a pixel-count script; save under Screenshots/<date>_<card>/.
  • Grass system: LOD0 real blades at 150-400 blades/m² within 20 m, 3 LODs with a dither fade to 150 m. Ground cover ≤1.5 ms GPU and ≤0.3 ms CPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070, and 0 frames > 50 ms caused by grass on R_SMOKE/R_BRIDGES. Measure with the Unity Profiler, Logs/PROF and stats.json.
  • Near terrain: ≥400 px/m effective texel density within 10 m, no visible tile repeat within 60 m (A/B review at CAM_14/CAM_18), height-blended transitions 0.1-0.5 m wide (frame debugger on the VC_Terrain pass).
  • Far terrain (500-2000 m): luminance standard deviation in a 256x128 px window on the CAM_18 centre hill ≥2x today's capture, and hue within ΔE ≤10 of the near grass. Measure with a Python script on the review PNGs.
  • Biomes: each of the 8 districts shows ≥4 distinct ground materials and ≥3 ground-cover species from Data/land_cover_rules.csv, and 0 grass or clutter instances on carriageways, plazas or footprints. Check with the GrassMasks/LandCoverBaker report and an 8-camera district contact sheet.
  • Ground contact: 0 grass or clutter bases floating or buried > 5 cm (validator sampling 10k instances against TerrainData heights). Every building, tree and boulder within 150 m of a review camera has a contact band (splat ring or decal).
  • Clutter: forest floor ≥30 clutter instances per 100 m² within 40 m; urban verges and kerbs ≥2 weeds or litter items per 10 m. Verify in the CAM_10 and CAM_16 reviews and a per-chunk instance count log.
  • Wind and weather: grass and clutter sway at 0.3-1.5 Hz with gust fronts (Recorder clip). At _VC_Wetness = 1, terrain albedo is ×0.6-0.75 and smoothness ≥0.5 in low cavities (frame debugger values plus a 12h vs 22h_wet pixel compare).

Assets to make or source

  • Ground-cover species set, original and authored in Blender (Tools/Blender): meadow grass, mown lawn, dune grass, dry alpine grass, weeds-in-cracks, 2 wildflowers, fern. LOD0 blade clumps 60-200 tris, LOD1 card 8-16 tris, LOD2 single quad. One 2048² atlas: albedo+alpha BC7, normal BC5, mask with AO/translucency/smoothness.
  • Terrain material sets (10-12 layers: meadow soil, mown lawn, needle floor, leaf litter, cliff rock, scree, beach sand, wet sand/mud, gravel shoulder, dry dirt, farmland, urban dirt). 2048² each (4096² for rock and forest floor): albedo+height, normal (BC5), mask (AO, height, smoothness = 1-roughness). Source CC0 scans from ambientCG or Poly Haven with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads, or author in Blender. Calibrate albedo to Appendix B.
  • World macro maps baked from Veyra data: land-cover ID 8192x6144 R8, macro colour 4096x3072 BC7, macro and detail normal 4096x3072 BC5, tree-influence mask 2048x1536.
  • Clutter meshes, all original with LOD0+LOD1: 6 pebbles (50-200 tris), 6 rocks 0.3-3 m (300-3k tris), 8 twigs and branches, pine cones, needle and leaf litter cards (2048² atlas), urban litter (paper, can, bottle; unbranded labels), rubble and gravel patches.
  • Cliff and outcrop rock kit, 6 pieces, original Blender sculpt and bake: LOD0 8-15k tris, LOD1 40 %, LOD2 10 %, 2048² textures plus a shared rock detail normal.
  • Ground decal atlas 2048² for URP Decal Projectors: foundation grime, tree-base litter, road-edge dirt, tyre-dirt at junctions, puddle masks.
  • Two tileable 256² wind noise textures (gust and flutter), generated procedurally in the editor.

7. Water: sea, lake and shore

Score today: 2 / 10   By day the water is a flat, sky-only, opaque tinted plane that never shows the skyline, hills or bed; at night it is near-black with a fake diagonal light grid, far from GTA V harbour water or Cyberpunk's wet-neon reflections.

The water looks like a flat tinted sheet for five main reasons. (1) It mirrors only the sky. The only probe over the sea and the lake renders just the sky layer, and the project has no planar reflection or SSR, so the skyline, hills, pines, windows and neon never show in the water. (2) It is drawn as opaque paint lit like a matte surface, with no depth colour, no refraction and no view of the bed. That is why Mirror Lake looks like pale cyan ice and every shore ends in a hard cut. (3) The sea is a flat 24 km plane built from 1000 m quads. Its only 'swell' is two fixed sine waves in the normals, which draw regular bands. (4) The ripples come from one 512 px noise map that repeats every 9 m. Far water is flattened into a pale mirror instead of being given more roughness. (5) At night a world-fixed noise trick draws diagonal orange grid lines in place of real light reflections. There is also no response to rain or wind, no wakes or splashes, no river flow, a static foam strip at the coast and ocean surf foam on the lake. Plan for URP 17.6 Forward+: (a) draw the water after opaques, sampling _CameraDepthTexture and _CameraOpaqueTexture (both already enabled on the PC tiers), for absorption, refraction, intersection foam and caustics; (b) add a half-resolution planar reflection for the main water surface, SSR as a fallback, and a sky cubemap the water samples directly; (c) use 3 FFT cascades (Gerstner fallback) on a camera-centred clipmap mesh, driven by the existing wind globals; (d) add specular anti-aliasing (Toksvig), a shore distance field for shore waves and wet sand, flow maps for rivers, rain rings, and a ripple simulation for splashes. A licensed alternative is Crest 5 or KWS2 for URP, both paid Unity Asset Store packages. Their price, Unity 6.6 Render Graph support and runtime Shader.Find use must be checked, and their licence must be filed in ArtSource/Downloads. Estimated budget (to be measured): about 2.5 ms GPU and 1 ms CPU for all water at 1440p on the RTX 4070.

Findings and fixes (12)

WAT-01Water reflects only the sky: no planar/SSR reflection, world probes only on D1/D6 land critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Across Crown Bay (CAM_03) the towers have no reflection. Mirror Lake (CAM_18) stays pale sky-blue under a dark olive hill and pines. The water reads as a tinted sheet, not a mirror.
Root cause

The water's specular comes only from URP's cubemap path (UniversalFragmentPBR, VC_ForwardPass.hlsl:108). Over the sea and the lake that cubemap holds only the sky, so every reflected ray returns sky. A cubemap also cannot act as a flat mirror: at grazing angles the reflected rays hit nearby shores and towers that a 128 px cube placed hundreds of metres away puts in the wrong place. Losing the mirrored world removes the strongest cue that a surface is liquid. GTA V renders a separate low-detail reflection view for water; Cyberpunk uses SSR or ray-traced reflections with a probe fallback.

Fix
  1. Add URP's built-in ScreenSpaceReflectionRendererFeature to VC_Renderer_PC_High and PC_Ultra (half resolution on High, full on Ultra). Add #pragma multi_compile_fragment _ _SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION to VC_Water.shader (and to the road and facade shaders for wet streets), and register the variants in VC_Variants.shadervariants. GlossyEnvironmentReflection then blends SSR over the probes. This covers on-screen skyline, hills and neon in CAM_03/04/18. 2) Optionally, for edge and occlusion gaps on the sea: a VeyraPlanarReflection MonoBehaviour that renders a mirrored, oblique-clipped camera through RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest at half resolution (R11G11B10, Terrain/Buildings/Neon/Sky layers, shadows off, LOD bias 0.4, 3 km far plane), used only on the sea plane. 3) Add D2/D3 waterfront probes baked for day and night. Budget (estimate): SSR 0.6-1.0 ms, planar at most 1.5 ms GPU; measure in stats.json.
Cost
Estimated +1.0-1.5 ms GPU and +0.5-1 ms CPU (second cull and draw) at half resolution with a reduced mask. Skip the pass when no water renderer is visible; update at 30 Hz when the camera is still.
Editor note
Unity 6.6 URP has no supported screen-space reflections: the SSR renderer feature is a preview that compiles only behind the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION scripting define and is aimed at Unity 6.7. Try it on a Git branch only; the production path in this report is box-projected probes per block plus a low-resolution planar reflection for water, or the 6.7 LTS upgrade at a milestone boundary (CLAUDE.md).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/SkyReflection.cs:30-41 (128 px, cullingMask 1<<31 = sky only, boxProjection false), :60-64 (set as the custom default reflection)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraReflectionProbes.cs:40-58 (probes only in the D1/D6 polygons), :78-80 (bridge probes), :116-122 (128 px); Assets/_Veyra/World/ReflectionProbeDirector.cs:31 (256 px on Ultra)
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_Ultra.asset:68: SSAO is the only renderer feature. URP 17.6's own ScreenSpaceReflectionRendererFeature exists in Library/PackageCache/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal@66a8e36658da/Runtime/RendererFeatures/ScreenSpaceReflectionRendererFeature/ but is not added, and Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Water.shader:37-46 has no _SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION multi_compile

BUG-068
WAT-02Night water is black with a fake world-fixed diagonal light grid; city lights and neon do not reflect critical effort M (1-3 days) impact 5/5 verified
You see
At 22:00 in the rain (CAM_04) the bay is near-black brown, crossed by straight dotted orange lines that converge like a grid. The lit towers and waterfront throw no light columns onto the water.
Root cause

Emissive windows, signs and neon are not lights, so only a reflection pass (planar or SSR) can mirror them, and there is none (WAT-01). The night patch fakes the reflections with world-space noise that ignores light positions and the viewing direction, so it reads as a pattern painted on the water. On real rippled water, each light forms a vertical streak below it, stretched towards the viewer by the ripple slopes; that is the signature of Cyberpunk's wet neon. The occlusion hack also suppresses whatever sky reflection is bound.

Fix

Delete the cityGlow/streak block (hlsl:89-95). In SkyReflection.cs, publish probe.realtimeTexture as a global _VC_SkyCube and sample it directly in the water shader for the sky term; this does not depend on Forward+ probe selection and also covers sea outside the probe box (the sea reaches ±12 km). Then remove the night occlusion (hlsl:83). Take the night reflections from WAT-01's planar/SSR texture, where emissive windows and VC_Neon appear automatically. Stretch them vertically with a 9-tap blur whose length (0.5-4 % of screen height) follows ripple slope variance, to get light columns. Keep the GGX highlights from Forward+ point and spot lights. Acceptance: CAM_04 at 22h wet shows a reflected column under every lit tower block and waterfront neon, and no straight diagonal lines.

Cost
Removing two noise evaluations saves ALU; the vertical blur adds about 0.1 ms (estimate). The real cost sits in WAT-01.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:93-95: the 'streak' noise varies only along dot(xz,(0.7,0.7)) times a 1.1 m value-noise lattice (VC_Common.hlsl:29-39), one warm tint, placed regardless of where any light is
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:80-83: s.occlusion = lerp(1, 0.12, night) removes 88 % of the environment reflection at night; its comment notes the runtime sky probe 'had no effect' under Forward+
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_04_DT_Skyline_Night_22h_wet.png: converging orange lines at x 30-300, y 690-1100 and dotted rows across y 700-1000; no vertical reflections below the lit facades at x 550-1530
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_15_IP_Berth_Dusk_22h_wet.png: the yard lamp at x 890, y 390 lights neither water nor wet quay; the frame is uniformly dark
Tracked as
new
WAT-03Opaque, Lambert-lit water: no depth absorption, refraction or bed visibility (pale 'ice' lake) high effort M (1-3 days) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Mirror Lake is pale cyan like ice (CAM_18), and the sea turns a flat light blue towards the horizon. Sand and rocks never show through shallow water, and every shoreline is a hard cut.
Root cause

Water colour should come from light refracted and absorbed below the surface, mixed with reflection by Fresnel. Here the surface is opaque, so the bed never shows and every shore is a hard cut. On the lake, near-grazing views make Fresnel reflection dominate. That reflection holds only the bright sky (WAT-01), and with smoothness 0.95 and distance flattening it turns into a uniform pale band. The shallow albedo (0.16,0.36,0.34)*0.75 within 5 m of depth adds chalky teal, and the grazing tint lerp runs backwards. AAA water draws after opaques and uses scene depth and colour.

Fix

Move Veyra/Water to queue 2900 (Transparent-100) with ZWrite On, and remove its DepthOnly/DepthNormals passes so the depth texture holds the bed. In VC_WaterSurface.hlsl: albedo 0. Thickness = scene depth minus water depth. Refraction = SampleSceneColor(uv + n.xz x 0.04 x saturate(thickness / 2)), re-checking depth so foreground objects do not leak in. Transmittance = exp(-σ·thickness), σ ≈ (0.45, 0.09, 0.06) per m for the sea and (0.30, 0.06, 0.05) for the lake (tune). Add in-scatter (0, 0.07, 0.09) x sun x (1-F) as emission. Result = lerp(refracted x T + scatter x (1-T), reflection, Fresnel). Caustics: rebuild the bed position from depth and add a 512² flipbook x sun x exp(-thickness / 1.5). Opaque texture downsampling: None on Ultra, 2x on Medium and High. Keep the height field only as the Mobile fallback. Acceptance: the CAM_18 lake is no brighter than the reflected hill.

Cost
Adds one depth sample, one colour sample and one caustic tap per water pixel: about 0.2-0.4 ms (estimate) with water filling the screen at 1440p.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Water.shader:23 (RenderType Opaque, no Queue tag = 2000), :30 ZWrite On, :56-91 DepthOnly/DepthNormals passes write water depth
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:56-62 (tint lerps towards _GlassColor at grazing angles), :69-71 (shallow albedo 0.16,0.36,0.34 x0.75), :48-52 (far normals flattened to a mirror)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/WaterMaterial.cs:37-39 (deep 0.012,0.045,0.065; glass 0.05,0.2,0.22; smoothness 0.95)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GroundHeightField.cs:7 (10 m per texel bed height)
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:22-24 (and Medium, Ultra): depth and opaque textures on, 2x downsampling, unused by water
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_18_RV_MirrorLake_Morning_12h.png: the lake (y 565-645) is uniformly pale blue-white with a hard edge at y ≈ 640
Tracked as
new
WAT-04No wave geometry: the sea is a flat 24 km plane of 1000 m quads, and its 'swell' is two fixed sine waves in the normals high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
The sea is dead flat: the horizon line is ruler-straight, with no wave silhouettes or crests. Regular parallel bands across the bay (CAM_03) look like a tiled texture rather than wind-driven sea.
Root cause

A real sea is a broad spectrum of waves of many lengths and directions. Added together randomly, they give non-repeating chop aligned with the wind, with sharp crests and rounded troughs. Two pure sine waves give a periodic interference pattern the eye spots at once, and without displacement the silhouette, the horizon and the shoreline never move. GTA V displaces a water grid centred on the camera; Crest and KWS use FFT cascades.

Fix

Replace SeaPlane with a camera-centred clipmap, VeyraOceanMesh: a 128x128-quad centre plus 6 hollow rings of 128x128 quads (12,288 quads each), 0.5 m quads in the centre, doubling per ring, snapped to each ring's grid. That is about 180k triangles (estimate), plus a flat skirt to ±12 km for the horizon. Build it in 250 m tiles to satisfy BUG-037. Displacement: Tessendorf FFT in a compute shader, JONSWAP spectrum driven by _VC_WindStrength and _VC_WindDirection, 3 cascades (250 m, 47 m, 9 m) at 256² RGBA16F, outputting displacement, normals and the Jacobian (for crest foam), sampled in vertex and fragment. Fallback for Medium and Mobile: a sum of 8 Gerstner waves (4-60 m, steepness at most 0.5, within ±40° of the wind). Inland water: amplitude x0.1 through _MacroVariation. For buoyancy later, read back cascade 0 asynchronously.

Cost
FFT 3x256² ≈ 0.2-0.3 ms GPU; clipmap vertex cost ≈ 0.1 ms (estimates). Fewer overdraw problems than one huge plane.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:850-875: SeaPlane is a 25x25-vertex grid over 24 x 20 km (quads about 1000 x 833 m) at y -0.15, with nothing to displace
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_ForwardPass.hlsl:38-54: the vertex stage has no displacement
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:38-44: the swell is two cosines (38 m and 17 m) in fixed directions, normals only; _VC_WindDirection (VC_Common.hlsl:12) is never read
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_03_DT_Skyline_Golden_12h.png: evenly spaced horizontal bands at y 690-800; the horizon at y ≈ 683 is a straight line
Tracked as
new
WAT-05Ripples: one 512² noise normal map repeating every 9 m, and distance flattening that turns far water into a pale mirror high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Water near the camera looks like grainy blue noise (CAM_03 foreground). Farther out it smooths into an even pale band with no sun glade. It reads as a texture, not ripples.
Root cause

Small wind ripples are elongated crests perpendicular to the wind. FBM noise gives round blobs, so the surface reads as noise, and using one map at three scales repeats its features. Flattening normals with distance does stop aliasing, but it also removes the roughness that makes far water widen the sun into a glade and blur its reflections, so the far water becomes a pale mirror. AAA water instead turns the lost normal detail into roughness (specular anti-aliasing, e.g. Toksvig or LEAN mapping).

Fix

Author an original, tileable wind-wave normal set baked in Blender: T_Water_Ripple_N 1024² (5 m tile) and T_Water_Chop_N 1024² (23 m tile, so the tile sizes do not divide evenly), BC5, mipmaps, aniso 16. Once WAT-04 lands, use the FFT cascade normals instead. Blend layers by adding partial derivatives, not by normalize(sum). Replace the farFlat block with specular anti-aliasing: roughness² += the variance from the length of the mip-filtered normal (Toksvig) or from ddx/ddy of the normal, with smoothness clamped to 0.75-0.97. Remove the hand-added glint emission (hlsl:84-88), because GGX with correct roughness forms the glade; if a near-field sparkle stays, multiply it by the main light's shadowAttenuation. Acceptance: no visible repeat within 100 m at 1440p, and a sun glade in a sun-facing review camera.

Cost
Roughly neutral: the same number of texture taps (2 textures instead of 3 reads of one), plus a few ALU for the Toksvig term.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/ProcTextures.cs:198-201: Water_N is a 512² round-blob (isotropic) FBM height converted to a normal map, with no wave shapes
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/WaterMaterial.cs:41: tiling 1/9 per metre; Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:26-29: all three layers read the same texture (9 m, 11.7 m rotated, about 43 m)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:48-52: normals flatten to 25 % between 25 m and 180 m; :84-88 add HDR sun-glint emission that ignores shadows (GetMainLight without a shadow coordinate)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_03_DT_Skyline_Golden_12h.png: blotchy noise at y 850-1125 and a uniform pale band at y 685-760; CAM_14_AC_Lighthouse_Golden_12h.png: the sea at x 1100-2000, y 590-745 is flat pale blue with fine horizontal striping
Tracked as
new
WAT-06Shoreline: hard waterline, static foam decal strip, no shore waves or wet sand, ocean surf on a calm lake high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Water meets land on a hard line: no breaking waves, wash or wet sand at the cliff foot (CAM_14) or the promenade (CAM_03). The calm mountain lake has a white surf rim (CAM_18, right side).
Root cause

Viewers judge water by its edge. Real shores show waves rising and breaking, a thin moving wash and darker, glossy wet sand, all in sync. A static decal and a depth band with 10 m resolution cannot follow the real waterline, never move with a wave, and treat a still lake as surf. GTA V and Forza combine foam from the scene-depth intersection with shore-wave timing taken from a distance field of the coastline.

Fix
  1. Intersection foam from scene depth (needs WAT-03): foam = 1 - saturate(thickness / 0.6 m), broken up by an original 1024² foam texture, plus FFT crest foam on the sea. 2) New idempotent editor tool, ShoreDistanceBaker: bake a signed distance and shore direction texture (2 m per texel over the coast cells) from the districts.json coastline. Use it for shore waves: wave phase along the distance (2-3 wave trains, period about 8 s), vertex lift up to 0.4 m in the last 25 m, wash foam at the run-up. 3) Wet band in the terrain shader: albedo x0.6 and smoothness 0.85 inside the run-up. 4) A WaterProfile ScriptableObject (Sea, Lake, River) on each water body: lake surf off, contact line only. Retire the SurfFoam strips once steps 1-3 pass review.
Cost
Distance texture: a few MB in R16G16 (estimate, depends on coast area); +2 texture taps in water and terrain shaders.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/ShorelineBuilder.cs:92-109: surf foam is a static strip from 6 m seaward to 2 m landward at sea level +0.04 m; :190-245 fixed 512x256 texture on URP Lit transparent, never animated
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:65-79: shader foam band wherever height-field depth is under 1.2 m, on every water body; Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/WaterSurface.cs:38-44 changes only swell and bump on inland water
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GroundHeightField.cs:7: 10 m texels decide where the foam sits
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_18_RV_MirrorLake_Morning_12h.png: white rim at x 1500-2000, y 620-650; straight hard bank at y ≈ 640
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_14_AC_Lighthouse_Golden_12h.png: cliff-to-water contact at x 1100-1450, y 600-690 with no foam or wet band; the far sandbar (y 597-645) is a flat beige strip
Tracked as
new
WAT-07Water ignores rain and wind medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
In the 22:00 rain (CAM_04) the sea looks exactly as when dry: no rain rings, no roughened sheen. Wind strength and direction change nothing on the water.
Root cause

At night, rain is the strongest motion cue on water: thousands of rings break up reflections and roughen the whole surface, and wind sets the direction and size of the chop. When the water ignores the weather that the roads, sky and particles show, it looks pasted into the scene.

Fix

Call VC_Ripples (VC_Common.hlsl:57) in VC_WaterSurface.hlsl for water within 60 m, scaled by _VC_RainIntensity, at about 2x the road frequency. Raise roughness by 0.08 x rain over the whole surface and cut the sun glint when cloud cover is high. Replace the fixed d1/d2 directions (hlsl:40) with _VC_WindDirection.xz rotated ±25°, and scale the swell or FFT spectrum by _VC_WindStrength. In the rain particle setup, add a low layer of drifting rain mist over water (soft particles, at most 150 particles).

Cost
About 0.05 ms (estimate): one procedural ripple evaluation, near field only.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl (all 98 lines): never reads _VC_RainIntensity or _VC_Wetness (declared at VC_Common.hlsl:9-10); swell directions are hard-coded at :40
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:86-87: roads already add animated rain rings with VC_Ripples (VC_Common.hlsl:57) while _VC_RainIntensity > 0
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_04_DT_Skyline_Night_22h_wet.png (rain 1.00 per review.txt:23): water at y 690-1125 shows no rain pattern
Tracked as
new
WAT-08Streaming hitch: WaterSurface scans every renderer in the scene on each cell load medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Driving across 1 km cell borders can stutter. Each streamed cell triggers a full-scene renderer scan to re-skin the water, which is one of the CellLoaded subscribers blamed for 215 hitches over 50 ms on R_BRIDGES.
Root cause

Swapping the material at runtime by name was a patch for the generator assigning the old 'Water' material. Every cell load scans every renderer, including inactive ones, on the main thread. The lambda cannot be unsubscribed, so it also outlives scene reloads.

Fix

Assign M_Water when the world is built. CityBuilder.BuildWater (CityBuilder.cs:850-905) and the cell export take VeyraMaterialLibrary slot WaterSurface directly; the editor stays idempotent. Add a WaterBody component with a serialized WaterProfile (Sea, Lake or River) that applies its property block in OnEnable, so streamed cells configure themselves without a scan. Remove the CellLoaded subscription. If a runtime fallback stays, use a static registry of WaterBody instances, not FindObjectsByType. Acceptance: rerun Tools/World/card_checks.sh A2 --routes R_BRIDGES; the [IterCost] line shows no water cost and hitches over 50 ms drop.

Cost
Removes a whole-scene scan per cell load, an O(renderers) main-thread spike; part of BUG-112.

BUG-112
WAT-09Rivers do not flow: same fixed world scroll as the sea, no flow map, no banks medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
River ripples drift in the same fixed world direction as the sea instead of downstream. There is no current, no foam at bridge piers, and the banks are bare.
Root cause

A river is read by its direction: ripples and foam move downstream, gather behind obstacles and speed up in narrow stretches. Ripples that slide sideways across the river, the same as at sea, break that cue at once. AAA games advect the normals and foam along a flow map.

Fix

New idempotent editor tool, RiverFlowBaker: from the river centre line in districts.json (39 points) and the existing river water mesh, write a flow vector into each vertex's UV2: the downstream tangent x speed, with speed from the elevation drop along the data profile (no new coordinates). Shader: two-phase flow-map advection (1.5 s period, half-offset blend) of the ripple normals and foam; pier foam from the depth intersection (WAT-03); river FFT amplitude 0. Banks: until bank lines exist in the data (BUG-119), add the wet band from WAT-06 along the river edge.

Cost
Two extra normal taps on river pixels only; negligible (estimate under 0.05 ms).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:26-28: scroll offsets are constant world vectors (0.031, 0.017), (-0.023, 0.029), (0.009, -0.006)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/WaterSurface.cs:35-44: inland water only gets swell x0.25 and bump x0.6
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/SeawallBuilder.cs:20 and Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:83 (BUG-119): the river has a centre line only, no banks
Tracked as

WAT-M1Water ignores rain: no rain rings or roughness change at 22h wet, although the road shader already has them medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
In the 22:00 rain captures (CAM_04, CAM_15) the sea and harbour look exactly as they do when dry: no impact rings, no softened sheen. Rain falls on a glassy surface, which breaks the wet-night mood.
Root cause

Raindrop rings and the matte micro-roughness they add are the main cue that it is raining on water. Cyberpunk and GTA V both add animated ring normals and raise water roughness in rain. Here the water shader never reads the rain global, so the wet-night captures show still water under falling rain.

Raise roughness by 0.08 x rain beyond 40 m. Reuse the road's ripple helper so the look matches. Optional: a splash particle VFX at the water plane near the camera (pooled, at most 2k particles). Acceptance: rings visible within 30 m at 22h wet in the CAM_15 berth capture.

Cost
About 2 extra ALU-only ripple evaluations per water pixel near the camera; negligible (estimate, under 0.05 ms).

WAT-10No interaction: no splashes, wakes, ripple rings or underwater view medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
A car leaving the Lantern Strait bridge into the sea (the BUG-050 case) or driving into the surf makes no splash, rings or wake. The surface never reacts, and below it the camera sees nothing.
Root cause

Reacting to the player proves a surface is liquid: displaced water, rings and foam trails. Nothing in the project detects water contact or writes into the water surface, so the water behaves like a painted floor the car falls through.

Fix

Add Gameplay/WaterInteraction.cs. Water height query: sea at y -0.15, the lake at its registry level, plus the FFT/Gerstner offset when present. On wheel or body entry, spawn pooled splash VFX (Particle System; original 8x8 flipbook, 2048², BC7; at most 400 particles) and foam ring decals. Dynamic ripples: a 512² R16F wave-equation texture over 64 m around the camera, 2 compute passes per frame; rigidbodies stamp into it, and the water shader adds its normals and foam. Underwater: a fog and tint volume when the camera drops below the surface. Crest and KWS include these features if one of them is licensed.

Cost
Ripple simulation at 512² ≈ 0.05-0.1 ms GPU (estimate); splashes are event-driven and pooled (no GC).

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • Delete the night 'cityGlow' streak block (VC_WaterSurface.hlsl:89-95) to remove the diagonal orange grid in CAM_04 (about 1 h).
  • Turn off shader surf foam on inland water using the existing per-renderer field: foam only where _MacroVariation ≤ 0.001, i.e. the sea (hlsl:65-79). Removes the white rim on Mirror Lake (about 1 h).
  • Reverse the tint: lerp towards _GlassColor when looking down and on crests, not at grazing angles (hlsl:62), and cut the shallow albedo to about (0.03, 0.08, 0.08) (hlsl:70). The lake stops reading as ice (about 2 h).
  • Publish SkyReflection's realtime cubemap as a global _VC_SkyCube, sample it in the water shader, then remove the night occlusion of 0.12 (hlsl:83). Gives a correct night sky on all water, including the sea outside the probe box (half a day).
  • Replace the distance normal flattening (hlsl:48-52) with Toksvig roughness and drop the extra glint emission (hlsl:84-88). Gives a real sun glade with no pale mirror band (half a day).
  • Take swell directions from _VC_WindDirection and add VC_Ripples rain rings scaled by _VC_RainIntensity in the near field (about 2 h).
  • Remove WaterSurface's CellLoaded lambda and full-scene scan; assign M_Water when the world is built (half a day, BUG-112).
  • Add the CAM_19-22 water review cameras to ReviewCameraTools.cs (about 2 h).

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Reflections: in CAM_03 (12h) and CAM_04 (22h wet), every tower over 60 m within 2 km shows a reflection directly below it in the water. Measure by comparing the luminance column under each tower with open water in the PNG (scripted check), plus a visual sign-off.
  • Night lights: CAM_04 and the new CAM_21 at 22h wet show a vertical light streak under every lit facade block, lamp and neon sign on the waterfront, and no straight lines that stay fixed in world space. Measure with a visual review and a 2 s Recorder clip.
  • Depth and colour: at the CAM_20 beach camera, the bed is visible through at least 1.5 m of water and fades to the deep colour by about 8 m. The Mirror Lake surface is no brighter than the reflected hill (mean sRGB value in the CAM_18 lake band ≤ that of the hill band). Measure from the PNG pixels plus GroundHeightField depth.
  • Waves: FFT or Gerstner displacement with at least 3 wind-aligned scales. Autocorrelation of a 1440p water strip shows no repeat under 100 m, and the horizon silhouette moves by at least 2 px over a 2 s clip. Measure with a scripted analysis of the Recorder frames.
  • Shoreline: animated wash foam within 2 m of the waterline along at least 90 % of the natural coast (validator samples the coastline points); Mirror Lake has only a contact line of at most 0.5 m; a wet sand band is visible at CAM_14 and CAM_20.
  • Specular anti-aliasing: with a static camera, the mean frame-to-frame luminance change over water pixels farther than 100 m stays under 2 % (target) in a 2 s capture. Measure with the Recorder plus a script.
  • Weather and interaction: at 22h wet, rain rings are visible within 30 m; splash VFX and ripple rings appear on any rigidbody entering water (PhysicsTestTrack test).
  • Performance: all water (FFT, surface, reflection, ripple simulation) costs at most 2.5 ms GPU and 1 ms CPU at 1440p on the RTX 4070, frame p99 ≤ 16.6 ms, and no hitch over 50 ms on cell loads. Measure with Profiler markers Veyra.Water.* in Development player stats.json on CAM_03 and R_BRIDGES.

Assets to make or source

  • T_Water_Ripple_N and T_Water_Chop_N: 1024² tileable wind-wave normal maps (5 m and 23 m tiles), BC5. Original, baked from a Blender Ocean modifier scene in ArtSource/Blender/Water.blend.
  • T_Water_Foam: 1024² tileable foam albedo and alpha (BC7, sRGB). Original, from Blender noise or particles; or CC0 from ambientCG/Poly Haven with the licence text in ArtSource/Downloads/<source>/.
  • T_Water_Caustics: 512² flipbook of 32 frames (BC4), procedural Voronoi caustics, original.
  • Splash and spray VFX: an 8x8 flipbook at 2048² (BC7) plus 2-3 low-poly spray meshes (under 300 tris each, LOD0 only, small props), original.
  • Shore distance field and river flow data, baked by the new idempotent tools ShoreDistanceBaker and RiverFlowBaker from districts.json (coastline, river centre line). No new coordinates.
  • WaterProfile ScriptableObjects (Sea, Lake, River): absorption σ, scatter colour, wave amplitude, foam on or off, referenced from WaterBody components; materials stay in VeyraMaterialLibrary.
  • Optional licensed route: Crest 5 (Wave Harmonic) or KWS2 Water System, both for URP. Paid Unity Asset Store packages; check current price and per-seat terms, Unity 6.6 / URP 17.6 Render Graph support, and whether the runtime creates materials with Shader.Find (that would break the CLAUDE.md rule and need patching). Store the licence or receipt in ArtSource/Downloads/<package>/ before import.

8. Rain, smoke and other effects

Score today: 2.5 / 10   Rain, wet roads, spray, tyre smoke, steam, lens drops and nitro all exist, but each is an unlit blob or line generated in code, with no lighting, no contact with the world and no flipbook detail. Wet nights look like rain lines pasted over a mostly dry scene.

Every weather and vehicle effect is a Shuriken system built in code at runtime, using URP Particles/Unlit copies and 8-64 px radial textures made with SetPixel. VFX Graph is not installed, and there are no flipbooks, no lit particles, no scene collision and no Art/VFX folder. Rain is 6000 unlit streaks in a 60x60 m box that follows the camera. The streaks keep a fixed colour, so they look the same over black facades as under neon. They fall at 24 m/s, have no splashes and pass through any cover. The wet look exists only in the VC_Road shader, where the ripples form a regular one-ring-per-cell grid. The WetSurfaces hook for other materials is empty, so facades, cars and props stay dry. Tyre smoke, spray, steam and crash dust are flat grey dots. Their brightness is set by a script value instead of real lighting, so at night they either disappear (spray, steam) or glow white (tyre smoke). Nitro flames are crossed quads that the chase camera sees edge-on, so they barely show. Lens drops are one tiled sticker texture that scrolls as a sheet. The PC_High captures run at 111-155 FPS on an RTX 4070 Laptop, which leaves GPU room for a proper VFX Graph pipeline: lit GPU rain, a rain occlusion map with splashes, six-way lit smoke and a wet layer in every shader.

Findings and fixes (14)

VFX-01Rain is a sparse, unlit, constant-colour particle box around the camera critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Rain is a field of identical thin lavender lines, as bright over black unlit facades as under neon. Some streaks near the camera are thick bars, and beyond about 30 m there is no rain at all, so the distance stays crisp.
Root cause

Rain is one CPU Shuriken box with an unlit material and a fixed colour, so a drop's brightness never depends on the light around it. Real rain is visible only where something lights it from behind (lamps, neon, headlights). GTA V and Cyberpunk light their rain and layer it: near streaks, mid-distance sheets, far haze. Here drops fall at 24 m/s, about 2.7x the real terminal speed of about 9 m/s (estimate). Streak length ignores camera motion, and 6000 drops in a 60x60 m box leave everything beyond about 35 m dry.

Fix

Install com.unity.visualeffectgraph 17.6.0 (same SRP line as URP 17.6.0). Author Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX/Rain/VFX_Rain.vfx: 40-60k GPU streaks in an 80x40x80 m volume that follows the camera and wraps, so no gaps open at speed. Fall speed 9-11 m/s plus _VC_WindDirection x (2 + 6 x wind). Stretch each streak along (drop velocity - camera velocity) x 1/60 s. Width 2-3 mm, clamped to at least 1 px with alpha compensation. Shade with a Shader Graph 'VC_RainLit' (Support VFX Graph on, Transparent Unlit target). A Custom Function node loops over GetMainLight plus the Forward+ additional lights (LIGHT_LOOP_BEGIN) and adds a Henyey-Greenstein forward-scatter term (g about 0.6), plus ambient x 0.05 at night, so drops show only inside light cones. Far layer: 4-6 depth-faded rain-sheet cards out to 250 m. RainIntensity drives count and size. Reference the asset through a serialized field or VeyraMaterialLibrary.

Cost
Estimate: 0.3-0.6 ms GPU for 50k thin streaks at 1440p on an RTX 4070, since overdraw is tiny. It also takes the 6000-particle Shuriken simulation off the CPU main thread.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:558-602 - the only rain is one Shuriken system: a 60x60 m box (589) placed at camera +14 m up and +12 m forward (599), emitting 5500/s (601) with a 1.1 s lifetime and at most 6000 particles (572-578)
WorldEnvironment.cs:584-585 - fall speed is fixed at -24 m/s with a fixed x drift of -1.5 m/s, and the global wind (_VC_WindDirection) is ignored; 593-595 renders Stretch with velocityScale 0.04
WorldEnvironment.cs:565-567 and 580 - the material is a copy of the URP Particles/Unlit template (Editor/VeyraMaterialFactory.cs:37,53) with a constant colour (0.8,0.85,1,0.3) x (0.75,0.82,0.95,0.35), the same at 12 h and 22 h
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_22h_wet.png: blue-white streaks over the unlit black facades (x 0-1130 and 1340-2560, y 0-640 of 2560x1440) are as bright as the streaks next to the lit shopfronts
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: a near-camera streak about 13 px wide runs from the top edge to y about 1075 at x about 1520; the shopfront strip at about 150 m (x 345-2395, y 646-717) is perfectly sharp with no rain veil

VFX-02Wet layer exists only in the road shader; facades, props, terrain and cars stay dry high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
When it rains only the asphalt gets wet. Facades, the plaza beside the road, props, grass and cars stay dry and matte, and no drops bead or run on car paint or glass.
Root cause

Wetness is the strongest rain cue at night: porous surfaces darken and turn glossy, so every light appears twice, once more in its reflection. Here only VC_Road reacts to rain. The hook for URP Lit materials is empty, so most of the frame stays dry in a downpour. GTA V and Cyberpunk apply a wet layer to every shader: darkening by porosity, gloss, puddles, streaks on walls and drops on cars, all masked by rain occlusion.

Fix

Create Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Wet.hlsl with one function. Albedo *= lerp(1, 0.45-0.7, wet x _Porosity). Smoothness lerps towards 0.85-0.95. Normal strength drops 30 %. On vertical faces, a world-space triplanar rain-streak flow texture scrolls down at 0.3 m/s. Puddles only where normal.y > 0.9. Mask everything by the rain occlusion map (VFX-04). Include it in VC_Facade, VC_Road (refactored onto the shared code), the neon housings and the terrain and vegetation shaders, and add a per-material _Porosity property. For URP Lit materials (cars, props), build a Shader Graph 'VC_LitWet' with the same sub-graph, assigned in the Editor by VeyraMaterialFactory. Car paint: a droplet-bead normal map (1024², 0.2 m tiling) on upward-facing panels and streaks on the sides that run back with speed. Extend MaterialAuditor to flag any material that has no wet response.

Cost
Estimate: +0.1-0.3 ms GPU (1-2 extra texture samples on facades and cars). No new render passes.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders: only VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:66-89, VC_Common.hlsl, VC_Input.hlsl, VC_Road.shader and VC_SkyDome.shader reference _VC_Wetness or _VC_RainIntensity. VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl, VC_NeonSurface.hlsl and VC_WaterSurface.hlsl have no wet term
Assets/_Veyra/Scenes/VeyraCity.unity:183165 'WetSurfaces: []' (the list is consumed at WorldEnvironment.cs:314-318 and filled by Editor/CityBuilder.cs:101)
WorldEnvironment.cs:281 wet = max(Wetness, WetNight x WetNightLevel): Downtown and Neon Mile are 'wet' every night, but only the road responds
No car paint or glass rain response in Gameplay; only CarSprayFX.cs:86 and GameFlow.cs:1386 read Wetness
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: the open plaza right of the road (x about 1660-2560, y about 720-1280) is uniform matte grey with no reflections

VFX-03Tyre smoke is a faint, flat, unlit blur with no volume (and self-glows at night) high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
A 99 km/h drift leaves only a thin grey haze at the rear tyre. Close up, the smoke is one flat, featureless fog blob with no lit side, no rolling billows and no trail.
Root cause

Smoke looks real through self-shadowing (bright top, dark core), detail that changes over time (flipbook), turbulence and volume that lingers. Here each puff is one blurred 64 px dot on an unlit material, so every particle has the same tone. Effective alpha of about 0.55 x 0.55 x falloff and a 2 s life give no body. Because the material is unlit and the colour is white, the smoke also glows at night. GTA V uses lit, normal-mapped flipbooks; Unity 6 offers six-way lit smoke.

Fix

Build VFX_TyreSmoke.vfx with the Six-way Smoke Lit output. Use an 8x8 flipbook with two 2048² BC7 six-way maps, baked in Blender Mantaflow as original content. Limits: at most 600 particles per car, lifetime 3-6 s, size growing from 1.2 to 6 m. Spawn per metre of sliding distance instead of per second. Inherit 30 % of the car's velocity and add turbulence (intensity 0.6, frequency 0.35) plus _VC_WindDirection. Peak alpha 0.75, soft-particle fade 0.3 m. Take the colour from the SurfaceDefinition. On Dirt and Gravel, emit a continuous dust trail that scales with speed, not only on slip (VehicleFX.cs:403-409). LOD: halve the count beyond 60 m and for AI cars. Fallback on lower tiers: a lit particle template with a normal-mapped flipbook.

Cost
Estimate: 0.3-0.8 ms GPU when smoke fills the screen, mostly from overdraw. Limit this with a half-resolution transparent pass or a cap on screen coverage.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it6_drift.png: during a 99 km/h drift only a faint haze shows behind the rear wheel (x about 1150-1280, y about 970-1150)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it9_crash_closeup.png: the smoke at the rear wheel (x about 1715-2050, y about 615-900) is a uniform grey cloud with no light or dark side
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleFX.cs:224-237 - the texture is a 64x64 radial gradient made with SetPixel (no mips, no flipbook) on the URP Particles/Unlit soft template, with base alpha 0.55
VehicleFX.cs:358-394 - at most 220 particles, lifetime 1.6-2.8 s, gradient peak alpha 0.55, no noise, wind or inherit-velocity modules; 407 emits 55/s by time, not by distance
VehicleFX.cs:409 sets startColor to white on tarmac with no night dimming, unlike CarSprayFX.cs:99 and CarDamage.cs:327

VFX-04Rain has no contact with the world: no splashes, no occlusion under cover high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Rain falls straight through viaduct decks, tunnels, awnings and the car. Nothing splashes on the road, roofs or bonnet, so the rain never seems to touch the world.
Root cause

The rain has no collision, no sub-emitter and no occlusion data, so it ignores cover and leaves no impact. AAA rain is grounded by thousands of tiny splash crowns and a rain shadow. GTA V stops rain under bridges and in tunnels. Cyberpunk-style renderers use a top-down occlusion height map so each drop ends at the first surface it meets and spawns a splash there.

Fix
  1. Add an idempotent Editor tool, Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RainOcclusionBaker.cs. For each 1 km cell, render an orthographic top-down depth of static geometry (roads, viaducts, tunnels, buildings, canopies) into a 1024² R16 texture (about 1 m per texel) and save it under Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX/RainOcclusion/<Cell>. At runtime, bind the 3x3 cells around the camera as a Texture2DArray. 2) VFX_Rain kills drops below the occlusion height and sends GPU events to VFX_RainSplash: 2-4k alive, 0.12-0.25 s life, a 4x4 crown flipbook at 512², lit like the rain. Add 'Collide with Depth Buffer' for dynamic cars. 3) Sample the same map in VC_Wet and VC_Road so roads under decks stay dry and free of ripples. 4) Spawn splashes on the player car's roof and bonnet from its mesh surface.
Cost
Estimate: about 2 MB per cell (R16), 9 cells resident = about 18 MB. Splashes at 0.1-0.2 ms GPU.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:568-597 - the rain system has only main, velocity, shape, emission and renderer modules: no collision, no sub-emitters and no cover test
A grep for 'Splash' over Assets/_Veyra *.cs, *.hlsl and *.shader finds no splash effect
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png and 2026-11-11_iter36/it4_rain_night.png: the road around the car shows only shader rings, no splash crowns and no mist at the tyre contact

VFX-05All VFX are code-built Shuriken systems with runtime SetPixel textures; no VFX Graph, flipbooks or prewarm high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Effects look like soft blurred dots with no internal motion, and the first rain, drift or crash of a session can stall a frame.
Root cause

Every effect is a Shuriken system built in code with a 32-64 px radial blob generated at runtime. There are no flipbooks, so the effects have no evolving detail. There are no mipmaps, so small particles can alias at distance. Artists cannot iterate on any of it. Creating everything on first use causes allocations and texture uploads mid-game. AAA effects are authored assets with flipbook animation, lit outputs and GPU simulation, prewarmed at load.

Fix

Add com.unity.visualeffectgraph 17.6.0. Create Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX/{Rain,Smoke,Spray,Sparks,Nitro,Debris,Steam,Textures}. Author flipbooks as power-of-two BC7 textures with mips (CLAUDE.md), 8x8 frames, 2048² at most. Register each VisualEffectAsset, prefab and particle material in a new VeyraVfxLibrary ScriptableObject held by a scene object, next to VeyraMaterialLibrary. Runtime code then instantiates prefabs instead of calling AddComponent<ParticleSystem> or new Texture2D. Prewarm pools for the player, 8 AI cars, rain and steam at scene load behind the loading screen. Add the particle shader keywords to VC_Variants.shadervariants. Add VFX GPU and CPU markers to FrameCost for budget tracking.

Cost
Simulation moves to the GPU. Estimate: frees 0.3-0.8 ms of CPU main thread in rain with traffic and removes first-use spikes (target: no hitch over 50 ms).
Evidence
Packages/manifest.json:14 has URP 17.6.0 but no com.unity.visualeffectgraph, and Library/PackageCache contains no VFX Graph package
There is no Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX folder. Textures are generated at runtime with SetPixel: VehicleFX.cs:224 (64², no mips), CarSprayFX.cs:25, TrafficSpray.cs:85-86 (32²), AI/StreetFurniture.cs:289, CarDamage.cs:204 and 212
WorldEnvironment.cs:562-597 builds the rain system on the first rain; VehicleFX.cs:221-244 and CarDamage.cs:200-222 build materials and textures on first use
