VFX-07Rain ripples tile as a regular lattice of identical rings on all wet asphalt high effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 verified
You see
The road around the car is stamped with a grid of identical rings that pulse in lockstep, with one big ring under the car. It looks like wallpaper rather than drops landing in puddles.
Root cause

A procedural ripple with one ring per cell at a single scale and rate makes a periodic pattern the eye picks up instantly. Real ripples are sparse, random in time, 3-15 cm in radius and visible mainly in standing water. Here they cover all wet asphalt, and the high-frequency normals turn into sparkle. GTA V and Cyberpunk use precomputed ripple normal textures in several time-offset layers, confined to puddles and paired with splash particles.

Fix

Replace VC_Ripples with a precomputed ripple texture (the Lagarde method from 'Remember Me'). Generate T_RainRipple 512² RGBA (normal xy, time offset, mask) with an Editor tool and store it under Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX/Textures as BC7 with mips. Sample 2 layers at 0.75 m and 1.13 m tiling, the second rotated 37° and phase-shifted. Weight by _VC_RainIntensity, with a per-drop density gate hash > 1 - rain. Apply only where puddle > 0.3 (drop the 0.25 base on bare asphalt), cap the radius at 0.15 m, and keep the 8-30 m distance fade plus mip-based fading against sparkle. Mask with the rain occlusion map (VFX-04).

Cost
Cheaper than now: 2 texture samples replace an unrolled 3x3 hash loop with sin and exp on every wet road pixel.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Common.hlsl:57-78 - exactly one ring per hash cell (59-60, 68-69); every ring has the same period of 1/0.85 s (70); the radius reaches up to 0.9 of a cell; the normals come from sin(ring x 42) waves (74)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:86-87 - sampled at world xz x 1.7 (0.59 m cells) on all wet road, weighted 0.25 + 0.75 x puddle
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: a lattice of near-identical rings at x about 510-1150, y about 960-1150, and a large red-lit ring at about (1240, 1400)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it4_rain_night.png: concentric red rings below the car at x about 1215-1370, y about 1250-1440

new
VFX-06Road spray, steam and crash dust are unlit and dimmed by script, so they vanish at night and never catch neon medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
At 63 km/h in rain no spray rises behind the car or traffic. Manhole steam is a dull uniform grey that never takes on the colour of neon, tail lamps or headlights.
Root cause

Mist and steam are participating media: invisible in darkness, and glowing in the colour of whatever light is behind or inside them. Unlit particles with a global night multiplier (0.15-0.16) can only be uniform grey, so they either vanish or glow falsely. The spray is also thin (0.45-0.9 s life) and follows only the weather's Wetness. Cyberpunk's steam vents read because local lights and signs light the steam.

Fix

Add a lit soft particle template, FxParticleLitSoft, to VeyraMaterialFactory using the VC_ParticleLit shader from VFX-01 (Forward+ light loop plus forward scattering), served through VeyraMaterialLibrary. Move spray, steam, crash dust and engine smoke to it and delete the scripted brightness dimming. Spray: 160-250/s per wheel at 100 km/h, life 0.6-1.2 s, size 0.8 to 3 m, inherit 50 % of velocity, at most 300 particles. Trigger it from max(Wetness, PostVolumeDirector.WetNight x WetNightLevel). Steam: 8x8 flipbook, soft fade 0.4 m, size 0.5 to 3 m, 16-24 pooled emitters, plus rooftop and alley vents in Neon Mile.

Cost
Estimate: 0.2-0.4 ms GPU for 8 spray and 16 steam emitters as lit soft particles under Forward+.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it4_rain_night.png: no plume behind the rear tyres (x about 1050-1510, y about 1010-1060) at 63 km/h on a wet road
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/CarSprayFX.cs:10-11 says 'lit alpha mist', but line 34 uses NewParticleSoft (URP Particles/Unlit, VeyraMaterialFactory.cs:55) and 97-99 dims the colour to 0.16 at night; 88 and 94 give about 34/s per wheel at 63 km/h
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficSpray.cs:35-37, 59 and 67 do the same unlit dimming and are driven only by weather Wetness, so the D1/D6 wet-night preset (WorldEnvironment.cs:281) produces no spray
Assets/_Veyra/AI/StreetFurniture.cs:272-273, 298 and 310-326: steam is unlit and not soft, its colour lerps to (0.42,0.38,0.34) at night, and there are only 8 emitters within 140 m

new
VFX-08No rain atmosphere: no wet haze, light halos or light shafts medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
In heavy rain the air is perfectly clear. Street lamps and shopfronts 150 m away are crisp, with no glow halos, no light cones and no misty depth.
Root cause

In real rain the air fills with droplets and spray. Light scatters into halos and visible shafts, and distant objects lose contrast. That aerial perspective gives depth and mood, as in Cyberpunk's neon haze and GTA V's rainy evenings. Here the fog barely changes with rain and nothing scatters around lights, so the scene reads as clear air with rain lines on top.

Fix

Add a froxel volumetric fog ScriptableRendererFeature (Assets/_Veyra/World/Rendering/VC_VolumetricFog). Use a 160x90x64 froxel grid with density = base + rain x 0.004 and a 30 m height falloff. Scatter the shadowed main light plus Forward+ additional lights within 80 m, with temporal reprojection, composited before transparents. Enable it on PC High and Ultra only. Weather presets: rain fog density 0.0025-0.004 (about 600-900 m visibility, estimate). On Medium and Android, scale halo size and alpha in the existing lamp sprite pool (WorldEnvironment.cs:486-530) with RainIntensity. Keep the CarLampFX beam cones (CarLampFX.cs:265-267).

Cost
Estimate: 0.8-1.5 ms GPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070 (compute-based fog). The halo-sprite fallback costs under 0.1 ms.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:253-255 - rain adds only 0.0006 to the exponential-squared fog density (night total about 0.00165, which is about 10 % fog at 200 m; estimate)
Library/PackageCache/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal@66a8e36658da/Runtime has no VolumetricFog class, so URP 17.6 has no built-in volumetric fog
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: the shopfront strip (x 345-2395, y 646-717) is sharp
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it4_rain_night.png: the lamp heads at about (1300, 320) and (1920, 297) have no halo

VFX-09Nitro flames are invisible from the chase camera; trails and speed lines look like arcade overlays medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
On nitro at 178 km/h there is no visible flame, only a glow blob and two solid orange bands painted on the road. White scratch-lines fly across the sky, and RGB fringing appears on objects.
Root cause

Planes that lie along the jet axis disappear when viewed along that axis, which is exactly the chase-camera view. The static texture has no turbulence, so even when visible it looks like a decal. The light trails and speed lines are arcade overlays that break the photoreal look. GTA V and Cyberpunk show exhaust flames as short, flickering, camera-facing volumes with heat haze, and rely on motion blur to sell speed.

Fix

Rebuild nitro as VFX_NitroFlame.vfx: 20-40 particles that face the camera while aligned with the exhaust axis. Use an 8x4 flame flipbook at 1024² (Blender fire simulation, original) in HDR with emissive 8-30 so bloom picks it up, a blue-white core shading to the mode colour, and Mach diamonds only on Overdrive. Heat haze: a distortion quad that samples _CameraOpaqueTexture (already on in PC_High) through a 256² scrolling normal map at strength 0.01-0.02. Read exhaust positions from new Exhaust_L/Exhaust_R sockets in the vehicle pipeline. Move the light trails, SpeedStreaks and HUD speed lines behind an 'Arcade FX' option, off by default, and cap ChromaticAberration at 0.1.

Cost
Estimate: under 0.1 ms GPU. The heat haze adds one opaque-texture sample over a small screen area.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it19_nitro_fx.png: a glow blob at about (1280, 975); orange bands run from x 1060-1540 at y 910 to the bottom edge; white lines at (540-740, 165-320) and (1485-1535, 60-190); dashed streaks at the left edge (0-256, 245-420); RGB fringing on the lighthouse (2075-2240, 510-720)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleFX.cs:265-282 - the flame is three quads crossed along -Z, which are edge-on when seen from behind; the texture is static (287-311); 133 and 157 hard-code the exhausts at +/-0.35 m; 151-166 add TrailRenderers (0.35 s, 0.22 m)

VFX-10Lens drops are one tiled grey sticker sheet sliding over the whole screen medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
In rain the whole screen, including the sky, is covered with evenly spaced grey dots and teardrops that slide down together as one sheet and repeat across the frame.
Root cause

Real drops refract the scene behind them, showing a small flipped image, and each one moves on its own: it stops, merges and leaves a trail. A tiled grey sticker sheet that slides uniformly reads as a UI overlay. In GTA V's third-person camera there are no lens drops, and Cyberpunk uses only sparse refracting drops on specific cameras.

Fix

Turn lens drops off by default in the chase view, and fade them in only when the camera is still and nothing covers it (photo mode, bumper camera). If they are kept, use a URP Full Screen Pass Renderer Feature (Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_LensRain.shadergraph) that refracts the scene colour through a drop normal buffer. Simulate at most 60 drops on the CPU (2-6 spawned per second x rain, sliding under gravity with a speed-dependent wind push, merging, trails decaying) into a 320x180 RG16 render texture, so nothing tiles. Draw it before the UI so the HUD stays clean.

Cost
Estimate: 0.1-0.2 ms GPU for the fullscreen pass. CPU cost is negligible.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter36/it4_rain_night.png: about 200 grey discs and teardrops spread evenly, e.g. at (512,154), (1677,154), (832,832) and (2304,896)
Assets/_Veyra/UI/HudController.cs:378-411 - 140 drops baked into one 512² texture (384); 429 sets uvRect width 3.4, so the pattern repeats about 3.4 x 1.9 times; the whole sheet scrolls through uvRect.y; drop rims are drawn at brightness 200-255 with no refraction

VFX-11Crash debris is 8x8 point-filtered square billboards; no glass shards or impact sparks medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
Crash bursts are flat coloured squares (paint colour, black, pale blue) spinning like confetti. No glass shards glint, no sparks fly on hard hits, and the dust is a soft grey ball.
Root cause

Billboards always face the camera, so the 'shards' never tumble, catch the light or show any thickness, and a point-filtered square gives hard pixel edges. Real crash debris is mostly tumbling glass and plastic that flashes highlights. GTA V uses mesh debris, glass particles and spark showers sized to the energy of the impact.

Fix

Switch to ParticleSystemRenderMode.Mesh, or a VFX Graph mesh output, with 6-10 original shard meshes of 12-60 tris made in Blender. Use a lit URP material: paint fragments take the car's paint colour through the particle colour, and glass shards get smoothness 0.95 with strong Fresnel. Add VFX_ImpactSparks: 30-120 stretched HDR particles (emissive 10-20), gravity 1, bounce 0.4 off the depth buffer, 0.3-0.8 s life, fired when the impact speed change exceeds 6 m/s or on Metal surfaces. Dust uses the lit flipbook from VFX-06. Keep the existing scaling of count with impact speed, and pool and prewarm per car.

Cost
Estimate: under 0.1 ms. 36 mesh particles x 60 tris is trivial.
Evidence

CarDamage.cs:223-238 - 80 camera-facing billboards of 2.5-9 cm with low-quality world collision; 335 and 340 emit 6-36 per hit in paint, black or pale-blue colours
Sparks exist only for wall scrapes (VehicleFX.cs:168-196 and 61-75), not for impacts

VFX-M1Wet-night streets throw no spray: spray reads only rain wetness, not the wet-night preset medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 added by verifier
You see
On glossy, wet-looking Neon Mile and Downtown streets at night the cars kick up no spray or mist at all, so the wet road reads as a shiny floor rather than water.
Root cause

The wet-night preset is applied only to the shader globals. Gameplay VFX (spray) and grip read the raw Wetness value, so what the road looks like and what the effects do disagree. In GTA V a wet road always produces tyre spray and wake mist; their absence breaks the illusion.

Add a public EffectiveWetness property in WorldEnvironment that returns the same max() as line 281. Use it in CarSprayFX.cs:86, TrafficSpray and Pedestrians.cs:149. Decide deliberately whether grip (GameFlow.cs:1386) should follow it. Scale the spray rate by speed (up to about 200 particles/s per wheel at 100 km/h).

VFX-M2Lens drops are a full-screen tiled UI sticker scrolled as one sheet in the third-person view medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 added by verifier
You see
In rain a repeating polka-dot pattern of identical drops slides down the whole screen as one layer, even though the camera is a chase camera that no rain should reach.
Root cause

A single tiled texture scrolled in UV space gives every drop the same size, motion and spacing, which reads as a screen overlay. The drops also do not refract the scene. GTA V shows no drops in the chase camera, and Cyberpunk applies only short, sparse refraction drops in first person.

Fix

Disable lens drops in the chase camera by default (the HudController toggle). If they are kept for bonnet or cockpit cameras, replace them with a URP Renderer Feature (Render Graph full-screen pass) that renders 20-60 individual drop instances. Give each drop a normal-map sprite (256² atlas) that refracts the colour buffer, a random lifetime of 1-4 s and gravity sliding, spawned at a rate set by RainIntensity x (facing up).

Turning it off saves a full-screen UI blend; a refraction pass would cost an estimated 0.1 ms at 1440p.

VFX-12Binary weather: no drizzle-to-storm range, no gusts, no lightning low effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
Rain switches on at one fixed strength, with no build-up from drizzle to storm, no lightning flashes and no gust-driven slant. Every rainy night looks the same.
Root cause

Weather feels alive through variation: intensity changes, gusts swing the rain angle, and storms flash the whole city and outline the skyline. A binary emitter tied to the weather enum, not to RainIntensity, gives the same image every time.

Fix

Store weather presets as versioned ScriptableObjects: Drizzle (rain 0.3, fog 0.0015), Rain (0.7) and Storm (1.0, wind 0.8, gusts at 0.2 Hz). Drive VFX_Rain count, drop size and slant from RainIntensity x wind. Storm lightning: 2-4 flash pulses of 60-200 ms on a dedicated directional light without shadows, plus a _VC_Lightning term in VC_SkyDome.shader. Add distant bolt strips (HDR emissive, 0.2 s) on the horizon and thunder delayed by distance / 343 m/s, with one strike every 8-25 s.

Cost
Negligible. The flash light casts no shadows, so it adds no shadow-map spikes.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:17 defines only WeatherKind { Clear, Overcast, Rain }; 160-174 apply linear ramps and random auto-weather
WorldEnvironment.cs:601 - the rain emission is either 5500/s or 0; RainIntensity does not drive the particle count
A grep for 'Lightning' over Assets/_Veyra finds no lightning code
Tracked as
new

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • VehicleFX.cs:236 and 409: dim and tint the tyre smoke by NightFactor, as CarSprayFX.cs:99 already does, so it stops glowing white at night. Also raise peak alpha from 0.55 to 0.8, lifetime to 3-5 s and size-over-lifetime to 4x, and enable a Noise module (strength 0.5, frequency 0.4) plus inheritVelocity 0.3. About 2 h.
  • WorldEnvironment.cs:584-601: lower the rain fall speed from 24 to 10 m/s and take the drift from _VC_WindDirection. Scale the rate by RainIntensity, and multiply the colour by (0.15 + 0.85 x (1 - NightFactor)) so night rain is dark except where lit. Add one Physics.Raycast up from the camera every 0.25 s and stop emission under decks and in tunnels. Half a day.
  • VC_Common.hlsl:70 and VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:87: skip a ripple cell when h.y > 0.35 + 0.4 x rain, add a second layer rotated and scaled to 2.9, and drop the 0.25 base outside puddles. This breaks the visible ring lattice in about 2 h.
  • CarSprayFX.cs:86 and TrafficSpray.cs:35: use max(Wetness, PostVolumeDirector.WetNight x WetNightLevel) so wet-night D1/D6 streets throw spray, and raise the rate from 90 to 200 per second per wheel.
  • HudController.cs:429 and ChaseCamera.cs:287-321: turn off lens drops and SpeedStreaks by default in the chase view and lower ChromaticAberration from 0.4 to 0.1. This removes the sky scratch-lines, the polka-dot screen and the RGB fringing.
  • VeyraMaterialFactory.cs:154: reduce the soft-particle far fade from 1.2 to 0.4 m so ground-level smoke and spray stay dense near the road.
  • CarDamage.cs:204: replace the 8x8 point-filtered white square with a 32² bilinear shard texture that has an irregular alpha shape and mips.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Rain is lit by local light: in CAM_06 and CAM_13 22h_wet, the mean luminance of rain pixels within 10 m of a lamp or neon sign is at least 4x that in unlit areas. Measure with a script that diffs rain-on and rain-off captures and masks the regions.
  • Rain respects cover: zero rain streaks or splashes in a new review camera under the R1 viaduct deck and in the Kestrel Tunnel (rain-on vs rain-off diff under 1 % of pixels), and at least 200 splashes per second within 15 m of the camera (VFX Graph debug counter).
  • Ripples are not periodic: they appear only where the puddle mask is above 0.3, and a 2D FFT of a 512² road crop in CAM_13 22h_wet shows no spectral peak above 3x the median at the ripple cell frequency. Measure with a Python script under Tools.
  • Everything gets wet: every material family (road, pavement, facade, glass, car paint, props, terrain, foliage) changes smoothness by at least 0.25 and albedo by at least 15 % at wetness 1. MaterialAuditor, extended with a wet-response check, reports zero materials without one.
  • Smoke looks lit and has body: in it6_drift (3 s drift at 90-100 km/h), smoke covers 10-25 % of the frame with a top-to-bottom luminance gradient of at least 25 %. At 22 h, smoke luminance stays within 1.5x of the nearby lit road (no self-glow).
  • Performance: all VFX together (heavy rain, 8 spray emitters, player drift smoke, 16 steam vents) cost at most 1.5 ms GPU and 0.3 ms CPU main thread at 1440p on an RTX 4070 (Profiler GPU markers and FrameCost). Frame p99 stays at or below 16.6 ms, with no hitch over 50 ms on the first rain, drift or crash (stats.json).
  • Asset hygiene: a grep of runtime VFX code finds zero 'new Texture2D' or 'AddComponent<ParticleSystem>'. All VFX textures are power-of-two BC7 with mips, and flipbooks are 8x8 at 2048² or less, under Assets/_Veyra/Art/VFX.

Assets to make or source

  • Six-way smoke flipbook (8x8 frames, two 2048² BC7 maps holding right/top/back and left/bottom/front lighting plus alpha) for tyre smoke, engine smoke, dirt dust and steam. Bake in Blender Mantaflow with a Tools/Blender script and 6 lights; original content, so no licence file is needed.
  • Rain textures: a streak atlas at 256x1024 (8 variants), a tiling rain-sheet card at 512x1024, a 4x4 splash-crown flipbook at 512², and a 512² ripple texture (normal xy, time, mask) generated by an Editor tool.
  • Wet-layer textures: a tiling 512² vertical rain-streak flow map, a 1024² car droplet-bead normal map and a 512² puddle-edge noise mask.
  • Nitro: an 8x4 flame flipbook at 1024x512 in HDR (Blender fire simulation, original) and a 256² heat-distortion normal map.
  • Sparks: a 64x256 HDR streak texture, a 4-frame ember sprite and a 256² impact-flash sprite.
  • Debris meshes made in Blender: 6 paint and plastic shards, 4 glass shards and 2 trim pieces, 12-60 tris each, with a lit shard material.
  • Steam: an 8x8 wispy flipbook at 2048² (Blender, original).
  • Lightning: 4 bolt strip textures at 256x2048 plus thunder and rain-loop audio, either self-recorded or CC0 with a licence file in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • Unity's VFX Graph sample content is under the Unity Companion License: use it only as a learning reference. Any third-party flipbook needs a licence file in ArtSource/Downloads before import, and no game-ripped VFX may be used.

9. Lighting, sky, fog and post-processing

Score today: 2.5 / 10   By day the image is clean but flat and CG, with no GI, no sky occlusion, no distant shadows and no real sky. Nights are crushed to black with clipped emissives and no wet reflections. That is far from GTA V by day or Cyberpunk at night.

Lighting is the biggest reason Veyra looks like a "kids' game". The setup is one realtime sun plus a flat Trilight ambient that a script sets every frame. There is no GI: APV is off, nothing is baked, and the VC shaders only sample SH. Exposure is fixed per time of day under ACES. Fog is a single global exp² term keyed to Camera.main. The sky is the built-in procedural skybox with a 2D noise cloud deck on top. There is no SSR, sun shadows stop at 160 m, and night streets get 48 weak unshadowed spot lights plus additive decal "light pools". The result is a day that looks clean but flat and CG, and a night that is crushed to black, with windows clipped to white and no wet reflections. The review process hides part of this. Every day capture is taken at 12:00 (iterate.sh:43), so golden hour and dusk are never checked. Fixed review cameras render the fog and street lights for the chase camera, which sits about 400 m underground during those shots. That is why CAM_01 is a white-out and why the fixed night shots have no real street lights (inferred from the code).

Proposed plan for Unity 6.6 / URP 17.6, with GPU cost estimates for PC_High at 1440p on an RTX 4070: (1) Review fixes: a ViewCamera override, a sweep over several times of day, and histogram stats per shot. No GPU cost. (2) APV: one baking set over Boot and the 48 cell scenes, with sky occlusion and 4 lighting scenarios (08:00, 13:00, 18:30 with sun bounce, 22:00 with emissive GI), blended by time of day. VC shaders get the PROBE_VOLUMES variants. About 0.3 ms. (3) Histogram auto exposure, HDR grading mode and authored LUTs. About 0.15 ms. (4) Per-pixel height fog with aerial perspective (about 0.15 ms), plus froxel volumetric fog for D1, D6, D3 and rain (about 0.8 ms). (5) URP 17.6's built-in SSR renderer feature, plus baked per-block reflection probes. About 0.7 ms. (6) A physically based atmosphere from LUTs, plus 2.5D raymarched clouds. About 0.9 ms. (7) Sun shadows out to 350 m, screen-space contact shadows and a larger SSAO radius. About 1 ms. (8) 128 to 192 importance-sorted real lights with cookies, 4 to 8 of them shadowed, and real headlight shapes. About 1.3 ms. The total is about 5 to 6 ms GPU in the worst case (estimate). That only fits once the CPU-bound frame in BUG-120 is fixed. Avoid URP's Surface Cache GI: it is experimental and only compiles under the SURFACE_CACHE define (SurfaceCacheGIRendererFeature.cs:1).

Findings and fixes (12)

LGT-01No global illumination: flat script-set ambient, no bounce, no sky occlusion critical effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Shaded facades are one flat dark tone at noon (CAM_05: the left block is near-black beside a sunlit plaza). At night, lit shopfronts and windows light nothing around them and trees stay black silhouettes.
Root cause

Lighting is one directional light plus a uniform 3-colour ambient that ignores geometry. With no probes or lightmaps, a rooftop and the bottom of a 100 m street canyon get the same sky light. Nothing receives bounce from sunlit ground, and emissive windows and neon never light anything. This is the classic CG cue: no soft gradients, no occlusion falloff, no colour bleeding. GTA V bakes sky visibility and AO into its world and scales it with the time cycle; Cyberpunk uses probe-based GI that also picks up neon.

Fix

Adopt Adaptive Probe Volumes. On the High and Ultra URP assets: Light Probe System = Probe Volumes, SH L1 (L2 on Ultra), disk and GPU streaming on, Lighting Scenarios and Scenario Blending on. Use one Baking Set over VeyraCity_Boot plus the 48 Cell scenes. Probe spacing: 1 m minimum near geometry, 9 m maximum in the city, 27 m in D4/D8. Turn Sky Occlusion on so sky light follows the time of day; use Virtual Offset and Rendering Layers against light leaks. Bake 4 scenarios: 08:00, 13:00 and 18:30 (sun Mixed with Baked Indirect: bounce is baked, direct light stays realtime) and 22:00 (lamps, window and neon emissives contribute GI). WorldEnvironment blends the two nearest scenarios by TimeOfDay. VC shaders: add multi_compile _ PROBE_VOLUMES_L1 PROBE_VOLUMES_L2, replace SampleSH (VC_ForwardPass.hlsl:104) with URP's SAMPLE_GI path, and add the variants to VC_Variants.shadervariants. Replace the Trilight ambient with the sky ambient probe. Bake through an idempotent Editor tool, on a Git branch.

Cost
Estimate: APV L1 sampling 0.2-0.4 ms GPU at 1440p, L2 adds 0.1-0.2 ms. With 4 scenarios and streaming, about 100-250 MB of VRAM stays resident (estimate; measure with the APV debug view and the Memory Profiler). Each scenario takes hours to bake with the GPU baker. No CPU cost apart from setting the blend factor.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:36,39-42 - m_LightProbeSystem: 0 (APV off); probe streaming, scenarios and scenario blending all 0
Assets/_Veyra/Scenes/VeyraCity.unity:96-97 - m_LightingDataAsset is the built-in default and m_LightingSettings is 0: nothing baked, no lightmaps, no APV
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:241-250 - ambient = AmbientMode.Trilight with hand-keyed sky/equator/ground colours set every frame
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_ForwardPass.hlsl:104 - bakedGI = SampleSH(normal) only; Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Facade.shader:59-67 compiles no LIGHTMAP_ON or PROBE_VOLUMES_L1/L2 variants

LGT-02Fixed exposure and an ACES toe crush nights to black while emissives clip critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Night aerials and rural views are pure black (CAM_01/02/11 at 22h). In streets the road is black while windows and shopfronts clip to flat white. There are no mid-tones, the opposite of Cyberpunk's readable neon nights.
Root cause

Exposure is set per time of day instead of being metered. Night lighting is about 2.5 EV below day, but exposure rises only 0.35 EV, and ACES' toe crushes the low values while emissives clip. That explains street shots like CAM_07 and CAM_13. The fully black FIXED night aerials (CAM_01/02/11/17) have a different main cause. Fog keyed to the underground chase camera runs at full ground density (about 0.00165 when wet), and its colour is near-black (WorldEnvironment.cs:251-266), so the geometry is fogged out. The procedural sky exposure also drops to 0.06 at night (WorldEnvironment.cs:303).

Fix

Fix the fog first (LGT-03/LGT-08 ViewCamera), then re-capture before tuning exposure. Then add auto exposure, which URP lacks: an in-house ScriptableRendererFeature plus a 'VC Exposure' VolumeComponent. A compute shader builds a 64-bin log-luminance histogram from a 1/4-res copy, ignoring the top 2 % and bottom 40 %. Adaptation: 1.5 EV/s up, 3 EV/s down. Apply it as a multiplier before URP post, with no asset writes. Put per-time-of-day min/max EV and compensation in VP_Global_Day/Golden/Night. Rebalance emissives in VeyraMaterialFactory: windows 0.5-3 HDR with ±30 % variation per window, neon 4-12 HDR. Set Color Grading Mode to HDR (VC_URP_PC_*.asset m_ColorGradingMode). A/B the Neutral tonemapper plus LUT against ACES at night. Keep the night sky exposure from going below about 0.3 so it carries the city glow.

Cost
Estimate: histogram plus adaptation 0.05-0.1 ms GPU at 1440p. HDR grading mode adds about 0.05 ms. No CPU cost if the exposure value stays on the GPU; the volume-driven variant needs an async readback with 1-2 frames of latency.

Assets/_Veyra/Settings/Post/VP_Global_Night.asset:157-159 - tonemapper mode 2 (ACES)

LGT-03Fog is one global exp² term keyed to Camera.main: aerial white-out, no height falloff or aerial perspective high effort M (1-3 days) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Aerial views are a pale sheet (CAM_01: the city is barely visible) or heavy grey haze (CAM_02: hills gone at 2-3 km). At night the haze is near-black, so the distant city and skyline vanish instead of glowing.
Root cause

Unity's fog depends on camera distance only, with one colour for the whole screen. Real haze thins with altitude, and its colour changes per pixel with the angle between view and sun (aerial perspective): far hills turn blue while the near city keeps its contrast. GTA V and Cyberpunk both render per-pixel height fog with scattering. The script fakes height fog by scaling the global density from Camera.main's altitude. That breaks for every other camera and fogs high sight lines as if they ran through ground haze. At night the dark fog colour darkens the city instead of glowing.

Fix

Replace RenderSettings fog with analytic exponential height fog plus aerial perspective in VC_Common.hlsl. Density(h) = d0·exp(-(h-h0)/H), integrated in closed form along the view ray, with H ≈ 600 m by day and 250 m at night (estimates). Inscatter colour per pixel = sky ambient mixed with sun colour × Mie phase (g 0.7) by the view-sun angle, using the rendering camera's _WorldSpaceCameraPos. Apply it in VC_ForwardPass, replacing MixFog at line 109. For URP/Lit and kit materials, apply the same function in a FullScreenPass renderer feature after opaques that reads depth. Transparent VC shaders (water, rain, glow pools) call it directly. At night the inscatter colour = district light pollution (estimate 0.10-0.20 linear in D1/D6), so haze glows. Drive d0, H and colours from a VolumeComponent in the Day/Golden/Night profiles. Delete the Camera.main altitude hack (lines 259-261). Later, feed it the atmosphere LUT from LGT-06.

Cost
Estimate: the full-screen height-fog pass costs 0.1-0.2 ms at 1440p; the per-pixel term in VC shaders is close to free. It also removes a Camera.main lookup every frame.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:251-266 - RenderSettings exp² fog: density 0.00035 by day, 0.0007 at night, plus 0.0006 wet and 0.00035 cloud; night colour (0.035, 0.045, 0.075)
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:259-261 - 'height fog' just multiplies the global density by 0.12 when Camera.main is above 120-1020 m; there is no per-pixel height falloff
Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/VeyraReviewCapture.cs:152 + Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/ReviewCameraRig.cs:108-121 - for FIXED shots the car is parked at (0,-400,0) and the review camera is not Camera.main, so the underground chase camera keeps full ground density. For CAM_01 at about 7.3 km: exp(-(0.00035*7300)^2) ≈ 0.2 % of the scene remains (computed)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/AtmosphereScatter.cs:23-37 - one fog colour per frame, tinted by Camera.main's view direction, plus a constant sodium tint at night
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_01_World_Overview_12h.png - below y≈330 the frame is a uniform pale blue plane with faint city shapes; CAM_02_DT_Aerial_Day_12h.png - downtown at about 2.8 km is washed grey (about 60 % fog computed for full density) and the hills are lost

BUG-059
LGT-04Night light budget too small and faked: 48 weak unshadowed spots, additive decal pools, neon emits no light high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
At night the streets are black between lamps. Road 'light pools' are flat glowing ellipses (CAM_13: a pink oval floating on the road) that never light the car or kerbs. Neon signs glow but colour nothing around them.
Root cause

A city at night is lit by hundreds of overlapping sources. Here only the 48 nearest lamps are real lights, about 4 EV under the fixed exposure, and the rest is additive paint. Additive quads ignore albedo, normals, wetness and occluders, so they read as stickers: no specular streak on the wet road, no light on a passing car, visible edges at grazing angles. GTA V mixes many real lights with thousands of distant light sprites. Cyberpunk uses clustered lighting with hundreds of lights plus volumetrics.

Fix

In PoolSizeForQuality, raise the pool to 128 (High), 192 (Ultra) and 48 (Medium). Select lights by importance (intensity / distance², weighted by frustum) using the rendering camera instead of nearest-to-a-point. Tune intensity against the exposure from LGT-02; target: pool centre 1.5-2 EV below sunlit asphalt, measured. Give lamps an original 256² IES-like cookie and keep the district colours from LampColour. NeonSignBuilder adds 1-3 point or spot lights per sign (range 8-15 m, sign colour) to the same importance pool. Allow 4 (High) or 8 (Ultra) shadowed spots nearest the camera, at shadow tier Medium (1024). Replace VC_GlowPool quads within 150 m with real lights; keep them only as a distant LOD that fades out near the camera. Bake far lamps into the APV night scenario (LGT-01). Cookies need the _LIGHT_COOKIES variant in VC shaders (LGT-10).

Cost
Forward+ cost scales with lights per cluster. Estimate: 0.8-1.5 ms GPU for about 150 unshadowed lights at 1440p on a 4070, plus 0.1-0.2 ms per shadowed spot. CPU: an importance sort of 2-3k lamps every 0.25 s is under 0.1 ms with Burst. Measure with the Frame Debugger and the GPU profiler on R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:334-341 - real-light pool of 96 lights on Ultra, 48 on High, 24 on Medium
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:365-370,427 - spot lights at 110°, range 26 m, no shadows, intensity 4.5 × 1.5 = 6.75; about 0.1 at 8 m by inverse square vs the sun's 1.9, roughly 4 EV lower (computed, approximate)

BUG-073
LGT-05Wet streets reflect almost nothing: no SSR, coarse 128 px probes 400 m apart high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
In rain at night the road reflects only the tail lights. Bright shopfronts, windows and neon leave no streaks on the wet asphalt (CAM_07 and CAM_13 at 22h wet), so the 'wet neon' look never appears.
Root cause

Wet asphalt is a rough mirror, and its look comes from long streaks of nearby bright sources. The only reflection sources are 128 px realtime box probes on a 400 m grid at terrain +14 m and a 128 px sky-only city probe. Neither can contain the shopfront beside the car, and the 440 m box projection gives the wrong parallax, so the road mirrors the dark sky. URP 17.6's SSR is compiled out: it needs the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION define. Even with the define set, VC shaders would not sample it, and their depth-normals would not carry the wet smoothness.

Fix

Add URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION to the PC Standalone scripting defines on a Git branch (it is experimental; check that it compiles cleanly). Add the SSR feature to VC_Renderer_PC_High and PC_Ultra, and the 'Lighting/Screen Space Reflection' override to VP_Global_Day and VP_Global_Night: half resolution (full on Ultra), Hierarchical marching, minimum smoothness about 0.55. In the VC shaders, add multi_compile_fragment _ _SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION. Let VC_ForwardPass go through URP's GlobalIllumination path, which samples SSR. Give VC_Road a custom DepthNormals pass that writes its wet and puddle smoothness. Add the variants to VC_Variants.shadervariants. Wet road smoothness: 0.85-0.95 in puddles, 0.6-0.7 elsewhere. In D1/D6, use per-block baked box probes at 60-100 m spacing, 3-6 m above the road profile, 256 px HDR, one set per day and night scenario. Keep the car's probe realtime. Keep VeyraReflectionProbes idempotent.

Cost
Estimate: half-res hierarchical SSR costs 0.5-0.9 ms at 1440p on a 4070. Baked probes cost only memory, about 1.5 MB per 256 px HDR cube with mips; stream them per cell. Removing realtime probe re-renders also frees time-sliced cube renders.
Editor note
Unity 6.6 URP has no supported screen-space reflections: the SSR renderer feature is a preview that compiles only behind the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION scripting define and is aimed at Unity 6.7. Try it on a Git branch only; the production path in this report is box-projected probes per block plus a low-resolution planar reflection for water, or the 6.7 LTS upgrade at a milestone boundary (CLAUDE.md).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_High.asset:27-28,68 - SSAO is the only renderer feature
Library/PackageCache/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal@66a8e36658da/Runtime/RendererFeatures/ScreenSpaceReflectionRendererFeature/ScreenSpaceReflectionRendererFeature.cs:1 - '#if URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION'; ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:800 scriptingDefineSymbols: {}

BUG-068
LGT-06Sky is a built-in procedural skybox plus a flat 2D noise deck: streaky clouds and a dark band below the horizon high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Clouds are smeared brush strokes that stretch toward the horizon (CAM_05: a diagonal streak next to flat, saturated blue). From height the world ends in a dark navy band (CAM_01, top quarter). Sky and light colours don't match.
Root cause

A believable sky needs depth: clouds with volume, lit and shadowed sides, perspective that shrinks toward the horizon, and a sky colour that physically agrees with the sun and ambient light. 2D noise on a plane divided by view height turns into radial streaks. Hand-picked colours for sky, sun, fog and ambient drift apart (a blue sky over grey ambient). Cyberpunk renders volumetric clouds; GTA V layers authored HDR clouds over a scattering-model sky driven by its time cycle.

Fix
  1. Physically based sky as an in-house URP renderer feature (Hillaire 2020 LUT method). Transmittance LUT 256x64 and multi-scattering LUT 32x32, rebuilt only when the atmosphere changes; sky-view LUT 192x108 every frame; aerial-perspective froxels 32x32x32 feeding the LGT-03 fog. The CPU evaluates the same transmittance analytically for Sun.color and Sun.intensity. The ambient SH is projected from the sky-view LUT (async readback) and replaces Trilight. Materials come through VeyraMaterialLibrary. 2) Clouds: a 2.5D raymarched layer at 1.5-4 km altitude, 1/4 res with temporal reprojection. Use 128³ Perlin-Worley plus 32³ detail noise, a 512² weather map for coverage per WeatherKind, Beer-Powder lighting, and cloud shadows projected onto the ground. Cheaper fallback: original HDR cloud panoramas rendered in Blender, lit using stored normals. 3) Below the horizon, output the fog/ocean colour (fix at line 105) and extend the sea to the horizon.
Cost
Estimate: atmosphere LUTs 0.05-0.1 ms per frame. Volumetric clouds 0.6-1.2 ms at 1/4 res on a 4070 (temporal: 1/16 of the pixels traced each frame). The panorama fallback costs about 0.05 ms.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Materials/Sky.mat:11 - the base sky is the built-in Skybox/Procedural shader (fileID 106), created in Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:401
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_SkyDome.shader:133-135 - clouds are 5-octave value-noise fBm on a plane projected as v.xz/max(up, 0.04): they stretch at the horizon and have no volume or self-shadowing
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_SkyDome.shader:80,105 - the haze is multiplied by 'above', so below the horizon the dome is transparent and the procedural skybox's dark ground colour shows through
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:226-228,241-244 - sun colour, sun intensity and ambient colours are hand-keyed, not derived from the sky
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png - smeared diagonal cloud streak at x≈1340-1660, y≈130-540; CAM_01_World_Overview_12h.png - dark navy band at y 0-330 above the map edge

LGT-07Shadows end at 160 m and AO reaches only 0.8 m: no cast shadows from height or distance, no contact grounding high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
From height or distance, buildings cast no shadows (CAM_02: towers lit only by face shading, streets shadowless). Up close, cars, benches and planters sit on the ground without dark contact, so objects look pasted on.
Root cause

Cast shadows are the strongest cue for scale and for how objects sit in the world. Beyond 160 m everything is shadowless, so distant blocks become flat-shaded boxes. PCF soft shadows blur by a constant amount, so there is no contact hardening. A 0.8 m SSAO radius cannot darken under cars or in the corner where a wall meets the street. GTA V uses cascades plus a distant shadow layer and baked AO; Cyberpunk adds contact shadows and large-radius AO.

Fix

Shadows (estimates; verify the shadow-pass cost): PC_High 160 → 350 m and Ultra 250 → 600 m, 4 cascades split at about 0.03/0.10/0.30, 4096 maps. Cull casters by LOD: shadows-only LOD2 proxies for buildings beyond cascade 2, and no shadows from props under 2 m or from grass. Beyond the last cascade, APV sky occlusion (LGT-01) supplies the darkening. Contact shadows: an in-house screen-space pass for the main light (16-step depth ray march over 0.5-1.0 m, half res, temporal), composited into the shadow mask. AO: SSAO radius 1.6-2.0 m, falloff 250, Blue Noise, half res on High. Bake per-vertex AO into building and kit meshes with Tools/Blender/vc_tools.py, and add an AO blob decal under cars (needs the URP Decal feature, BUG-118). Put all values in VeyraQualityTiers so the tool keeps writing them.

Cost
Estimate: 350 m cascades add 0.4-0.8 ms to the GPU shadow pass (more draw calls; LOD proxies keep this bounded). Contact shadows about 0.2 ms; the larger SSAO radius about +0.1 ms. Measure with GPU profiler markers on CAM_02 and CAM_06.
Editor note
GameFlow.ApplySettings forces shadowDistance 160/240 m at runtime, so the URP-asset change only works after the Phase 0 settings-ownership fix (IQ-01).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:57-61 - shadow distance 160 m, 4 cascades split at 0.06/0.18/0.45 (≈10/29/72/160 m); VC_URP_PC_Ultra.asset:57 is 250 m. The default Standalone tier is PC_High (ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:308)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraQualityTiers.cs:49-51 - the tier table that writes these values

LGT-08Reviews never see golden hour or dusk, and fixed-camera captures use the wrong camera's fog and lights high effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Golden hour, dusk and morning ship unreviewed. Reviewers judge a noon-only world, a white-out CAM_01 and fixed night shots without real street lights, none of which matches what the player sees.
Root cause

The capture tool renders a separate, untagged camera, while the world systems follow Camera.main, which sits 400 m underground during fixed poses. Fixed shots therefore show fog, lights and haze computed for a different viewpoint. Day shots are always at noon, the flattest light of the day. Lighting quality is mostly decided at low sun angles and at dusk, so noon-only review never tests the most cinematic hours, the ones GTA-style trailers use most.

Fix
  1. Add WorldEnvironment.ViewCamera, falling back to Camera.main. VeyraReviewCapture sets it to the review camera; fog, AtmosphereScatter, the light pool, lamp sprites, district haze and SkyDome all read it. For fixed poses, park the car out of view near the pose instead of underground. 2) Change iterate.sh:43 to each camera's own hour plus a time-of-day sweep: 07:30, 12:00, 18:45 (golden), 19:45 (dusk), 22:00 dry and 22:00 wet. 3) Write per-shot luminance stats to review.txt: mean log-luminance in EV, % of pixels ≥ 0.98, % ≤ 0.02, and the sky/ground ratio. Flag a shot when more than 3 % clips or more than 25 % is crushed (proposed thresholds). 4) Add a golden-hour contact sheet to every lighting card.
Cost
No runtime cost. Capture time grows about 3x (6 times of day instead of 2). The stats are computed on the CPU from the existing PNG readback.

LGT-09No volumetric light: no neon haze, light shafts or lit rain medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Neon signs, lamps and headlights have no glow in the air. The DUMPLINGS sign (CAM_13) is a sharp sticker with faint bloom, rain streaks are lit the same everywhere, and there are no sun shafts between towers by day.
Root cause

In humid or rainy air, light scatters into visible cones and halos. That in-scattered glow is what makes Cyberpunk's neon streets feel atmospheric and gives the image depth layers. Bloom is a screen-space effect centred on the source, so it cannot create shafts or haze between the viewer and the light. Additive beam meshes have hard edges and ignore occlusion.

Fix

Build a froxel volumetric fog renderer feature, in-house or from an MIT-licensed community URP package with its licence file in ArtSource/Downloads. 160x90x64 froxels (1440p/16), exponential depth slices out to 150 m. Lighting: the main light with cascade-shadow lookup, plus up to 32 of the nearest local lights from the importance pool, unshadowed. Density: LGT-03's height fog plus local volumes (D6 alleys, D3 steam, tunnels, rain). Use temporal reprojection with blue-noise jitter. Integrate once; opaques sample it through a full-screen pass, transparents (rain, neon glass) sample it directly. Turn it on at night in D1/D6/D3 and in rain or overcast weather, with low density by day for sun shafts. Remove the CarLampFX beam meshes on PC_High and above. Tiers: Ultra 160x90x96, High 160x90x64, Medium off.

Cost
Estimate: 0.6-1.0 ms GPU on High on a 4070 (inject, scatter and integrate compute passes over about 0.9 M froxels). Cost scales with froxel count and local light count; it is only active at night, in rain or in district volumes.
Evidence
Library/PackageCache/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal@66a8e36658da/Runtime/Overrides - URP 17.6 has no volumetric fog component
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_High.asset:27-28 - no volumetric renderer feature in the project
Assets/_Veyra/AI/CarLampFX.cs:262-268 - headlight 'beams' are additive meshes (0.09 × night × (1 + 2.2 × rain))
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png - the sign at x≈690-1040, y≈380-445 has no halo or haze; rain streaks are equally bright near and away from lights

LGT-10Headlights are one unshaped spot with no cookie or shadow, and VC shaders cannot show cookies medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
At night the car throws a single round blob of light ahead (CAM_08 and CAM_10 at 22h), with no low-beam cut-off, no two-lamp pattern, and no shadows of traffic or poles in the beam.
Root cause

Real low beams come from two lamps with a sharp horizontal cut-off and a brighter hot spot toward the kerb. That shape, plus the shadows that cars and poles cast inside the beam, is how the eye recognises headlights; a single circular spot reads as a torch. GTA V and Cyberpunk both use shaped, projected headlights with shadows on the player's car.

Fix

Use two spots per car at the vehicle's lamp sockets. Each gets an original 512² low-beam cookie (cut-off line, kerb-side kick, soft edges), and a second cookie serves high beam. Tune intensity against the LGT-02 exposure. Player car: one shared shadowed spot covering both lamps (Additional Light Shadows, Medium tier 1024, soft). AI racers stay unshadowed. Traffic keeps emissive lamps plus a projected URP decal light patch (needs the decal feature from BUG-118). Add #pragma multi_compile_fragment _ _LIGHT_COOKIES to VC_Facade, VC_Road, VC_Neon and VC_Water, register the new variants in VC_Variants.shadervariants, and recheck the variant count (BUG-106). Cookie textures go in Art/Textures, referenced from a ScriptableObject.

Cost
Estimate: the extra cookie samples per lit pixel are negligible, and one 1024 shadowed spot costs 0.1-0.2 ms. _LIGHT_COOKIES doubles the affected fragment variants, which raises build time; check against the BUG-106 limits.

LGT-11Grading is generic numeric tweaks under ACES in LDR mode; no authored LUTs per time of day or district medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Day frames look clean but generic: a saturated flat blue sky over a grey city, with no warm/cool split. At night, magenta neon pushes toward white. Nothing gives Veyra a recognisable photographic look.
Root cause

AAA looks are authored. A colourist grades reference frames into LUTs per time of day and per district, with controlled hue shifts (warm highlights, cool shadows), and picks a tonemapper for how it rolls off saturated lights. ACES shifts saturated hues (magenta neon toward pink-white, reds toward orange) and has fixed contrast. LDR grading mode works at 8-bit precision, which can band in dark night gradients.

Fix

Switch Color Grading Mode to HDR on the PC tiers (VeyraQualityTiers writes VC_URP_PC_*.asset:79), and raise the LUT size from 32 to 64 on Ultra. Tonemapper: A/B Neutral plus a LUT against ACES on the night set, and keep whichever clips fewer neon pixels (stats from LGT-08). Author original LUTs: export URP's neutral LUT strip, then grade it in DaVinci Resolve or Photoshop against the review captures. Make LUT_Day, LUT_Golden, LUT_Night, LUT_NeonMile and LUT_Ironport, applied through ColorLookup in the existing VP_* volumes by the idempotent VeyraPostVolumes builder. Keep numeric overrides small (contrast ≤ 5). On Ultra, use a 64-bit HDR buffer if night gradients band (check the CAM_13 sky).

Cost
Estimate: HDR grading with a 64³ LUT costs about 0.05 ms; a 64-bit colour buffer adds 0.1-0.2 ms of bandwidth at 1440p.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraPostVolumes.cs:81,119-141 - every profile is ACES plus contrast 8-12, saturation 4-10, temperature and vignette; no ColorLookup (LUT) anywhere
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:79-80 - Color Grading Mode LDR, LUT size 32
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:27 - HDR colour buffer is 32-bit R11G11B10, on every tier
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png - saturated blue sky band (x≈1330-1720, y≈260-770) against neutral grey facades
Tracked as
new
LGT-M1Runtime writes into the shared Sky.mat asset; the night sky drops to exposure 0.06 medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
At night the base sky is near-black with no city glow from below the horizon. In the editor, after Play mode the Sky material keeps its night values (exposure 0.06), so day previews look wrong.
Root cause

WorldEnvironment animates the shared skybox material asset itself. In the editor, every Play session dirties Sky.mat, so the git diff fills with noise and the editor sky ends up at night values. At runtime, an exposure of 0.06 leaves the night sky about 4 EV darker than day. Real urban night skies carry orange or violet light pollution near the horizon, and Cyberpunk's skyline glows against it. Here only the dome's _CityGlow haze adds any glow, and only above the horizon.

Fix

Instantiate the sky material once at runtime (new Material(SkyboxMaterial) from the serialized asset, not Shader.Find), assign it to RenderSettings.skybox, and animate only that copy. Restore Sky.mat to its day values with an idempotent CityBuilder step. Set the night _Exposure floor to about 0.3-0.4 and the night tint to the district light-pollution colour. Make VC_SkyDome output the city-glow colour below the horizon at night (see LGT-06). Verify on CAM_03/04 at 22h that the skyline's silhouette reads against the sky.

Cost
No measurable cost: one material copy, with the same per-frame SetFloat calls as now.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:301-305 - ApplyMaterials calls SkyboxMaterial.SetFloat(_Exposure, Lerp(1.15, 0.06, night)), plus _AtmosphereThickness and _SkyTint, directly on the asset
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Materials/Sky.mat:29-36 - the serialized _Exposure is 0.06 and _SkyTint is the night tint (0.25, 0.28, 0.45): the asset was saved with runtime night values
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/contact_sheet.png - frames 19, 20 and 29: the sky area is as black as the ground

Wet streets start reflecting storefronts and neon (about 4 h including the depth-normals check).
  • VC_SkyDome.shader:105: stop multiplying the horizon haze by 'above', so below the horizon the dome outputs the fog colour. This removes the dark navy band in CAM_01/02 (30 min).

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • GI: APV baked for VeyraCity_Boot and all 48 cells, with sky occlusion and 4 lighting scenarios. Zero opaque renderers whose shader lacks probe-volume sampling (keyword validator). At noon, the shaded-to-sunlit facade luminance ratio is between 1:3 and 1:6 in fixed ROIs of CAM_05 and CAM_06, measured from the captures.
  • Exposure: for each review shot, mean log-luminance within ±0.7 EV of the time-of-day target. At night in CAM_07 and CAM_13, clipped pixels (≥ 0.98) ≤ 3 % and crushed pixels (≤ 0.02) ≤ 20 %. Stats come from the luminance histogram written to review.txt.
  • Fog and atmosphere: rendering a review camera at the chase camera's pose gives the same image as the chase camera (pixel difference < 2 %). On a clear day the 50 %-fog distance at ground level is 6-10 km; on a clear night it is 2-4 km, and the haze over D1/D6 is brighter than the unlit ground. Measure from CAM_01, CAM_02 and CAM_03 at 12h and 22h.
  • Reflections: SSR on for PC_High and Ultra. In CAM_07 and CAM_13 at 22h wet, every storefront and neon sign within 60 m leaves a visible streak on the wet road (reviewer checklist). Reflection probe spacing ≤ 100 m in D1/D6 (probe validator report).
  • Night lights: at least 120 real lights resolved per frame in Neon Mile on PC_High (Frame Debugger, Forward+ light count). No VC_GlowPool quad renders within 150 m of the camera.
  • Shadows and AO: sun cast shadows reach at least 350 m on PC_High (CAM_02 and CAM_03 show building shadows). Visible contact shadow under every wheel in a CAM_06 close-up. Shadow pass ≤ 2.0 ms GPU (profiler marker).
  • Sky and time-of-day review: no dark band below the horizon (sky pixels below the horizon within 10 % of the fog colour). Each lighting card is signed off on 6 captures per camera: 07:30, 12:00, 18:45, 19:45, 22:00 dry and 22:00 wet.
  • Performance: lighting plus post stays within 6 ms GPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070 on PC_High (sum of GPU profiler markers for shadows, SSAO, SSR, volumetrics, clouds, fog and exposure). Frame time p99 ≤ 16.6 ms and no hitch over 50 ms on the R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT autotest.

Assets to make or source

  • APV baking set and 4 lighting scenarios (08:00, 13:00, 18:30, 22:00) over VeyraCity_Boot and the 48 Cell scenes. This is generated project data, so no licence is needed; budget disk space for streaming.
  • Light cookies, all original and authored in-house (Photoshop/Substance or rendered in Blender), stored in Assets/_Veyra/Art/Textures: street-lamp IES-like cookie 256², car low-beam and high-beam cookies 512², neon spill cookie 256².
  • Colour LUTs (LUT_Day, LUT_Golden, LUT_Night, LUT_NeonMile, LUT_Ironport), 32³ or 64³, graded in-house from URP's neutral LUT strip. Original, no third-party LUT packs.
  • Cloud data: Perlin-Worley 128³ base noise, 32³ detail noise, curl noise 128², and a 512² weather map per WeatherKind, all generated by script. Alternative: HDR cloud panoramas rendered in Blender Cycles (original, sources in ArtSource/Blender).
  • Blue-noise textures (128² tiles) for SSAO, volumetric and contact-shadow jitter. Generate them in-house, or use a CC0 set with its licence file in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • Optional third-party code (a URP volumetric fog or atmosphere package) only if MIT/Apache-licensed and verified for Unity 6.6 / URP 17.6. The licence file goes in ArtSource/Downloads before import.
  • Reference set for grading and exposure targets: the project's own photographs or captures of real streets at noon, golden hour, dusk and wet night. No game screenshots used as assets.

10. Image quality: the pixelated look

Score today: 3.5 / 10   The pipeline settings are close to AAA, but gameplay runs with no temporal AA, a 0.4 LOD bias, aliased procedural facades, a debug pixel font on signs and low-resolution terrain, so in motion the image reads as shimmering, blocky and soft, far below GTA V or Cyberpunk 2077.

The URP assets are set up the right way (STP, MSAA off, blue-noise LOD crossfade, 4096 soft shadows), but in gameplay that stack never runs. GameFlow.ApplySettings writes MSAA 4x into the URP asset, and URP then turns off TAA and STP. The player logs show 60 and 13 'Disabling TAA and STP' warnings in iter21/22. A debug LOD bias of 0.4 has also leaked into PC_High. The review PNGs come from a different path: an off-screen camera with no MSAA and only 3 frames of STP history. So they look cleaner than the game, and they cannot show crawl or LOD pops. Other causes of the look are found in the content: window edges drawn with hard step() and the same room wedge repeated in every window, terrain blended from 3.9 m splat texels with noise-based textures, signs built from the 5x7 debug pixel font, trees that switch LOD and vanish per cell with no crossfade, bilinear filtering with no coverage-preserving mips on grass and leaves, and sun shadows that stop at 160 m.

Findings and fixes (12)

IQ-01MSAA set at runtime disables STP/TAA: gameplay has no temporal anti-aliasing critical effort S (< 1 day) impact 5/5 verified
You see
While driving, window mullions, road lines, wires, rain streaks and foliage edges crawl and sparkle, and LOD dither and SSAO show as raw noise. Review PNGs look cleaner than the game because the captures avoid this path.
Root cause

This is a regression of BUG-074. VeyraQualityTiers sets MSAA off with STP, but GameFlow.ApplySettings writes msaaSampleCount 2 or 4 into the shared URP asset at startup. URP turns off TAA, and with it STP, whenever MSAA > 1. WorldEnvironment then strips SMAA because the asset still says STP. MSAA resolves only triangle edges. Shader-drawn edges (procedural facades), specular highlights, alpha cutouts and dithered LOD fades stay aliased. GTA V Enhanced and Cyberpunk 2077 rely on temporal AA (TAA, DLSS) to resolve all of these over several frames.

Fix

GameFlow.cs:1063-1071: when urp.isStpUsed, write msaaSampleCount = 1 on every tier and change only renderScale (Low 0.67, Medium 0.77, High 0.87, Ultra 1.0; STP at 1.0 works as native TAA). Move these tier values into a versioned ScriptableObject. Keep camera AA at None (URP forces TAA under STP) and remove the serialized SMAA in CityBuilder.cs:456 and TestTrackBuilder.cs:92. Log the AA state at startup and fail automated runs on any 'Disabling TAA' line. Do not offer MSAA on PC: it fixes only triangle edges and blocks STP. A 'no temporal AA' option should be SMAA High with MSAA off. DLSS/FSR2: URP 17.6 contains an IUpscaler framework (core Runtime/Upscaling/DLSSIUpscaler.cs:5) that compiles only with ENABLE_UPSCALER_FRAMEWORK and com.unity.modules.nvidia. It is inactive here (the assets have no m_SelectedUpscalerName), and its production status in 6000.6 is uncertain, so test it on a branch. VeyraQualityTiers.cs:136 would then have to write upscalerName.

Cost
Dropping MSAA 4x saves an estimated 1-2 ms GPU at 1440p (colour and depth resolves in Forward+). STP at native resolution costs an estimated ~1 ms on a 4070; at 0.87 scale the frame is cheaper overall. Measure with Profiler GPU markers.
Editor note
Settings ownership: GameFlow.ApplySettings overwrites renderScale, shadowDistance and MSAA on the shared URP asset at runtime, so edits to the URP assets alone never reach gameplay. Fix this first (Phase 0).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GameFlow.cs:1061-1071 (msaa = {1,2,4,4}; urp.msaaSampleCount = msaa[q]; renderScale 1.0 at q 2-3)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GameData.cs:53 (default Quality = 2, so MSAA 4x)
Library/PackageCache/com.unity.render-pipelines.universal@66a8e36658da/Runtime/FrameData/UniversalCameraData.cs:459-471 (TAA, and therefore STP, needs msaaSamples == 1); Runtime/Passes/PostProcess/StpPostProcessPass.cs:50-59; Runtime/TemporalAA.cs:381
Logs/ITER/21/review_player.log: 60x and Logs/ITER/22/review_player.log: 13x 'Disabling TAA and STP because MSAA is on. Turn MSAA off on the camera or current URP Asset.'; Logs/B1/Dev_R_BRIDGES/player.log: 1004x
Assets/_Veyra/World/WorldEnvironment.cs:462-470 forces camera SMAA off because the asset says STP, so the net result is MSAA only
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraQualityTiers.cs:134-136 authors MSAA 1 + STP correctly (VC_URP_PC_High.asset:28-30), but GameFlow overrides it
Tracked as
BUG-074
IQ-02PC_High LOD bias 0.4 leaked from a debug render: low LODs appear 5x closer high effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Buildings, kit chunks and HLOD meshes switch to coarse LOD1/LOD2 a short distance away, so the mid-ground looks blocky and soft and LOD changes happen just ahead of the car.
Root cause

A batch camshot for BUG-123 wrote lodBias 0.4 into the active quality level, and Unity saved ProjectSettings. Judging by the file times, the iter21/22 players were built with it (an inference, not a log check). At bias 0.4 every LODGroup switches at 2.5x the screen size of bias 1, which is 5x closer than the committed value of 2. Coarse LOD steps near the camera read as 'low-poly game'. AAA open worlds keep near-field geometry at full detail and hide their transitions.

Fix

Set PC_High lodBias back to 2 (QualitySettings.asset:203, matching PC_Medium/Ultra) and keep maximumLODLevel 0. In WorldBuildBatch camshot, save QualitySettings.lodBias before the override and restore it in a finally block, so a debug render can never persist. Add an EditMode test that asserts lodBias >= 1.5 on PC tiers and checks MSAA/STP consistency against VeyraQualityTiers. Rebuild, rerun the 18-camera review and compare CAM_02/05/06 tris in stats.json.

Cost
Bias 2 renders more LOD0 triangles. Check that tris per frame in stats.json for CAM_02/06 stays within Appendix C; GPU Resident Drawer can offset the CPU cost.
Evidence
ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:203 lodBias 0.4 for PC_High (uncommitted; git diff shows 2 -> 0.4); :7 m_CurrentQuality 3 = PC_High; PC_Medium/Ultra keep 2 (:144, :262)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/WorldBuildBatch.cs:206-207 camshot sets QualitySettings.lodBias from -veyraLodBias and never restores it
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:87 (BUG-123 edit-mode tests at 'LOD bias 1 and 0.4'); QualitySettings.asset was modified 2026-11-11 11:52, before the iter21 build log (Logs/ITER/21/build_unity.log, 17:12)
Tracked as
new
IQ-03Procedural facade shader: hard step() edges and distance-only room LOD alias and form a sawtooth lattice high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
On the right-hand glass tower in CAM_05, every window shows the same light/dark diagonal wedge, forming a sawtooth moiré over the facade. Frames and mullions stair-step and crawl in motion, and distant towers flatten to grey boxes.
Root cause

Windows are drawn in the pixel shader with binary step() thresholds, so each edge is a single in/out decision per pixel that MSAA cannot smooth. Without temporal AA (IQ-01) these edges crawl. The fake-room ray cast picks its mip from distance only, so at steep upward angles the ceiling and side-wall wedges keep full contrast. Every window uses the same room geometry, so the wedges repeat in a perfect lattice, and the eye reads a periodic pattern as CG. Real towers, like GTA V's, vary blinds, lights and reflections per window.

Fix

In VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl, replace step(a, x) with an analytic aastep: compute w = fwidth(x) in uniform control flow before UNITY_BRANCH, then use saturate((x - a) / w + 0.5). Widen the existing aa fade so it starts at 3-4 px cells. Compute the room mip from ddx/ddy of the room UV outside the branch, pass it in, and add _GlobalMipBias.x. Fade rooms out sooner at grazing angles (nearK *= saturate(NdotV * 3)). Vary each window through its hash: room depth 0.6-1.4x, ceiling tone, blind height, and 16 interior variants in a 4096 atlas. At grazing angles, blend toward the reflection probe. Verify with a 1:1 crop of CAM_05 and a 3 s pan recorded with Recorder.

Cost
aastep adds a few ALU per pixel (negligible). Per-window variation reuses the existing hash. A 4096 BC7 interior atlas is about 21 MB with mips.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png: right tower, x 1700-2560, y 0-1060 (2560x1440 px): the same diagonal wedge repeats in every window cell
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:97-133 window, mullion, frame, fascia and sill masks built from binary step() (43 step() calls, 4 smoothstep, 1 fwidth in the file)
VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl:140-142 the fwidth fade starts only when a cell is under about 2.5 px; :199-210 the interior room is sampled with SAMPLE_TEXTURE2D_LOD at lod = log2(dist/12), which ignores grazing angle and _GlobalMipBias (see the comment at :29-30)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_03_DT_Skyline_Golden_12h.png x 1100-1500, y 480-800: the windows of distant towers average out to flat grey
Tracked as
new
IQ-04Low-resolution terrain: 3.9 m splat texels, 2 m basemap from 450 m, noise-based albedo high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Ground beside roads and on hills is smeared brown noise with no pebbles, cracks or crisp grass/dirt borders. Distant slopes are flat colour washes, and grass clumps sit on top like stickers (CAM_10, CAM_18).
Root cause

Ground looks sharp when it has micro-detail at 0-30 m (stones, cracks, height-blended layer borders) and macro variation further out. Here the layer textures are smooth procedural noise at about 128 px/m with no height channel. Layer borders blend bilinearly across 4 m splat texels, which gives soft blotches, and beyond 450 m the terrain switches to a 2 m basemap. GTA V uses dense ground texture sets, height-blended transitions, macro colour maps and scattered debris.

Fix

In CityBuilder.cs:496-497 and 559-560 (idempotent rebuild), set alphamapResolution 512 (about 2 m, the native source resolution), baseMapResolution 1024, basemapDistance 1000, and heightmapPixelError 2 (High) or 1 (Ultra). Replace the TerrainLayerMaps noise albedo with authored 2048 px sets per layer at a 4 m tile, which gives 512 px/m: albedo, BC5 normal, and a mask with height in B so URP Terrain Lit can height-blend. Add a 4096x3072 world macro variation map built from the Veyra landcover data, and a 0.5 m detail normal, in a terrain material variant. Keep each terrain at 4 layers or fewer where possible, because every further 4 layers add a pass.

Cost
A 512 alphamap is 1 MB per control texture, so about 96 MB for 48 tiles with 2 controls each, streamed with cells. Eight 2048 layer sets are about 130 MB of BC7/BC5 (estimate). Height blending adds minor ALU.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:488,496-497 alphamapResolution 256 and baseMapResolution 512 for 1000 m tiles (terrain_meta.json tileSize 1000): 3.9 m per splat texel, 1.95 m per basemap texel
CityBuilder.cs:559-560 heightmapPixelError 4, basemapDistance 450
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/TerrainLayerMaps.cs:37-47 1024 px albedo built from fBm noise; L_*.terrainlayer tile sizes 6-14 m (about 73-170 px/m)
ArtSource/VeyraData/terrain/splatmap_0_4096x3072.png (about 2 m/px source) is downsampled to 3.9 m
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_10_BR_Halcyon_Drive_12h.png x 1500-1900, y 800-1050: blurry brown ground; Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_18_RV_MirrorLake_Morning_12h.png lower half
Tracked as
new
IQ-05Instanced trees and props: hard per-cell LOD switch and cull, no crossfade, no impostors high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Whole patches of pines and broadleaf trees snap from detailed to coarse at about 90 m and blink out at the draw distance. Their shadows vanish at 140-160 m, all at once for each cell.
Root cause

The renderer picks one LOD for an entire cell of instances from the cell's nearest bound and swaps meshes in a single frame, and the shadow cut-offs work the same way. A sudden change of silhouette is one of the strongest 'game' tells, because vision is tuned to notice onsets. AAA games crossfade per instance, keep 3-4 LODs, and end the chain with octahedral impostors that keep the silhouette and lighting at range.

Fix

Option A (preferred in Unity 6): enable GPU Resident Drawer (URP asset m_GPUResidentDrawerMode 1; Forward+ is already on; BatchRendererGroup variants = Keep All). Emit trees as MeshRenderers with LODGroups: LOD0 8-25k tris, LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %, LOD3 impostor, with blue-noise crossfade. GRD then handles instancing and crossfade. Option B: keep InstancedPropRenderer, but choose the LOD per instance in a Burst job, pass a per-instance fade value through a StructuredBuffer, and dither-clip in the foliage shader (STP resolves the dither). Fade instances out over the last 10 % of DrawDistance, and draw shadows from LowMesh. Bake octahedral impostors (8x8 frames, 2048 albedo+normal atlas) per species for >300 m with Tools/Blender.

Cost
GRD cuts CPU submission cost. Dither fades add overdraw only inside the fade band. Impostors cut far-field tris by a large factor (estimate >90 %).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/InstancedPropRenderer.cs:95-114: distance measured to the cell bounds, binary low = dist > LodDistance, Graphics.RenderMeshInstanced with no LOD fade or dither
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:939-942 pine LOD 90 m / draw 1400 m / shadow 160 m; deciduous 90/1100/140; only Mesh + LowMesh (no LOD2, no impostor)
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:33-34 crossfade is enabled (blue noise) but only LODGroup renderers use it
Tracked as
new
IQ-06Neon and backlit signs use the 5x7 debug bitmap font high effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 verified, corrected
You see
Night signs such as 'DUMPLINGS' in Neon Mile are built from square blocks, like an 8-bit LED toy. Strokes stair-step and the glow halo is clipped, so signs read as retro pixels instead of real neon tube.
Root cause

Text is the most legible scale cue in a city. Letters drawn from 3x3-texel squares magnify into visible blocks at 5-20 m, and their outlines alias. Rows are packed with no padding, so lower mips mix neighbouring words and the halos are cropped. The atlas is not power of two, against the CLAUDE.md rule. Cyberpunk 2077 uses clean vector-style typography, tube meshes with emissive cores and bloom; GTA V uses high-resolution authored sign textures.

Fix

Use TextMeshPro (part of com.unity.ugui 2.6) SDF font assets made from 3-4 OFL fonts, with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads/Fonts: 4096 atlas, about 90 px sampling, padding 10. In NeonSignBuilder, either spawn TMP 3D text with a serialized neon SDF material held in VeyraMaterialLibrary (core = smoothstep on the distance using fwidth, halo = a wider band, HDR emission 4-12), or bake words into a power-of-two 4096 SDF atlas with 16 px gutters and sample it in VC_NeonSurface with fwidth AA. Keep PixelFont for contact sheets and for deliberate dot-matrix LED boards only (round dots, at least 4 px per LED at 10 m). For hero signs, generate tube meshes (8 sides, 1.5-2 cm radius) along the glyph outlines in Tools/Blender.

Cost
TMP SDF costs one quad per glyph and works with the SRP Batcher. A 4096 Alpha8 SDF atlas is 16 MB. Batch signs per cell.
Editor note
Same issue as RD-04 (and the font half of BLD-10); counted once.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png x 698-1030, y 384-442 (2560x1440): blocky 5x7 'DUMPLINGS' letters; smaller 'BAR' and 'SUSHI BAR' signs at about x1115-1220, y470-540 use the same font
Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/PixelFont.cs:7-12 (5x7 bitmap font for contact-sheet labels)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/NeonSignBuilder.cs:36 (AtlasW 1024, 48 rows x RowH 21 = 1008 px high, NPOT), :374-379 (PixelFont at 3x scale), :381-395 (3 px halo clipped at row bounds), :398-405 (npotScale None, mipmaps on, no gutters)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaletteRecipes.cs:461-474 (LED board text from PixelFont on a 64x64 LED grid), :477-490 (backlit 'NOODLES' face from PixelFont on a 64x32 grid)
Tracked as
new
IQ-07Texture import and filtering: bilinear everywhere, mixed aniso, no coverage-preserving mips on grass/leaves, 4K sources capped at 2048, no mip streaming medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Road, plaza and terrain textures smear at shallow angles a few metres ahead. Grass and leaf cards thin out and look sparse with distance, and downtown kit facades are softer than their source textures.
Root cause

Bilinear filtering switches mip levels abruptly, which shows as bands on the ground, and aniso below 16 blurs grazing surfaces. Force On only raises textures to a global minimum level whose default value is uncertain. Grazing surfaces dominate a driving view. Mips of alpha-tested cards average alpha below the cutoff, so foliage erodes with distance, and a 256 px grass card is soft up close. Capping 4K kit textures at 2048 halves facade texel density. AAA PC titles use trilinear filtering with 16x aniso, coverage-preserving foliage mips, and mip streaming to afford 4K hero textures.

Fix

Add an idempotent AssetPostprocessor (OnPreprocessTexture for Art/ and World/Generated/) with these settings. Ground, road, facade and terrain maps: Trilinear, anisoLevel 16. Props: aniso 4. Every alpha-clipped texture: mipMapsPreserveCoverage on and alphaTestReferenceValue equal to the material's _Cutoff. Standalone: maxTextureSize 4096 BC7 for hero sets (facades, road, terrain, vehicles) and 2048 elsewhere; normals BC5. Call Texture.SetGlobalAnisotropicFilteringLimits(16, 16) on PC tiers. Enable mip streaming: streamingMipmapsActive 1, budget 3072 MB (12 GB VRAM), max level reduction 2, streaming on for each texture. Regenerate GrassBlades as a 1024x1024 atlas with 4-8 blade variants, and patch ProcTextures, PaletteTextures and TerrainLayerMaps to write the same settings.

Cost
Trilinear filtering with 16x aniso costs an estimated <0.3 ms on a 4070 at 1440p. A 4096 BC7 texture is about 21 MB with mips, so rely on mip streaming. Coverage-preserving mips cost nothing at runtime.
Evidence
All 763 texture .meta files under Assets/_Veyra: filterMode 1 (bilinear) and streamingMipmaps 0; aniso 1 (306), 4 (282), 8 (175); maxTextureSize 2048 on every texture
Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Grass/GrassBlades.png.meta:14-15 mipMapsPreserveCoverage 0, alphaTestReferenceValue 0.5 vs M_Grass.mat:96 _Cutoff 0.4; the texture is 256x256. Assets/_Veyra/Art/Textures/Palette/Pal_Leaves_A.png.meta:14 coverage preservation 0

IQ-08Review captures do not show the gameplay image (different AA path, 3 frames of temporal history, stills only) medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Evidence screenshots look smoother than what players see. Shimmering, crawling and LOD pops appear only in motion and are never captured, so image-quality regressions pass review.
Root cause

The rig renders an off-screen camera on demand. This avoids the asset's MSAA, so STP runs, but STP gets only 3 jittered frames, far from converged, and the output is a single still. Temporal artefacts such as crawl, pops and ghosting only show in motion, so a single frame cannot reveal them. The evidence rule in CLAUDE.md therefore misses the main IQ failure.

Fix

Capture from the gameplay camera's backbuffer: call ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture after WaitForEndOfFrame once the pose has been held for at least 32 frames, so STP has converged. Also record a 3 s Recorder clip per camera with a slow 2°/s pan or dolly for temporal checks. Write the AA state to review.txt (MSAA samples, STP enabled, render scale, lodBias, aniso mode), and fail the run on any 'Disabling TAA' log line. Add 1:1 crops (facade, foliage edge, sign, ground) to the contact sheet.

Cost
Affects review runs only; no runtime cost.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/VeyraReviewCapture.cs:170-171 renders two WarmUp frames and then Render, so STP has 3 frames of history
Assets/_Veyra/World/Testing/ReviewCameraRig.cs:134,146 renders into RenderTexture.GetTemporary(...) with antiAliasing 1; :119 sets camera AA to None

IQ-09Sun shadows end at 160 m; far cascades are soft medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Beyond about 160 m, buildings, trees and hills cast no shadows, so skyline and mountain views look flat and pasted on. Mid-distance shadows turn into soft blobs.
Root cause

Cast shadows are the main cue that anchors objects to the ground and gives a skyline depth. Cutting them at 160 m leaves everything beyond lit by N·L alone, which reads as flat. Estimated cascade 3 (72-160 m) resolution is about 0.2 m per texel. GTA V draws shadow cascades much further out and adds a cheap distant shadow; Cyberpunk 2077 adds contact shadows.

Fix

Move the shadow values from GameFlow literals into the per-tier quality ScriptableObject. High: 300 m with splits about 0.04/0.12/0.33 (12/36/100/300 m). Ultra: 500 m. Both: 4096 atlas, soft shadows High, depth bias 1 and normal bias 1, then tune against CAM_05/06 for acne and peter-panning. Raise the instanced tree ShadowDistance to 300 m and draw those shadows from LowMesh. Bake vertex AO into building shells so distant bases still darken.

Cost
Longer cascades add shadow-caster draws. Enable GPU Resident Drawer and GPU occlusion culling, and budget an estimated +1-1.5 ms for the shadow pass at 300 m; measure in the Profiler.
Editor note
Same issue as LGT-07; counted once.

You see
Buildings and trees more than about 160 m away lose their cast shadows and contact darkening, so skylines and hillsides look flat-lit and pasted on. A shadow line moves visibly ahead of the car.
Root cause

Without far shadows, objects have no grounding or form cues beyond 160 m, which is most of what an 8x6 km city shows from roads and overlooks. The eye reads this flat lighting as a 'game' tell. GTA V draws cascades to several hundred metres plus distant shadow maps and baked far-field occlusion. Cyberpunk 2077 adds distant and RT shadows. Here the first cascade is only about 9.6 m, so most of the 4096 atlas is spent close to the camera.

Beyond that, bake a world-space far-shadow/AO height texture from the Veyra heightmap plus building footprints and sample it in the terrain/facade shaders. Verify the shadow pass at ≤2.5 ms GPU on CAM_02/03/09.

IQ-10Night post-processing: film grain, chromatic aberration and medium-quality motion blur add noise and softness low effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
Neon Mile and downtown look grainy at night and slightly smeared at the screen edges. At speed, the blur shows stepped ghost copies instead of a smooth streak.
Root cause

Grain and chromatic aberration are lens artefacts that lower effective resolution. Without temporal AA (IQ-01), the grain adds to the aliasing noise in dark scenes. Medium-quality motion blur takes few samples, so strong blur shows discrete copies. Cyberpunk 2077 keeps these effects subtle and lets players turn each one off; GTA V uses mild motion blur.

Fix

VP_Global_Night: FilmGrain intensity 0.04 (Thin1, response 0.8) and ChromaticAberration ≤ 0.03. VP_Local_NeonMile: CA 0.03. ChaseCamera: quality High, clamp 0.04, start at 120 km/h, maximum 0.3 plus 0.1 under nitro. Add toggles for film grain, chromatic aberration and motion blur to the GameFlow settings menu. Verify with a 100 km/h Recorder clip.

Cost
High-quality motion blur costs an estimated +0.2-0.4 ms at 1440p; lowering grain and CA costs nothing.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/Post/VP_Global_Night.asset:13-40 ChromaticAberration 0.06, FilmGrain intensity 0.12 (type 0)
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/Post/VP_Local_NeonMile.asset:13-28 ChromaticAberration 0.08
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/ChaseCamera.cs:34,241,266-272 MotionBlur CameraAndObjects, quality Medium, clamp 0.06, intensity up to 0.45 + 0.15 under nitro from 90 km/h
