new
IQ-1132-bit HDR colour buffer (R11G11B10) risks banding in night gradients and glows low effort S (< 1 day) impact 1/5 self-checked
You see
Possible banding rings in dark night-sky gradients, fog, neon glow and headlight pools (not yet confirmed at 1:1).
Root cause

R11G11B10 stores 6-7 mantissa bits per channel. In very dark, smooth gradients such as night sky, fog and bloom tails, the quantisation steps can show after tonemapping and grading, especially with saturated neon colours. Cyberpunk-style nights are built from exactly these gradients.

Fix

Add an Hdr64 field to the PC_High and PC_Ultra tiers in VeyraQualityTiers that writes m_HDRColorBufferPrecision 1 (64-bit RGBA16F). Compare 1:1 night crops of CAM_04 and CAM_13, plus a luminance histogram, with and without it, and keep 64-bit if the banding drops. Keep film grain at about 0.04 as a dither.

Cost
A 64-bit buffer doubles colour-target bandwidth, an estimated +0.2-0.5 ms at 1440p on a 4070; measure it.
Editor note
No banded frame was observed. Try camera dithering and HDR grading (LGT-11) before paying for a 64-bit colour buffer.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:27 m_HDRColorBufferPrecision 0 (32-bit R11G11B10); Ultra and Medium are the same
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png sky region y 0-300: dark purple gradient (banding not confirmed in the downscaled view)

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • GameFlow.cs:1063-1071: write msaaSampleCount = 1 whenever urp.isStpUsed and change only renderScale per tier (0.67/0.77/0.87/1.0). Then confirm 0 'Disabling TAA and STP' lines in a Dev run. About 1 hour; this turns temporal AA back on in gameplay.
  • Set PC_High lodBias back to 2 (ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:203) and make the WorldBuildBatch camshot restore lodBias in a finally block. About 30 minutes.
  • VC_FacadeSurface.hlsl: replace the hard step() window, mullion and frame edges with an fwidth-based aastep computed outside the branch, and fade interior rooms out sooner at grazing angles. Half a day; removes the CAM_05 sawtooth and the facade crawl.
  • Texture importer pass: mipMapsPreserveCoverage on, with alphaTestReferenceValue equal to _Cutoff, for GrassBlades, Pal_Leaves_A and T_Foliage_Atlas; Trilinear with aniso 16 on terrain, road and facade textures, set through ProcTextures, PaletteTextures and TerrainLayerMaps. Half a day.
  • Terrain rebuild through CityBuilder: alphamapResolution 512, baseMapResolution 1024, basemapDistance 1000, heightmapPixelError 2. Half a day including the rebuild.
  • Night post: FilmGrain 0.04, ChromaticAberration 0.03, motion blur quality High with clamp 0.04 and a 0.3 maximum. About 1 hour.
  • Review rig: hold each pose for 32 frames, capture the backbuffer, log the AA state and lodBias to review.txt, and add 1:1 crops to the contact sheet. Half a day.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Temporal AA is active in gameplay: 0 'Disabling TAA and STP' lines in 5-minute Dev player runs of R_BRIDGES and R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT, and the Frame Debugger shows the STP pass on the main camera at every PC tier.
  • Temporal stability: with a static camera held 60 frames at CAM_05 and CAM_13, the mean frame-to-frame luma change over the last 30 frames is ≤ 1/255. Measure with a script over backbuffer captures. A 3 s, 2°/s pan recording shows no visible crawl on mullions, road lines or foliage edges.
  • Cost: STP ≤ 1.2 ms GPU at 1440p (Profiler GPU markers), and frame time p99 ≤ 16.6 ms with no hitch over 50 ms in the R_BRIDGES run on the RTX 4070.
  • Texel density: at least 256 px/m (512 on Ultra hero sets) for road, terrain and facade surfaces within 30 m of the camera, and at least 64 px/m at 100 m. Check with a texel-density checker material or the Rendering Debugger mip view at CAM_05, CAM_06 and CAM_10.
  • Filtering audit: an importer audit script reports 0 violations. 100 % of world textures use trilinear filtering, aniso 16 on ground, road, facade and terrain, coverage-preserving mips with reference equal to cutoff on every alpha-clipped texture, power-of-two sizes, BC7 or BC5.
  • LOD: lodBias ≥ 1.5 on all PC tiers (EditMode test). No single-frame silhouette pops in a 60 s Halcyon Drive recording, meaning every LOD or cull change is crossfaded or dithered. Trees have LOD0/1/2 plus an impostor.
  • Signage: all sign text is SDF-rendered with no visible stair-steps on letter stems at 10-30 m in a 1:1 CAM_13 crop, and sign atlases are power-of-two with at least 8 px gutters.
  • Shadows: cast shadows reach at least 300 m on High and 500 m on Ultra, cascade 0 is ≤ 2 cm per texel (computed from splits and atlas size), there is no acne or peter-panning at CAM_05/06, and the shadow pass is ≤ 2.5 ms GPU.

Assets to make or source

  • 3-4 OFL-licensed display fonts (a condensed sans, a script suited to neon tube, a monospace for LED boards), with OFL.txt saved in ArtSource/Downloads/Fonts/<family>/. Convert them to TextMeshPro SDF font assets (4096 atlas, about 90 px sampling, padding 10). No real brand names on the signs.
  • 8 terrain layer material sets at 2048 px (albedo, BC5 normal, mask with height in B and smoothness in A) at a 4 m tile. Source them from CC0 libraries (keep the CC0 licence text in ArtSource/Downloads) or bake original sets in Blender. Also a 4096x3072 macro variation map derived from the Veyra landcover and splat data.
  • A 1024x1024 grass blade atlas with 4-8 blade variants plus dry and flower variants, authored in Blender (original), imported with coverage-preserving mips.
  • Tree LOD chains for pine, broadleaf and palm (LOD0 8-25k tris, LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %) and octahedral impostor atlases (8x8 frames, 2048 albedo+normal), baked in-house with Tools/Blender.
  • Facade interior atlas at 4096 with 16 room variants (offices, apartments, stairwells, blinds and curtains), original content.
  • Neon tube meshes for 10-20 hero signs, generated along the outlines of the OFL glyphs (the OFL allows derived outlines; keep the licence file with them).

11. Roads, street detail and signage

Score today: 3 / 10   The road geometry, markings and kerbs are correct, but hard-edged procedural patches, uniform asphalt, no decals, sparse primitive furniture, no road signage and pixel-font neon put streets far below GTA V by day and Cyberpunk by night.

The road layout is sound. Carriageways come from the drive surface, with crown, gutters, granite kerbs, sidewalks, dropped kerbs, markings, zebras and D4 guardrails. The surfaces and the roadside are what read as a toy city. The worst offender is the runtime RoadWear patch overlay: random rotated rectangles with knife edges that darken the asphalt by 38-78 %. They show as cut-out polygons in every daytime road shot (iter22 CAM_16 and CAM_10). There is no decal pipeline: the URP Decal feature is off and the Veyra shaders have no DBuffer variants. The wear data that RoadRenderBuilder already bakes into vertex colours is dropped because the road shader declares no COLOR input. Street furniture is sparse and built from flat-coloured boxes and cylinders, with no LODs or colliders. There are no traffic signs, gantries or delineators, and guardrails only exist in D4. Neon signage uses a 5x7 contact-sheet bitmap font on flat quads, which is the 'pixelated' look the user describes. Wet nights show a regular grid of half-metre ripple rings and flat additive light blobs where reflections should be. Several fixes take under a day: turn the patch overlay off or soften it, feed the vertex colours to the shader, drop the redundant runtime UV1 job, enable the decal feature, and rescale the ripples. Reaching AAA needs an original prop kit, a decal atlas with a seeding tool, an SDF sign and font system, and road signage from the road data.

Findings and fixes (13)

RD-01Road repair patches render as hard-edged dark polygons critical effort S (< 1 day) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Sharp-edged, near-black rectangles and parallelograms stamped across lanes and junctions, repeating every 14 m, like paper cut-outs lying on the road (iter22 CAM_16 junction centre, CAM_10 left lane ahead of the car).
Root cause

RoadWear generates the patch overlay at runtime as random rotated rectangles with a binary alpha edge and one flat tone, and the road shader then darkens the asphalt by 38-78 % inside them. Real patches differ from the surrounding asphalt by 10-25 % in tone, have a glossy tar seal band, a millimetre height step and ragged cracked borders, and follow trench or lane lines. High contrast, knife edges and random orientation (the UV0 of crossing roads in junctions) read as a texture error, not as wear.

Fix

Today: set _PatchAmount to 0 in RoadWear.cs:84. Short term: author the patch overlay in the editor (PaletteRecipes.AsphaltPatch) at 2048 px over 8 m. Use 4-8 px feathered alpha with 0.5-1 px noise erosion, lane-aligned rectangles only, tone 0.80-0.92 of the base, a 6-10 cm tar seam band with smoothness +0.25 and a -1 mm normal step, and crack fringes. In VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:37-40 change the mask gain from 10 to 3 and the darkening from lerp(0.22,0.62) to lerp(0.75,0.92). Drive placement from the baked vertex alpha (patch age per 40 m, RD-07) so patches cluster in wheel paths and trench lines instead of appearing everywhere. Long term: seed patch decals from the road decal atlas (RD-02). GTA V uses artist-placed mesh decals with feathered alpha and their own roughness for this.

Cost
Removes a 512x512 RGBA32 texture built at runtime in Start (a likely hitch source); shader cost unchanged.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png: 6-8 dark rotated polygons across the junction and approach, approx. x 1090-1985, y 820-1035 (2560x1440 px)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_10_BR_Halcyon_Drive_12h.png: arrow-shaped dark patch in the left lane, approx. x 665-1115, y 885-990
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:169-193 - patch texture = 14 randomly rotated rectangles, one flat tone 30-48/255, 1 px seam, alpha 200-235 (binary edge)
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:80-85 - switched on for every asphalt material at runtime (_PatchAmount 0.45/0.85, detail scale 1/14 = 14 m repeat)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:34-42 - noise mask sharpened x10, albedo multiplied by 0.22-0.62 inside patches
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaletteRecipes.cs:47-62 - M_Asphalt_Patched overlay uses the same hard rectangle logic

BUG-121
RD-02No decal system: roads, kerbs and walls have no grime detail high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Roads have no oil stains, skid marks, gully drains, real manhole covers, paint stencils or leaf litter. Every metre looks machine-clean, which reads as a toy city (CAM_06, CAM_08, CAM_16).
Root cause

The renderer assets never received URP's Decal feature, and the custom Veyra shaders compile no _DBUFFER_MRT variants, so projected decals cannot reach roads or facades. All wear is a tiling procedural overlay, which cannot carry the specific, non-repeating marks (stains under parked cars, skids at junctions, patched trenches, stencils) that give real streets a history. GTA V and Cyberpunk place thousands of decals per block for this.

Fix
  1. Add DecalRendererFeature to VC_Renderer_PC_High/Ultra: Technique DBuffer, surface data Albedo+Normal+MAOS, max draw distance 80 m, GPU instancing on every decal material. PC_Medium uses Screen Space (normal blend Medium). 2) In VC_ForwardPass.hlsl add '#pragma multi_compile_fragment _ _DBUFFER_MRT1 _DBUFFER_MRT2 _DBUFFER_MRT3' and call ApplyDecalToSurfaceData before UniversalFragmentPBR (Veyra/Road, Veyra/Facade); add the variants to VC_Variants.shadervariants. 3) Build an atlas Art/Textures/Decals/T_RoadDecals (4096x4096, BC7 albedo+alpha, BC5 normal, BC7 mask) with about 48 original decals. 4) Write an idempotent Editor tool 'Veyra/World/Seed Road Decals', seeded per 250 m chunk from road s/lateral data: 25-40 decals per 100 m urban, 8-12 rural. Skids within 40 m of junctions, oil at lane centres and stop lines, gully drains every 25-30 m. Long items (sealed cracks, stencils) become mesh decals in RoadRenderBuilder. Budget: 350 visible projectors or fewer.
Cost
Estimate: DBuffer pass 0.4-0.7 ms GPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070 with ~300 instanced projectors, ~0.1 ms CPU culling; measure the DBuffer marker in R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_High.asset:27-29 and VC_Renderer_PC_Ultra.asset:27-29 - m_RendererFeatures has a single entry (ScreenSpaceAmbientOcclusion, line 68); no DecalRendererFeature
Grep 'DBUFFER|ApplyDecal' in Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders: no match, so Veyra/Road and Veyra/Facade would ignore DBuffer decals even with the feature on
Assets/_Veyra/AI/StreetFurniture.cs:435-439 - manholes are 14-sided 2.4 cm cylinders with a flat cross bar, untextured
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_08_R1_Viaduct_Night_12h.png: lower half (y 820-1440) is flat black asphalt with no stains, joints or tyre marks
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:82 - BUG-118 open

BUG-118
RD-03Street furniture is sparse, flat-coloured primitives without LODs high effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Pavements are nearly empty: a hydrant or bin every few dozen metres, built from plain boxes and cylinders in flat paint. Suburbs show only lamps, with no signs, mailboxes, utility poles or wires (CAM_06 right pavement, iter22 CAM_16).
Root cause

Furniture is a runtime procedural fallback for missing placement data (BUG-008: all 2,777 furniture rows are lamps). Low spawn chances and only 8 vertical prop types give low density and little variety. Untextured primitives have no bevels, wear, stickers or material contrast, so they read as placeholders. GTA V streets carry dense, layered, grimy props (estimate: a vertical item every 3-6 m downtown) that also give constant scale cues.

Fix

Author an original prop kit in Blender (Tools/Blender/vc_tools.py, VC_Unity_Prop, pivot at base centre) with 35-45 props: bins (2), hydrants (2), mailbox, newspaper boxes (3), single and double parking meters, utility cabinets (3), phone kiosk, bus shelters (2, with an advert lightbox), bike rack, bollards (3), tree grate, planters (2), A-frame board, cafe set, dumpsters (2), trash bags, cones and barriers, a D5/D8 utility pole with crossarm, transformer and wires, fire-alarm box, ATM. Budgets: LOD0 300-4,000 tris, LOD1 40 %, LOD2 10 % for props of 2 m or more; a shared 2048x2048 trim sheet plus 2 atlases at 512 px/m; UBX_/UCX_ colliders on anything over 0.3 m. Put placement rules in Assets/_Veyra/Data/street_furniture_rules.csv (kerb zone 0.5-0.8 m, frontage, corners, never in the carriageway). Densities: Downtown 1 per 4-6 m, Neon Mile 1 per 3-5 m, suburbs 1 per 10-15 m plus a pole every 35-45 m. Render instanced through InstancedPropRenderer, with no runtime materials.

Cost
Instanced: estimate +60-120 batches and +0.3-0.8 M tris in a downtown view; shadows off for props under 0.5 m; must stay inside the BUG-117 SetPass budget.

BUG-008
RD-04Neon signs use a 5x7 bitmap contact-sheet font on flat quads high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Night signs such as DUMPLINGS look like blocky LED-scoreboard pixels on flat cards; blade signs are blank glowing slabs; the same 48 words repeat across the city (CAM_13 22h_wet, upper left).
Root cause

Sign text is rasterised once from a debug label font into a low-resolution atlas (7 px glyphs scaled x3) and mapped onto single flat quads, so every glyph pixel becomes a 13 cm block and no geometry catches light. Real neon is a 12-15 mm bent glass tube with a round profile, dark unlit returns, standoffs and a transformer box; storefronts mix channel letters, lightboxes and screens. Cyberpunk layers these with distinct fonts, logos, mixed scripts and flicker; 48 repeated words give no identity.

Fix

Replace PixelFont with signed-distance-field text. Generate an SDF/MSDF atlas in an Editor step (2048x2048 R8/BC4, or 4096x2048 BC7 for MSDF) from 3-4 OFL-licensed fonts, with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads, using TextMeshPro's Font Asset Creator or an editor MSDF baker. In Veyra/Neon, sample the SDF to shade a tube: a hot HDR core, a glass falloff, and dim unlit segments where letters connect; this stays sharp at any distance. Give signs depth: 5-8 cm backplate boxes; D1 channel letters extruded 8-12 cm with emissive faces; D6 tube neon with standoffs and transformer boxes (300-1,500 tris per sign, LOD1 = flat SDF card). Expand to 150+ original names and icons (noodle bowl, cocktail, arrows), with a per-sign flicker phase in vertex colour and 5-10 % broken letters. Keep atlases power-of-two.

Cost
One extra texture fetch per sign pixel; estimate +0.5-1 M tris at LOD0 in D6, merged per chunk as today.
Evidence

RD-05Wet roads: stamped ripple grid and painted light blobs, no reflections high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
In rain the road shows a carpet of identical half-metre rings. Neon and lamps never reflect; flat coloured ellipses lie on the asphalt like paint, and the magenta storefront leaves the road black (CAM_13 and CAM_07 at 22h wet).
Root cause

Ripples use a 0.59 m cell grid in which every cell fires a ring every 1.2 s, so the pattern is regular and about 5-10x too large. Real lamps and lights give stretched specular, but the gloss cap of 0.72 makes it broad. Emissive signs, windows and strips are not lights, so they never reflect. The night occlusion x0.2 kills probe reflections, and URP 17 has no SSR, so additive blobs fake the rest. GTA V/Cyberpunk use SSR plus probes stretched by roughness.

Fix

VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:87: change the scale from w*1.7 to w*9 (about 11 cm cells) and use two offset layers. Let about 30 % of cells fire by hash and cap the ring at 0.45 cell. Gate the ripples by the puddle and gutter mask, or bake a 512x512 4x4 normal flipbook in an Editor step. Reflections: add a custom half-resolution SSR ScriptableRendererFeature (RenderGraph API; URP 17 has none built in) used by Veyra/Road, with vertical stretch driven by roughness. Fall back to box-projected night reflection probes per block (baked at 22:00, 128-256 px), and remove the x0.2 night occlusion where those probes exist. Once reflections are real, raise wet smoothness to 0.82-0.88 with 0.95 in puddles, then remove the GlowPool blobs over asphalt.

Cost
Estimate: half-res SSR 0.8-1.3 ms GPU at 1440p on an RTX 4070; gated ripples cost no more than today's 9-cell loop.
Editor note
Unity 6.6 URP has no supported screen-space reflections: the SSR renderer feature is a preview that compiles only behind the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION scripting define and is aimed at Unity 6.7. Try it on a Git branch only; the production path in this report is box-projected probes per block plus a low-resolution planar reflection for water, or the 6.7 LTS upgrade at a milestone boundary (CLAUDE.md).
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: grid of identical rings approx. x 485-1150, y 975-1150; flat red pool ellipse x 755-1140, y 820-845; a vertical magenta specular streak x 665-770, y 830-1150 and the taillight streak below the car show that punctual-light specular already works
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_07_DT_Drive_Meridian_Night_22h_wet.png: emissive magenta strip with no reflection on the road (not opened by this reviewer; per auditor)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Common.hlsl:57-79: every cell fires one ring every 1/0.85 s, and the ring grows to 0.9 cell; VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:87 uses w*1.7, so cells are about 0.59 m
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_RoadSurface.hlsl:81 caps wet gloss at 0.72, and :98 sets occlusion x0.2 at night, which removes environment reflection
Logs/C/neon_signs.txt:1: 1,038 night light pools
Tracked as
BUG-060
RD-06No road signs, gantries or delineators; guardrails only in D4 high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Motorways have no overhead direction gantries or speed signs, mountain roads have no reflector posts or curve warnings, and city junctions have no stop or give-way signs. That removes the strongest scale and 'real place' cues (CAM_08, iter22 CAM_10).
Root cause

Roadside equipment is limited to D4 guardrails and chevrons, plus parapet or median walls on structures. No tool generates regulatory, warning or guide signs, gantries or delineators from the road network. Signs and gantries are the strongest known-size scale cues in a driving view (a stop sign about 0.75 m, gantry clearance 5-6 m). Without them, roads read as a test track, whereas GTA V lines its freeways with gantries, exit signs and barriers.

Fix

Add a new idempotent Editor tool Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadSignBuilder.cs (WorldBuildBatch step 'signs'), taking positions only from road_network_geometry.json. It places: MW/EX/HW overhead truss gantries (1.2 m deep, 5.5 m clearance) at 1 km, at 500 m and at each exit; speed-limit signs after each junction, by road class; curve warnings and chevrons for curves under 150 m radius in every district; delineator posts every 50 m on rural HW/AR/MT; stop and give-way signs on minor approaches without signals (taken from the TrafficLights site list); F-shape median barriers (0.81 m) on divided MW/EX; W-beam with flared ends on every rural drop of 1.5 m or more (lift the InD4 gate at RoadRenderBuilder.cs:458). Sign faces use an original Veyra sign system with an OFL road font as SDF (shared with RD-04) on a 2048x2048 atlas, plus a retroreflective night term. Gantry LOD0 up to 6k tris, signs up to 400, LODs per CLAUDE.md, UBX_ colliders on posts.

Cost
Static and instanced; estimate +20-40 draws on a motorway view.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_08_R1_Viaduct_Night_12h.png: multi-lane road to the horizon with no gantry, speed sign or delineator; a plain concrete median wall separates the carriageways at x 0-1190, y 715-1035
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_10_BR_Halcyon_Drive_12h.png: mountain road with no posts, signs or barriers
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png: four-way junction with no stop or give-way signs
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:453-458: Roadside returns for urban classes, for structures and outside D4
Grep gantry|DirectionSign|SpeedLimit|delineator over Assets/_Veyra/**/*.cs: only the port crane (BuildingGenerator.cs:520); StreetFurniture.cs:440-445 street-name sign = two blank green boxes

RD-07Baked road wear data is discarded; RoadWear redoes UV1 at runtime medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 self-checked
You see
Asphalt looks the same everywhere: junction approaches are no darker or more polished, gutters are no grimier, and puddles do not collect where the road is low (CAM_06, CAM_16).
Root cause

The road builder already bakes the wear story GTA V paints by hand (polish on junction approaches, kerb grime, puddles in low spots, per-stretch age). Because the Veyra/Road vertex input never declares COLOR, the shader drives wear only from UV1 and global noise. RoadWear then re-derives UV1 on the CPU at runtime, duplicating build-time data, keeping meshes readable, and reaching only about 12-40 meshes per review run.

Fix

Add 'half4 color : COLOR' to VC_Attributes and VC_Varyings (VC_ForwardPass.hlsl) and pass it into VC_FragInput. In VC_RoadSurface.hlsl, use the channels as follows. R scales wheel-path darkening (0.25-0.45) and polish (smoothness +0.15). G drives gutter grime (replace the 1.7x 'pale dust' with darker grime). B sets the puddle mask as max(color.b, fbm), so water collects in gutters and low spots. A blends New/Worn tone (±12 %) and the crack mask. Set _TyreWear and _PatchAmount on the material assets in VeyraPalette Configure instead of at runtime. Delete RoadWear's UV1 job and its material mutation, and turn Read/Write off on the RD_ meshes to save memory.

Cost
Frees up to 0.8 ms CPU main per frame plus the readable mesh copies; one extra interpolator in the road shader (negligible).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:322-324, 636-648, 668-670 - the carriageway gets UV1 (across, lanes) and vertex colours: R wheel-track wear (more within 40 m of junctions), G edge grime, B puddle mask, A patch age per 40 m
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_ForwardPass.hlsl:14-22 - VC_Attributes declares POSITION, NORMAL, TANGENT and TEXCOORD0/1 only; with no COLOR, all four baked channels are dropped
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:109-166 - recomputes UV1 per mesh at runtime (up to 0.8 ms/frame) for 2,049 meshes; Logs/ITER/21/review_player.log:194 (2049 pending) vs :1423 (2037 pending)
Tracked as

RD-08Asphalt: black motorways, uniform hairline cracks, no aged look medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 self-checked
You see
Motorway decks read as flat black rubber. City streets are one even grey with the same thin hairline cracks spread evenly over every square metre (CAM_08 lower half, CAM_16 foreground).
Root cause

The albedo targets fresh asphalt (about 0.04-0.07 linear), although roads in a lived-in city are aged and dusty (estimate: 0.10-0.18 linear), so motorways go black at distance and lose specular contrast. Cracks have one type, width and density everywhere, which reads as a tiled texture. Real roads have joint and transverse cracks every 5-15 m, tar-sealed bands, alligator cracking only in wheel paths, ravelled edges, and sun bleaching between wheel paths.

Fix

In PaletteRecipes.Asphalt raise the AsphaltNew binder from 0.042 to about 0.07 (mean about 0.10). Make AsphaltWorn mean about 0.14 with lighter exposed aggregate, and set VeyraPalette Min/MaxAlbedo to 0.08-0.18; check against the palette albedo report. Remove the random cracks from the base tile and add crack classes as separate masks: transverse cracks every 6-14 m and longitudinal lane-joint cracks as 3-5 cm tar-sealed bands (smoothness 0.55), alligator cracking masked by vertex R, edge ravelling by vertex G. Add a 0.5 m detail normal (512 px) that fades beyond 15 m, and a 50-200 m macro layer for tone and roughness. Give motorways their own original M_Asphalt_Motorway with rubber lines, plus concrete joints every 30-60 m on structures.

Cost
One extra 512x512 detail-normal sample in the road shader; otherwise neutral.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_08_R1_Viaduct_Night_12h.png: deck at y 820-1440 is near-uniform black with no visible aggregate
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png: foreground x 830-2560, y 895-1345 has evenly spaced 1 px cracks
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaletteRecipes.cs:16-20 - M_Asphalt_New binder 0.042, mean about 0.07; :33-40 cracks on every 4 m tile, gated only by fbm
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:230 - all MW/EX/HW roads use M_Asphalt_New (M.AsphaltFast)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/VeyraPalette.cs:121-124 - asphalt albedo clamped to 0.05-0.12

RD-09Markings: stubby zebras, uneven dashes, two paint systems, pristine paint medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Pedestrian crossings look like a dashed line rather than a zebra, lane dashes vary in length, and all lines are crisp, bright and unworn, with few arrows or stencils (CAM_06, CAM_07).
Root cause

Markings are emitted per 2 m drive-surface row, so dash ends and zebra depth snap to the row grid instead of the arc length, and the zebra fits inside one row. A second runtime system paints crossings with other dimensions and a flat material. Paint wear is a 2 m tiling chip texture unrelated to traffic, so lines stay perfectly bright. Real paint wears in wheel paths, keeps ghost lines and reflects headlights at night through glass beads.

Fix

In RoadRenderBuilder, split marking quads at exact dash boundaries along s (3 m dash / 6 m gap urban, 3/9 m fast roads). Make zebra stripes 0.5 m wide with 0.5-0.6 m gaps and 3.0 m deep, spanning rows. Place the 0.4 m stop line at TrafficLights.StopLineDistance so it matches the signals. Remove Kind.Crosswalk, StopLine and Arrow from StreetFurniture; emit arrows, STOP/BUS/SLOW stencils and give-way triangles from the builder as mesh decals from a 2048x2048 marking atlas. Wear: per-vertex wear from lateral position against the wheel paths (alpha erosion with a 2-4 cm noise edge, 20-60 % loss in wheel paths) and 5 % ghost lines. Night: a retroreflective term for marking materials in VC_Road (view-aligned specular boost while _VC_NightFactor > 0).

Cost
Neutral; removes the runtime paint boxes.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png: crossing x 75-1625, y 855-885 is a thin band of short dashes
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_07_DT_Drive_Meridian_Night_22h_wet.png: the same stubby crossing at x 0-1700, y 875-910
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:410-412 - zebra stripes 0.5 m wide, covering only 0.3-1.0 of one 2 m row (1.4 m deep)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:234, 418 - dashes switch on or off per 2 m row: the urban 3/9 m cycle gives alternating 4 m and 2 m dashes; fast roads get 4 m dashes instead of 3 m
Assets/_Veyra/AI/StreetFurniture.cs:80-94, 415-434 - a second crossing, stop-line and arrow system using 3.2 m flat untextured boxes (FurnRoadPaint) at a different distance from the junction

RD-10Junction seams, jointless kerbs and sterile pavements medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Cross streets meet along ruler-straight seams between two asphalt tones. Kerbs are endless clean extrusions without joints, and pavements have no drains, covers, tree grates, stains or driveway aprons (CAM_06 lower left, iter22 CAM_16 left).
Root cause

Each road is a separate ribbon with its own material, so a junction is two overlapping surfaces with a hard material edge instead of one paved box with seams. Kerbs are swept profiles without 0.9-1 m stone joints, chips or base grime. Pavements lack the utility layer (drains, covers, grates, tactile paving) that real cities and GTA V use to break up large flat areas.

Fix

In RoadRenderBuilder, build one junction box mesh per node (polygon from the overlapping carriageways) with M_Asphalt_Worn and vertex wear R=1. Lay a glossy 8 cm tar-seam mesh decal along each junction mouth. Give the KerbGranite recipe 1.0 m segments with 6 mm joints and edge chips; add per-segment tone in vertex colour and base grime from vertex G; build corners as radiused pieces (3-6 m radius). Pavement layer: gully drains every 25-30 m, 0.6 m utility covers, 1.2 m grates around street trees, original tactile paving at dropped kerbs, and driveway aprons from building frontages in D5. Blend a 0.3 m terrain grass fade along pavement edges. Seed everything from road data, idempotently.

Cost
Small; meshes and decals merged per 250 m chunk.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png: darker cross-street asphalt meets the worn approach along a straight seam from about (0, 975) to (830, 885)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png: left kerb x 0-830, y 1010-1410 is one continuous speckled extrusion; the pavement ends in a hard straight edge to grass
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:230, 303 - material chosen per road class; inside a junction the weaker road drops 6 mm so the stronger one draws on top (no blend)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaletteRecipes.cs:81-90 - granite kerb texture is speckle only, with no segment joints

RD-11Viaduct parapets, median and decks are plain slabs medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Elevated roads are lined by flat concrete walls with one thin rail, the median is a featureless vertical slab, and decks show no joints, drains or lamp plinths (CAM_08 left and right).
Root cause

Bridge edges come from the blockout plus minimal generated strips. Nothing provides the safety-shape profile, chamfers, drip edges, expansion joints, scuppers or stains that say 'cast concrete at highway scale'. The sloped lower face of a real F-shape barrier carries a highlight line along the road, a strong perspective cue. GTA V viaducts add joints every 30-60 m, drainage stains and lamp plinths.

Fix

Add a profile library to RoadRenderBuilder.Profile, which already sweeps (offset, rise) points. F-shape barrier: 0.6 m base, steep lower face to 0.25 m, near-vertical upper face to 0.81-1.07 m, 3 cm chamfers, for structure edges and divided MW/EX medians. Viaducts also get a steel post-and-rail with round 76 mm tubes (12 sides at LOD0). Every 40 m on structures add a steel finger expansion-joint mesh decal across the deck and a scupper drain with a streak decal down the parapet face. Set lamp masts on 0.6 m plinths. Use a concrete trim sheet at 2048x2048 (formwork lines, leaching, tyre scuffs) at 512 px/m. Keep the existing COL_ collision. LOD1 drops the chamfers; LOD2 is a 2-quad profile.

Cost
Estimate: +8-12 tris per 2 m row per side, about +5-8 % road chunk triangles.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_08_R1_Viaduct_Night_12h.png: right parapet x 1485-2560, y 730-1060 is a flat wall with one rail; the left median wall x 0-1190, y 715-1035 is a vertical slab
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:341-356 - on structures: a 0.2 m parapet kerb, two 6 cm flat rail strips and 6 cm square posts every 2.5 m
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BridgeDeckFiller.cs:24, 180-190 - filled decks get 1.1 m box parapets
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/HighwayLightsBuilder.cs:76 - masts are square-section tapers from 0.26 to 0.14 m with no base plinth

RD-M1RoadWear rescans every streamed cell on load and rewrites shared road materials medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
Small hitches when cells stream in while driving. Road look can change mid-session as asphalt materials are reconfigured and meshes get new UV1 data.
Root cause

The other session's uncommitted change makes RoadWear one more synchronous CellLoaded subscriber, the hitch class BUG-112 names. It walks each loaded cell's whole hierarchy on the main thread, mutates shared materials at runtime, and queues a CPU UV1 job that overwrites UV1 already baked in the editor. That duplicates RoadRenderBuilder work and costs up to 0.8 ms of main-thread time per frame while the job runs.

Fix

Move all road-wear setup to edit time. Set _DetailMap, _PatchAmount, _TyreWear and _MacroVariation on the serialized asphalt materials in VeyraMaterialFactory/PaletteRecipes, so nothing is mutated at runtime. Make RoadRenderBuilder the only source of UV1, and bake UV1 for any remaining asphalt ribbons in the editor as well. Then remove RoadWear's CellLoaded subscription and its UV1 job, or delete the component. Verify by measuring hitch count on R_BRIDGES (Tools/run_autotest.cmd, stats.json) before and after the change.

Cost
Removes per-cell hierarchy scans and up to 0.8 ms per frame of main-thread UV1 work during streaming.
Editor note
Same issue as RD-07; counted once.
Evidence
git diff Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs (uncommitted): Start() now subscribes CellStreamer.CellLoaded -> OnCellLoaded, which runs GetComponentsInChildren<MeshRenderer>(true) over every root of the loaded scene and calls ScanRenderers
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:62-87: per renderer, string checks on shader and material names, then SetTexture/SetFloat on shared materials
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:109-166: per-vertex NearestRoad UV1 job (0.8 ms budget per frame) on meshes that RoadRenderBuilder.cs:325-326 already bakes UV1 for
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:76: BUG-112, streaming hitches come from the per-cell CellLoaded subscribers

BUG-112
RD-12Traffic signals and street-name signs are untextured cubes low effort M (1-3 days) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
Signal heads are grey boxes on a cube arm and street-name blades are blank green slats with no lettering, so junctions lack readable detail that anchors a real city.
Root cause

Signals and signs were built from primitives as functional placeholders so the traffic logic could run, and no art pass followed. Without visors, backplates, lenses, pedestrian heads, push buttons, control cabinets and legible street names, junctions miss the mid-scale detail GTA V uses as landmarks, and lens glow at night has no housing.

Fix

Model an original signal family in Blender (VC_Unity_Prop): a 3-aspect head with visors and a backplate with a reflective border (LOD0 about 1.5k tris), a pedestrian head, a mast-arm pole with base plinth and control cabinet, and a push-button box. Lenses are an emissive submesh driven by MaterialPropertyBlock, with no runtime material creation. Street-name signs get SDF text from the road-sign font (RD-06), using street names from road_network_geometry.json on both faces. Merge per junction into one renderer with a LODGroup (LOD1 40 %, LOD2 10 %).

Cost
Fewer GameObjects and renderers than now (one per junction instead of one per primitive).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficLights.cs:189-194 - signal head = Unity primitive cube 0.38 x 1.05 x 0.32; :165-167 arm = cube; :197-207 one GameObject and renderer per primitive
Assets/_Veyra/AI/StreetFurniture.cs:440-445 - street-name sign = a pole plus two green boxes, no text

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Road albedo: urban asphalt mean 0.10-0.16 linear, motorway 0.09-0.13; no patch or seam edge with a luminance step over 25 % across less than 2 cm. Measure with the VeyraPalette albedo report plus luminance crops of CAM_06, CAM_08 and CAM_16 at noon.
  • Decals: 25 or more road decals per 100 m of urban carriageway and 8 or more rural; 350 or fewer visible projectors; DBuffer pass 0.7 ms GPU or less at 1440p on an RTX 4070. Measure with the seeding report in Logs/ and the Profiler/Frame Debugger DBuffer marker on R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT.
  • Street furniture: 30 or more unique original props, each with LOD0+LOD1 (LOD2 if 2 m or taller). Density downtown at least 1 vertical item per 6 m of kerb per side, suburbs at least 1 per 15 m; every prop over 0.3 m has a collider. Measure with a placement report (counts per km by district) and the LODGroup validator.
  • Signage: no bitmap-font signs; all text from power-of-two SDF atlases; 150 or more original sign designs; no visible stair-stepping when a sign fills 300 px at 2560x1440. Measure with zoom crops of CAM_13 and CAM_07 at 22h wet and the NeonSignBuilder report.
  • Road equipment coverage: an MW/EX/HW gantry every 1 km or less and at every exit; a speed sign after every junction; delineators every 50 m on rural roads; guardrail on 100 % of rural edges with a drop of 1.5 m or more within 6 m, in all districts. Measure with RoadSignBuilder/RoadRenderBuilder log counts per km against road_network_geometry.json.
  • Markings: dash and gap lengths within ±5 cm of spec, zebra stripes 3.0 m deep, exactly one crossing and one stop line per approach. Measure with a new marking validator over the generated road meshes.
  • Wet night: ripple ring radius 10 cm or less with no visible grid at 5 m; neon and lamp reflections stretch toward the camera on wet asphalt in CAM_07 and CAM_13 at 22h wet; reflection pass 1.3 ms GPU or less. Measure with review captures and the Profiler.
  • Performance: roads, props, decals and signs together add 1.5 ms GPU or less and 0.5 ms CPU main or less; Dev p99 frame time 16.6 ms or less with no hitch over 50 ms on R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT and R_BRIDGES. Measure with Tools/run_autotest.cmd stats.json.

  • Road signage kit: original Veyra regulatory, warning and guide sign faces (follow real-world conventions, never copy another game's designs); overhead truss gantry in 2-3 variants (6k tris or fewer); delineator posts; W-beam with flared terminal ends; F-shape concrete barrier profile; a 2048x2048 sign atlas with a retroreflective mask.
  • Fonts: 3-4 OFL-licensed typefaces (a condensed road-sign sans, a display face, a script for neon, a monospace for LED), with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads, baked to SDF/MSDF atlases in an Editor step.
  • Neon kit: SDF word atlas with 150+ original names, 30 original icon shapes (noodle bowl, cocktail, star, arrow), tube-standoff and transformer-box meshes, channel-letter extrusion tool in Tools/Blender.
  • Traffic signal family: 3-aspect head with visors and backplate, pedestrian head, mast-arm pole with plinth, control cabinet, push button; street-name sign blanks with SDF text.
  • Concrete trim sheet 2048x2048 (formwork lines, chamfers, leaching, tyre scuffs) and a steel finger expansion-joint mesh for viaducts.
  • Rain ripple normal flipbook, 512x512 4x4, generated procedurally in an Editor step (no third-party content).

12. People, traffic and cars

Score today: 2.5 / 10   The only convincing vehicle is a real-brand-derived download, while the people are primitive mannequins and traffic is low-poly with frozen wheels, no drivers and sprite lights, far below GTA V/Cyberpunk 2077 crowds and traffic.

At street level, the strongest "kids' game" cue is the people. Each pedestrian is assembled at runtime from 12 rigid, untextured primitive meshes in flat colours, and the arms and legs swing on one sine wave with no knees or elbows. Traffic uses 19 low-poly downloads (3.9k-21k tris, no normal maps, interiors or drivers). The cars slide along with frozen wheels, and no review shot shows them because the review harness turns traffic off.

The player car in every chase shot is a Chevrolet Corvette C8 with the badges removed (stanton_sidewinder). With four other rebadged cars (Ferrari SF90, Rolls-Royce Ghost, Koenigsegg Gemera, Aston Martin DB11), it breaks the CLAUDE.md:86 rule and blocks release (BUG-066). The ardente_furia files still carry "Ferrari" in their names and inside the FBX. 17 more "homage" models arrived with brand-style badges. Five of the seven project-owned cars have no model and render as primitive boxes and cylinders.

At night the car lights are glow sprites, one raised spot light with no cookie, and a red disc that floats on the wet road behind the car. The player car's clear-coat paint is a sound base, but it has no flake (the PaintFlake tool was never run), no dirt and no wet layer.

Fix order:

  1. Legal cleanup and the capture car (days).
  2. Traffic wheel motion, light fixes and seeded traffic in reviews (days).
  3. A character pipeline: Rocketbox/Mixamo characters, skinned near crowds, VAT-instanced far crowds (weeks).
  4. An original line-up of 28 vehicles (months).

Findings and fixes (11)

LIFE-01Pedestrians are runtime-built primitive mannequins with stick-swing animation critical effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Faceless, handless dolls built from rounded capsules in flat primary colours. Their legs swing stiffly from the hip with no knees or footfall. Up close (CAM_16) they read as toy figures, not people.
Root cause

There is no character pipeline. Pedestrians.cs assembles each walker from rigid, untextured primitive parts (no hands, face, hair strands or clothing detail) and animates them with transform rotations from one sine. Silhouette, micro-detail and motion all read as mannequins. Shadows only on the torso and no ambient probe detach the figures from the lighting. GTA V uses mocap-animated skinned characters with interchangeable heads, torsos, legs and props, plus LODs and ambient scenarios.

Fix

Replace the figures with rigged, textured humans. First set: Microsoft Rocketbox Avatar Library (MIT; add the licence file to ArtSource/Downloads and register rows). Retarget Mixamo or CMU mocap to a Unity Humanoid avatar. Spec: LOD0 10-16k tris, ~60 bones, body 2048² + head 1024² BC7; LOD1 ~40 % (4-6k); LOD2 ~10 % (1-1.5k); 6-8 heads × 8 outfits × tint masks gives ≥ 300 combinations. Animator: walk/stroll/brisk blend tree driven by Ped.Speed (1.1-1.7 m/s), 4 idles, umbrella, phone and chat layers, foot IK on LOD0 only. New idempotent Editor tool PedestrianCatalogBuilder writes the prefabs, LODGroups and authored materials (referenced through VeyraMaterialLibrary). Pedestrians.cs keeps its anchors, AI and dodging, but Create()/Dress()/the walk pose code are replaced by prefab pooling and Animator parameters. Use LightProbeUsage.BlendProbes and cast shadows on all LODs within 40 m.

Cost
Today: 85 walkers × 12 renderers ≈ 1,020 renderers with CPU transform writes (~0.5 ms for 70 per Pedestrians.cs:23). Target: ≤ 24 skinned LOD0/1 near the camera with GPU skinning and Animator culling; mid and far crowds instanced as described in LIFE-08. Budget ≤ 1.5 ms CPU, ≤ 1.0 ms GPU at 1440p (estimate).
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter22/CAM_16_WH_Street_Day_12h.png: blue-shirt capsule figure at ~(77,806) of 2560x1440 with no hands or face; all-black figure at ~(934,800); distant walkers are flat-colour sticks
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:10-15 (procedural low-poly figures, no third-party models); 553-584 Create() = 21 GameObjects, 12 MeshRenderers (umbrella shaft and canopy inactive unless raining)
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:665-682 rounded/sphere meshes; 27-39 palettes of 10 shirt, 5 trouser, 4 skin and 4 hair colours, untextured
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:476-491 walk cycle = one sine: legs ±26°, arms ±22°, hip bob 0.035 m, no knee/elbow/foot roll
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:644-656 shadows only when shadows=true, used only for TorsoMesh (563); LightProbeUsage.Off (654)

LIFE-02Player car and four other cars are badge-stripped real production cars (release blocker) critical effort M (1-3 days) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
The red player car in CAM_06/07/13 is plainly a Chevrolet Corvette C8 (louvred engine cover, quad centre exhausts, split tail lamps). The garage sheet shows SF90-like and Rolls-Royce-Ghost-like cars.
Root cause

CC-BY 4.0 covers only the modeller's copyright in the mesh. Section 2(b)(2) excludes trademark rights, and the modeller cannot license the manufacturer's body design (trade dress, design rights). Removing badges leaves the silhouette that identifies the car. The user's 2026-11-10 decision to import the five cars (BUG-066) contradicts the unchanged CLAUDE.md:86 rule. The capture default (GameFlow.cs:277) then put a C8 in every chase shot.

Fix
  1. Ask the user to resolve BUG-066 one way or the other: amend CLAUDE.md:86, or drop the five cars. Recommend dropping them; CLAUDE.md:86 says real-brand cars are never used. 2) Change the default regardless: set GameFlow.cs:277 iterCar and the review save to an unrestricted car (iron_howl or orion_skylark). 3) If the cars are dropped: remove them from GameDatabase.Cars, git mv their Art/Vehicles folders and zips to ArtSource/Downloads/_Quarantine, set register rows L-051..L-055 to QUARANTINED, and drop their credits (GameDatabase.asset:339-387, printed by GameFlow.cs:1290). 4) Add a LicenceGateTests EditMode test that fails on any Cars/TrafficModels entry whose register row says 'RESEMBLES A REAL CAR'. It also fails on brand words in Art/Vehicles paths or FBX strings. 5) Any release with these cars needs a written IP-lawyer opinion first.
Cost
No runtime cost. Dropping ardente_furia (11.3 MB FBX) and stanton_sidewinder (10.5 MB FBX) reduces build size.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_07_DT_Drive_Meridian_Night_22h_wet.png: player car at ~(1280,960) of 2560x1440 shows a C8-style rear (visual identification)
Docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_REGISTER.csv:40-44 (L-051..L-055) SF90 XX, Rolls-Royce Ghost, Koenigsegg Gemera, Aston Martin DB11, Corvette C8
ArtSource/Vehicles/stanton_sidewinder/process_report.json: source Chevrolet_Corvette_C8_Stingray_790c40cc.zip, real_brand 'chevrolet', Body_LOD0 120000
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Vehicles/ardente_furia/ardente_furia.fbx: 123 'Ferrari' occurrences (grep -a -o)
Assets/_Veyra/Data/GameDatabase.asset:339,351,363,375,387 credits; Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GameFlow.cs:1290-1291 prints them beside 'All place, crew and car names are fictional.'
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GameFlow.cs:277 iterCar = "stanton_sidewinder"; CLAUDE.md:86; Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:68 BUG-066 (user decision 2026-11-10)

BUG-066
LIFE-03Traffic and parked cars are low-poly downloads without normal maps, interiors or drivers high effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Traffic looks like a toy set. Bodies are faceted, grilles and panel lines are painted on, and dark see-through glass shows empty cabins. Most cars are 1970s-90s classics, and taxis or police cruisers turn up in every district.
Root cause

Traffic is the free Generic passenger car pack plus 9 stylised 'low poly' classics. Silhouettes facet at chase distance. Lamps, grilles and panel gaps are flat texture, so they never catch light. Empty glass removes the 'someone is driving' cue. The uniform model pick ignores district and time of day. In GTA V, traffic uses the same detailed, drivable models as player cars, with drivers, chosen by zone and time.

Fix

Build an original traffic fleet (plan in LIFE-06) of 14 archetypes: city hatch, compact and mid sedan, estate, compact crossover, full SUV, pickup, minivan, panel van, box truck, city bus, plus taxi and police variants of the mid sedan. Per car: LOD0 35-60k tris including wheels and a simple interior shell; LOD1 40 %; LOD2 10 %; LOD3 ≤ 2.5k beyond 150 m. Textures: body 2048² BaseColor/Normal/ORM (BC7), a shared 2048² trim sheet (lamps, grilles, plates with original fonts) and shared tyre/rim sets. Use real panel gaps and modelled lamp internals. Glass: alpha 0.25-0.4, with a seated driver on LOD0/1 taken from the LIFE-01 character set. Colour: 8 authored paint variants per model in VeyraMaterialLibrary instead of a MaterialPropertyBlock. Spawn weights by district and hour live in a versioned TrafficProfile ScriptableObject (e.g. D1 taxis 8 %, D3 trucks 20 %, police ≤ 2 %).

Cost
About 15 cars × 35-60k tris within 60 m ≈ 0.5-0.9M tris (estimate), easy on an RTX 4070. Paint variants instead of a MaterialPropertyBlock put traffic back on the SRP Batcher path.
Evidence
ArtSource/Vehicles/generic_*/process_report.json: 10 traffic cars with Body_LOD0 3,856-5,614 tris, 485-614 tris per wheel
ArtSource/Vehicles/{doutrive_78,riverside_88,lct_3000_95,shvan_92,illinois_90,fairheaven_lt,negotiator_80,canyon_75_taxi,fairheaven_cop}/process_report.json: Body_LOD0 15.2k-21.3k tris
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Vehicles/generic_sedan/Materials: 5 materials; no generic_* material has _NORMALMAP; doutrive_78/Textures has no normal map
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Vehicles/generic_sedan/Materials/M_generic_sedan_Glass_5.mat:133,138 transparent black glass (alpha 0.68) with no interior behind it
Assets/_Veyra/Data/GameDatabase.asset:399-418 (19 TrafficModels); Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficSystem.cs:219 uniform random pick
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:65-79 paint tint via MaterialPropertyBlock only on M_Paint*/Traffic_Body* (generic M_Livery_* keep their baked colour)

LIFE-04Traffic slides: wheels never spin or steer, no suspension motion, no indicators high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified, corrected
You see
Moving traffic glides like pieces on rails: wheels stay frozen and straight through turns, bodies never dip under braking or lean in corners, and no indicator blinks before a turn. Not visible in reviews because traffic is off (LIFE-07).
Root cause

TrafficCar is a kinematic point follower that only moves the root transform. It lacks all the secondary motion that tells the eye a car has mass and a driver: wheel spin, steering, pitch, roll and signals. GTA V's ambient vehicles run a simplified vehicle model with spinning, steering wheels, suspension travel and indicators.

Fix

TrafficCar changes:

  1. On Begin, cache Wheel_FL/FR/RL/RR and their radii.
  2. Spin += speed/r·dt. Front steer = atan(wheelbase·κ), clamped to ±35°, with κ from the lane path or the turn Bezier.
  3. A body child gets pitch = clamp(-0.6°·accel, ±2°) and roll = clamp(k·v²·κ, ±2.5°), smoothed over 0.2 s.
  4. Update rate: every frame under 60 m, every 2nd frame at 60-150 m, none beyond.
  5. Amber indicator at 1.5 Hz from 3 s before a turn; add LampFn.Indicator.
  6. Lane changes on multi-lane AR/HW roads when the gap is ≥ 2 s.
  7. Replace the loop at :105-122 with a per-road spatial hash. Run wheel and body transforms via IJobParallelForTransform (Burst).
Cost
Transform writes for ~50 cars with distance-tiered updates < 0.1 ms CPU (estimate). The spatial hash removes the O(n²) car-following cost before density rises (LIFE-07).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs and Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficSystem.cs: grep -i 'wheel' returns 0 hits
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficSystem.cs:223-228 kinematic Rigidbody moved by script
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleFX.cs:32 LampFn { Head, Rear, Reverse, Combined, None }: no indicator
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:23-29 junction turns are Bezier curves; no lane-change state
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:105-122 car following iterates every car (foreach at :108), O(n²)
Tracked as
new
LIFE-05Night car lights: cookie-less raised spot, floating red disc, sprite-only traffic lamps high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
At night no shaped headlamp beam reaches the road. A single round red disc floats on the wet road behind the car, cut off from the tail lamps, and distant traffic shows flat glow sprites.
Root cause

The lamps are stand-ins. Glows are additive billboards rather than emissive lens geometry. The head spot is one non-physical source above the bonnet with a soft round falloff and no cut-off line. The tail spot paints a hot disc instead of the wet road stretching two lamp images. GTA V and Cyberpunk use per-lamp projected lights with cookies (sharp cut-off, hotspot) and HDR emissive lenses, with bloom and long wet-road streaks.

Fix

CarLampFX changes:

  1. Player and AI racers: two spot lights at Lamp_FL/FR (55° outer, 25° inner, range 60 m) with a 256×128 headlamp cookie (flat top cut-off, kerb-side kick-up, hotspot). Soft shadows on the player car only (one 512 px atlas tile). Keep the beam cones faint (alpha ≤ 0.05).
  2. Delete the centred tail spot. Let HDR emissive tail lenses (linear emission 6-15, brake ×3) carry the light through the wet-road reflection. If a spill is wanted, use two 3 m point lights at the lamp positions, intensity ≤ 0.3.
  3. Traffic: RealLightBudget 12 with cookie spots and no shadows (Forward+ clustering); sprites only beyond 130 m.
  4. Split lamp materials by function at import (closes BUG-064).
  5. Make sure VC_Variants covers _EMISSION.

Acceptance: CAM_07 and CAM_13 at 22h_wet.

Cost
Forward+ handles 12-20 unshadowed spots cheaply. One shadowed spot costs one 512² atlas tile, an estimated < 0.3 ms GPU.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_07_DT_Drive_Meridian_Night_22h_wet.png: one centred red disc at ~(1280,1345) of 2560x1440, below the tail lamps (~y 980) with a dark gap; two lamps would mirror as two streaks
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_13_NM_Lumen_Night_22h_wet.png: same centred disc at ~(1250,1355)
Assets/_Veyra/AI/CarLampFX.cs:55-69 one headlight spot for both lamps, raised 0.55 m, 78° cone, no cookie, no shadows; :273 intensity 230 × night
Assets/_Veyra/AI/CarLampFX.cs:70-84 one centred tail spot aimed 28° down, 150° cone, range 8 m (the likely source of the disc)
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficSystem.cs:86-87, 91-109 only the 6 nearest traffic cars get real lights; the rest are camera-facing glows (CarLampFX.cs:9-12)
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:58 BUG-064 shared lamp material: braking also brightens the headlights
Tracked as

The dealership's own cars (Arden Strata, Volkhart 4S, Rourke Hammerhead, Marlowe Sovereign, Castellan Vento R) appear as boxes on cylinder wheels. The Arden Kite is still an untextured interim model.
Root cause

The project has names and handling data for an original fleet but no art for it. Every presentable car is a Sketchfab download, and 22 of the 24 carry real-brand or brand-style origins (LIFE-02, LIFE-10). Without original models there is no legal hero car and no route to AAA traffic.

Original line-up, built in-house or as work-for-hire with full rights (the L-010 pattern):

LIFE-07Streets read empty: review harness disables traffic; density is one global number high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see

Root cause

There are two causes. First, the review harness switches traffic off for repeatability, so the 36 shots per round never show or judge traffic. TrafficSystem turns it into a target for the loaded ring, capped at 90 cars within 300 m on PC High. 3) Set ParkedCars occupancy per district (D1 0.7, D5 0.5, D3 0.3). Log counts per shot to review.txt. Acceptance: ≥ 8 moving cars visible in CAM_06 at noon.
Cost
Car-following is O(n²) (TrafficCar.cs:31). Going from 51 to 90 cars roughly triples that cost (estimate) unless the LIFE-04 spatial hash lands first. Measure FrameCost 'Traffic' in stats.json.

LIFE-08Crowd density, scenarios and rendering tech cannot reach GTA-level street life medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
A downtown avenue at noon shows only 4-5 tiny walkers (CAM_06). Nobody sits on the plaza benches, talks in groups or uses a phone. Reactions are limited to a sideways dive.
Root cause

The population is capped at 85 for the whole 150 m bubble because each walker is a CPU-animated hierarchy of 12 renderers, which does not scale. Behaviour is a path follower with a dodge and no scenario points (benches, shelters, shop doors, phone use, conversation groups). Even the walkers that are present read as moving markers rather than people.

Fix

Use a tiered crowd: (a) Near: ≤ 24 skinned Animator characters within 30 m (LIFE-01). (b) Mid: 60-80 at 30-90 m as LOD1 meshes animated by baked bone or vertex-animation textures, drawn with Graphics.RenderMeshInstanced. No GameObjects; state lives in a NativeArray updated by a Burst IJobParallelFor that reuses the existing anchor/S/lateral logic. (c) Far: 150-250 at 90-200 m as VAT LOD2 meshes or 8-view impostor quads. Scenario points come from an idempotent Editor tool: PlazaBuilder benches, bus shelters and storefront doors from the Veyra placement CSVs. Actions: sit, wait, chat (groups of 2-4), phone, window-shop. Keep 'never run over', but play stumble and flee clips. Targets (estimates to tune): D1/D6 at noon ≥ 120 within 150 m, D5 30-50, D3/D7 ≤ 15.

Cost
Instanced VAT crowds cost about 1 draw per mesh/material/LOD. Estimated 0.2-0.5 ms GPU for 200 people at 1-4k tris, and ~1,000 GameObject renderers go away.
Evidence
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png: walkers at ~(550,736), (909,710), (1485,719), (1754,723) of 2560x1440 along a wide downtown avenue
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_05_DT_Plaza_Closeup_12h.png: about 10 figures at far left, none on the benches
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:21, 24 MaxPeds 85, spawn 30-150 m; :376 DistrictDensity; :156-158 updates every 3rd frame beyond 80 m
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:553-584 12 renderers per walker (~1,020 at 85); :23 comment '70 walkers at ~0.5 ms'
Assets/_Veyra/AI/Pedestrians.cs:10-13 behaviours: stroll, turn, zebra crossing, dive; Docs/WORLD_ITERATION_LOG.md:28 plaza ring strolling
Tracked as
new
LIFE-09Hero paint is spotless uniform clear coat: no flake, dirt or rain layer medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Up close the car paint is one even glossy colour. It has no metallic sparkle, no road grime on sills or diffuser, and no rain beads in the wet night shots, only a clean mirror coat.
Root cause

Imports were converted to Complex Lit with a clear coat, but nothing layers real-world paint response on top: no flake normal (the tool was never run), no dirt or dust masks from AO/curvature, and no wetness or droplet layer driven by WorldEnvironment. Spotless, uniform paint is a classic CG tell. GTA V drives a dirt level per car, and Cyberpunk layers grime and rain beads on car paint.

Fix
  1. Review PaintFlake.cs, extend its filter to M_Livery_* and run Veyra/Vehicles/Apply Paint Flake after LIFE-02's removals. Check sparkle at 1-3 m and no shimmer at 20 m under STP; drop strength from 0.18 to 0.1 if it shimmers. 2) Build a VC_CarPaint Shader Graph as an authored material in VeyraMaterialLibrary: base + clear coat + flake + dirt. Dirt mask = baked AO × curvature (Blender bake, 1024² per car) × _Dirt (0-1, through 8 material variants), plus a world-height dust gradient on the lower body. _Wetness from WorldEnvironment drives a 512² droplet-normal flipbook and darkens the dirt. 3) Bake a 2048² normal from each full-res source onto the 120k LOD0 to recover lost curvature. 4) Add the _WET/_DIRT keywords to VC_Variants.shadervariants.
Cost
Adds 3-4 texture samples on car paint pixels only; negligible at 1440p.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/PaintFlake.cs:16-27: the tool writes Assets/_Veyra/Art/Vehicles/Shared/T_PaintFlake_N.png and filters on M_Paint*. That folder does not exist and 0 of 18 M_Paint*.mat contain _DETAIL_MULX2, so it was never run (the file is untracked)
18 M_Livery_*.mat (all 10 generic traffic cars plus v12_goblin) are excluded by the M_Paint filter
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CarImportTools.cs:240-289 Complex Lit clear coat (mask 1, smoothness 0.85-0.98): a sound base
ArtSource/Vehicles/{stanton_sidewinder,iron_howl,orion_skylark}/process_report.json tris.Body_LOD0 120000 (decimated); ardente_furia also 120000

LIFE-1017 'homage' models came with brand-style badges; 9 of them drive in traffic medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Several 'fictional' cars still read as specific real models. In the garage sheet the white/black two-tone coupe (row 5, tile 1) looks like an AE86, and the yellow chequer-band cab (row 1, tile 4) like a Checker cab.
Root cause

The Zhabotinsky 'low poly' series uses parody names but is modelled on real vehicles; the register itself records that brand-style badges had to be removed. CC-BY covers the mesh, not the maker's design. A low-poly caricature lowers the trade-dress risk but does not remove it, and it conflicts with the CLAUDE.md all-original rule.

Fix

Add these 17 to the BUG-066 legal review. Write a likeness sheet per car from ArtSource/Vehicles/<id>/<id>_preview.png plus front, side and rear renders. Until a decision: keep them out of hero, marketing and default-capture use, and use them as traffic filler only until the LIFE-03/06 originals land. BUG-066
LIFE-11Damage is only vertex dents and cracked glass; no loose panels or paint scuffs low effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 2/5 self-checked
You see
Hard crashes only push the shell inward and crack the glass. Bumpers, bonnets and doors never hang or fall off, and the paint never scrapes, so heavy hits look mild next to GTA V.
Root cause

Damage is one radial vertex push on a CPU copy of the body. The imported cars are single welded shells without separately pivoted panels, and the paint shader has no damage mask. Nothing can swing, detach or scuff.

Fix

Original cars (LIFE-06) get separately pivoted parts: Hood, Trunk, Door_FL/FR/RL/RR, Bumper_F/R and Mirror_L/R, each with UCX_ hulls, exported with VC_Unity_Prop. CarDamage tracks health per part. At 40 % a part swings on a HingeJoint with limits; at 100 % it detaches as pooled rigidbody debris (max 6 live, despawn after 20 s). Move denting to the GPU: a 64×64 RGBA16F offset texture per car, sampled in the VC_CarPaint vertex stage, so meshes can stay Read/Write off. Its alpha holds a scratch mask that exposes primer or metal.

Cost
GPU damage removes the readable mesh copies (24 cars now) and the per-hit mesh uploads.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/CarDamage.cs:10-13 vertex dents on a readable mesh copy; :103-140 glass crack via MaterialPropertyBlock; :197 debris chips
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/CarDamage.cs: no detach, bumper, door or scratch code (grep finds 0 hits)
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Vehicles/*/*.fbx.meta: 24 of 35 import with isReadable: 1 for the dent copy

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Pedestrian LOD0 10-16k tris, skinned, ~60 bones; LOD1 ≈ 40 %; LOD2 ≈ 10 %; VAT or impostor beyond 90 m; ≥ 300 visual combinations. Measure with the LOD stats tool and an EditMode prefab audit.
  • D1/D6 at noon: ≥ 120 pedestrians within 150 m and ≥ 25 visible in CAM_05/CAM_06, at a crowd cost ≤ 1.5 ms CPU (FrameCost 'Pedestrians') and ≤ 1.0 ms GPU at 1440p. Measure with stats.json and a Profiler capture.
  • Animation: foot sliding ≤ 5 cm per step at LOD0; ≥ 12 locomotion/idle clips and ≥ 6 scenario types in use. Measure with a debug overlay and a CAM_05 frame sequence.
  • Traffic, with -reviewTraffic: D1 at noon 30-45 moving cars within 200 m and ≥ 8 visible in CAM_06; D5 8-15; highways 25-40. Measure from counts logged to review.txt/stats.json.
  • Traffic models: ≥ 14 original archetypes; LOD0 35-60k tris including wheels, interior and driver; normal and ORM maps; wheels spin and steer; indicators work. Measure with process_report.json and an EditMode catalog test.
  • Hero car: LOD0 150-250k tris; clear coat + flake + dirt/wet layers; cookie-projected twin headlamps; emissive lenses split per function. Measure with a close-up review camera at 12h and 22h_wet.
  • Legal: 0 GameDatabase.Cars/TrafficModels entries whose register row says 'RESEMBLES A REAL CAR', and 0 brand-word hits in Art/Vehicles file names or FBX strings. Measure with the LicenceGateTests EditMode test and a register audit.
  • Night: no detached light discs, and the headlamp pool has a sharp cut-off and reaches 25-40 m. Measure with CAM_07/CAM_13 at 22h_wet.

Assets to make or source

  • Realistic rigged pedestrians: Microsoft Rocketbox Avatar Library (MIT licence; 115 rigged characters with LODs) as the first set. Add the licence file to ArtSource/Downloads and register rows. Add commissioned or Character Creator characters later for variety, after checking the vendor's game-export licence.
  • Animation: Mixamo locomotion, idle, phone, sit, umbrella and flee clips (royalty-free in games under Adobe's terms; no standalone redistribution) and CMU Graphics Lab mocap (free including commercial use, no resale), retargeted to a Unity Humanoid avatar.
  • Original vehicle fleet: 8 hero cars (Kite, Kite R, Strata, Volkhart 4S, Hammerhead, Sovereign, Vento R, an EV hypercar), 14 traffic archetypes and 6 service vehicles. Built in-house in Blender or as work-for-hire with a full rights transfer (L-010 pattern), each with a likeness sign-off.
  • Seated driver and passenger meshes (LOD0/1) from the same character set, for traffic and the player car.
  • Headlamp cookie textures (256×128, made in-house) and per-function emissive lens masks (head, tail, brake, indicator, reverse).
  • Paint flake normal (generated by the PaintFlake tool), a 512² rain-droplet normal flipbook, and per-car AO/curvature dirt bakes (Blender).
  • Original tyre set with fictional sidewall lettering (2048² normal + ORM) and 6-8 original rim designs.
  • Baked VAT/bone-animation textures and impostor atlases for mid and far crowds, generated by an idempotent Editor tool.

13. Performance, pipeline and development process

Score today: 3.5 / 10   Good foundations (perf harness with stats.json and verify, time-sliced cell streaming, HLOD v1, PSO warm-up), but four things leave almost no budget for AAA content: draws are submitted from a CPU-bound main thread with no GPU Resident Drawer or occlusion, street content is generated at runtime, texture streaming is off, and the LOD bias drift plus uncontrolled perf runs make the measurements unreliable.

Current budget, from iteration 21 (Logs/ITER/21/*/stats.json; Dev Mono build, 2560x1440, PC_High at 0.9 render scale with STP upscaling, RTX 4070 Laptop, D3D12, with traffic, parked cars and audio switched OFF):

  • GPU: p95 7.7-8.0 ms against a 13 ms budget, so about 5 ms (40 %) headroom.
  • CPU main thread: p95 8.8-8.9 ms against 10 ms, so about 1 ms headroom. p99 is 14-25 ms.
  • Draw calls: average 2.4-4.3k, p95 4.7-6.6k, max 10.4k. SetPass p95 is 440-486 against a 450 budget.
  • Triangles: p95 3.2-4.8 M, max 9.0 M, against 8 M.
  • Memory: 5.5 GB of VRAM in use (of 8 GB on the laptop GPU), 2.6 GB reserved RAM.
  • Hitches: R_BRIDGES had 86 frames over 50 ms.

These numbers are optimistic for three reasons:

  • The PC_High LOD bias was silently changed from 2 to 0.4 in uncommitted ProjectSettings, so objects drop to low LODs about 5x closer and the triangle counts are understated.
  • Gameplay systems were off during every perf run.
  • The last Release IL2CPP measurement (2026-11-10) predates streaming, the new roads and the kits.

Verdict: the GPU can afford AAA materials, lighting and higher-poly LODs. The CPU and memory cannot, because the architecture does too much on the CPU:

  • Draws are submitted from the CPU. The VC_* shaders are not DOTS-instanced, so the GPU Resident Drawer is off, and there is no occlusion culling.
  • Street dressing is generated at runtime in code from primitives, plus whole-scene scans on every cell load (BUG-112).
  • Vegetation goes through a managed per-camera instancing loop with 2 LODs and 448-triangle trees.
  • Texture streaming is off and every generated mesh is Read/Write.
  • The streaming window keeps about 20 of 48 cells, plus terrain and props, resident.
  • Buildings are C#-extruded shells merged per 250 m chunk with a single LOD (BUG-038). Only 70 of 2,708 buildings use kits.

Order of work:

  1. Restore the LOD bias and re-measure in a Release IL2CPP build with gameplay systems on and the laptop on a performance power plan.
  2. Move to GPU-driven rendering: DOTS-instanced VC shaders, GPU Resident Drawer, GPU occlusion.
  3. Bake the runtime dressing in idempotent editor tools.
  4. Turn on texture streaming and make generated meshes non-readable.
  5. Build HLOD v2 and tree impostors.
  6. Build a Blender kit/trim-sheet pipeline with automated LODs and a content validator.

Process changes: one git worktree per session, perf gates that fail the card, stable GUIDs in the builders, and review captures at 6 lighting states with automatic image checks instead of noon and 22:00 only.

Findings and fixes (14)

ENG-02CPU-bound draw submission: no GPU Resident Drawer, no occlusion culling, 4-7k draw calls critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Not visible yet. But every AAA addition (denser props, per-building meshes, traffic) pushes frames past 16.6 ms. R_BRIDGES already has p99 20.7 ms and 86 frames over 50 ms.
Root cause

Every visible renderer is culled and submitted on the CPU. The GPU has about 5 ms of its 13 ms budget left, but the main thread has about 1 ms of its 10 ms, and that was measured without traffic, parked cars or audio. The custom VC_* shaders lack DOTS instancing, so the GPU Resident Drawer (BatchRendererGroup) cannot draw city materials. With no Umbra or GPU occlusion, Downtown towers hidden behind other towers are still drawn. GTA V and Cyberpunk rely on occlusion plus GPU-side instance submission for exactly this.

Fix
  1. Make VC_Facade/Road/Water/Neon/GlowPool BRG-compatible: '#pragma target 4.5', '#pragma multi_compile _ DOTS_INSTANCING_ON', all properties inside CBUFFER UnityPerMaterial, UNITY_DOTS_INSTANCING_START(MaterialPropertyMetadata) for per-instance overrides, UNITY_SETUP_INSTANCE_ID in every pass (including ShadowCaster and DepthNormals). Add the DOTS variants to VC_Variants.shadervariants. 2) Set GraphicsSettings BatchRendererGroup variants to Keep All. On the PC URP assets: GPU Resident Drawer = Instanced Drawing, GPU Occlusion Culling on, small-mesh screen percentage 0.5 %. Mobile stays off. 3) Replace MaterialPropertyBlocks on static renderers (the WaterSurface calm block) with material variants, because GRD skips renderers that use them. 4) Split the 250 m merged building chunks into per-block renderers of 60 m or less so occlusion can reject them. 5) Re-measure in Release IL2CPP with traffic on.
Cost
Expected to cut CPU culling and submission substantially (Unity reports large savings in scenes with many objects; the size for Veyra is unmeasured). GPU occlusion removes hidden towers in D1/D6. The depth pyramid costs about 0.2-0.4 ms GPU (estimate).
Evidence
Logs/ITER/21/Dev_R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT/stats.json render block: CPU main avg 4.57 / p95 8.87 / p99 14.43 ms; GPU p95 7.97 ms; draw calls avg 4,292 / p95 6,554 / max 10,428; SetPass p95 486 (budget 450)
Logs/ITER/21/Dev_R_BRIDGES/verify.txt: CPU main p95 8.76 / p99 24.87 ms, GPU p95 7.65 ms, triangles max 9,005,888, hitches 86
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_URP_PC_High.asset:85 m_GPUResidentDrawerMode: 0 and :88 GPU occlusion 0 (same in all 5 URP assets); :72 SRP Batcher on. ProjectSettings/GraphicsSettings.asset:59 m_BrgStripping: 0
Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders/VC_Facade.shader:56,70 use '#pragma target 3.5' and multi_compile_instancing only; no VC_* shader (Road, Water, Neon, GlowPool, SkyDome) declares DOTS_INSTANCING (BUG-115, Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:79)
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:78 BUG-114: no occlusion culling in D1/D6
Tools/World/card_checks.sh:46 and Tools/World/iterate.sh:48 run every route with -veyraNoTraffic -veyraNoParked -veyraMute, so real CPU load is higher

BUG-115
ENG-03Street dressing is generated at runtime in code: primitives, scene scans, material edits high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Hydrants, bins, rooftop units, traffic lights and pedestrians look like toy primitives (boxes, spheres, cylinders). Frames stall when cells stream in. The player build differs from the editor (the BUG-123 frame exists only in the player).
Root cause

Visual iterations added content as MonoBehaviours that build geometry, textures and material values at boot or on cell load, because that was faster than an authored asset path. The result: no authored meshes, texture sets or LODs for these props (they read as programmer art), main-thread spikes on every cell load, and look values that exist only at runtime. GTA V and Cyberpunk place authored prop prefabs offline and stream them with the map; at runtime only behaviour runs (signal phases, pedestrian AI).

Fix

Turn StreetFurniture, RooftopDetails and TrafficLights (geometry part) into idempotent editor bakers run by WorldBuildBatch. They write per-chunk merged meshes or prefab instances with LOD0/LOD1 and UBX/UCX colliders into the cell scenes via CellDistributor. At runtime keep only signal phase and pedestrian AI. Replace primitive and procedural meshes with authored Blender kit props: shared 2048² atlas, 0.5-5k triangles, LOD0+LOD1. Bake the RoadWear values into the M_Asphalt_* assets, and stop the UV1 rewrite once every road mesh comes from RoadRenderBuilder; this also removes RoadWear's need for readable meshes, which ENG-07 requires. Assign the water material in the builder and delete the WaterSurface scan. Route any remaining cell work through a 1 ms/frame queue. Gate: CellLoaded callbacks max 2 ms or less.

Cost
Removes about 0.84 ms/frame average (RoadWear on DOWNTOWN_NIGHT, from IterCost) and the 15-31 ms streaming spikes. Authored props add GPU triangles, but those go through the GPU Resident Drawer and LODs. Baked content adds disk size per cell.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/GameFlow.cs:159-181: WorldMap, WaterSurface, RoadWear, TrafficLights, StreetTrees, SkyDome, Pedestrians, StreetFurniture and RooftopDetails are added with AddComponent at boot (44 AddComponent calls in the file)
Runtime meshes: AI/TrafficLights.cs:121-126 (PrimitiveMesh cylinder/cube combined; runtime particle material copy) and :148 (CreatePrimitive); AI/RooftopDetails.cs:380 and AI/StreetFurniture.cs:557 (ToMesh builders); AI/Pedestrians.cs:668-674 (procedural box/rounded body parts)
Gameplay/WaterSurface.cs:15 subscribes to CellLoaded, and :29 runs FindObjectsByType<MeshRenderer>(Include) over the whole scene with a material-name compare on every cell load
Gameplay/RoadWear.cs:47-52 scans only the new scene's roots; :79-89 SetFloat/SetTexture on the shared M_Asphalt materials; ~:159 SetUVs(1), which Editor/RoadRenderBuilder.cs:606/669 already writes
World/Streaming/CellStreamer.cs:313 invokes every CellLoaded subscriber synchronously in one frame
Logs/ITER/21/Dev_R_BRIDGES/player.log:29974 [IterCost]: CellLoaded callbacks max 31.3 ms (9 frames over 8 ms), TrafficLights max 15.1 ms. DOWNTOWN_NIGHT: RoadWear avg 0.84 ms
Logs/PROF/smoke.txt:7 RoadWear.Update 3,954 ms self time over 2,000 frames (06:49 profile, Silent power plan)
