BUG-112
ENG-04Buildings are C#-extruded shells merged per 250 m chunk with one LOD; only 214 of 2,708 use kits high effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
Facades are flat walls with a painted window grid and no depth (CAM_06 left tower). Blocks look extruded and repeated, and they cannot gain detail without the cost exploding.
Root cause

The city's buildings come from a C# extrusion generator that bakes footprints into chunk meshes with tiled facade textures. Placement is data-accurate, but there is no silhouette, no depth (window recesses, ledges, AC units), no per-building LOD or culling, and the repetition shows. GTA V and Cyberpunk build from modular kits and trim sheets on authored lots, with a LOD chain per building plus HLOD.

Fix

Keep the Veyra data as the authority for footprint, height and ground level, but output kits:

  • In Blender, author modular facade sets per district: bays 3-4 m wide, floors 3.2-4 m high, corners, shopfronts, cornices and parapets. Use 2-3 shared 4096² trim sheets (albedo/normal/ORM, BC7) at a texel density of 512 px/m, with window recesses of 0.15-0.3 m and an interior-mapping glass shader.
  • vc_make_lods plus a validator produce LOD1 (about 40 %) and LOD2 (about 10 %).
  • BuildingAssembler snaps modules to each footprint and gives every building its own LODGroup, which feeds the GPU Resident Drawer (ENG-02) and HLOD v2 (ENG-06).
  • Budgets: mid-rise LOD0 20-60k triangles, towers 60-150k.
  • A content validator (triangle budgets, LOD ratios, texel density, BC7, Read/Write off, UCX/UBX naming) runs on import.
  • Keep NYC/SF-derived pieces out of shipping content.
Cost
Per-building renderers raise the object count, which is fine with the GPU Resident Drawer and occlusion. Triangles in D1 views rise (estimate 2-4x) and need the LOD chain to stay under 8 M p95. Trim sheets keep SetPass low (2-3 materials per district).
Editor note
The kit count is 214 of 2,708 (208 of them stretched) since world iteration 1, not 70; see BLD-01/BLD-12. Counted once in the plan with BLD-01.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BuildingGenerator.cs:607-624: one mesh per (250 m chunk, material) and a single LODGroup level (LOD0 culled at 0.15 % screen height), with no LOD1/LOD2. BUG-038 is still open (Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:29)
Docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md:45: 70 of 2,708 buildings are kit models. The kits come from 12 CC-BY packs, including NYC/SF packs (risk of real-landmark lookalikes); the Cyberpunk and Japanese-street packs are on hold
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png: the left tower (x~0-1126, y~0-755 at full res) is a flat brick plane with the same window tile on every floor: no sills, recesses or cornices
Tools/Blender has no building, kit or trim-sheet tooling. vc_tools.py covers export, pivots and vc_make_lods (:155) only
Tracked as
BUG-038
ENG-05Vegetation renderer: CPU loop per camera, 2 hard-switched LODs, 448-triangle trees, no impostors high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Trees are identical dark cones and low-poly blobs (CAM_09 pine rows), they snap between two LODs, and shadows from trees just outside the view vanish.
Root cause

The renderer was built for instance count, not fidelity. Managed per-camera loops cap how much geometry and how many LOD levels it can afford, so trees stayed at about 450 triangles with two LODs, no crossfade and no impostors. AAA vegetation (GTA V, Cyberpunk) uses 10-30k-triangle trees near the camera, 3-4 LODs, dithered crossfades and impostor billboards in the distance, with GPU or job-based culling so density stays cheap.

Fix

Replace the per-cell RenderMeshInstanced loop with GPU-driven submission. Either (a) bake trees near roads into the cell scenes as MeshRenderer+LODGroup instances, so the GPU Resident Drawer culls and LODs them (after ENG-02), or (b) use Graphics.RenderMeshIndirect with a compute pass for frustum, Hi-Z occlusion and LOD selection, plus a light-frustum pass for shadow casters. Per species:

  • LOD0 8-25k triangles (foliage cards), LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %.
  • LOD3: octahedral impostor, 8x8 views, 2048² albedo plus normal/depth, BC7.
  • Dithered crossfade through the LOD_FADE_CROSSFADE path the VC shaders already compile.

If selection stays on the CPU, run it in a Burst IJobParallelFor. Reflection probes render the impostor level only.

Cost
Higher-poly trees cost GPU time (estimate +0.5-1.5 ms at 1440p with impostors beyond about 150 m). GPU culling removes the managed loop and fixes missing off-screen shadows. Alpha-test overdraw must be measured.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/World/InstancedPropRenderer.cs:84-116: a per-camera C# loop over all cells, including reflection-probe cameras. Only whole cells are frustum-tested (:97). Two LOD levels with a hard switch (:98, LodDistance 120 m). Shadows only from cells inside the view and within 120 m (:102)
Logs/B1/trees.txt: VC_Tree_Broadleaf LOD0 448 tris, VC_Tree_Pine LOD0 471 / LOD2 42, VC_Tree_Palm 270
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:33 BUG-043: 67,499 trees and 2,777 lamps go through this renderer
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_09_BR_Overlook_Golden_12h.png: mid-ground pines (y~770-1280 at full res) are repeated dark cones with identical silhouettes; distant hillside trees are flat green specks
Logs/PROF/smoke.txt: Inl_RenderPipeline.BeginCameraRendering 1,669 ms self time over 2,000 frames; this callback runs there (attribution is an estimate; the profile was taken on the Silent power plan)
No runtime asmdef references Burst or Collections (for example Assets/_Veyra/World/VeyraCity.World.asmdef)
Tracked as
new
ENG-06The far field is boxes on a 3.9 m/px ground texture: HLOD is too coarse for skyline views high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
From the hills or the sea, the city beyond 1.6 km is pale grey boxes with no windows or roof detail, sitting on a blurry ground texture (CAM_09 horizon, CAM_03 right side).
Root cause

HLOD v1 was sized as a placeholder (30k triangles or fewer per cell, one shared 2048² atlas) and has no baked lighting or window detail. The skyline is the main scale cue in an open world. GTA V uses hand-built low-detail meshes per block with baked colour textures; Cyberpunk uses proxy meshes with emissive window atlases. Both keep distance reading as a dense, lit city.

Fix

HLOD v2 in HlodBuilder:

  • HLOD1, shown from 1.0 to 2.5 km, 80k triangles or fewer per cell: each building's LOD2 plus roof clutter, merged per material.
  • HLOD2, beyond 2.5 km, 20k triangles or fewer: simplified hulls.
  • Bake each building's facade into one BC7 2048² atlas per cell (RGB albedo with baked AO, A = window-emissive mask for night), rendered orthographically from four sides in the editor; about 0.5-1 m per pixel on facades.
  • Ground atlas: 1024 px per cell (about 1 m per pixel).
  • Crossfade between HLOD and loaded cells with LOD_FADE_CROSSFADE dithering instead of a hard proxy toggle.

Outputs stay idempotent and gitignored. Validate on CAM_03, CAM_09 and CAM_11 at four lighting states.

Cost
HLOD triangles rise from 166k to roughly 1-2 M map-wide (estimate), but they replace loaded cells, so LoadRadius can shrink (ENG-07). 48 atlases at 2048² BC7 with mips come to about 256 MB, so they should be streamed.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/HlodBuilder.cs:16-23 and :31-32: a 33x33 ground grid with 256 px per 1 km cell (about 3.9 m per pixel). Cells whose buildings exceed 27k triangles fall back to boxes. Shadows off
Logs/A2/distribute.txt:4: 48 proxies, 165,744 triangles in total, 936 buildings as boxes
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_09_BR_Overlook_Golden_12h.png: pale translucent boxes on the left horizon (x~230-666, y~666-698 at full res); Westhaven reads as orange dots (x~1600-2560, y~700-780)
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_03_DT_Skyline_Golden_12h.png: featureless grey block right of centre (x~1766-1958, y~685-838)
Tracked as
new
ENG-07Streaming keeps about 20 of 48 cells plus terrain and props resident; no texture streaming; meshes are Read/Write high effort M (1-3 days) impact 4/5 verified
You see
No direct symptom yet, but there is no memory left for AAA texture sets: blockout-level content already uses 5.5 GB of the laptop GPU's 8 GB.
Root cause

Streaming was added in A2 to remove load hitches, not to save memory. The window is large, the heaviest systems stay global, and every generated mesh keeps a CPU copy. Without mip streaming, every texture's full mip chain is resident. AAA open worlds stream by distance and priority (GTA V streams per map block; Cyberpunk streams sectors plus texture mips), so memory follows what is near the player, not the whole map.

Fix
  1. Mip streaming: PC_High on with a 2,048 MB budget (Ultra 3,072, Medium 1,536), plus an AssetPostprocessor that sets streamingMipmaps on every texture.
  2. Clear Read/Write on generated meshes after CreateAsset (SerializedObject m_IsReadable = 0). Keep an allow-list for meshes runtime code must read; collision is prebaked already.
  3. Stream terrain tiles and instanced-prop cells with their 1 km cells. Cut LoadRadius to 1,000-1,200 m once HLOD v2 covers the distance.
  4. Install Memory Profiler and snapshot at 0 and 10 minutes per route (BUG-113).
  5. Add one Addressables group per cell once cells pass about 50 MB.

Gate: gfx memory 4.5 GB or less on the laptop with current content.

Cost
Mip streaming costs about 0.1 ms CPU (estimate) and can show blurry pop-in if the budget is too low. Non-readable meshes free CPU RAM roughly equal to their GPU size (estimate: several hundred MB).
Evidence
Logs/ITER/21/Dev_R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT/stats.json: memory gfx 5,527 MB, total_reserved 2,643 MB; streaming cells_loaded_end 20
Assets/_Veyra/World/Streaming/CellStreamer.cs:43: LoadRadius 1600 m / UnloadRadius 2500 m (about a 5x5 window of 1 km cells)
Logs/A2/distribute.txt:2: 'World groups kept in the main scene: Terrain, Sea, Props', so terrain and the 67k instanced props never stream
ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:207-209: PC_High streamingMipmapsActive 0, budget 512. All 764 texture .meta files have streamingMipmaps 0 and maxTextureSize 2048
m_IsReadable: 1 in 200/200 sampled Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Roads/*.asset and 300/300 sampled Generated/Meshes/*.asset; the Roads folder is 819 MB on disk. ProjectSettings.asset:101 bakeCollisionMeshes 1
Packages/manifest.json has no com.unity.addressables (BUG-116) and no com.unity.memoryprofiler (blocks the BUG-113 memory pass)
Tracked as
BUG-116
ENG-08Perf numbers are unreliable: Dev Mono builds, gameplay systems off, laptop power plan, perf gates skipped high effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Nothing directly. But decisions about how much detail the game can afford rest on numbers that swing 2-3x between runs (31-48 FPS versus 94-101 FPS).
Root cause

The harness itself is good (FrameTimingManager, ProfilerRecorder, IterCost). What is not controlled is the context: development Mono builds, gameplay systems turned off, an unchecked laptop power state and background processes, varying vsync, and a perf gate that never runs. That is how regressions such as BUG-120 and the lodBias drop passed silently.

Fix

Add a perf profile to iterate.sh and card_checks.sh: Release IL2CPP build, traffic, parked cars and audio on, vsync 0, 2560x1440 PC_High, the three routes plus a 5-minute free-roam loop. Script pre-flight checks:

  • powercfg /getactivescheme reports a performance scheme (or the OEM performance mode is on), and the laptop is on AC.
  • No other VeyraCity.exe is running.

Write the power scheme, lodBias, render scale, upscaler, git hash and a dirty flag into stats.json. In verify_run.ps1, perf rules fail the card on release runs, and any frame over 50 ms fails in every build. Rebuild Resources/PSO/VC_PSO_Direct3D12.graphicsstate (built 06:19, before iterations 1-22) in the same nightly run.

Cost
No runtime cost. IL2CPP usually lowers CPU time compared with Mono (unmeasured on current content); traffic and audio will raise it, which is the point of measuring them.
Editor note
Only the CPU timings were distorted (Silent power plan, BUG-120). GPU ms, draw and SetPass counts in Logs/C1/*/stats.json are usable, but come from a laptop RTX 4070.
Evidence

ENG-01PC_High LOD bias silently dropped from 2 to 0.4 (uncommitted) medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
Buildings, roads and kit models drop to their coarsest LOD or vanish about five times closer than designed. Mid-distance blocks read as flat grey boxes and detail pops in late.

It affects LODGroups with more than one level (70 kit buildings, imported FBX props and vehicles) and culls small single-LOD renderers about 2.5x earlier. Re-run the routes and the review captures as the new baseline.

ENG-09The render pipeline lacks the passes AAA looks depend on: decals, SSR, volumetric fog medium effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 self-checked
You see
Wet Neon Mile streets barely reflect anything: signs and lit shopfronts do not mirror in the road (CAM_13 wet). There are no road decals, and no fog glow or light shafts around the neon.
Root cause

URP provides SSAO and decals but no screen-space reflections or volumetric fog, and the project has added only SSAO. Cyberpunk's wet night is mostly SSR on low-roughness asphalt plus volumetric scattering around emissives. GTA V's daytime street realism leans on decals and contact AO. The glow-pool quads fake light on the ground, but they cannot reflect geometry.

Fix

Add Render Graph renderer features on the PC tiers, together with the lighting track:

  1. URP Decal feature (DBuffer, normal blend) for road and sidewalk decals: up to 2,000 projectors per view, one 4096² BC7 atlas.
  2. A custom SSR pass: half-resolution hierarchical ray march on the Hi-Z pyramid (shared with GPU occlusion), roughness 0.3 or below, falling back to the box probes, with temporal accumulation from STP motion vectors. Enabled for wet weather and night only.
  3. Froxel volumetric fog (160x90x64) lit by the sun plus the nearest 32 Forward+ lights, temporally reprojected.

Give each pass a quality toggle and a GPU timing marker written to stats.json. Validate on CAM_07 and CAM_13 wet.

Cost
Estimates at 1440p on a 4070 Laptop: decals 0.3-0.6 ms, half-res SSR 1.0-1.8 ms, volumetric fog 0.8-1.5 ms. Together they fit the roughly 5 ms of GPU headroom only alongside GPU occlusion and the lodBias re-baseline; measure them with the markers.
Editor note
URP 6.6 ships decals but not SSR or volumetric fog; see the Unity 6.6 feature table in the research chapter.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Settings/VC_Renderer_PC_High.asset:28 lists one renderer feature, ScreenSpaceAmbientOcclusion (:68). There is no Decal feature (BUG-118), no SSR and no volumetric pass
Assets/_Veyra/World/ReflectionProbeDirector.cs:31: realtime probes at 128 px (256 on Ultra), rendered one face per frame

ENG-10Process: two sessions share one working tree, 86 uncommitted entries, reviews cover only 2 lighting states medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 4/5 self-checked
You see

Root cause

The workflow is optimised for speed (22 iterations in a day) over isolation and review depth. Two agents share one working tree and ProjectSettings, each commit bundles several iterations, and the review gate only proves that a file was written. Noon light is the least flattering; AAA art reviews judge golden hour, overcast, dusk and night.

  • In ReviewCameraRig, fail a frame when its luminance standard deviation is below 0.03 or more than 85 % of its pixels are within 5 % of the fog colour.
  • Produce an A/B diff against the last accepted set.
  • Keep the unity-mcp package out of the game manifest, or pin it to a commit hash.

ENG-11Editor builders delete and recreate assets: GUID churn and Git LFS bloat medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Nothing in game. For the team, every world rebuild rewrites thousands of assets, breaks references, and hides in git what actually changed.
Root cause

The builders were written as 'clean regenerate' instead of 'update in place'. Scene objects are idempotent, as CLAUDE.md requires, but asset identity is not. Each new GUID orphans references held by other scenes and prefabs, invalidates the import cache, and stores a new LFS object even when the mesh did not change.

Fix

Add VeyraAssetWriter.SaveMesh(path, mesh): when the asset exists, Clear() it and copy the vertices, indices, UVs and bounds (or use EditorUtility.CopySerialized) and SetDirty it, so the GUID is kept. Skip the write when a content hash stored in userData is unchanged. Delete only assets whose source item is gone. Use it in CityBuilder, BuildingGenerator, RoadRenderBuilder and the other builders. Decide per folder whether to commit generated output (stable GUIDs, hashed) or gitignore it and regenerate in BuildScript, as is already done for Cells and HLOD. Add an EditMode test that runs WorldBuildBatch twice and asserts 0 GUID changes and 0 modified files.

Cost
No runtime effect. Shorter reimports, less LFS growth, and incremental rebuilds of only the changed chunks.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/CityBuilder.cs:634 deletes every Generated/Meshes/*.asset before recreating them; :1000-1001 Save() is DeleteAsset followed by CreateAsset
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/BuildingGenerator.cs:600 deletes all Meshes/Buildings assets on every run; 33 editor tools call AssetDatabase.DeleteAsset/CreateAsset

ENG-12God classes: GameFlow (128 KB) boots the world, and Gameplay references every assembly medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Nothing directly. It slows every visual upgrade and causes merge conflicts between the two parallel sessions.
Root cause

Features were appended to whichever class already had the context. Systems are created in code instead of being authored in scenes or prefabs, so their settings live in C# constants, cannot be previewed in the editor, and every change recompiles and touches the same file that both sessions edit.

Split GameFlow into WorldBoot, SessionFlow (free roam and races), GarageFlow, MenuFlow and SettingsService (about 300-500 lines each). Add a VeyraCity.World.Dressing asmdef (props, rooftops, signals) that references World only; AI keeps traffic and pedestrians. Replace FindObjectsByType scans with registries that CellContent fills on load. Move the map overlays to an editor bake. Cost
Neutral to slightly positive: fewer scene scans and faster domain reloads during iteration.

ENG-M2Unity terrain-detail grass generates patch meshes on the main thread, causing 50-165 ms frames medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 added by verifier
You see
Stutter while driving past grassy terrain; grass pops in at a 120 m ring.
Root cause

Grass uses Unity's legacy terrain detail system. It builds patch meshes as the camera moves and waits on them, so cost scales with density and distance. The power plan made it worse but is not the only cause. AAA games draw grass with GPU-instanced or compute-generated clumps from a density texture.

Fix

Re-profile on a performance power plan. If DetailMesh frames over 8 ms remain, replace terrain details with a GPU-instanced grass renderer: per-cell density mask from GrassMasks, compute or Burst culling, 2-3 clump LODs, dithered fade at 80-150 m. Its material comes through VeyraMaterialLibrary, and it streams with its 1 km cell. Gate: 0 frames over 50 ms on R_SMOKE and R_BRIDGES with grass on.

Cost
Removes the main-thread mesh generation spikes. GPU-instanced grass costs about 0.5-1 ms GPU at 1440p (estimate, needs overdraw measurement).
Editor note
Same issue as TER-07; counted once.
Evidence
Logs/PROF/smoke.txt:34-42: the worst frames are dominated by DetailMesh.MeshGenerationJob 51-164 ms per frame (06:49 profile, Silent power plan)
Assets/_Veyra/Editor/GrassBuilder.cs:35 and :55-56: terrain details in InstanceCountMode, detailObjectDistance 120, density 1
Logs/A2/distribute.txt:2: Terrain stays in the main scene and is never streamed
Docs/BUG_TRACKER.md:84 BUG-120 names the grass mesh generation as a cause

ENG-M1Realtime reflection probes switched on in uncommitted ProjectSettings, cost unmeasured medium effort S (< 1 day) impact 2/5 added by verifier

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • Restore PC_High lodBias to 2 (ProjectSettings/QualitySettings.asset:203). Make the WorldBuildBatch camshot step restore the value it changes, and log lodBias in DeviceReport and stats.json. About 1 hour plus a re-capture.
  • Run one Release IL2CPP baseline with traffic, parked cars and audio on, a performance power plan, AC power, vsync 0 and no other VeyraCity.exe running. Make verify_run.ps1 fail on any frame over 50 ms. About 2 hours.
  • Turn on texture mip streaming (PC_High budget 2,048 MB) and add an AssetPostprocessor that sets streamingMipmaps on all textures. About 3 hours.
  • RoadWear: write its _TyreWear, _PatchAmount and _DetailMap values into the M_Asphalt_* assets and delete the runtime UV1 rewrite, which RoadRenderBuilder already bakes. Saves about 0.84 ms/frame average on DOWNTOWN_NIGHT.
  • WaterSurface: assign the water material in the builder and remove the FindObjectsByType scan that runs on every CellLoaded. About 1-2 hours.
  • Clear Read/Write on generated meshes after CreateAsset, with an allow-list for meshes runtime code must read. Half a day including a regression run.
  • Add review capture times 07:30, 17:45, 19:30 and 21:00 dry to iterate.sh, plus a luminance-variance check in ReviewCameraRig so fog-wall frames such as CAM_01 fail. Half a day.
  • Give the world and vehicle sessions separate git worktrees, and have each owner commit or revert the 86 pending entries (ProjectSettings and manifest reviewed explicitly). About 1-2 hours.
  • Rebuild the PSO collection (Resources/PSO/VC_PSO_Direct3D12.graphicsstate, last built 06:19) from fresh route and review traces. About 1 hour.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Frame time: Release IL2CPP, PC_High, 2560x1440, desktop RTX 4070 (laptop as the floor), traffic, parked cars and audio on, routes R_SMOKE/R_BRIDGES/R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT plus 5 minutes of free roam: p99 16.6 ms or less and 0 frames over 50 ms outside teleport windows. Measured by stats.json and verify_run.ps1, with perf rules failing the card.
  • Headroom after the AAA content lands: GPU p95 11 ms or less and CPU main thread p95 7 ms or less, keeping at least 2 ms in reserve on each. Measured by the FrameTimingManager values in the stats.json render block.
  • Draw submission: SetPass p95 300 or less, draw calls p95 2,500 or less, and at least 90 % of static renderers drawn by the GPU Resident Drawer, with GPU occlusion on. Measured by the ProfilerRecorder counters and the GPU Resident Drawer statistics in the Rendering Debugger.
  • Streaming: CellLoaded callbacks max 2 ms or less ([IterCost] in player.log), streaming_hitches_over_50ms = 0 on R_BRIDGES at 90 km/h, LoadRadius 1,200 m or less with HLOD v2 beyond. Measured by player.log and the stats.json streaming block.
  • Memory: GPU memory 6 GB or less on the 8 GB laptop with a 2 GB mip-streaming budget; only allow-listed meshes are Read/Write; reserved memory grows 50 MB or less over 10 minutes. Measured by Memory Profiler snapshots at 0 and 10 minutes (BUG-113).
  • Content LOD: 0 single-level LODGroups and 0 renderers wider than 250 m. Every building and prop has LOD0, LOD1 (about 40 %) and LOD2 (about 10 %), plus an impostor or HLOD level. Trees LOD0 8-25k triangles; mid-rise buildings LOD0 20-60k. Measured by the content validator report on every build.
  • Pipeline: two consecutive WorldBuildBatch runs change 0 GUIDs and 0 files; a full world rebuild takes 15 minutes or less; one iteration loop takes 30 minutes or less. Measured by an EditMode test and the step timings in Logs/ITER/*/.
  • Review: 18 cameras x 6 lighting states (07:30, overcast noon, 17:45, 19:30, 21:00 dry, 22:00 wet) captured from a Release build, with every frame passing the automated image check. Measured by review.txt and the contact sheet.

Assets to make or source

  • Modular facade kits per district (bays, corners, shopfronts, cornices, parapets, roof clutter) on 2-3 shared 4096² trim sheets (albedo/normal/ORM, BC7), authored originally in Blender with source .blend files in ArtSource/Blender. Retire the NYC/SF-derived CC-BY kit pieces from shipping content.
  • Tree set (3 broadleaf, 2 pine, palm, bush): LOD0 8-25k triangles, LOD1 about 40 %, LOD2 about 10 %, and an octahedral impostor (8x8 views, 2048² albedo plus normal/depth). Make them with an upgraded vc_trees.py or from CC0 sources (for example Poly Haven), with licence files in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • Street prop kit to replace the runtime primitives (hydrant, bin, meter, mailbox, bench, bus shelter, signal mast and heads, AC unit, water tank, stair housing): 0.3-5k triangles each, LOD0+LOD1, UBX/UCX collision, one shared 2048² atlas. Original models.
  • Pedestrian set: 4-6 base bodies of 10-20k triangles with LOD1/LOD2, a shared skeleton and walk/idle/dodge clips. Original or CC0 (for example MakeHuman exports) with licence files; check Adobe's terms before using any Mixamo content.
  • Road and sidewalk decal atlas (cracks, patches, manholes, oil, tyre marks, gum, drains), 4096² BC7. Original or CC0 (for example ambientCG) with licence files.
  • HLOD v2 bake outputs per cell (BC7 2048² facade atlas with an emissive window mask, 1024 px ground tile), generated and gitignored, plus an editor impostor and atlas baker script.
  • Unity packages listed in CLAUDE.md but missing from Packages/manifest.json: com.unity.memoryprofiler (now), com.unity.recorder (review videos), com.unity.addressables (once cells exceed about 50 MB).
  • A content validator rule file (for example Assets/_Veyra/Data/content_rules.json): triangle budgets per class, LOD ratios, texel density 512 px/m (plus or minus 25 %) near the camera, power-of-two textures, BC7/ASTC formats, Read/Write off, pivot rules and COL_/UCX_/UBX_ naming. Run it on import and in every build.

14. Beyond graphics: audio, HUD and world life

Score today: 3 / 10   The driving camera and HUD layout are competent. But audio is a placeholder synth in a silent world with no music, and traffic, pedestrians and activities lack the reactivity and density that make GTA V and Cyberpunk feel alive.

The camera and HUD layout are already decent. The camera has trauma shake, speed FOV, G-lag and collision. The HUD has a tach, nitro, a rotating minimap, a GPS panel and speed-trap panels. Everything else about how the game feels is still prototype level. All audio is synthesised in code, and Assets/ArtSource contain zero audio files. The engine is 6 sine harmonics, every tyre and impact uses one noise band and one thud, and there is no music, ambience, rain, traffic or reverb audio and no mixer. Traffic runs kinematically on rails with no behaviour. Pedestrians are primitive procedural figures whose only reaction is a dodge. Police and pursuits do not exist. Most of the 106 activity hooks only mark a place as found. HUD text is default Arial legacy Text. There is no free-look and no rumble. Review captures run with traffic off, no HUD and no audio, so none of this is ever measured. The two biggest gains are recorded audio (engine plus world) and reactive traffic and pedestrians. Note: the red hero car in the review shots is a rebadged real-brand model ('Stanton Sidewinder' = Corvette C8, GameDatabase.asset:376-387). This is still open as BUG-066.

Findings and fixes (14)

FEEL-01The engine sound is a 6-sine synthesiser and the project contains no recorded audio critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified, corrected
You see
A buzzy, electric-toy drone that only changes pitch. Every car sounds alike apart from cylinder count. There is no lift-off crackle, turbo or shift bark, and the bonnet camera sounds the same as the chase camera.
Root cause

A real engine sound is exhaust-pulse resonance, intake roar, valve-train and gear whine, and its spectrum changes with load. Here 'load' is only one brightness scalar on a few additive sines, which gives a clean periodic tone that the ear calls synthetic within a second. GTA V, Forza and Cyberpunk play recorded on-load and off-load loops at many RPM points, crossfaded by RPM and throttle, with interior and exterior perspectives and event one-shots.

Fix

Add an EngineSoundBank ScriptableObject per car, referenced from the GameDatabase car entry so VehiclePhysics stays audio-free. Contents: idle plus on-load and off-load loops at 8-12 RPM points (1,000-8,000 rpm), 48 kHz mono, seamless 1-3 s, OGG Vorbis q0.7, Decompress On Load. In VehicleAudio: equal-power crossfade of the two nearest RPM loops per layer, pitch shift at most ±12 %, throttle crossfading between on-load and off-load. One-shots: shift cut, limiter, turbo spool and blow-off, and 2-5 random lift-off pops when the throttle drops above 4,500 rpm. The bonnet camera uses an Interior snapshot (low-pass 3.5 kHz, +3 dB at 120 Hz). AI cars get a reduced 3-loop bank. Keep the synth as a fallback when no bank is assigned. Sources: recordings of your own cars (dyno or track day), or a commercial engine library whose licence allows game redistribution. Log it in L-011 with the licence in ArtSource/Downloads. Never use sounds from other games.

Cost
About 10 voices for the player and 3 per AI car. Estimated 10-20 MB of decompressed loops per hero bank. Audio-thread cost should drop compared with today's per-sample sine synthesis for every car.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleAudio.cs:7-11 summary: 'Procedural vehicle audio (no recorded assets yet)'
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleAudio.cs:37-48 Bind: cylinder count 4/6/8 guessed from peak kW (:39-40); four AudioClip.Create PCM-callback streams (engine, tyres, wind, boost) plus one impact AudioSource that plays a synthesised thud
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleAudio.cs:116-142 EngineRead: 6 sine harmonics whose upper weights scale only with a throttle 'bright' term, a noise pulse, a one-pole low-pass and tanh
find Assets ArtSource -iname *.wav/*.ogg/*.mp3/*.mixer: 0 files
Tracked as
new
FEEL-02The world is silent: no ambience, weather, traffic, horn, siren or reverb audio critical effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 5/5 verified
You see
In heavy rain in Neon Mile, or downtown at noon, you hear only your own engine. There is no rain hiss, city hum, passing cars, horns, crowd, surf or tunnel echo.
Root cause

Sound supplies what the eye cannot see: distance, enclosure, weather and life off-screen. A city with only an engine sounds like an empty diorama, and rain that is visible but silent breaks belief at once. GTA V layers district ambience beds, point emitters, traffic pass-bys and tunnel reverb. Cyberpunk adds crowd walla, neon hum and distant music. No ambience system, emitter data or reverb setup exists in the project.

Fix

Add World/AmbienceSystem.cs. Its clips come through a VeyraAudioLibrary ScriptableObject held by a scene object (same pattern as VeyraMaterialLibrary). Layers: (1) 8 district beds × day/night, 60-120 s stereo loops, streamed and crossfaded over 50 m using WorldData.DistrictAt. (2) Point emitters from placement data: shoreline surf, fountains, port cranes, neon hum (3D, max distance 40-80 m). (3) Weather driven by WorldEnvironment.RainIntensity and Wetness: exterior rain, rain-on-roof for the bonnet camera, wet tyre hiss (speed × wetness), thunder one-shots. (4) Traffic: a pooled 3D engine loop on the nearest 8 TrafficCars (Doppler 0.5), plus horn and brake-squeal one-shots. (5) AudioReverbZones (presets StoneCorridor for tunnels, City for downtown) placed by an idempotent Editor tool from tunnel and viaduct segments in the road data. Use original, CC0 or royalty-free recordings, each with a licence file.

Cost
About 24-32 extra voices (8 traffic, 4-6 beds and weather, 10-15 emitters). Streamed beds keep memory under about 30 MB (estimate). Reverb DSP is cheap. Requires the larger voice budget from FEEL-09.
Evidence

FEEL-03Pedestrians are rigid primitive figures whose only reaction is to dive clear high effort XL (> 2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Action-figure people with box torsos, stick limbs and a stiff gait. A big downtown plaza at noon holds about 15 walkers. Nobody turns, flinches, films or gathers when you rev, boost or crash.
Root cause

People are the strongest scale and realism cue. The eye judges proportion, cloth, weight shift and secondary motion instantly, so primitive limbs make the whole city read as a toy set, and a thin crowd breaks the 'downtown' read. GTA V streams skinned, mocapped pedestrians with reactions (flinch, shout, film, flee, gather at crashes). Cyberpunk layers crowd density by district and time of day. Veyra's figures are code-built primitives moved by transforms.

Fix

Replace the figure builder with skinned characters: 8 base bodies (LOD0 8-12k tris, LOD1 3k, LOD2 0.8k, billboard beyond 80 m), modular outfits on a 2048² albedo/normal/mask atlas, and tint variants. Animate with vertex-animation textures baked in Blender (walk at 3 speeds, run, 3 idles, phone, look-at, flinch, cheer) and draw them instanced with Graphics.RenderMeshInstanced. Add behaviours: look at the car within 15 m when it revs or boosts; flinch plus a voice bark on a near miss; 6-10 people gather around a crash for 20 s; cheer at car meets; run in rain (umbrellas already exist). Density: 150-250 within 150 m in D1 and D6, at least 60 visible in CAM_05. Sources: your own Blender models or CC0 packs (e.g. Quaternius). Mixamo animations are allowed under Adobe's terms. Put licence files in ArtSource/Downloads.

Cost
Today 70 walkers cost about 0.5 ms (Pedestrians.cs:23). With VAT instancing, 250 people at LOD1 come to about 0.75 M tris (estimate). Move the walker logic to Burst jobs to stay at or under 1 ms.
Editor note
Same issue as LIFE-01; counted once.

FEEL-04Traffic runs on rails: no lane changes, horns, swerves or emergency response high effort L (1-2 weeks) impact 4/5 verified
You see
Cars glide in fixed lanes like a conveyor belt. They never overtake, indicate or honk when blocked. A crashed car becomes a loose box that vanishes after 10 s while nobody reacts.
Root cause

Believable traffic comes from small imperfections and responses: drivers hesitate, change lanes, honk, brake-check and slow to look at a crash. Kinematic rail motion with a constant lane offset gives perfect, identical spacing that the eye reads as scripted. GTA V's reactive traffic (honks, swerves, road rage, ambulances and police at incidents) is a large part of its living city. Cyberpunk added reactive traffic after launch for the same reason.

Fix

Give TrafficCar a small state machine (Cruise, ChangeLane, Yield, Panic, Crashed, Recover). Lane changes on multi-lane roads use a MOBIL-style gap check, blend the offset over 3 s and drive the indicator lamps through CarLampFX. Each driver gets traits: cruise speed ±12 %, time gap 1.2-2.2 s, reaction time 0.4-0.9 s. Panic: on a head-on closing speed above 15 m/s within 40 m, brake at 7 m/s² and swerve half a lane. Horn one-shot when the player has blocked the car for more than 2 s (existing check at :117-123) or after contact. After a crash: hazards on, stop for 20-40 s, then drive off if possible. Add ambulance or tow-truck response on arterials, and yield-to-siren (pull over to the kerb). Replace the O(n²) following loop with a Burst spatial hash before raising MaxCars above 64 (TrafficSystem.cs:17).

Cost
The spatial hash removes the 64×64 loop. The behaviour logic adds an estimated 0.2 ms or less at 64 cars. Horns and engines share the 8 pooled voices from FEEL-02.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:8 summary: 'lane following on a road polyline until hit, then free physics'
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:52: the lane offset is set once in Begin and never changed
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:96-130 Step does only three things: car-following (O(n²) loop at :108-116), stopping for the player in its lane (:117-123) and stopping at red lights
Assets/_Veyra/AI/TrafficCar.cs:237-247: a crash makes the car a non-kinematic Rigidbody; :102 expires it after 10 s
Screenshots/2026-11-11_iter21/CAM_06_DT_Drive_Crown_12h.png: no moving vehicle on about 1 km of Crown Avenue at noon (the capture runs with traffic off, VeyraReviewCapture.cs:17)

You see

Root cause

Open worlds feel alive when something meaningful is always within a minute's drive and the world talks back. GTA V mixes missions, random events and side activities. (6) A random street challenge every 2-4 minutes of free roam: an AI car flashes its lights and races you to a registry point. Voice the story: the existing characters, 5-10 lines per chapter, original actors with signed releases, delivered as phone calls while driving. Acceptance: at least one playable activity within 400 m of 90 % of road length (script over activities.json and road_network_geometry.json).

Mostly logic and data. Car meets add 10-15 static cars and a crowd, spawned only near the player.

FEEL-07The HUD uses default Arial legacy Text: no SDF fonts, glyph icons, no HUD capture medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Every HUD and menu string is default Arial, the classic prototype font. Legacy text scaled to 1440p is expected to look soft. The minimap and GPS arrows are font '▲' glyphs.
Root cause

Typography is the first production-value signal, and default Arial says 'prototype'. Legacy Text rasterises a bitmap atlas per point size that the CanvasScaler then rescales, so edges soften and outline or glow effects look crude. GTA V uses a bespoke condensed face with a strong outline and shadow, readable over any scene. Cyberpunk uses a custom techno face with glow and scanline accents. The HUD's content (tach, nitro, minimap, GPS, speed trap) is already rich.

Fix

Change UIKit.Text to create TextMeshProUGUI; TMP ships in com.unity.ugui 2.6.0, which is already in the manifest. Every caller then converts in one place. Fonts: two SIL OFL families, e.g. Chakra Petch or Rajdhani for display and Inter or Barlow for body, with OFL.txt in ArtSource/Downloads. Build SDF atlases at 2048², SDFAA, padding 10. Add an outline of 0.12-0.18 plus a soft underlay so text reads over bright sky, and use tabular numerals for speed and timers. Replace the '▲' glyphs with original SVG-derived sprites for the player arrow and blips. Add a HUD scale setting (80-130 %) and a colour-blind palette as INPUT_AND_ACCESSIBILITY_SPEC.md:39 requires. Capture HUD-on review shots of CAM_06 and CAM_13 at 1440p and 1080p.

Cost
TMP SDF rebuilds are cheaper than legacy dynamic-font rebuilds. Keep fast-changing labels in the existing nested canvases (HudController.cs:121-126).
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/UI/UIKit.cs:24-33: Font = LegacyRuntime.ttf (Arial); :118-130: every label is a legacy UnityEngine.UI.Text
Assets/_Veyra/UI/UIKit.cs:78-80: CanvasScaler reference resolution 1920×1080 (scaled ×1.33 at 1440p)
Assets/_Veyra/UI/HudController.cs:111 'Big' text at 150 px, :150 speed at 84 px, :525 and :542 arrows drawn as the '▲' glyph
No .ttf/.otf or TMP assets in Assets and no TMPro usage in code. No HUD or menu screenshot exists under Screenshots/ (review captures hide the HUD)

FEEL-08No free-look camera and no gamepad rumble medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
You cannot swing the camera around the car with the mouse or right stick, only look back. The pad never vibrates on kerbs, crashes, the limiter or nitro, so road feel is numb.
Root cause

GTA V players constantly orbit the camera to admire the car and check their surroundings, so a camera they cannot steer feels on rails. Haptics carry kerbs, impacts, slip and the limiter to the hands; GTA V, Forza and Cyberpunk all use them. Without haptics a gamepad car feels weightless even with good physics. The camera rig itself is good; only the control and the haptics are missing.

Fix

Add a Look action (<Gamepad>/rightStick and <Mouse>/delta, with a sensitivity setting) and a Horn action (<Keyboard>/h, <Gamepad>/leftStickPress) to VeyraControlsJson with new fixed binding ids. In ChaseCamera, add yaw (±180°) and pitch (−10° to +35°) offsets on top of smoothedDir. Recentre automatically after 1.5 s without input (0.8 s above 40 km/h, exponential rate 3/s). Free-look is off in Bonnet mode. Add a new Gameplay/Haptics.cs that reads VehicleState: the low-frequency motor follows suspension compression change plus trauma; the high-frequency motor follows slip, kerbs, a limiter buzz and nitro. Cap both at 0.6, zero them on pause and in menus, and add an intensity slider. Optional: a cinematic camera mode using Cinemachine 3 at trackside points taken from the registry data.

Cost
Negligible CPU cost.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Input/VeyraControlsJson.cs:18-108: the actions are Steer, Throttle, Brake, Handbrake, Nitro, LookBack, CameraCycle, ShiftUp, ShiftDown, Reset and Pause (no Look, Horn or Radio)

FEEL-09No audio mixer, a 32-voice budget, and no priorities or snapshots medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
With 8 cars, Unity must drop some of about 41 looping voices (expected drop-outs). Pause, tunnels and the bonnet view sound the same, and the balance between engine, tyres and beeps is arbitrary.
Root cause

AAA mixes are built on buses, snapshots and priorities: the player's engine and tyres always win, ambience ducks under action, and tunnels and the cockpit change the whole mix. With no mixer, every source plays straight to master at a fixed volume. Adding ambience and traffic (FEEL-02) would push further past the 32-voice limit.

Fix

Create Assets/_Veyra/Audio/VC_Mixer.mixer with groups Master → Music, Dialogue, SFX (PlayerEngine, Tyres, Impacts, AICars, Traffic), Ambience, Weather and UI. Snapshots: Default; Interior (bonnet camera, 3.5 kHz low-pass on exterior groups); Tunnel (reverb send +6 dB); Pause (SFX −20 dB, 800 Hz low-pass); Countdown. Dialogue ducks Music by 8 dB using a Duck Volume effect. Priorities: player engine 0, player tyres 16, AI cars 64-96, ambience 128, traffic 160. In AudioManager set m_RealVoiceCount to 64. Expose Master, Engine, Effects, Music and UI dB parameters and wire them to SettingsData. Loudness target: −16 ±2 LUFS integrated over a 5-minute race, true peak at or below −1 dBTP, recorded with UnityEngine.AudioRenderer in a Development player.

Cost
64 real voices is well within PC budgets. Mixer effects cost an estimated under 0.1 ms on the audio thread.

FEEL-10Tyres and impacts use one generic synth sound for every surface and hit medium effort M (1-3 days) impact 3/5 self-checked
You see
Gravel, grass, kerbs and wet or dry asphalt all sound the same. A 150 km/h wall hit and a bin tap make the same short low thud. There is no scrape, glass or metal crunch.
Root cause

Tyre and contact sounds are how drivers feel grip and surface: squeal pitch rises with slip, gravel crunches, wet roads hiss and kerbs rumble. AAA games key these sounds to surface and slip and layer impacts by material and speed. One noise band and one thud carry no information and sound cartoonish.

Fix

Add audio fields to SurfaceDefinition (rollLoop, skidLoop, crunchOneShots[]) and fill them for Asphalt, WetAsphalt, Concrete, Gravel, Grass and Dirt, plus Kerb and Metal once BUG-042 adds those surfaces. In VehicleAudio, take each wheel's surface from the existing wheel raycast. Roll-loop volume follows speed/40 m/s with pitch 0.8-1.2. Skid-loop pitch follows slip ratio and slip angle. Wet hiss follows speed × WorldEnvironment.Wetness. Impacts: 3 intensity bands × 3 variations each for metal, concrete, glass and plastic/foliage, chosen by the collider's surface and the impulse. Play a scrape loop while the car is in contact and sliding. Kerb rumble comes from the kerb trauma already computed in ChaseCamera.GatherTrauma (ChaseCamera.cs:197-199). Sources: CC0 or royalty-free libraries with licence files, or your own recordings.

Cost
Adds 2-3 voices for the player; one-shots are pooled.
Evidence
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleAudio.cs:144-156 TyreRead: one fixed band-pass noise (f 0.19, q 0.06) for every surface; :90-96: slip only drives the volume
Assets/_Veyra/Gameplay/VehicleAudio.cs:75-81 and 179-196: one procedural thud (55 Hz sine plus noise) for every collision, with volume scaled by impulse
Assets/_Veyra/VehiclePhysics/SurfaceDefinition.cs:7-11 has only Id, GripMultiplier and RollingResistanceMultiplier; E:/PROJECT BANGERRR/Docs/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md:49 plans 'VFX/audio' per surface

Evidence

new

Quick wins (each under a day)

  • Audio mixer and voice budget (about half a day): create VC_Mixer with Engine/Tyres/Impacts/Ambience/Weather/Music/UI groups and Pause and Interior snapshots. Route the 5 per-car sources (VehicleAudio.cs:58-72) and the UI source (GameFlow.cs:189). Set priorities (player engine 0) and raise m_RealVoiceCount from 32 to 64 in ProjectSettings/AudioManager.asset.
  • Rain audio (about half a day): two CC0 loops, exterior rain and rain-on-roof for the bonnet camera, driven by WorldEnvironment.RainIntensity. Add wet-road hiss to the existing tyre synth, scaled by Wetness × speed.
  • Horn and traffic honks: add a Horn action to VeyraControlsJson. Traffic plays a horn one-shot when the player has blocked it for more than 2 s; TrafficCar.cs:117-123 already detects the player ahead in its lane.
  • Gamepad rumble: Gamepad.current.SetMotorSpeeds driven by ChaseCamera trauma (low motor) and wheel slip/limiter (high motor), capped at 0.6 and zeroed on pause.
  • Free-look: right stick and mouse delta orbit in ChaseCamera, with auto-recentre after 1.5 s.
  • TMP font swap: UIKit.Text is the single place all labels are created; switch it to TextMeshProUGUI with one OFL font plus an outline and underlay. Every HUD and menu label changes at once.
  • Tunnel and downtown reverb: an idempotent Editor tool that places AudioReverbZones (StoneCorridor in tunnels, City downtown) from the road data's tunnel segments.
  • Review 'life' pass: a -reviewLife flag in VeyraReviewCapture with traffic, pedestrians and HUD on, frustum counts in review.txt, and 15 s AudioRenderer WAVs per camera.

Done means (acceptance criteria)

  • Engine audio: each player car uses at least 2 load layers × 8 RPM loops of recorded audio, with pitch shift of at most ±12 % between neighbouring loops. Check with an EngineSoundBank validator in the Editor, plus a blind A/B with 3 testers where the recorded bank scores 4/5 or better for 'sounds like a real car'.
  • World audio coverage: at least 3 active world layers (bed plus weather/emitter plus traffic) at any point on the road network, and no silent stretch longer than 5 s (RMS below −50 dBFS) in a 10-minute free-roam AudioRenderer recording. Measure with an audio debug overlay and a WAV analysis script.
  • Mix: −16 ±2 LUFS integrated and true peak at or below −1 dBTP over a 5-minute race recording; music ducks at least 6 dB under dialogue; the player's engine and tyre AudioSources never become virtual during an 8-car race (log AudioSource.isVirtual every frame).
  • Traffic life: at least 12 moving vehicles in the CAM_06 frustum at 12 h in the -reviewLife pass; at least 1 lane change per car per km on multi-lane roads; a horn or brake reaction within 0.5 s in a scripted 'player blocks lane' test. Read the counts from review.txt and the test log.
  • Pedestrians: at least 60 visible in CAM_05 at 12 h and at least 30 in CAM_13 at 22 h wet; skinned/VAT characters with at least 6 animation states; the pedestrian system at or under 1.0 ms CPU with 200 walkers (Profiler marker in a Development player).
  • HUD: 100 % of text is TMP SDF; minimum x-height of 9 px at 1080p; contrast of at least 4.5:1 against the brightest background in HUD-on CAM_06 at noon; HUD scale 80-130 % and a colour-blind palette available. Check with HUD-on captures at 1080p and 1440p and a contrast script.
  • Content density: at least 1 playable (not 'found only') activity within 400 m of 90 % of road length, and all 14 activity categories in activities.json have a working loop. Check with a script over activities.json, road_network_geometry.json and ActivityHooks modes.
  • Camera and haptics: free-look recentres within 1.5 s, and rumble fires on kerb, impact, limiter and nitro events. Check with an input/haptics debug log during an R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT autotest run.

Assets to make or source

  • Engine sound banks for 5 archetypes (I4, flat-6/V6, V8, V12 hero, turbo I4): idle plus on-load and off-load loops at 8-12 RPM steps, limiter, shift cut, turbo spool and blow-off, and 10 lift-off pops each, 48 kHz mono WAV masters. Best source is your own recordings (dyno or track day, with written permission from the car owner). Otherwise a commercial library whose licence allows game redistribution, logged in licence register row L-011. Never sounds from other games.
  • Tyre and surface set: roll and skid loops for dry asphalt, wet asphalt, concrete, gravel, grass/dirt, kerb and metal; wet hiss. Sources: CC0 (Freesound filtered to CC0) or royalty-free bundles that allow commercial game use (e.g. Sonniss GDC bundles; check each year's licence). Licence files go in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • Impact set: metal, concrete, glass and plastic/foliage × 3 intensities × 3 variations, plus scrape loops and glass shatter. CC0 or royalty-free, or your own recordings.
  • Ambience: 8 district beds × day/night (16 stereo loops, 60-120 s), surf, port machinery, gulls, wind in trees, neon hum, crowd walla (day plaza and night Neon Mile), distant sirens, aircraft pass-bys, rain (exterior, on roof), thunder ×6. CC0/royalty-free or field recordings.
  • Traffic audio: 3 generic engine loops (small car, van, truck), 6 horns, bus air brake, brake squeal, original siren designs.
  • Music: 3-4 stations × 8-12 original tracks, plus 4-stem race tracks. Commissioned with a written buy-out or sync licence that also covers streaming and video capture.
  • Voice: story lines for the existing characters (Juno Reyes, Mara Voss and others). Original actors with signed release and buy-out forms; no celebrity soundalikes.
  • Fonts: two SIL OFL families (e.g. Chakra Petch or Rajdhani for display, Inter or Barlow for body), with OFL.txt in ArtSource/Downloads.
  • UI: an original icon set as SVG then sprites (player arrow, blips, activity icons), 20 original UI sounds, and portrait art for the story characters.
  • Pedestrians: 8 base bodies with modular outfits (own Blender models or CC0 packs such as Quaternius), an animation set (own mocap, CC0, or Mixamo under Adobe's terms) baked to vertex-animation textures; licence files required.
  • Ambient movers: a gull model (about 400 tris) with VAT flap, 2 original aircraft, a ferry and a small boat, a bus with an original livery, and crane moving-part rigs, all with LODs per CLAUDE.md.

15. What GTA V and Cyberpunk 2077 do, and the Unity 6.6 URP equivalents

Neither game reaches its look with one magic feature.

  • GTA V by day is conventional techniques done with discipline: HDR with adaptive exposure, four soft shadow cascades, half-resolution AO, a per-frame environment cubemap and a planar water reflection, a weather-and-hour "timecycle", height fog with light shafts, a hand-built LOD hierarchy with merged distant proxies, and above all street-by-street prop and decal density researched from photographs.
  • Cyberpunk 2077 at night is a dense stack of emissive signage, light that spills from it onto wet reflective streets, volumetric haze that makes the light visible in the air, and heavy decal and grime layering on a shared material library. Its ray-traced modes add accuracy, but the raster version already reads as "Night City".

Both look real for the same reasons: correct scale cues, no visible repetition, contact and bounce lighting, layered micro-detail, and light that is visible in the air. Today Veyra is missing every one of them.

What Unity 6.6 URP can and cannot do today

FeatureIn Unity 6.6 URP?Use in Veyra forNote
Adaptive Probe Volumes (lighting scenarios, sky occlusion, streaming)YesGlobal illumination: day/night bounce light, sky occlusionNeeds PROBE_VOLUMES keywords in the VC shaders (Phase 1)
SSAOYesContact shadows and groundingGTAO is a 6.7 preview
Screen-space reflectionsPreview onlyWet streets, waterCompiles only behind the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION define, unsupported; aimed at 6.7. Use probes plus planar water now.
Real-time GI (Surface Cache GI)6.7 previewDynamic bounce lightUse APV scenarios in 6.6
Volumetric fog and lightsNoNeon haze, light shafts, rain mistMIT "Unity URP Volumetric Light" package, or commercial Volumetric Fog & Mist 2

Decal projector (DBuffer)YesRoad grime, stains, posters, leaksNeeds _DBUFFER_MRT keywords in the VC shaders

Sources: Unity 6.x documentation, Unity forum and Issue Tracker posts, and the research below. Preview status was checked against 6000.6 and 6.7 material. Whether the SSR define works in 6000.6.8f1 specifically is unverified.

How GTA V (2013/2015 PC and 2025 Enhanced) achieves daytime realism, mapped to Unity 6.6 URP (Forward+) on RTX 4070 at 1440p

GTA V's daytime look comes mostly from a disciplined set of conventional techniques rather than costly ones. Lighting is deferred in FP16 HDR, with adaptive exposure and a Hable filmic curve. The sun uses 4 cascades with dithered, depth-aware blurred soft shadows and cloud shadows merged in. SSAO runs at half resolution. Car reflections come from a 128 px environment cubemap re-rendered every frame, and water uses a 240x120 planar reflection. A sky dome and a cloud ring are driven by hand-authored per-weather, per-hour timecycle keyframes, with fog that carries raymarched light shafts. The engine leans on a hand-built HD→LOD→SLOD hierarchy, merged single-draw distant proxies, 2x2 stipple LOD fades and instanced 32x32 'LOD light' quads for night skylines. Reference-driven prop and decal density (block-by-block photo research) completes the look. GTA V Enhanced adds RTGI covering all light sources (with a later second-bounce option), RTAO, RT shadows, RT reflections and DLSS/FSR. URP 6.6 has no hardware ray tracing. The nearest equivalents are APV with scenario blending and sky occlusion, SSAO, probes and custom planar reflections. URP SSR, GTAO and Surface Cache GI are Unity 6.7 previews. For a small team the copy order is: (1) HDR, a custom auto exposure and a timecycle ScriptableObject; (2) cascade and cookie cloud shadows plus SSAO with a shared DepthNormals prepass; (3) a car-following time-sliced reflection probe plus baked box probes; (4) chunk HLOD/SLOD proxies with GPU Resident Drawer, GPU occlusion and dithered cross-fades; (5) DBuffer and mesh decals, vertex-baked AO and dirt, near-only POM; (6) height fog/aerial perspective and MIT volumetric light; (7) instanced grass to ~80 m plus impostors; (8) STP upscaling. A plausible daytime GPU split at 1440p is shadows 2-3 ms, prepass and AO 1-2 ms, opaque 4-6 ms, water/transparent 1-2 ms, volumetrics 1 ms, post 1-1.5 ms, which totals ~11-14 ms. All ms figures are estimates to be profiled in the project.

TechniqueWhy it reads as realHow to do it in Unity 6.6 URPCost on an RTX 4070
HDR, filmic tone mapping and adaptive exposure
GTA V lights in FP16 HDR. It measures average luminance on a 1/4-res copy with a compute shader into a 1x1 texture, adapts exposure gradually (faster dark-to-bright than bright-to-dark), extracts bloom via a bright-pass down to 1/16 res, then applies a Hable (Uncharted 2) filmic curve.
Daylight contrast exceeds display range. A filmic shoulder keeps highlights on car paint and sky from clipping, and eye adaptation sells tunnel exits, underpasses and shade without per-area hand-tuned exposure.URP asset: HDR on, 64-bit HDR precision if the sky bands. Volume: Tonemapping Neutral or ACES (a Hable look needs a custom LUT in Color Lookup), Bloom with High Quality Filtering, threshold ~1, low intensity. URP lacks auto exposure (roadmap only), so add a Render Graph compute pass that reduces centre-weighted log-luminance to a 1x1 buffer. Read it via AsyncGPUReadback and drive Color Adjustments Post Exposure on a runtime Volume, with faster dark-to-bright adaptation.Tonemap/LUT <0.1 ms; HQ bloom ~0.3-0.5 ms; luminance reduction ~0.05-0.1 ms on RTX 4070 at 1440p (estimates).
Cascaded sun shadows, soft filtering and cloud shadows
Four sun cascades sit in one 1024x4096 atlas. Sampling is dithered, then a depth-aware blur runs with a 1/8-res early-out mask, and cloud shadows merge into the same term. The PC port added PCSS/contact-hardening soft shadows, extended shadow distance, long dusk shadows and high-res shadows.
Crisp contact shadows that soften with distance, plus cloud shadows sweeping streets and hills, are among the strongest daylight cues. NVIDIA notes that shadows under cars, overhangs and foliage depend on these settings.URP asset: Main Light shadows, 4 cascades, 4096 res, Max Distance ~250-350 m for driving, splits so cascade 0 covers ~15 m, Soft Shadows at High quality, bias tuned per cascade. Cloud shadows: put a tiling cloud-density texture on the directional light Cookie (Cookie Size ~2-4 km) and scroll UniversalAdditionalLightData.lightCookieOffset from the weather controller. Beyond shadow range, rely on APV and AO. Cover the _LIGHT_COOKIES and soft-shadow keywords in VC_Variants.Cascades in a dense city ~1.5-3 ms GPU plus CPU culling (GRD reduces it); High soft shadows ~+0.3 ms; cookie ~0.05 ms (estimates).
Half-resolution ambient occlusion
GTA V computes SSAO at half resolution from linear depth, then denoises it with horizontal and vertical depth-aware blurs. The PC port exposed AO quality options, and Enhanced adds ray-traced AO.
Contact darkening where kerbs meet road, under cars, at wall bases and inside foliage grounds objects. Without it the city looks pasted together, especially in overcast or ambient-dominated light.SSAO renderer feature: Source Depth Normals, Method Blue Noise (with TAA/STP), Downsample on, Samples Medium, Blur High (bilateral), Radius ~0.3-0.5 m, Falloff ~150 m, Direct Lighting Strength ~0.2-0.3 so sunlit faces stay clean. The DepthNormals prepass it needs is also needed by DBuffer decals, so it is paid once. Evaluate URP GTAO (6.7 preview, MODERN_SSAO define, Volume-driven, temporal filter) on an upgrade branch at a milestone boundary.Half-res, 8 samples, bilateral blur ~0.4-0.7 ms; DepthNormals prepass ~0.5-1.5 ms in dense streets, unless decals already require it (estimates).
Per-frame environment cubemap and probe reflections
Every frame GTA V renders a 128x128 HDR cubemap of scenery only (~30 draws per face, no peds or cars) and converts it to dual-paraboloid maps favouring the top hemisphere, which serves car roofs and bonnets. Next-gen consoles added RT reflections, and Enhanced PC has RT reflections with a later high-res option.
Car paint, glass and wet asphalt read as real only when they reflect the actual sky and skyline around them. Re-rendering with the time of day keeps reflections consistent with the lighting.Baked box-projected probes per street block or plaza, with blend distance (box projection enabled in the URP asset and per probe; Forward+ blends per pixel). Add one realtime probe parented to the player car: 128-256 px, refresh via scripting, time-slicing Individual Faces, culling mask = static world plus sky layers, re-rendered every few frames or on movement/time change. URP SSR is a 6.7 preview; test it on an upgrade branch only.Time-sliced realtime probe ~0.3-0.6 ms per frame (CPU culling plus 1 face); baked probes ~0.1 ms sampling, VRAM only (estimates).
Water: low-res planar reflection, refraction, depth tint, data-driven waves
GTA V water redraws the scene upside down at only 240x120 (~650 draws) for the planar reflection. It adds a half-res refraction map with depth tint and caustics, Fresnel blending, bump normals and a moving ocean mesh. Community docs show wave quads (amplitude, direction) and calming quads that damp canals and pools.
Correct Fresnel (sky at grazing angles, see-through near the camera), depth colour, shore foam and locally calm harbours make water believable. Data-driven wave zones avoid uniform ocean chop inside canals and docks.Custom Shader Graph/HLSL water: Gerstner or FFT displacement, with amplitude from a wave/calm mask derived from Veyra coast and harbour data. Depth colour and refraction come from the camera depth and opaque textures. Foam from depth difference plus a baked shore distance field. A Fresnel blend mixes the reflection probe with a low-res planar texture: a quarter-res camera, large-object layers only, via SubmitRenderRequest outside the render loop. Alternatively Crest 5 (paid).Water shading ~0.3-0.8 ms; quarter-res planar pass ~1-2 ms GPU plus CPU culling, skipped when water is off-screen (estimates).
Sky dome, cloud ring and a weather x hour timecycle
The sky is a single dome draw using noise textures. Clouds are a ring mesh with seamless 2048x512 density and normal textures. Sky, fog and ambience come from timecycle data keyed by weather and time, while building, street and porch lights follow a separate time schedule.
Hand-authored keyframes per weather and hour let artists push golden hour, smog and overcast moods beyond strict physics while staying coherent. Weather changes are interpolations, so the world never pops.VeyraTimecycle ScriptableObject: per weather, hourly keys for sun colour/intensity, sky tint, ambient, fog density/height/colour, exposure bias, bloom, cloud coverage and grading weight. A runtime controller interpolates hours and weather blends, then writes into Volume profiles, the sun Light and serialized sky/cloud materials from VeyraMaterialLibrary. Sky: the community MIT physically based sky (URP 14+) or a custom skybox. Clouds: a ring mesh with scrolling layers that match the cookie shadows.Sky ~0.1-0.3 ms; cloud ring ~0.1-0.2 ms; controller CPU negligible (estimates).
Haze, height fog, aerial perspective and light shafts
GTA V raymarches a half-res light-shaft (volumetric shadow) map against the sun shadow map and blurs it. The depth-based fog pass then uses it, so haze carries sun shafts and the distant skyline fades into sky-coloured atmosphere.
Aerial perspective (contrast and saturation loss toward sky colour) conveys kilometres of scale and hides LOD changes. Warm low-altitude haze reads as a hot city and frames the skyline.Use the CristianQiu URP volumetric fog (MIT, render graph, supports up to 6000.6) for main-light shafts plus Forward+ additional lights, at reduced resolution. Add aerial perspective via the community PB sky's atmospheric scattering/height fog, or a custom full-screen pass that blends scene colour toward the sky ambient by height-exponential optical depth. Drive density, anisotropy and colour from the timecycle.Volumetric fog at half/quarter res ~0.6-1.5 ms; analytic height fog or aerial perspective ~0.1-0.2 ms (estimates).
Vegetation: GPU LOD selection, instanced grass, wind, impostors
A compute pass decides visibility and LOD per object, and distant grass and flowers are simply not drawn. Grass is stored as point batches with per-instance attributes. PC adds tree tessellation, and Grass Quality controls density, distance and grass shadows, which are expensive in rural areas.
Dense near grass and swaying trees sell nature, while far grass is invisible anyway. Spending density only near the camera, with consistent wind and colour variation, gives richness cheaply.Trees as LODGroup prefabs (LOD0-2 plus an impostor) drawn by GPU Resident Drawer (SpeedTree supported), with Blue Noise LOD cross-fade. Grass from placement CSVs via terrain details (GPU Instancing, Read/Write on in 6.6) or a custom BatchRendererGroup draw, density fading to zero by ~60-100 m and no grass shadows beyond ~30 m. Wind in the vertex shader from a global wind vector and vertex-colour weights; hue jitter from a position hash. Octahedral impostors beyond ~250 m.Near grass 1-3 ms in fields (alpha-test overdraw dominates); trees with GRD ~1-2 ms; impostors cheap (estimates).
City LOD hierarchy (HD/LOD/SLOD), merged proxies and dithered transitions
GTA V map entities form HD→LOD→SLOD parent/child chains with per-level distances. Distant regions are hand-merged meshes with one texture: Vinewood Hills is ~2,500 triangles in one draw call, and Little Seoul is one draw. LOD fades use a 2x2 stipple plus a smoothing pass, and streaming needs no loading screens.
Hand-tuned proxies keep the skyline's silhouettes and colour intact at kilometres without thousands of draws. Dithered transitions hide pops at driving speed.An idempotent editor tool builds an HLOD per 250 m chunk: merged building shells, albedo baked to one atlas, ~5-10k tris; plus a coarser SLOD per 1 km cell. Nest them in LODGroups (cell → chunks → buildings) with cross-fade (Blue Noise or Stencil dither, smoothed by TAA/STP). Stream HD content per cell via Addressables and keep SLODs resident. Use Mesh LOD (6.2+) for props imported without hand-made LODs.Proxies cut distant draws ~10-100x; skyline GPU <0.5 ms; RAM for resident SLODs; editor bake time (estimates).
GPU-driven culling and draw submission
GTA V uses a compute shader to choose per object whether and at which LOD to render, and sorts G-buffer draws front-to-back for early-Z. It still issues ~4,155 draw calls per frame (G-buffer ~1,900, shadows ~1,000).
Prop and building density is what makes streets look lived-in. It is affordable only when CPU submission and occlusion scale, so detail is not traded away for frame rate.URP asset: SRP Batcher on and GPU Resident Drawer set to Instanced Drawing (requires Forward+, already used). On the Universal Renderer, enable GPU Occlusion. Keep materials SRP-Batcher compatible (no MaterialPropertyBlocks; do variation in the shader). Instance static props from placement CSVs with shared meshes and materials. Verify coverage, culling and LOD in the 6.6 GPU Resident Drawer profiler view.Unity cites up to 50% CPU frame-time reduction for GameObject rendering; GPU occlusion adds ~0.1-0.3 ms and can lose time where little is occluded.
Distant LOD lights for night skylines
Beyond the range of real lights, GTA V draws each distant bulb as a quad with a 32x32 texture, tens of thousands batched as instanced geometry. Distant cars become just a pair of headlights. Map data keeps separate lodlights and distant-light sets baked from entity lights.
A night skyline reads through lights, not geometry. Thousands of points along streets, towers and hills give scale and city life at near-zero cost, and they line up with the real lights as you approach.An editor tool bakes, per 1 km cell, light positions and colours from placement data (lamps, windows, signs) into a GraphicsBuffer. One indirect draw per cell renders camera-facing HDR quads with distance-scaled size and intensity, faded by timecycle hour and crossfaded with real Forward+ lights inside ~150-250 m. Traffic LOD: headlight/taillight quads moving along road splines.~50k quads in a few draws <0.2 ms; a few MB of memory; replaces hundreds of real lights (estimates).
Decals, mesh decals and parallax occlusion mapping
GTA V layers street and building detail with decals, detail maps and parallax occlusion mapping (tied to Shader Quality, which NVIDIA says adds bumpy surface detail and is lost at Normal). Vehicles gather grime through dirt maps.
Real streets are covered in stains, patches, cracks, gum, oil, paint and grime. Decals break tiling and tell a history, and POM gives cobbles and brick depth at grazing angles.Decal Renderer Feature with the DBuffer technique (albedo, normal, MAOS), since the DepthNormals prepass is already paid for SSAO. Max Draw Distance ~80-120 m, atlased decal textures. Use mesh decals (thin alpha-blended strips from Blender, merged into chunk meshes) for kerb grime, wall streaks and road patches. POM only on near materials (cobblestone, brick) with a distance fade; detail normals elsewhere.DBuffer decals ~0.3-1 ms depending on screen coverage; mesh decals nearly free; near-only POM ~0.2-0.5 ms (estimates).
Vertex-baked ambient terms and vertex-blended dirt/wear
GTA V assets carry baked lighting terms in vertex colours. Community docs describe channels for night-time ambient occlusion, artificial light near lamps and moonlight response. Exact per-shader blending use is only community-documented.
Baked occlusion, plus grime where water runs and feet wear surfaces, makes the city feel aged and grounded. Vertex data is nearly free and stops tiling textures from reading as clean CG.In Blender (vc_tools.py), bake per-vertex AO/sky visibility to R and a dirt/wear mask to G (cavity, ground proximity, drip direction), with B for moisture or variation. A shared Lit-compatible Shader Graph multiplies AO into indirect light and height-blends a grime layer by vertex mask times noise. Keep enough vertices at building bases. Per-instance tint comes from an object-position hash for facade and car variation.Negligible GPU (~0.1 ms for one extra layer); one extra vertex stream; Blender bake time (estimates).
Indirect lighting: baked probes now, realtime GI later (RTGI analogue)
GTA V 2013 has no dynamic GI. It relies on timecycle ambient, baked vertex ambient, SSAO and cubemaps. Enhanced adds RTGI covering all light sources (later with an optional second bounce), plus RTAO, RT shadows and RT reflections. Digital Foundry praised the RTGI but noted heavy CPU load.
Bounce light adds colour bleeding and keeps shadowed façades from looking flat or grey. GI that follows the day cycle keeps that right at every hour.URP Adaptive Probe Volumes: bake per cell with the 6.6 Compute light baker, coarse bricks in open land and fine bricks in streets. Use Lighting Scenario Blending for dawn/noon/dusk/night, plus Sky Occlusion for time-of-day sky colour. URP has no hardware RT path. Surface Cache GI (realtime diffuse, optional HW RT) is a 6.7 preview to evaluate on an upgrade branch.APV sampling ~0.1-0.3 ms; 48 km² of probe data is tens to hundreds of MB and needs streaming; bake takes hours (estimates).
Camera post: DOF, flares, motion blur, chromatic aberration
GTA V's DOF uses a signed circle of confusion, extracts only the out-of-focus foreground and blurs it at full resolution in two compute passes. Sun flares use 12 streak quads and 70 sprites, headlights get anamorphic streaks, chromatic aberration comes via lens distortion, and motion blur follows camera rotation.
Subtle lens artefacts make images read as photographed. Kept restrained, they sell speed while driving without hurting readability.Volume: Motion Blur in Camera Only mode, low intensity, Medium quality, scaled by speed from script on a runtime Volume. DOF off in gameplay; Gaussian for photo mode, Bokeh for cutscenes. Lens Flare (SRP) asset on the sun plus low-intensity Screen Space Lens Flare. Chromatic Aberration ≤0.05, weak Vignette, light Film Grain.Motion blur ~0.2-0.4 ms; Gaussian DOF ~0.2-0.4 ms, Bokeh 1-2 ms; flares <0.1 ms (estimates).
Temporal anti-aliasing and upscaling
GTA V PC shipped FXAA, MSAA and TXAA (temporal, requires MSAA). Enhanced added DLSS (later DLSS 4 with multi frame generation), FSR 3 and frame generation.
Thin wires, railings, foliage and specular shimmer betray CG instantly in motion. Temporal AA removes the crawl, and upscaling buys budget for shadows and AO.URP asset Upscaling Filter = STP (needs SM5 compute; turns on TAA automatically) at render scale ~0.75-0.85 for 1440p output, or TAA at native if budget allows. Make sure moving renderers write motion vectors (vehicles; wind-animated foliage needs vertex motion, or accept some ghosting). DLSS is not built into URP; a store plug-in is the route.STP ~0.5-0.8 ms at 1440p output, recovering ~25-40% of pixel-bound cost at 0.75 scale (estimates).

Art-direction lessons

  • Reference first, block by block. Rockstar wrote a brief per city block, sent scouts and came back with hundreds of thousands of GPS-stamped photos. Do the same per Veyra district from real-world analogues, never from other games.
  • Curate, do not replicate. Rockstar's art director said rebuilding a real city outright would be less compelling. Compress distances and amplify each district's signature materials, props and silhouettes.
  • Prop density per street is a working heuristic, not a Rockstar figure: per 100 m of block face, lamps every 25-35 m plus 6-12 street-furniture items (bins, hydrants, signs, bollards, benches, utility boxes), 15-30 ground decals (oil, gum, patches, cracks, manholes) and 2-4 vertex-dirt grime bands. Place everything from Veyra placement data, never on the carriageway.
  • Material variation: no two neighbouring façades or cars should share exact tint or roughness. Use shader-side hash tints (±5-10% value/hue), 2-3 albedo variants per kit piece and weathering masks so repetition does not read.
  • Age everything. Use streak grime under sills, dirt at wall bases and kerbs, worn lane paint, darker tyre tracks on asphalt and dust on horizontal surfaces. Clean CG surfaces are the main giveaway.
  • Haze and aerial perspective carry scale. Distant hills and the skyline should lose contrast and saturation toward the sky colour. A warm, low-altitude, slightly desaturated haze reads as a hot coastal city, and haze strength is a timecycle key per weather and hour.
  • Grade for daylight plausibility. Keep albedo within PBR ranges so grading behaves, prefer a neutral-to-warm daytime grade with a gentle filmic shoulder, avoid crushed blacks, and reserve heavy colour moods for dusk, night (Neon Mile/Downtown) and weather.
  • Treat exposure as a feature. Adapt faster when going dark-to-bright than bright-to-dark, as GTA V does, so tunnel exits and underpasses bloom briefly. Clamp the EV range per timecycle key so noon never looks grey.
  • Author the sky and light per weather and hour, not only physically. Keep street, building and window light schedules separate from the weather data, as GTA V ties city lights to time and sky/ambience to the timecycle.
  • Reflections on cars carry the look. Top-hemisphere detail matters most (sky and skyline on roofs and bonnets), so give the car's realtime probe priority over generic probe density.
  • The night skyline is built from light points. Make sure every lamp, window cluster and sign has a far LOD light so the city still reads at several kilometres.

Caveats

  • Courrèges' study reverse-engineers one 2015 DX11 PC frame. Counts and sizes depend on the scene and settings, and it does not cover decals, MSAA or LOD-light internals.
  • GTA V Enhanced RT details (RTGI on all lights, second bounce, high-res RT reflections, DLSS 4) come from secondary coverage of Rockstar and Digital Foundry. Neither the Rockstar Newswire post nor the DF video/article was opened directly.
  • The water.xml wave/calming quads, HD/LOD/SLOD ymap hierarchy, lodlights/distant lights, grass batches and vertex-colour channel meanings come from modding-community documentation. Field semantics are unofficial and may be imprecise. Use them as concepts only and never copy GTA data, names or assets (project rule: all content original).
  • Several pages could not be fetched (network errors): PC Gamer Enhanced analysis, gamegpu settings tests, Sollumz LOD-lights page, the cfx.re water how-to, the FiveM LOD guide and the Garbut interviews. Those points rely on search-result snippets.
  • URP SSR, URP GTAO and Surface Cache GI are Unity 6.7 previews (alpha builds, GTAO behind the MODERN_SSAO define). The SSR post mentions trying it from 6000.6.0a7 alphas; whether any of it is usable in 6000.6.8f1 is unverified. CLAUDE.md allows the 6.7 LTS upgrade only at a milestone boundary on a Git branch.
  • Gaps in URP 6.6 confirmed in this research: no built-in auto exposure (roadmap only), no physically based sky or volumetric clouds/fog, no planar reflection probe and no water system. These need custom passes or third-party packages.
  • Every ms cost in the techniques is my estimate for an RTX 4070 at 1440p, not a measurement. Per project rules, each must be profiled (Profiler, Frame Debugger, GPU timings, stats.json) in named review-camera shots before being treated as fact.
  • Forward+ SSAO (Depth Normals) and DBuffer decals both need the DepthNormals prepass, which re-renders opaque geometry. In a dense city that prepass can cost more than the effects themselves.
  • UniversalRenderPipeline.RenderSingleCamera deprecation status has been inconsistent. SubmitRenderRequest called inside beginCameraRendering can trigger recursion errors, and calling it from LateUpdate makes the planar reflection one frame late.
  • Unity 6.6 no longer enables mesh Read/Write at build time for Terrain Detail meshes, Particle System shape meshes or Mesh Colliders that need CPU data, and builds fail if it is missing. The project default is Read/Write off, so check grass detail meshes and COL_ colliders.
  • APV for an 8x6 km world: memory and streaming limits, and whether URP supports disk streaming in 6.6, were not verified.
  • Shader keywords toggled at runtime (light cookies, SSAO, decals, soft shadows, LOD cross-fade, custom water/exposure passes) must be covered by a material or by VC_Variants.shadervariants. Runtime code must not call Shader.Find; sky, water and cloud materials go through VeyraMaterialLibrary.
  • Licence statements for the eldskald and jiaozi158 repos come from listings or READMEs; the LICENSE files were not read. The BoatAttack licence is unknown. Per project rules, put a licence file in ArtSource/Downloads before importing any third-party package.

How Cyberpunk 2077 achieves its neon wet night, mapped to Unity 6.6 URP (Forward+) on RTX 4070 at 1440p/60

Cyberpunk 2077's night look rests on four things: a very dense stack of emissive sources (neons, screens, billboards, thousands of in-world UI instances), coloured light spilling from those sources onto wet, highly reflective streets, volumetric haze that makes that light visible in the air, and heavy decal and grime layering on a shared multilayer material library.

The raster path fakes this with hand-placed local lights. Only about 10 of them cast shadows. A probe-based GI grid (1.5–4 m spacing) supplies indirect diffuse, and reflections come from SSR plus probes. The RT and path-traced modes (RTXDI/ReSTIR) make every emitter a real shadowed light, but they depend on DLSS.

URP 6.6 cannot do the RT path. It can get close with:

  • Forward+ (256 visible lights per camera on desktop)
  • fill lights matched to each sign, with cookies
  • a strict budget of shadowed spot lights
  • a day and a night APV Lighting Scenario with baked emissive bounce, blended at runtime
  • a third-party raymarched volumetric fog
  • box-projected reflection probes, plus SSR later (official URP SSR is aimed at 6.7)
  • a global wetness/puddle shader with DBuffer decals
  • VFX Graph rain that collides with the depth buffer
  • tuned Bloom and Neutral tonemapping with manual exposure
  • STP upscaling

Every Unity cost figure here is an estimate and must be measured on the target hardware.

TechniqueWhy it reads as realHow to do it in Unity 6.6 URPCost on an RTX 4070
Layered emissive signage stack
Night signage is built in depth layers: 3D neon tubes and channel letters, backlit lightboxes, LED video walls, holographic ads, small shop-level signs and lit windows. Each layer has its own brightness band and animation, so streets read dense but structured.
Night City treats neons, screens and billboards as primary light sources, and CDPR's UI tech supports thousands of independent in-world UI instances.
