CDPR later found ad clutter became unreadable and added grouping and negative-space rules for Phantom Liberty.Opaque URP Lit for tubes and lightboxes with HDR emission; one atlas Shader Graph for LED screens (flipbook/UV scroll from _Time, pixel-grid mask) instead of per-sign RenderTextures. Starting bands above base exposure: neon +3 to +5 stops, lightboxes +2 to +3, screens +1 to +2. Keep materials in VeyraMaterialLibrary, cover _EMISSION in VC_Variants.shadervariants, and use a few material variants rather than MaterialPropertyBlocks (they break SRP Batcher). Holograms unlit additive, small screen area.Opaque emissives cost ordinary shading; animated screen shader under ~0.2 ms. Transparent holograms are overdraw-bound; keep under ~5% screen coverage. Estimates, profile on target.
Sign-matched fill lights (raster stand-in for emissive GI)
Emissive pixels do not light the world in raster rendering, so every significant sign gets one or two real lights whose colour and intensity follow it. Walls, wet roads, cars and pedestrians then pick up the coloured spill.
Cyberpunk's RT diffuse illumination lights surroundings from billboard emissives, and Overdrive's RTXDI turns every neon, LED billboard and headlight into a shadowed light. Without RT, CDPR relied on placed local lights; the coloured spill is what sells neon.Forward+ removes the per-object limit; desktop allows 256 visible lights per camera including the main light. An idempotent Editor tool places unshadowed spots from sign data, colour taken from each sign's average emission. A runtime light manager ranks lights by screen size and distance, fades them out beyond ~60-90 m and keeps ~100-150 visible. Prefer spots aimed at the street over points. Rendering Layers keep sign fills off interiors and distant geometry.Forward+ per-pixel cost grows with lights per tile; ~100 unshadowed lights at 1440p estimated 0.5-1.2 ms on RTX 4070. Check worst-case tile density.
Prioritised shadow-casting light budget
Only a few local lights cast shadows at once, picked by importance and distance with hysteresis; all others are unshadowed fills placed so they do not visibly leak through walls.
In non-RT mode CDPR allowed about 10 shadow-casting lights, split between static and dynamic geometry; designers chose which lights shadowed, and unshadowed lights shining through walls was a known artefact that path tracing later removed.URP puts all spot and point shadows into one additional-light atlas: a spot uses one slice, a point six. Use a 4096 atlas with tiered resolutions; a script enables shadows on the top ~8 spots by priority and distance and disables the rest. Model street lamps as spots, not points. Use Custom Shadow Layers to limit casters and keep fills away from thin walls.Each shadowed spot re-renders its casters, roughly 0.1-0.4 ms depending on scene; a point light costs about six times that. Cap local shadows near 1.5 ms.
Light cookies for shaped sign spill
Coloured cookie textures on spot and point lights project sign shapes, louvres, window grids and flicker onto roads and facades, so spill reads as coming from a specific sign rather than a round blob.
CDPR describes RTXDI and path tracing as giving grounded, dimensional light and smooth gradients, because each emitter keeps its real shape. A cookie is the cheapest raster approximation of a shaped emitter. CDPR also presented capsule area lights for tube-like sources.URP supports cookies on directional and spot lights (2D texture) and point lights (cubemap), but not area lights. Cookies share an additional-light atlas whose resolution and format are set in the URP Asset; overflow scales cookies down. Bake 256-512 px colour cookies from sign textures, use a 2048 atlas, and animate flicker by swapping cookies or animating Cookie Offset.One texture sample per lit pixel per light; a 2048 RGBA32 atlas is ~16 MB. Watch for atlas overflow when many cookie lights are visible.
Night APV Lighting Scenario with baked emissive bounce
Indirect diffuse is baked separately for day and night; at night, static neon and lightbox emissives inject coloured bounce into probes so alleys glow without hundreds of realtime lights, and the two bakes blend through dusk.
Raster Cyberpunk used a hybrid GI probe system at a fixed world-space spacing set per location (usually 1.5-4 m), indirect diffuse only; RT modes added emissive-driven bounce. Coloured ambient fill carries much of the neon mood.Adaptive Probe Volumes with Lighting Scenarios (Day, Night) and Scenario Blending enabled in the URP Asset; drive the blend factor from WorldEnvironment's time of day. Set static sign materials' GI flag to BakedEmissive and enable Contribute Global Illumination on their renderers. Denser probes in D1 and D6. Probe positions must not change between scenario bakes. Try the new Unity Compute Light Baker in 6.6; Surface Cache GI is a 6.7 preview.Runtime probe sampling roughly 0.2-0.4 ms at 1440p; scenario blending small. Memory scales with probe density; measure per 1 km cell.
Volumetric haze and light scattering
Low-density height fog plus local fog lit by spot and point lights, giving neon halos, light shafts and depth separation between foreground signs and distant towers; heavier during rain.
Cyberpunk renders volumetrics in their own pass even under path tracing, and its Volumetric Fog Resolution is one of the heaviest settings; haze is what makes coloured light visible in the air.URP 6.6 has distance fog only, no volumetric fog. Add a raymarched renderer feature: Unity-URP-Volumetric-Light (MIT; main, spot and point lights with shadows and cookies, APV, Forward+ and Deferred+, listed up to 6000.6) or Volumetric Fog & Mist 2 (commercial). Half resolution, 120-200 m range, URP exponential fog beyond; density per district Volume and weather; let only the ~20-40 most important lights scatter.Estimated 0.8-2.0 ms at half-res 1440p with ~30 scattering lights; scales with steps x lights. Players report ~10-12% FPS from Cyberpunk's fog setting.
Layered reflections for wet neon
Layered specular: reflection probes as baseline, screen-space reflections for on-screen neon in wet asphalt, glass and car paint, sky as last fallback; ray-traced reflections where the engine allows.
Cyberpunk's RT reflections trace one bounce on all surfaces over several kilometres, with SSR still layered in; Digital Foundry found neon billboards glow more convincingly once RT replaces SSR. Neon on wet roads is the signature image.No shipped URP SSR in 6.6: Unity targets 6.7, with an alpha preview from 6000.6.0a7 behind URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION (verify in 6000.6.8f1). Now: box-projected probes per block with per-pixel blending (max two per object), baked in the Night scenario, plus one camera-following realtime probe updated one face per frame for car paint. Optional interim SSR: UnitySSReflectionURP (MIT) at half resolution. Move to official SSR at the 6.7 milestone.Baked probes cost memory, not time. Time-sliced realtime probe ~0.3-0.8 ms. Half-res SSR estimated 0.6-1.5 ms at 1440p.
Wet surface system and puddles
A global wetness value darkens porous albedo, raises smoothness and flattens normals; puddle masks in low spots become mirror-smooth with ripples. Wetness lingers after rain and dries over minutes.
CDPR runs a dedicated Wet Surfaces System; when patch 1.3 broke wet roads players noticed at once, and 1.31 restored them with more detail. The multilayer library also gives each material template wetness overrides.A Shader Graph subgraph in road, kerb, facade and terrain materials reads globals (_VC_Wetness, _VC_Rain) set from WorldEnvironment with Shader.SetGlobalFloat: darken albedo 30-50% by porosity, push smoothness to ~0.9-0.95 in puddles, reduce normal strength. Puddle mask from road vertex colour or a world-space mask built from road data. Extra puddles and streaks as DBuffer decals (Albedo Normal MAOS). Ripple normal flipbook. Apply after unpacking smoothness from metallic alpha.~0.1-0.3 ms extra ALU across opaques. Decals share the DBuffer prepass. The real cost is reflections (see reflections technique).
GPU rain with depth-collision splashes
A camera-local box of GPU rain streaks with motion-stretched sprites, ground splashes, drips from edges, vehicle spray and windshield droplets; streaks brighten near lights; density follows weather.
Rain multiplies the night look: backlit streaks show where light is, and splashes ground the wet surfaces. Cyberpunk's rain appears particle-based with splashes, judging from mod descriptions (not confirmed by CDPR).VFX Graph box following the camera, 30-80k streaks. Unity 6 lets URP particles Collide with Depth Buffer; use Trigger Event On Die to spawn splashes. Tint streaks from a low-res light texture or nearest-light parameters, since VFX lit outputs may not receive every Forward+ light (verify). Windshield drops as a car-glass shader effect. Unity 6.6 adds VFX-to-Shader-Graph templates.Estimated 0.3-0.8 ms GPU for ~60k particles plus splashes at 1440p. Overdraw near the camera is the risk; keep streaks thin.
Layered materials, decals and grime
Surfaces built from a shared tileable material library blended by sparse masks, then dressed with decals: stains, leaks, graffiti, posters, oil, kerb dirt and tyre marks, concentrated at bases, joints and drip lines.
CDPR's multilayer supershader blends shared Substance-made templates with small masks and microblend height maps, with weathering and wetness overrides. Texel density was double The Witcher 3's, and extensive decals covered remaining texel-density problems.Trim sheets plus a Shader Graph layered material (base and two height-blended overlays). URP Decal Renderer Feature: DBuffer with Albedo Normal MAOS, so decals change colour, emission, normals and smoothness; Max Draw Distance 80-120 m; Rendering Layers keep street grime off cars and pedestrians. Place decals from data with an idempotent Editor tool; atlas decal textures to share materials.DBuffer needs a DepthNormal prepass, estimated 0.3-0.7 ms at 1440p; each decal adds fill-rate, so limit large overlapping projectors.
Steam vents, smoke and holograms (transparency layer)
Localised steam from vents, manholes, food stalls and AC units, plus holographic ads, rendered as soft lit particles and additive layers that catch coloured light.
CDPR presented a dedicated transparency pipeline at SIGGRAPH 2021 and a holographic character shader for cyberspace. Modders note the game's steam and fog look over-bright under RT, which suggests they were art-tuned against raster neon lighting.URP Particles Lit (receives additional lights) or VFX Graph, with soft particles from the depth texture, flipbooks with motion vectors and short lifetimes; emitters placed from data. Holograms: unlit additive Shader Graph with fresnel, scanlines, flicker and depth fade, no depth write, few stacked layers. Nearby sign fills light the steam.Overdraw-bound: a plume close to the camera can cost 0.3-1 ms at 1440p. Cap near-camera particle size, fade by distance, limit emitters per view.
Night bloom, tonemapping and exposure
Night exposure set so emissives clip into a wide, low-intensity bloom while shadows still read; neutral tonemap plus district grading LUTs; HDR output with a calibration menu.
Cyberpunk exposes HDR paper white, peak brightness and a tone-mapping midpoint, clarified in patch 2.3, showing a tonemapper tuned around very bright emitters. Neon needs highlights to roll off without hue skew.Bloom: Gaussian, High Quality Filtering, Threshold ~1.0-1.2, Intensity 0.2-0.4, Scatter 0.65-0.75, Downscale Half, subtle Lens Dirt in rain. Tonemapping Neutral plus an HDR grading LUT per district Volume. HDR Output: Neutral with low Hue Shift Amount, Paper White calibration. URP lacks built-in auto exposure: drive Color Adjustments Post Exposure from a time-of-day curve plus an AsyncGPUReadback luminance estimate.Gaussian HQ bloom at 1440p roughly 0.3-0.6 ms; LUT grading ~0.1 ms; async luminance readback negligible. Estimates.
Tiered crowd density
Pedestrian density scales by district and hour, with LOD tiers: fully animated agents near the camera, cheaper instanced animation at mid range and impostors far away; nobody spawns in the carriageway.
Cyberpunk's Crowd Density is a CPU-heavy option, and modders describe separate 3D and 2D crowd tiers. People under neon make a street feel inhabited.Near (up to ~40 m): 30-60 SkinnedMeshRenderer NPCs with animator culling. Mid (40-150 m): vertex-animation-texture meshes drawn with RenderMeshInstanced or BatchRendererGroup; GPU Resident Drawer handles MeshRenderers only and needs Forward+. Far: billboard impostors. Density tables per district and hour as CSV in Assets/_Veyra/Data; walk paths from sidewalk data; Burst jobs for movement.Skinned NPCs are CPU-bound, roughly 0.02-0.05 ms each; ~1,000 VAT instances estimated 0.3-0.6 ms GPU. Watch p99 frame time.
Temporal upscaling to fund the night budget
Render below native resolution and upscale temporally so the neon, fog and reflection stack fits the 16.6 ms budget.
Cyberpunk's heaviest night modes lean on DLSS; NVIDIA's own RT Overdrive figure for an RTX 4070 Ti at 1440p (80 FPS) used DLSS 3.URP 6.6 offers STP and FSR 1; DLSS is listed for HDRP only. Enable STP in the URP Asset (it forces TAA and cannot combine with dynamic resolution) at Render Scale ~0.77-0.85 for 1440p. Check thin neon tubes, rain streaks and LED screens for ghosting and shimmer from review cameras.STP pass estimated 0.4-1.0 ms at 1440p output, but saves roughly 25-40% of per-pixel shading at 0.8 scale. Measure both.
Fixture presets and an offline reference (lighting sandbox)
A small library of physically plausible light fixtures (street lamp, neon tube, lightbox, LED wall, shop window) with fixed intensity, colour and range, so time of day and weather behave predictably everywhere without hand-tuning each street.
CDPR built a physically grounded lighting sandbox so light, time of day and weather changes stayed predictable, and checked engine lighting against the offline Arnold renderer early on.Fixture presets as versioned ScriptableObjects (light type, colour, intensity, range, cookie, shadow priority, emissive material link) applied by an Editor tool. URP lights have no physical units, so lock one night exposure and calibrate presets against Blender Cycles reference renders of the same review block. Validate from named review cameras.No runtime cost; saves art iteration. Presets also feed the light manager's priorities.

Art-direction lessons

  • Colour-code each district (CDPR gave every district its own palette). Suggested start for Veyra, all original: D6 Neon Mile uses magenta/hot pink plus cyan with amber accents over near-black wet asphalt. D1 Crown Downtown uses cool white and teal corporate light with one restrained accent hue. D3 Ironport uses sodium orange with industrial cyan work lights. D5 Westhaven uses warm residential tungsten with sparse shop neon.
  • Create Veyra's own wealth/style tiers, for example patched-cheap, pop-plastic, corporate-austere and luxury-organic. Map each to signage materials, sign density and light colour. Do not reuse CDPR's style names, brands or logos.
  • Plan signage by height: shop-level signs (2-5 m) and window glow, then blade and vertical signs (5-15 m), then large LED walls and lightboxes (15-40 m), then sparse roof and tower crowns with aviation lights. Give each band its own brightness range so the eye reads depth.
  • Use contrast and negative space. Group signs by colour and shape, give each block face one hero sign, and leave dark gaps between clusters. CDPR found uniformly dense ads turned to noise and added these rules for Phantom Liberty.
  • Typography: invent fictional brands only. Use 2-3 type families per district (for example a condensed grotesk, a rounded display face and a monospace tech face). Street signs need cap heights of at least ~0.5 m and tower signs ~2 m+ so they read at driving speed. Keep multilingual glyph signs original, never copied.
  • Animation discipline: most signs steady, a few chasing or scrolling, about 1-3% flickering or broken. Animated LED walls run slow loops so they do not strobe at 60 FPS under TAA/STP.
  • Keep a neutral key: warm sodium or LED-white street lamps provide the base, and saturated neon is an accent laid on top. Avoid a fully saturated frame; keep shadows a little above black so silhouettes, pedestrians and road markings stay readable (CDPR protected readability where NPCs walk).
  • Wet reflection is the highest-value feature. Place hero signs where a wet road, puddle or glass facade is below or opposite them, and check this from review cameras at driver eye height.
  • Grade haze by depth: thin fog close up, so foreground signs stay crisp, building to silhouetted distant towers with sparse window lights. Raise density during and just after rain.
  • Lighting must not compete with gameplay: no neon colour may mask lane lines, yellow centre lines or traffic signals, and the road surface stays the brightest readable plane in driving views.
  • Tell small stories through contrast, following CDPR's 'rule of contrast': a broken sign over a pristine corporate facade, or a food-stall steam plume under a luxury ad.
  • Day and night versions: by day signs dim or switch off and ads become printed or matte, so the neon payoff only happens at night. Bake this into the Day and Night APV scenarios.

Caveats

  • The GDC 2022 'Bringing Light to Night City' talk and other GDC Vault recordings need a pass. Light counts, fog and probe details here come from CDPR/NVIDIA interviews and 80.lv, not from the slides.
  • Many pages could not be fetched (DNS failures or 403): gamedeveloper.com, pcgameshardware.de, foundry.com, domusweb.it, rpgsite, wiki.redmodding.org, history.siggraph.org PDFs, fmx.de, cyberpunk.net, wccftech, tweaktown. For those, the claims rest on search-result summaries.
  • Digital Foundry was not opened directly; its findings come from secondary coverage (TweakTown, Notebookcheck, forum quotes).
  • Cyberpunk performance figures for fog, SSR and crowd density come from community guides or single systems, across different patches. Treat them as indicative only.
  • Crowd 2D/3D tiers come from modder documentation. The particle-based rain and art-tuned steam claims rest on mod pages and forums, not CDPR statements. No primary breakdown of Cyberpunk's holograms or steam VFX was found.
  • No primary source confirms a specific CDPR colour palette (for example cyan/magenta/yellow). The palette suggestions are design recommendations, not facts about Cyberpunk.
  • URP SSR: Unity's preview needs 6000.6.0a7+ alpha and a URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION define, and Unity aims to ship it in 6.7. Whether it is usable in 6000.6.8f1 is unverified. The project rule only allows upgrading at a milestone boundary.
  • Surface Cache GI is a 6.7 preview. Per Unity docs, when active it replaces lightmap/APV indirect lighting on opaques. Do not plan on it for 6.6.
  • The 6.6 resolution-scale docs list DLSS as HDRP-only. URP offers STP and FSR 1.
  • URP auto exposure: the roadmap lists 'Automatic Exposure' as planned and no URP override was found, so absence in 6.6 is likely but not confirmed.
  • Emissive contribution to APV via MaterialGlobalIlluminationFlags.BakedEmissive is documented for baked GI and probes generally. Confirm it in a test bake with the new Compute Light Baker.
  • The CristianQiu volumetric package lists support 'up to 6000.6'; verify on 6000.6.8f1. Kronnect light limits come from a Fab listing and mirrored READMEs.
  • Every Unity/RTX 4070 cost value is an engineering estimate, not a measurement. Per project rules, measure with Development player logs, stats.json and review-camera screenshots (p99 ≤ 16.6 ms, no hitch > 50 ms).
  • Project constraints: no runtime Shader.Find or creating materials by shader name. New materials go in Assets/_Veyra/World/Generated/Materials via VeyraMaterialLibrary. _EMISSION and any new keywords (wetness, SSR) must be covered by VC_Variants.shadervariants. Sign, decal and emitter positions must come from Veyra data, never invented.
  • All signage, brands, typography and style names must be original; nothing copied from Cyberpunk 2077.

Unity 6.x URP 17 rendering features for open-world realism (state as of Unity 6.6 / 6000.6, with 6.7 previews flagged)

On Unity 6.6, URP already has most of the realism foundations: Adaptive Probe Volumes (with lighting scenarios, scenario blending, sky occlusion, and GPU and disk streaming), Forward+ and Deferred+ clustered lighting, Rendering Layers, light cookies, DBuffer decals, the GPU Resident Drawer with GPU occlusion culling, STP upscaling with TAA, SSAO, VFX Graph lit, six-way and depth-collision particles, Terrain Shader Graph, and SpeedTree 9 Shader Graphs. Render Graph has been mandatory since 6.4.

Shipped URP still lacks several HDRP-class features: SSR, GTAO, real-time GI, volumetric fog, a physically based sky, auto-exposure, water, and built-in DLSS or FSR2. SSR and GTAO are in preview for 6.7, and so is Surface Cache GI (real-time diffuse GI). SSR and Surface Cache GI code appears in 6000.6 builds behind scripting defines, but that use is unsupported. Unity's 2026 strategy puts all new rendering work into URP and keeps HDRP in maintenance only. For an RTX 4070 open world, the practical stack is:

  • Forward+ with the GPU Resident Drawer and GPU occlusion culling
  • APV with sky occlusion and blended lighting scenarios for time of day
  • DBuffer decals, SSAO, STP, and reflection probes
  • third-party or custom volumetrics, sky and water
  • a planned move to 6.7 LTS for SSR, GTAO and Surface Cache GI
TechniqueWhy it reads as realHow to do it in Unity 6.6 URPCost on an RTX 4070
Adaptive Probe Volumes (scenarios, sky occlusion, streaming)
An automatic, brick-based light-probe grid that is sampled per pixel from the 8 nearest probes. Lighting Scenarios store several bakes that can be switched or blended. Sky occlusion multiplies a baked occlusion value by the runtime ambient colour. Cells inside the camera frustum stream from GPU or disk.
Cars, pedestrians and props get the same directional bounced light as static geometry, without flat ambient. Scenario blending plus sky occlusion make time of day possible instead of one frozen bake across 48 km².In URP since 2023.1; scenario blending and sky occlusion added in 6.0. URP asset > Lighting: Light Probe System = Adaptive Probe Volumes, SH Bands L1/L2, Lighting Scenarios + Scenario Blending, Enable GPU Streaming, then Disk Streaming. Lighting window > APV: Sky Occlusion (+ Sky Direction). Set ambient Source to Gradient/Color so the sky can change at runtime. Script: ProbeReferenceVolume.BlendLightingScenario, numberOfCellsBlendedPerFrame. Streaming and Addressables loading can't be combined. 6.6 adds the Unity Compute Light Baker.Memory grows with probe density, L2 bands, sky occlusion and sky direction. Quality leak mode does up to 3 sampling attempts. Bakes are long. Blending positions must match (Don't Recalculate).
Screen-space reflections (URP preview) + reflection probe fallback
Rays are marched through the depth and colour buffers to get glossy specular reflections. Rays that leave the screen or hit occluded areas fall back to reflection probes or the sky. Today URP relies on reflection probes only.
Wet asphalt, car paint, glass towers and puddles need view-dependent reflections of nearby neon and headlights. Probes alone give static, misplaced reflections, which hurts most in Neon Mile night scenes.No supported URP SSR in 6.6. Unity's preview targets 6.7 (forum post; needs 6000.6.0a7+). The Issue Tracker shows it in 6000.6.x behind the define URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION, which is unsupported. Custom shaders: multi_compile_fragment _ _SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION, then SampleScreenSpaceReflection(). Now: Reflection Probes with Box Projection, Probe Blending + Probe Atlas Blending (Forward+), and probe rotation (6.3). HDRP SSR is HDRP-only.Preview gives no ms figures. Half and quarter resolution look blocky on mirror-like water, and object thickness needs per-scene tuning. Realtime probes re-render cubemaps.
SSAO now, GTAO in 6.7 preview
Screen-space ambient occlusion. URP ships an SSAO renderer feature with 4, 8 or 12 samples and bilateral, Gaussian or Kawase blur. A horizon-based GTAO mode with optional temporal accumulation is in preview for 6.7.
Contact darkening under cars, kerbs, window reveals and foliage grounds objects in the scene. GTAO gives sharper contact shadows with less over-darkening than legacy SSAO.Add the Screen Space Ambient Occlusion renderer feature. Set Source = Depth Normals and Blur = High (Bilateral), tune Radius and Falloff Distance, and keep Direct Lighting Strength low. Downsample if GPU-bound. GTAO preview: 6000.7.0a1+, MODERN_SSAO scripting define, SSAO Volume override Mode = GTAO; the temporal path needs compute and motion vectors.Radius and Samples have high impact: going from 4 to 8 samples doubles GPU load, and Downsample cuts the pixel count 4x. Unity says GTAO costs a little more than SSAO.
Surface Cache GI (real-time diffuse GI, 6.7 preview)
Real-time indirect diffuse lighting in URP from a ray-traced patch cache arranged in camera-following cascades, with no baking. It uses hardware ray tracing on DX12 and a compute fallback elsewhere. Diffuse only; specular comes from probes or SSR.
Dynamic time of day, moving lights and changing geometry all get bounced light. This is the HDRP-class real-time GI URP has lacked, and it suits a 24 h city cycle.Documented only for 6000.7: add the Surface Cache Global Illumination renderer feature and the Volume override, then set Size, Resolution, Cascade Count, Distance Fallback and the Quality preset. Requires static batching off and Default Mesh Buffer Target = Raw. It replaces lightmap and APV indirect light on opaques and doesn't light transparents. A SURFACE_CACHE define existed in 6.6 alphas; that use is unsupported.No official ms figures. Cost scales with sample count, patch resolution and denoise passes. A tester reported a crash on a 15M-vertex scene. Disocclusion noise and lighting lag are known issues.
Forward+ / Deferred+ clustered lighting and light limits
Clustered light culling. Forward+ has no per-object light limit. Deferred+ (added in 6.1) uses the G-buffer for opaques and Forward+ for transparent and forward-only passes, with the same light limit as Forward+.
Downtown and Neon Mile nights need hundreds of signs, street lamps and car lights. Forward's 9-lights-per-object limit makes lighting pop and drop out.Universal Renderer > Rendering Path = Forward+, or Deferred+; the GPU Resident Drawer needs one of these. The desktop per-camera cap is 256 lights, and the main light counts in Forward+. Lower it in the URP Config package (MAX_VISIBLE_LIGHT_COUNT_DESKTOP plus k_MaxVisibleLightCountDesktop) to cut Lit variant compile time. Forward+ keeps MSAA and exact normals; Deferred paths lose MSAA. Set Depth Priming to Auto or Forced against overdraw.The visible-light cap drives shader compile time. Deferred adds G-buffer bandwidth, plus an extra target when Rendering Layers are on. Additional-light shadows share one atlas.
Rendering Layers + light cookies
Rendering Layer masks limit which renderers a light, decal or shadow affects, and Custom Shadow Layers separate shadow casting from lighting. Cookies project textures through lights; point lights take cubemaps. All additional-light cookies share one atlas.
Neon spill can be kept to façades and interior lights off the roads. Cookies shape street-lamp pools, window blinds and sign patterns, giving real light shapes instead of uniform cones.URP asset > Lighting (Advanced Properties) > Use Rendering Layers. Name the layers in Tags and Layers, then set Light > Rendering Layers and each renderer's Rendering Layer Mask. Enable Light Cookies (Show All Advanced Properties) and set Cookie Atlas Resolution and Format (Grayscale Low to Color HDR). Area lights take no cookies. Additional directional-light cookies arrived in 2023.2.Rendering layers on lights cost little in Forward. Cost steps up at 9, 17 and 25 layers. Decal layers force a DepthNormal prepass. The cookie atlas costs memory.
Decal Projector + Decal Renderer Feature (DBuffer vs Screen Space)
Projected Shader Graph decals. DBuffer blends albedo, normal and metallic/AO/smoothness into opaques during the opaque pass. Screen Space draws after opaques using reconstructed or G-buffer normals and blends normals only.
Tyre marks, oil, cracks, manhole grime, worn lane paint and puddle masks break up tiling across kilometres of road. Decals carry a lot of GTA-style ground realism.Add the Decal renderer feature. On PC use Technique = DBuffer, Surface Data = Albedo Normal MAOS; it needs the DepthNormal prepass and doesn't run on GL or GLES. Use Screen Space for the Android tiers (Normal Blend Low/Medium/High = 1/3/5 depth samples). Set Max Draw Distance. Atlas decal textures and turn on GPU Instancing for decal materials, because decals don't use the SRP Batcher. VFX Graph has a URP Lit Decal output.DBuffer adds a prepass and MRT bandwidth. Each decal is a draw call unless instanced. Decals don't project onto transparents, and DBuffer decals don't project onto particles or terrain details.
GPU Resident Drawer + GPU occlusion culling
The GPU Resident Drawer auto-batches compatible MeshRenderers through BatchRendererGroup, with persistent per-instance GPU data and DOTS instancing. GPU occlusion culling tests bounding spheres against a depth pyramid from the current and previous frames.
A dense city repeats props and building modules tens of thousands of times. Cutting CPU draw cost frees budget for density, and occlusion drops blocks hidden behind buildings.Unity 6.0+. Set Graphics > BatchRendererGroup Variants = Keep All, keep the SRP Batcher on, set GPU Resident Drawer = Instanced Drawing, use Forward+ or Deferred+, enable GPU Occlusion on the renderer, and turn static batching off. Eligible objects avoid MaterialPropertyBlock, OnWillRenderObject/OnBecameVisible callbacks, Light Probe Proxy, Enlighten and more than 128 materials. Shaders need DOTS instancing (URP Lit and Shader Graph have it). Use RSUV (6.3) for per-object variation, and look for Hybrid Batch Group in the Frame Debugger.Unity cites up to 50% less CPU frame time in complex scenes. GPU load rises slightly and builds take longer. In open views, culling setup can cost more than it saves.
STP / TAA upscaling (DLSS, FSR2 and XeSS not built in)
STP is Unity's compute-based spatial-temporal upscaler and anti-aliaser, built on TAA. URP also offers FSR 1.0, Bilinear and Nearest. DLSS 4, FSR2, TAAU and CAS are HDRP-only.
Temporal accumulation removes specular and foliage shimmer, and a lower render scale buys GPU time for heavier lighting, the same way AAA games use DLSS or FSR.URP asset > Quality > Upscaling Filter = Spatial-Temporal Post-Processing; Render Scale ≤ 1. The camera AA is forced to TAA. Needs Shader Model 5 compute and doesn't run on GLES. Not compatible with Dynamic Resolution, and TAA excludes MSAA and camera stacking. Check the result with the Rendering Debugger STP views. DLSS, FSR3 or XeSS only come through third-party plugins.Unity gives no ms figures; PC deringing is described as not significant. Ghosting on fast objects means vehicles and VFX need correct motion vectors.
MSAA + alpha-to-coverage
Hardware multisampling resolves triangle edges. Alpha-to-coverage (AlphaToMask) turns cutout alpha into MSAA coverage, which smooths foliage cards, fences and wires.
Power lines, railings, chain-link and tree cards alias badly at 1440p. Alpha-to-coverage smooths them without sorting transparency.URP asset > Quality > Anti Aliasing (MSAA) 2x/4x/8x. Works with Forward and Forward+ only, not Deferred, and can't be combined with TAA or STP. In custom HLSL passes, set AlphaToMask On (meant for MSAA only). Whether URP Lit alpha-clip uses alpha-to-coverage automatically is unconfirmed in the docs, so check the URP package source. Pair with SMAA for shader aliasing.Bandwidth and memory scale with sample count. MSAA doesn't fix specular or texture aliasing, and choosing it gives up STP upscaling.
Volumetric fog and lights (third-party in URP)
Ray-marched participating media that produce sun shafts and halos around headlights, lamps and neon. URP has only classic distance fog; Unity's volumetric fog is HDRP-only.
Atmospheric depth over an 8 km city, light shafts in the Blackridge valleys and neon haze in Neon Mile are signature looks of GTA V and Cyberpunk 2077.Unity's HDRP-to-URP VFX guide confirms URP has no volumetric fog. Options: the MIT-licensed URP Volumetric Light package (render graph; main, spot and point lights with shadows and cookies; APV for baked lights), added as a renderer feature plus a VolumetricAdditionalLight component; or Kronnect Volumetric Fog & Mist 2 from the Asset Store. Use Forward+ or Deferred+ when many lights scatter, and keep the fog distance-limited.Per-pixel ray marching, usually at low resolution then upsampled. Cost grows with scattering lights. In the MIT option, transparents don't blend with the fog.
VFX Graph in URP (lit, six-way smoke, depth collision)
GPU particles with URP Lit outputs, a Six-Way Smoke Lit output, a URP Lit Decal output, access to the camera depth and colour buffers, and collision against the depth buffer.
Tyre smoke, exhaust, dust, rain splashes and sparks that take scene lighting and collide with the world make speed feel physical.URP lit outputs arrived in VFX 12. Depth and colour buffer access in URP arrived in 2023.2 and 6.0, and depth collision in Unity 6. Six-way smoke uses two RGBA lightmaps and takes light from probes, APV and punctual lights, but gets no reflection-probe specular. Enable Depth Texture, plus Opaque Texture for colour sampling, in the URP asset. Needs compute; GLES is unsupported. The VFX 17.6 requirements still list URP support as preview.Simulation cost scales with particle capacity, and overdraw dominates. Depth collision only sees on-screen surfaces.
Terrain: Terrain Lit, Shader Graph terrain, detail instancing
URP Terrain Lit offers height-based layer blending and per-pixel normals. The Shader Graph URP Terrain Lit material type arrived in 6.3, the Terrain Properties node in 6.4, and detail meshes can be GPU-instanced.
The Blackridge and Redwood slopes need height-blended rock, soil and grass layers plus dense grass to avoid a flat, tiled look.Enable Draw Instanced in Terrain Settings; per-pixel normals need it. Terrain Lit draws 4 layers per pass and adds a pass for each further group of 4. Height-based Blend reads the mask map's blue channel; the 6.1 docs say it is ignored above 4 layers. For custom terrain, use Shader Graph > URP Terrain Lit with the Terrain Texture node. Details: Add Detail Mesh with Use GPU Instancing (mesh Read/Write on, batches of 1,023 or fewer, no probe or lightmap lighting).Each extra group of 4 layers is another full pass. Detail density and distance drive vertex cost, and instanced details use more GPU memory.
SpeedTree 9 in URP
The SpeedTree9Importer (Unity 6.0) imports .st9 files and assigns URP Shader Graph shaders, with Games and Legacy wind and LODs. SpeedTree GameObjects were made GPU Resident Drawer compatible in 6.0.
Believable tree silhouettes, wind motion and LOD transitions carry much of the realism in Redwood Valley and the Westhaven parks.Import .st9 files exported from SpeedTree Modeler 9 and tune wind on the importer's Wind tab. The importer chooses URP or HDRP shaders from the pipeline asset name (Issue Tracker), so keep standard names. Place trees as GameObjects for the GPU Resident Drawer; community reports say terrain-placed trees are not covered. LOD cross-fade falls back to static dither under the drawer. A 6.6 alpha bug report says the material converter has no SpeedTree8/9 upgrader.Leaf overdraw and alpha test dominate, and wind adds vertex work. Community reports say wind stops working with custom indirect instancing.
Water (no official URP system)
Ocean, river and pool surfaces with waves, foam, caustics, refraction and reflections. HDRP has a full Water System; URP has no official water.
The Azure Coast shoreline and Ironport docks need convincing waves, shore foam and reflections, and water is a hero element in GTA-style coastal views.Build water in Shader Graph or HLSL: Gerstner or FFT waves, depth-based colour and foam from the Depth Texture, refraction from the Opaque Texture, and reflections from probes, a planar camera, or URP SSR once it ships. Reference: Unity's Boat Attack water package, which is not officially supported. The HDRP Water System only works in HDRP, which is now in maintenance.The opaque-texture copy and depth texture add cost, and a planar reflection is an extra camera render. Wave simulation runs per vertex or in compute.
Sky, atmosphere and exposure
A physically based sky with aerial perspective plus physical exposure. HDRP has a PBR sky and exposure. URP has skybox materials and Skybox, Gradient or Color ambient, but no exposure Volume override.
Correct sky colour, haze and exposure from dawn to neon night set the realism of the whole image and must match the APV and fog.In URP, use a procedural or cubemap skybox and drive RenderSettings ambient colours so APV sky occlusion follows the sky. DynamicGI.UpdateEnvironment is very expensive, so call it only rarely. The MIT-licensed PBSky URP renderer feature adds a physical sky, atmospheric scattering, height fog and dynamic sky reflection and ambient. Unity's 2026 strategy lists a physical sky, a dynamic sky manager, physical light units and exposure for URP, with no version given.Precomputed scattering LUTs are cheap. Per-frame ambient or reflection updates are costly. Manual exposure needs a grade per lighting scenario.
Render Graph (mandatory for custom passes)
URP's frame-graph API. Passes declare the textures they read and write; the graph culls unused passes, aliases memory and merges passes. Introduced in 6.0, with Compatibility Mode fully removed in 6.4.
It isn't a visual feature by itself, but custom volumetrics, water, wet-road and outline passes depend on it to run without wasted bandwidth and in the right order.Write a ScriptableRendererFeature and ScriptableRenderPass with RecordRenderGraph, and use the AddBlitPass and AddCopyPass helpers (improved in 6.3). Inspect the frame in the Render Graph Viewer, which can also attach to device builds. The URP_COMPATIBILITY_MODE define is gone, so every third-party URP feature must support render graph.Runtime overhead is negligible, and compilation can be cached. The cost is migrating legacy Execute()-based features.

Art-direction lessons

  • Day (GTA V realism): use baked APV with SH L2, sky occlusion and Sky Direction as the indirect base, real-time sun cascades, subtle DepthNormals SSAO, and DBuffer Albedo Normal MAOS decals for road wear. Give each block a box-projected reflection probe and turn on Probe Atlas Blending (Forward+).
  • Time of day: bake APV Lighting Scenarios at key times (dawn, noon, dusk, night) and blend them with a numberOfCellsBlendedPerFrame budget. Use Gradient ambient so sky occlusion follows the sky colour, and drive the sun, fog and manual exposure from one time-of-day controller.
  • Night (Neon Mile and Downtown): use Forward+ clustered lights with HDR emissive signs and bloom. Use Rendering Layers so neon lights façades but not interiors or the far side of the road, cookie-shaped lamp pools, and third-party volumetric haze kept short-range.
  • Wet roads: until URP SSR ships (6.7), use dense box-projected probes and roughness and puddle decals. Consider a planar reflection only for hero water. Do not ship the hidden 6.6 URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION define as a final solution.
  • Anti-aliasing: STP (TAA-based) is the default for foliage and specular stability. Vehicles, wheels and VFX must write correct motion vectors to avoid ghosting. MSAA with alpha-to-coverage is a fallback only if STP ghosting can't be fixed.
  • City density: put repeated static city meshes through the GPU Resident Drawer with GPU occlusion. Avoid MaterialPropertyBlock on them and use RSUV (6.3+) or material variants for variation. Watch for Hybrid Batch Group in the Frame Debugger.
  • Effects: six-way lit tyre smoke, dust and exhaust that take APV and punctual lights, and depth-collision sparks and rain splashes. VFX Graph URP lit decals can add transient skid and impact marks.
  • Do not imitate HDRP-only features one-for-one. Physical sky, water and volumetrics come from MIT or Asset Store packages or custom render graph passes, kept in Assets/_Veyra/Art/Shaders with licence files.
  • Plan for 6.7 LTS (due end of 2026 per Unity) to get SSR, GTAO and Surface Cache GI. Per project rules, upgrade only at a milestone boundary and on a Git branch. Prototype on a separate branch first and compare it against the APV baseline using the named review cameras.

Caveats

  • SSR, GTAO and Surface Cache GI are previews aimed at Unity 6.7. The Issue Tracker shows SSR code in 6000.6.x behind the URP_SCREEN_SPACE_REFLECTION define, and a SURFACE_CACHE define existed in 6.6 alphas. Both uses are unsupported on 6000.6.8f1, and SSR fixes are marked for 6.7. GTAO needs 6000.7.0a1 or later. Surface Cache GI docs exist only for 6000.7.
  • The feature comparison page on docs.unity.com is auto-generated from 6000.1 data. I checked newer pages where they exist (rendering paths, the upscaler table and the GPU Resident Drawer).
  • GPU Resident Drawer rendering path: the 6.6 page allows Forward+ or Deferred+, while 6.3 says Forward+ only. Its line about Animator hierarchies is ambiguously worded, so verify both against the installed URP source.
  • Whether URP Lit alpha-clipped materials use alpha-to-coverage automatically is supported only by an unverified forum claim; check AlphaToMask in the URP Lit shader source.
  • Terrain: the note that height-based blend is ignored above 4 layers comes from the 6.1 docs. The 6.6 Terrain Lit page no longer mentions it, so treat it as uncertain.
  • SpeedTree: the claims that terrain trees bypass the GPU Resident Drawer and that wind fails with indirect instancing come from community forum posts, not Unity docs.
  • VFX Graph 17.6 system requirements still describe URP support as not out of preview.
  • Upscalers: DLSS, FSR2 and XeSS are not built into URP. The Conifer 'Upscaler' Asset Store listing showed as deprecated when I fetched it. Intel's XeSS Unity plugin repo is archived; a secondary news report says Intel ended support. ndepoel/FSR3Unity (MIT) targets Built-in and PPv2 only, not URP. I did not verify licence terms for other DLSS or FSR plugins.
  • The Kronnect render graph and rendering-path claims come from a Fab listing seen in search results, not from the Asset Store page I opened. Its price and version may change.
  • Unity's 2026 strategy promises a URP physical sky, physical light units, exposure, SSR and real-time GI, with no version numbers. The 6.7 LTS end-2026 date is Unity's target, not a confirmed date.
  • Some fetches failed because of DNS and network errors (the gamefromscratch article and parts of the Unity forums), so a few points rely on search-result summaries. No ms numbers are published for STP, SSR, GTAO, APV or Surface Cache GI, so measure them on the RTX 4070 at 1440p.
  • Under project rules, any 6.7 upgrade happens only at a milestone boundary, on a Git branch.

Production pipeline, budgets and legal asset sources for a believable large city and nature with a very small team in Unity 6 URP (RTX 4070, 1440p/60)

AAA cities are mostly reused content, not unique content. Studios build from grid-snapped modular kits, trim sheets and a library of tiling materials. Variety comes from vertex or mask layer blending, tint and wear overrides, and lots of decals (Insomniac, Ready at Dawn, CDPR). Rules place most of the content: Houdini or shape grammars generate buildings and street dressing (Matrix Awakens City Sample, Spider-Man, THE FINALS), and GPU or Houdini scatter fills the nature (Horizon Zero Dawn, Far Cry 5). Distance is handled by merged HLOD meshes (GTA V draws km-scale hills in one draw call) and impostors. In Unity 6 URP the matching tools are Shader Graph layered materials, the URP Decal feature, the GPU Resident Drawer (Forward+), Mesh LOD, mipmap streaming budgets and an idempotent editor tool that reads Veyra data. Licensing: as of Nov 2026 the whole Megascans library is free on Fab under the Fab Standard Licence, which allows any engine. Poly Haven and ambientCG are CC0. Sketchfab CC-BY needs credits. Mixamo is royalty-free. MetaHuman is reportedly allowed in Unity since UE 5.6. Reallusion, EmberGen and SpeedTree have tiered terms that must be checked per seat and per revenue level. No authoritative public triangles-per-frame figure was found, so budgets must be measured on the target hardware.

TechniqueWhy it reads as realHow to do it in Unity 6.6 URPCost on an RTX 4070
Grid-snapped modular facade kits
Assemble buildings from a small set of snap-together pieces (wall bays, corners, storefronts, cornices, roofs) on a fixed metric grid with shared pivots and footprints. A few artists can then produce hundreds of distinct buildings.
In Bethesda's Skyrim kit talk, grid size, footprints and snapping rules are the base of every kit. Embark's Houdini Building Creator built 100+ buildings for THE FINALS/ARC Raiders. The team started at 10–20 people with one tech artist, and each change took 4–6 minutes to iterate.Fix a Veyra grid in Blender, for example a 3 m storey and 1.5/3/6 m bays. Model each piece with a base-centre pivot, LOD0/1/2 and UBX_/UCX_ collision, and export with VC_Unity_Prop. Make prefab variants per district style. An idempotent editor tool reads the placement CSV and spawns pieces under each Chunk_<Cell>_<ij>. Keep 1–3 shared materials per kit so the SRP Batcher and GPU Resident Drawer can batch them.Many small instances raise CPU submission cost. The GPU Resident Drawer and per-chunk HLOD merging for distance offset this. Runtime cost is otherwise low.
Standard-layout trim sheets
One texture set holds horizontal strips (mouldings, sills, panels, pipes) with bevels baked into the normal map. Many meshes map their UVs onto these strips, and because the layout is standard, a material can be swapped without re-UVing.
Insomniac's GDC 2015 'Ultimate Trim' talk (Sunset Overdrive) presented this as solving production speed, memory use and performance together. Strip heights double from strip to strip and use 45° normal bevels.Author 2–3 trim sheets per district at 2048², BC7. Snap UVs to the strips in Blender. Pack for URP Lit: BaseMap, Metallic R plus Smoothness A (1 − roughness), and a normal map. Use the same strip layout in every district so one mesh can take any district's trim by material swap. Reserve one strip as an HDR emissive mask for lit windows and signage at night.Almost free at runtime. It saves texture memory compared with unique bakes, and one material per kit helps SRP batching.
Tiling layers blended by vertex colour plus height
Surfaces are mostly built from a library of tiling materials (brick, plaster, concrete, grime) blended at runtime by vertex colour. Height maps make each blend follow mortar lines and cracks instead of fading softly.
Ready at Dawn's SIGGRAPH 2015 talk reports that The Order: 1886 was 90–95% tiling materials, with up to 4 vertex-blended layers and height-aware blending. Naughty Dog's shading artist described the same tileable-plus-vertex-blend approach for Uncharted 4.Build one URP Lit Shader Graph that reads 2–3 layers from Texture2DArrays. Set each blend weight to a vertex colour channel sharpened by layer height. Paint vertex colours in Blender; FBX keeps them. Use vertex alpha plus an up-facing mask for leaks and dirt. Keep the shader SRP Batcher compatible and add its keywords to VC_Variants.shadervariants.Each layer adds about 3 texture samples per pixel, so cap facades at 3 layers. Paintable blends also need extra vertices.
Multilayer materials with sparse masks (CDPR style)
Each asset stores only a small mask. A shared library of tileable materials (concrete, painted metal, rust, plastic) is layered through that mask at runtime. Tint, wear and wetness overrides replace baked texture variants.
CDPR put multi-layered shaders on almost every Cyberpunk 2077 model because unique-texture memory and disk estimates were unacceptable. Layering gave higher texel density at a fraction of the disk size, and 'microblends' kept the mask transitions crisp.Use this for hero landmarks and close props in D1/D6. A Shader Graph reads a 256–512 RGBA mask that selects up to 4 library layers from Texture2DArrays. Each layer has tint, roughness-scale and wear properties, and a tiling microblend height/normal breaks up mask edges. Everywhere else, keep plain Lit with trims.Heavy pixel shader: 4 layers is roughly 12 or more samples. It saves a lot of memory. Profile at 1440p on the 4070.
Decals for uniqueness, grime and storytelling
Projected or mesh decals add leaks, posters, graffiti, cracks, road patches and kerb dirt on top of repeated modular surfaces. They break up repetition without new base textures.
CDPR says it used decals heavily in Night City and that they solved texel-density problems without changing base assets. Ready at Dawn also built a runtime decal projector system for damage, gore and puddles.Enable the URP Decal Renderer Feature (DBuffer on PC). Atlas the decals and enable GPU instancing on their materials so matching decals share a draw call. Set Max Draw Distance to about 60–120 m. On facades, prefer mesh decals (quads 1–2 mm off the wall) baked into kit prefabs. Use projectors for roads and dynamic marks, and use rendering layers to keep decals off vehicles.DBuffer needs a depth-normals prepass, and overlapping projectors add overdraw. Mesh decals cost vertices and alpha blending.
Rule-based building generation (shape grammar on lots)
Lot footprints are extruded into volumes, split into base and top styles, and filled with kit pieces by grammar rules. The output is a list of instance transforms, not unique meshes.
Epic's Matrix Awakens City Sample (about 4×4 km) generated its layout and metadata in Houdini and exported them as point clouds. A Rule Processor turned those into instanced modular kits, and buildings are shape-grammar volumes with base and top styles.Write the grammar in Blender Geometry Nodes (already in the toolchain) or Houdini Indie. The input is Veyra lot polygons and heights from the placement CSVs. The output is a CSV or JSON of kit piece IDs and transforms in Assets/_Veyra/Data. An editor tool spawns them per chunk and checks that nothing enters road corridors. The grammar only fills lots the data defines and never invents positions.Compute is offline only. Runtime cost is the instance count, handled by the GPU Resident Drawer and HLOD.
Procedural street dressing along road splines
Lamps, hydrants, bins, signs, street trees and parked cars are placed by rules along kerbs and sidewalks (spacing, offset, exclusion near junctions, crossings and driveways) rather than by hand.
Insomniac used procedural markup tools to build Manhattan. Spider-Man 2 used more than a dozen procedural systems to create and populate its world, and press coverage says Houdini removed chores such as placing individual street lamps.Load road_network_geometry.json into Unity Splines. Sample each kerb edge at spacing set per road class, offset inward, and skip junction radii, zebra crossings and driveways. Take heights from the validated road profile. Write a placement CSV and spawn from it with an idempotent tool. A validator then rejects anything inside the carriageway.Offline cost only. At runtime, share atlased materials so the GPU Resident Drawer can instance the props.
Biome-mask vegetation scatter
Vegetation, rocks and ground clutter are placed deterministically from 2D maps (slope, altitude, moisture, distance to road). A small team can then fill many kilometres consistently and regenerate after terrain edits.
Guerrilla placed all of Horizon Zero Dawn's vegetation procedurally on the GPU: 500+ asset types and 100,000+ objects at about 250 µs average GPU load. Ubisoft's Houdini tools for Far Cry 5 generated biomes, terrain texturing and cliffs for about 100 km² of terrain that changed daily.Bake per-cell masks offline from the terrain RAW tiles: slope, height, district and road distance. Scatter with seeded Poisson-disc sampling in Burst jobs and store per-chunk instance buffers. Render with the GPU Resident Drawer or BRG/RenderMeshIndirect, or with Nature Renderer 6 or GPU Instancer Pro for dense terrain details. Exclude roads and kerbs.Grass density and shadow casters dominate the cost. In HZD, placement took about 0.25 ms of GPU time.
Distant-city HLOD proxies
Beyond a few hundred metres, whole blocks are replaced by one merged, simplified mesh with a baked atlas, drawn in a single call. The same proxies feed reflection probes.
GTA V draws Vinewood Hills (several km², dozens of buildings) as one draw call of about 2,500 triangles at distance. Insomniac automated distant-city LODs for Spider-Man 2 because hand-making them could not scale to a city twice as large.Per 250 m chunk in Blender: merge the LOD2s, decimate or remesh to about 5–20k triangles, and bake an albedo, normal and emissive-window atlas (1–2k). Export Chunk_<Cell>_<ij>_HLOD with VC_Unity_World. A LODGroup on the chunk root swaps detail for the HLOD at about 400–600 m. Unity's open-source HLODSystem (com.unity.hlod) is an alternative. Bake night window emission into the HLOD for the skyline.One extra atlas and mesh per chunk. Draw-call savings at distance are large. Pop-in appears if the swap distance is too short.
Impostors for trees and far props
Distant trees and complex props are drawn as quads that sample a pre-rendered atlas of many view angles (octahedral or hemi-octahedral). This keeps the silhouette and shading for a few triangles.
Epic's Impostor Baker docs say Fortnite uses upper-hemisphere impostors for all trees when Nanite is off. SpeedTree and Unity tree LOD chains end in a billboard that cross-fades from the last mesh LOD.Tree LOD chain: LOD0 → LOD1 at about 40% → LOD2 at about 10% → impostor at roughly 150–250 m, with LODGroup cross-fade enabled in the URP asset. Bake with Amplify Impostors (test the URP 17 bake first) or SpeedTree .st9 billboards. A hemi-octahedral atlas is enough for a ground-level driving camera.About 2 triangles plus a small BC7 atlas per species. Alpha-test overdraw, and slight parallax error up close.
Unity Mesh LOD (6.2+) for third-party props
Unity generates LODs at import, stores them in the same mesh's index buffer and selects one by screen size, with no extra GameObjects.
Unity's docs say each Mesh LOD has about half the indices of the previous one and costs less overhead than a LOD Group. That lets a tiny team give every bought prop LODs quickly.Enable Mesh LOD generation in the model import settings for third-party or small props that ship without LODs. Do not combine it with a LODGroup on the same mesh; the docs warn this gives unexpected results. Keep authored LOD0/1/2 and LODGroups for chunks, trees and buildings as the project rules require. Avoid it on skinned meshes.Small import-time cost and some extra index memory. No extra draw calls.
GPU Resident Drawer (GPU-driven instancing)
Unity 6 automatically turns eligible MeshRenderers into BatchRendererGroup instanced draws. This cuts CPU draw submission for scenes with thousands of repeated kit pieces, props and trees.
Unity's docs say it reduces draw calls and CPU time and works best in large scenes. For scale: Courrèges's GTA V PC frame was built from about 1,900 draw calls, and Unity's Book of the Dead sample scene had 1,832 batches.In the URP asset: Forward+ (already set), SRP Batcher on, GPU Resident Drawer set to Instanced Drawing. In Graphics settings, set BatchRendererGroup Variants to Keep All. Optionally enable GPU occlusion culling. Verify 'Hybrid Batch Group' in the Frame Debugger and the SetPass count in Stats. A/B test on the same camera path.Longer builds from BRG variants and slight GPU overhead. A GPU-bound frame can get slower.
Texel-density standard plus mip-streaming budget
Set a texel density for each asset class and a GPU texture-memory budget. Mipmap streaming then loads only the mips the camera actually needs.
Common studio conventions are about 512 px/m for third-person games and 1024 px/m for first-person. CDPR raised Cyberpunk's density to roughly twice The Witcher 3's. Consistent density stops a blurry kit from sitting next to a sharp one.Suggested targets: road and kerb 512–1024 px/m through tiling; ground-floor facades 512 px/m; upper floors 256 px/m through trims; terrain 256–512 px/m plus detail maps. Check density in Blender. Enable Mipmap Streaming, read Texture.desiredTextureMemory in the busiest review camera, and set the budget slightly above that figure.Mip pop during fast driving if the budget is low. Higher density costs memory and disk.
Scanned and CC0 libraries with licence provenance
Use photoscanned and CC0 libraries (Megascans, Poly Haven, ambientCG) for nature, ground, rocks and surface materials. Trace each file to a recorded licence before it enters the project.
Scanned albedo and roughness give plausible PBR quickly. Unity's Book of the Dead forest used Quixel vegetation that came with LODs and wind vertex colours. Megascans is now free on Fab under the Fab Standard Licence, which allows any engine.Store each download in ArtSource/Downloads/<source>/<asset> with its licence text, URL and date. Repack for URP Lit: Metallic R plus Smoothness A (1 − roughness), and import the normal as a Normal Map. Use power-of-two BC7, generate LODs and rename to project names. Skip anything tagged UE-only, such as City Sample. For CC-BY, record author and link for the credits screen.Scans are heavy, often 10k–50k+ triangles and 4–8k textures. Decimate, re-bake and downsize.
Licensed characters and animation for crowds
Fill streets with pedestrians built from licensed character generators and animation libraries instead of bespoke rigs and mocap.
Pedestrian, traffic and vignette systems are what made Insomniac's Manhattan feel lived-in. Mixamo animations are royalty-free for commercial games. Since UE 5.6, MetaHumans are reportedly classed as non-engine products usable in Unity.Retarget Mixamo clips through Humanoid avatars. Give pedestrians 3 LODs plus a far crowd representation, either GPU Instancer Pro Crowd Animations or baked vertex-animation textures. Exported MetaHumans need heavy simplification for URP (hair cards, skin shader). Never ship raw Mixamo FBX files as standalone files, and keep licence records.Skinned meshes are expensive on both CPU and GPU. Cap the number of fully animated pedestrians near the camera and measure.

Art-direction lessons

  • Give each district 2–4 architectural styles, each built from one kit, one or two trim sheets and a district palette. Watch Dogs 2 represented 36 architectural styles across its Bay Area, so readable variety comes from styles, not unique buildings.
  • Hand-craft landmarks and hero intersections, and fill everything else procedurally. CDPR placed almost everything in Night City by hand, which needs a large team. Insomniac and Epic used rules for filler. A small team should hand-place only the top 5–10% of content.
  • For a driving game, put detail at street level: kerbs, drains, road patches, posters, shop signs and grime decals on the bottom 1–2 storeys. Upper floors can use cheaper trims and lower texel density.
  • Create variation with material overrides (tint, wear, wetness, grime) and decals instead of new textures, following CDPR's 'material overrides' and microblends.
  • Keep distant silhouettes and rooflines varied (water tanks, antennas, setbacks, cranes). GTA V's skyline holds up because the low-poly distant LOD keeps the shape even when detail is gone.
  • At night, drive window lights from an emissive mask strip in the trim sheet or HLOD atlas, randomised per instance. Keep neon saturated only in Neon Mile and Downtown so it stays a district signature.
  • Calibrate albedo: scanned and CC0 materials sit in physically plausible ranges. Do not tint them outside those ranges, or the GTA-style daylight realism breaks.
  • Fight art fatigue with mirrored or rotated kit variants, mixed storey counts per block, and decal sets that tell local stories (rent signs, graffiti, leaks), not uniform noise.
  • For nature, use few species with many size, tint and age variants, scattered by slope, altitude and moisture masks. A random scatter reads as fake, so let biomes follow terrain logic.

Caveats

  • Several primary pages could not be opened from this environment (connection refused): Unity support and Asset Store, Poly Haven, ambientCG, Textures.com and JangaFX. Their licence and price details come from search-result excerpts of those official pages. Re-check the live pages before buying or shipping.
  • Megascans 'free for good': the free-library news is dated 5–6 Nov 2026, with listings updating until 12 Nov 2026. Coverage disagrees on whether Epic committed to permanence. Each item's licence is fixed when you acquire it, so record the acquisition date and licence.
  • Fab UE-only content (Matrix Awakens City Sample buildings and vehicles, and some other Epic samples) is restricted to Unreal Engine and must not be used in a Unity project, even though it is on Fab.
  • MetaHuman: most 2025 coverage plus Epic's FAQ say use outside Unreal is allowed, but a March 2026 blog disputes it. Read the current Unreal EULA before relying on it.
  • Reallusion and ActorCore game-use terms are unclear: they mention Standard, Extended, Enterprise and an older free Mass Distribution licence. Get written confirmation from Reallusion before shipping.
  • SpeedTree's Indie revenue cap differs by source ($100k per Unity support, $200k per a third party; an old page says $1M). EmberGen's current prices are unconfirmed.
  • Prices for Asset Store tools (Crest, KWS2, GPU Instancer Pro, Nature Renderer, NatureManufacture) come from cached listing snapshots and change with sales; check the per-seat terms.
  • Unity 6 URP compatibility for Amplify Impostors, The Visual Engine and Nature Renderer 6 is not confirmed by the publishers for 6000.6; NatureManufacture's is from the listing only. Test each in a scratch project before importing into the main project.
  • No authoritative public figure for modern AAA triangles per frame or tree LOD triangle counts was found. The tree ratios come from vendor and Simplygon material. Measure in the Unity Profiler and RenderDoc on the 4070 at 1440p instead of assuming.
  • Distances (HLOD at 400–600 m, impostors at 150–250 m, decal draw distance), texel-density targets and memory budgets in the techniques are engineering recommendations, not cited figures. Validate them with review-camera screenshots and frame-time logs.
  • The GPU Resident Drawer can make GPU-bound frames slower, and one forum user saw worse FPS with it on. A/B test it on the actual Veyra review route.
  • Unity Mesh LOD conflicts with the project rule that meshes carry authored LOD0/1/2 with LODGroup. Use it only as a fallback for third-party props, and record any exception in PROJECT_STATUS.
  • GDC Vault talk contents (Spider-Man, Cyberpunk, Ghost of Tsushima) were available only as abstracts or secondary write-ups here. Detailed methods attributed to them are limited to what those summaries state.
  • This is a technical summary, not legal advice. Keep a licence file in ArtSource/Downloads for every third-party item, as the project rules require, and remove source brand names and logos from in-game content.

16. The plan: from prototype to a believable open world

Principles

  • Vertical slice first. Bring three places to the target before spreading out:
    • D6 Neon Mile at night (CAM_13)
    • Crown Avenue in D1 by day and at night (CAM_05, CAM_06, CAM_07)
    • the Blackridge mountain road by day (CAM_10)
    Every system gets proven there, then rolled out district by district: D1/D6, then D5, then D2, then D3/D7/D8.
  • Replace content; don't tune it. Stop iterating the procedural pines, tufts and box houses. Each remaining iteration buys very little. Put the time into modelled, textured, LOD'd assets made with the kit-and-trim-sheet method AAA studios use, or sourced under clean licences.
  • Order by look gained per millisecond and per week. Lighting, anti-aliasing and settings fixes are cheap and touch every pixel, so they go first. New content comes after the renderer can show it properly.
  • Keep the data as the authority. Every new building, tree, sign and prop sits on the Veyra placement data; generators fill in only the detail inside it.
  • Done means measured. Each phase ends with:
    • review captures at 07:30, 12:00, 18:45, 19:45, 22:00 and 22:00 wet
    • a 3-second motion clip per hero camera
    • a release-build stats.json within budget
    • a written critique against reference images

Phase 0: Stop the bleeding and measure properly (1-2 weeks)

  • Quick wins: every quick win in chapter 1, including TAA/STP back on, LOD bias 2, road patches off, the night-water grid removed, the floating frame fixed, rain speed and ripples, night exposure, and the pixel font.
  • One owner for quality settings. GameFlow.ApplySettings currently overwrites render scale, shadow distance and MSAA on the shared URP asset at runtime, so changes to the URP assets never reach gameplay. Move all tier values into a versioned ScriptableObject read by both VeyraQualityTiers and the settings menu.
  • Review rig:
    • capture times as above
    • WorldEnvironment.ViewCamera for fog and lights
    • hold each pose for 32 frames so STP converges
    • capture the gameplay backbuffer
    • seeded traffic and pedestrians (-reviewTraffic, -reviewLife)
    • new water, shoreline and D7 ground-level cameras
  • Measurement:
    • a Release IL2CPP baseline with traffic, parked cars and audio on, on a performance power plan
    • one run on a desktop RTX 4070
    • a decision on the uncommitted realtime reflection probes
  • Process: separate git worktrees for the world and vehicle sessions, commit or revert the ~86 pending entries, and a pre-commit check on ProjectSettings changes.
  • Legal: decide BUG-066 (real-brand cars) and stop using the rebadged car in captures.

Exit: no "Disabling TAA" log line. LOD bias 2 on every PC tier. All 18 cameras captured at the six times, with no near-black frame (mean luminance check). A release baseline is recorded in Logs/.

Phase 1 ("card zero"): shader parity and GPU-driven rendering (3-5 weeks)

The custom Veyra shaders (Facade on 92 % of the buildings, Road on every road, Water, Neon) compile none of the URP keywords that the next features depend on. Unless this is fixed first, APV, decals, cookies and the GPU Resident Drawer would skip most of the screen.

  • Add to VC_Facade, VC_Road, VC_Water, VC_Neon and the new foliage, grass and terrain shaders:
    • PROBE_VOLUMES_L1/L2 with SAMPLE_GI
    • _DBUFFER_MRT1/2/3 plus ApplyDecal
    • _LIGHT_COOKIES and _LIGHT_LAYERS
    • DOTS_INSTANCING_ON with a DOTS property block
    • a Meta pass (so surfaces bake albedo and emission into APV)
    • a MotionVectors pass (so vertex-animated water and foliage do not ghost under STP)
    Alternatively, port surfaces to Shader Graph Lit with custom functions.
  • Update VC_Variants.shadervariants, then measure the variant count and build time (BUG-106: keyword sets have cost more than an hour of compile before).
  • GPU Resident Drawer (Instanced Drawing) plus GPU occlusion culling, and BatchRendererGroup variants set to Keep All. Today draw calls average 3,823 and reach 6,476 at p99, against a 3,000 limit (ENG-02).
  • Mipmap streaming with a 2 GB budget on PC_High, and Read/Write off on generated meshes (ENG-07). Graphics memory is 5.0 GB today.
  • Runtime-generated dressing becomes editor bakers, so no meshes or materials are built at load (ENG-03): StreetFurniture, RooftopDetails, TrafficLights geometry, RoadWear and WaterSurface.

Exit: an APV, decal and cookie test scene shows all four VC shader families responding. Draw calls stay under 3,000 at p95 on R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT. CPU main thread under 10 ms at p95 in a release build.

Phase 2: Light the world (4-6 weeks)

  • Global illumination: APV with Day and Night lighting scenarios, scenario blending and sky occlusion, baked per cell (LGT-01). Emissive signs get BakedEmissive so the night scenario carries their coloured bounce.
  • Exposure and grading:
    • custom histogram auto exposure (LGT-02)
    • HDR colour grading with a LUT per time of day and per district (LGT-11)
    • an A/B of the Neutral and ACES tonemappers on the night set
  • Sky, fog and shadows:
    • analytic height fog with aerial perspective (LGT-03)
    • a physically based sky with a real cloud layer (LGT-06)
    • sun shadows to 300-350 m with rebalanced cascades and cloud shadows (LGT-07)
    • SSAO radius about 1.5 m with blue noise
  • Night lights: 128 importance-selected lights on High, cookies on lamps and headlights, sign-matched fill lights, and distant "LOD lights" for skylines (LGT-04, LGT-10).
  • Surfaces: DBuffer decals (RD-02), one wet layer in every shader (VFX-02), and reflection probes per block plus a low-resolution planar reflection for water. SSR is tried on a branch only until 6.7.
  • Volumetrics: volumetric fog with the main light and Forward+ lights (LGT-09), through an MIT URP package or an in-house froxel pass.

Exit: CAM_05, 06, 07 and 13 at golden hour and at night read correctly next to reference images (no black frames, no clipped emissives, wet streets reflecting signs). GPU time is within the budget table below.

Phase 3: Nature and water (6-10 weeks; parallel with Phase 4)

  • Trees:
    • a VC_Foliage shader: translucency, bent normals, wind in three layers, dithered alpha and wetness (VEG-02, -03, -06, -12)
    • new trees from SpeedTree or licensed scans: 8-25k triangles at LOD0, 3 LODs plus an octahedral impostor, and 3-4 species per district (VEG-01, VEG-07)
    • GPU-driven instancing with LOD chosen per instance (VEG-05, VEG-08)
    • undergrowth and forest floor (VEG-10)
  • Ground:
    • a land-cover map baked from the data (TER-03)
    • a GPU ground-cover renderer and grass shader, replacing Unity terrain details and their 50-165 ms hitches (TER-01, -02, -07)
    • a VC_Terrain shader with height blend, triplanar cliffs and macro maps (TER-04, -05)
    • scanned CC0 terrain sets (TER-06), and clutter and verge bands (TER-08, -09)
  • Water:
    • transparent water with absorption and refraction (WAT-03)
    • planar reflection plus the realtime sky cube (WAT-01, -02)
    • a wave clipmap, FFT or Gerstner (WAT-04), or Crest 5 if bought
    • shore and intersection foam (WAT-06), rain rings (WAT-07), and river flow (WAT-09)

Exit: CAM_10, CAM_11, CAM_14 and CAM_18 pass a side-by-side critique against reference images. Grass and trees fit their GPU budget, with no hitch over 50 ms on R_BRIDGES.

Phase 4: The city (10-16 weeks)

  • Facades and massing: FacadeAssembler, a modular facade and massing system on 2-3 shared trim sheets per district (BLD-01, -04, -05).
    • Real depth: 15-30 cm window reveals, sills, mullions, cornices and balconies.
    • Budgets: midrise 10-30k triangles and towers 30-80k at LOD0; LOD1 at 40 %, LOD2 at 10 % with baked facade pages.
  • House kit for the 1,923 suburban houses (BLD-02): 16 archetypes, 4-10k triangles each, with yard dressing.
  • Storefronts and rooftops: a recessed storefront set (BLD-03), a rooftop prop kit (BLD-11), interior mapping v2 (BLD-06), and night lighting for kits and tower crowns (BLD-07).
  • Ten hand-made landmarks on the registry points (BLD-08).
  • Street level:
    • a street furniture kit of 35-45 original props (RD-03)
    • SDF signage (RD-04), road signs, gantries and delineators (RD-06)
    • baked road wear and better asphalt and markings (RD-07..10)
  • Distance: HLOD v2 with baked facade atlases and impostors (ENG-06, BLD-13).
  • D7 Falcon Airfield venues: runway markings, hangars, the tower, grandstands, tyre walls and a ground-level review camera. The critic found that this race venue had no findings at all.

Exit: D1 and D6 fully rebuilt and passing the hero-camera critique. Triangles at p95 ≤ 8 M, SetPass at p95 ≤ 450, building textures ≤ about 1 GB (BC7).

Phase 5: Life, effects and sound (8-12 weeks; parts can start earlier)

  • Effects: install VFX Graph, then build rain with splashes and occlusion, six-way lit tyre smoke, spray, sparks and nitro, and steam vents in D6 (VFX-01, -03, -04, -05, -09).
  • People: rigged humans with a tiered crowd (LIFE-01, LIFE-08) and reactions (FEEL-03).
  • Traffic:
    • an original traffic fleet of 14 archetypes (LIFE-03, -06)
    • spinning, steering wheels with body motion (LIFE-04)
    • proper lamps with cookies (LIFE-05)
    • traffic behaviour: lane changes, yielding, horns (FEEL-04)
  • Audio:
    • recorded engine sound banks (FEEL-01)
    • ambience, weather, traffic and reverb zones (FEEL-02)
    • a mixer with snapshots (FEEL-09)
    • surface sounds (FEEL-10) and music (FEEL-05)
  • HUD: TextMeshPro fonts throughout (FEEL-07).

Exit: -reviewLife captures show at least the target counts of moving cars and people per hero camera. A 60-second capture clip per district carries sound.

GPU budget at 1440p on an RTX 4070 (allocation targets, all estimates until measured)

Measured today: GPU 8.5 ms average and 11.6 ms at p99 (laptop RTX 4070, Dev build, Logs/C1/Dev_R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT/stats.json). Draw calls are 3,823 average and 6,476 at p99, SetPass is 473 at p95, and graphics memory is 5.0 GB. The Appendix C limits are 13 ms GPU, 10 ms CPU main thread and 3,000 draw calls.

Running STP at render scale 0.87 instead of native 1.0 (with MSAA forced off) frees roughly a fifth to a quarter of the per-pixel cost, which pays for the features below. The GPU Resident Drawer and GPU occlusion pay the CPU side.

PassTarget ms (PC_High)Driven by
Opaque geometry (city, terrain, trees) and depth prepass4.0Phases 1, 3 and 4 (LODs, impostors, occlusion)
Sun shadows (4 cascades to 300-350 m) and about 8 shadowed spot lights1.8LGT-07, LGT-04
Grass and ground cover (alpha overdraw)1.5TER-01
SSAO, DBuffer decals, APV sampling1.2LGT-01, RD-02
Reflections (probes, low-res planar for water)1.0WAT-01, LGT-05
Volumetric fog (half resolution)1.2LGT-09
Transparent: water, VFX (rain, smoke, spray), glass0.9WAT-03, VFX-01
Post: STP/TAA, bloom, grading, exposure1.0IQ-01, LGT-02
Total, with about 0.4 ms margin12.6Limit 13 ms; p99 ≤ 16.6 ms frame

Every number is a planning allocation, not a measurement. Measure each feature with FrameCost markers on the desktop target and the laptop, in a release build, before accepting its card. If a pass goes over its line, it has to buy the time back elsewhere: lower its resolution, use fewer samples, or keep it to Ultra only.

Gaps the completeness critic found, and where they went

  • Shader feature parity was not priced by any area. It is now Phase 1, "card zero".
  • No cross-area frame budget. See the budget table above, and measure on a desktop 4070 as well as the laptop.
  • Licence and originality of the 214 kit buildings. See chapter 17. The plan replaces them in Phase 4.
  • D7 Falcon Airfield (the race and drift venue) had no findings. It is now in Phase 4, with a new ground-level review camera.

17. Assets, tools and licences

The realism gap is mostly a content gap. Below is what has to exist, whether to make it or source it, and the licence rules that go with it. CLAUDE.md applies throughout:

  • All content must be original or properly licensed.
  • No real brands and no ripped game assets.
  • Every download needs its licence file in ArtSource/Downloads and a row in the licence register.

Trees: 3-4 species per district, 3 variants each, 3 LODs plus impostorSpeedTree Modeler (you own the output)Scanned trees on Fab / Megascans; Asset Store nature packsSpeedTree Indie has a revenue cap (sources disagree; check). Fab Standard Licence allows any engine, but verify each item.
Ground: 10-12 terrain material sets, 4 bark setsSubstance DesignerPoly Haven and ambientCGCC0: no attribution needed. Still keep the licence text.
Buildings: 2-3 trim sheets per district, 6 style families of facade modules, storefronts, rooftop kit, 16 house archetypes, 10 landmarksBlender (vc_tools.py, VC_Unity_Prop and VC_Unity_World presets) and Substance PainterScanned surfaces (brick, concrete, plaster) as base texturesGeometry must be original. Do not use Fab "Unreal Engine only" content (for example the City Sample).
Decals: grime, leaks, stains, cracks, posters, graffiti, road markingsOriginal atlasesCC0 decal setsNo real brand names or logos on posters or signs.
Street furniture: 35-45 props; signals; road signs and gantriesBlender, original designsGeneric de-branded propsOriginal sign designs; no real road-sign typefaces under restrictive licences.
People: 8 base bodies, modular outfits, walk, idle and react clips—Microsoft Rocketbox avatars (MIT); Mixamo animationsMixamo is royalty-free for games. Reallusion/ActorCore game terms are unclear: get written confirmation. MetaHuman use outside Unreal is disputed: read the current EULA first.

Fonts: signage and HUD—OFL font familiesKeep the OFL text with the font; no font bundled under a desktop-only licence.
Audio: engine banks, tyres, impacts, ambience, rain, music, UIOriginal recordings and synthesis; commissioned musicCC0 sound libraries; commercial librariesAvoid NC licences. CC-BY sounds need credits.
Water, volumetrics, skyCustom URP renderer featuresCrest 5 or KWS2 (water); MIT URP volumetric light; MIT PB skyAsset Store EULA is per seat. For MIT packages, ship the licence text.

Legal risks to resolve now

  1. Real-brand cars (BUG-066, LIFE-02, LIFE-10). Five downloads are badge-stripped real production cars, and one of them is the default player and capture car. CC-BY 4.0 licenses the modeller's copyright only. It explicitly excludes trademark rights and cannot license the manufacturer's body design. A further 17 "homage" models came with brand-style badges. Recommendation:
    • drop the five
    • keep the 17 out of hero, marketing and capture use until each has a likeness review
    • move to the original line-up
  2. Kit buildings. The 214 kits come from CC-BY packs, some of which replicate New York, San Francisco, Chicago and Atlanta. They may carry real landmarks, shop names or logos. Uploader provenance is unverified for some "game-ready city" packs. The in-game credits currently list only vehicles, but CC-BY requires attribution for every pack. Recommendation:
    • audit the textures and silhouettes
    • generate the credits screen from the licence register
    • replace the kits with the original modular system in Phase 4
  3. Megascans and Fab. As of November 2026 the Megascans library is reported free on Fab under the Fab Standard Licence, which allows any engine. Reports disagree on whether that is permanent. The licence is fixed per item when you acquire it, so record the date and keep the licence page with each download.
