Texel-density standard for Veyra:

  • ground-floor facades, roads and kerbs: 512 px/m
  • upper floors through trims: 256 px/m
  • terrain: 256-512 px/m plus detail maps
  • hero props: up to 1,024 px/m

Check density in Blender before export.

18. Development process: working so the look keeps improving

The main lesson from the last two days of logs

The visual and world iteration logs record 57 rounds of capture, critique and fix. Most of them tuned procedural placeholders: pine brightness, needle stroke width, tuft height, patch strength, pool intensity. Each round was well done and well evidenced, but a better placeholder is still a placeholder.

The scores barely moved, because the limit is the content and the lighting model, not the parameters. From now on, an iteration should replace something (a shader with the full URP feature set, a real asset, a new system) rather than retune it.

Process changes

  • One session per working tree (ENG-10). The world and vehicle sessions share one tree, and about 86 entries are uncommitted. That is how a debug LOD bias of 0.4 and realtime reflection probes slipped into the quality settings unnoticed (IQ-02, ENG-M1).
    • Use a git worktree and branch per session, merged through pull requests.
    • Add a pre-commit check that rejects ProjectSettings changes a card does not list.
    • Commit after every passed card, as CLAUDE.md requires.
  • Review captures that match what players see (LGT-08, IQ-08, LIFE-07, FEEL-13):
    • six times of day instead of two
    • capture the gameplay backbuffer after 32 frames of STP history
    • the fog and lights follow the review camera
    • seeded traffic, pedestrians and HUD in the street cameras
    • a 3-second motion clip per hero camera, so shimmer and LOD pops are reviewed
    • a luminance check that fails near-black or white-out frames
  • Performance numbers you can trust (ENG-08):
    • Release IL2CPP builds
    • gameplay systems on
    • performance power plan on AC power
    • no stray game windows
    • VSync off
    • one regular run on a desktop RTX 4070 as well as the laptop
    Keep the per-pass FrameCost markers, and gate each card on the budget table in chapter 16.
  • Editor tools that keep GUIDs (ENG-11): overwrite mesh and texture assets in place and skip unchanged content, so rebuilds stop churning GUIDs and Git LFS.
  • Smaller classes (ENG-12): split GameFlow (about 125 KB of code) into WorldBoot, SessionFlow, GarageFlow, MenuFlow and SettingsService. Make the world systems scene-authored components.
  • A reference board. Every folder under ReferenceImages/ is empty, so critiques compare against memory. Fill it with reference captures made legally (your own screenshots from the games, press-kit images) for side-by-side comparison only. They never enter Assets/.
  • An art-direction checklist for every new asset:
    • real-world scale (door 2.1 m, storey 3.0-4.2 m, kerb 0.15 m, leaf 5-15 cm)
    • texel density
    • albedo range (the palette validator already exists)
    • no visible repetition at 50 m
    • contact with the ground (AO, decals, grime band)
    • a night look, a wet look, and LODs

Definition of done for any visual card

  1. Review captures at 07:30, 12:00, 18:45, 19:45, 22:00 and 22:00 wet, plus motion clips, saved under Screenshots/<date>_<card>/.
  2. A written critique against the reference board, naming the remaining gap.
  3. Release-build stats.json for R_SMOKE, R_BRIDGES and R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT within the budget table, with 0 errors and 0 pink materials.
  4. EditMode tests green, zero new warnings, and the variant count and build time recorded for shader changes.
  5. PROJECT_STATUS and BUG_TRACKER updated, then a commit.

Contradictions the critic found (resolved in the plan)

  • AA path: IQ-01 restores STP/TAA by removing the runtime MSAA, while VEG-03 blames speckled crowns on running 'without MSAA', and the research index lists both 'MSAA + alpha-to-coverage' and 'STP/TAA upscaling'. URP cannot run STP/TAA with MSAA, and no shader uses AlphaToMask (grep: 0 hits). Gameplay at the default Quality 2 (GameData.cs:53) gets MSAA 4x from GameFlow.cs:1063-1071, while the PC_High asset sets MSAA 1 with STP (VC_URP_PC_High.asset:28,30; VeyraQualityTiers.cs:134). Pick one: STP with MSAA off, plus coverage-preserving foliage mips and dithered alpha.
  • Settings ownership: LGT-07, IQ-09, IQ-M1 and IQ-01 propose fixes in the URP assets, but GameFlow.cs:1066-1071 overwrites renderScale (1.0), shadowDistance (160/240) and msaaSampleCount (4) on the shared URP asset at runtime. The asset's own values (renderScale 0.9, STP, 160 m) never reach gameplay, so asset-only fixes will show no change. One owner, either VeyraQualityTiers or the settings menu, must hold these values.
  • Reflections: ENG-M1 flags the realtime reflection probes switched on in the uncommitted QualitySettings (realtimeReflectionProbes 0→1 at three levels, git diff) as unmeasured cost. LGT-05, WAT-01 and RD-05 want denser or more reflections, and the GTA research proposes a per-frame environment cubemap. The plan needs one decision: for example, one camera-following, time-sliced realtime probe plus baked box probes per block, with its cost in ms.
  • WAT-01 planar water reflection against ENG-02: draw calls are already 3,823 avg / 6,476 p99 (Logs/C1/Dev_R_DOWNTOWN_NIGHT/stats.json), over the Appendix C limit of 3,000. A full planar reflection camera re-submits the scene. It must be low-res with a culled layer mask (HLOD and sky only), or the plan uses SSR plus probes instead.
  • Grass: TER-01 and TER-11 ask for denser grass, out to longer range, but TER-07 and ENG-M2 show that Unity terrain-detail patch generation already causes 50-165 ms frames (PROJECT_STATUS.md:45). Densifying with the current system makes the hitches worse, so the switch to GPU-instanced grass must come first.
  • Kit counts: ENG-04 says 'only 70 of 2,708 use kits' (stale, PROJECT_STATUS.md:45), while BLD-07, BLD-12 and BLD-M1 say 214 kits. Docs/WORLD_ITERATION_LOG.md:13 and :26 confirm 214 kits (208 stretched) after iteration 1.
  • Review warm-up: VEG-M2 says stills capture STP 'after one warm-up frame', while IQ-08 says '3 frames of temporal history'. The code (VeyraReviewCapture.cs:170-171) does 2 warm-ups plus 1 render, so IQ-08 is right. Both agree that the stills do not show a converged gameplay image.

Findings the critic flagged as thin

  • ENG-04: the '70 of 2,708 buildings use kits' figure is stale (PROJECT_STATUS.md:45); the current count is 214 (WORLD_ITERATION_LOG.md:13). Restate it with current data or merge it into BLD-01 and BLD-12.
  • VEG-03: the premise 'without MSAA' is wrong for gameplay, which runs MSAA 4x at the default Quality 2 (GameFlow.cs:1063-1071). The evidence is review stills on the STP path with 3 frames of history (ReviewCameraRig.cs:119, VeyraReviewCapture.cs:170), so the speckle needs a gameplay-path capture before it drives the AA choice.
  • VEG-M2: says 'one warm-up frame', but the code does two warm-ups plus the render (VeyraReviewCapture.cs:170-171). It duplicates IQ-08.
  • IQ-11: banding is only a 'risk', with no observed banded frame cited. R11G11B10 is the standard HDR format in AAA games, and night banding more likely comes from LDR grading and 8-bit output (LGT-11). The cheap fix is camera dithering, not a 64-bit colour buffer that costs bandwidth.
  • WAT-11: overstated. CAM_03 and CAM_04 already frame the sea as the lower ~40 % of the image at 12h (contact_sheet tiles 3-4), and CAM_18 shows the lake. The real gap is a shoreline, at-surface camera and a night water shot with city lights.
  • ENG-08: 'perf numbers are unreliable' is too broad. Only the CPU side was distorted (Silent power plan, BUG-120). The GPU timings (FrameTimingManager gpu_ms 8.5 avg / 11.6 p99) and the draw-call and SetPass counts in Logs/C1/*/stats.json are usable for budgeting, although they come from a Laptop 4070, not the desktop target.
  • Duplicates to count once in the plan (each pair or group is the same issue across domains): BLD-09 = BLD-M3 (BUG-123); BLD-12 = BLD-M1; LGT-07 = IQ-09 = IQ-M1; ENG-01 = IQ-02; TER-07 = ENG-M2; VEG-12 = VEG-M1; WAT-07 = WAT-M1; VFX-10 = VFX-M2; RD-04 = IQ-06 (and the font half of BLD-10); RD-07 = RD-M1; LIFE-01 = FEEL-03; RD-05 overlaps VFX-07 and LGT-05.
